# Gnomon — Paradigm 8.0 build spec (the kernel and shell beneath the armada)

Status: BUILD SPEC v3.9, Move 1 in build (PR-0..PR-5 landed on `feat/gnomon`: scaffold, probes, `gnomon-contracts` core, the kernel log, the daemon-lifecycle core; the kernel log now carries the D82–D85 anchor policy, reviewed); M1.0 probes run on macOS; CI green on macOS, Ubuntu and Windows (run 39073693925); the run modes of §3.4 are built (`manual`, `ask`, `auto-edits`), strictness levels are not, so until M2.10 the mode ceiling permits all three modes. Decision TD-2026-10-16-950 (the ten rulings) · founder rulings P8-D12..D88, requirements P8-R1..R9 (lore L-2026-10-16, tag `paradigm-8`; D54–D57 in L-2026-10-16-ascend-223456-001; D58 in L-2026-10-17-ascend-061036-001; D59–D63 in L-2026-10-17-ascend-063541-001; D68–D72 in L-2026-10-17-ascend-075830-001; R9 in L-2026-10-17-ascend-080105-001; D73–D76 in L-2026-10-17-ascend-081146-001; D77 in L-2026-10-17-ascend-084643-001; D78–D79 in L-2026-10-17-ascend-084905-001; D80–D81 in L-2026-10-17-ascend-172952-001; D82 in L-2026-10-17-ascend-183438-001; D83 in L-2026-10-17-ascend-191448-001; D84–D85 in L-2026-10-17-ascend-210023-001; D86–D88 in L-2026-10-17-ascend-230742-001; the D82/D83/D85 builds and review rounds in L-2026-10-17-ascend-190536-001, L-2026-10-17-ascend-210158-001 and L-2026-10-17-ascend-214000-001; the first all-green cross-OS CI in L-2026-10-17-ascend-220955-001; PR-4 builder notes in L-2026-10-17-ascend-180323-001; first probe evidence in L-2026-10-17-ascend-083200-001 and L-2026-10-17-ascend-084150-001, results in `packages/gnomon/probes/results/*.darwin.json`) · Atelier sessions `.atelier/sessions/2026-10-15-paradigm-8-spec/session.yaml` and `.atelier/sessions/2026-10-15-gnomon-aesthetic/session.yaml` (renders in its `refs/`: `walkthrough.html`, `papercraft.html`, `styles.html`, `workshop-mod.html`) · Research `.paradigm/research/paradigm-8/00–36` (06/12 Loid, 14/16/22/25/26/30/32/33/34/36 Aegis; 34 is the S34 review series of the D83–D85 kernel-log builds, which has no file and lives in the lore above and tasks T-2026-10-17-009, -012, -013 and -014; 15/17/23/24/27/29/31/35 Arky, 18 QM, 19/20 voice, 21 shell + mods, 28 librarian panel) · Atelier playbook marks `a-atelier/docs/playbook/review-marks-v0.md` (G1, E12) · First-party mod concept docs `docs/specs/gnomon-mods/gnome-persona.md`, `docs/specs/gnomon-mods/doodle.md` (proposals; where they differ from this spec, this spec wins).
Packages: `packages/gnomon` (`@a-company/gnomon`: kernel, the `gnomon-web` and `gnomon-link` transport children, adapters, CLI), `packages/gnomon-contracts` (the published client API), `packages/gnomon-gui` (the localhost GUI page), `packages/gnomon-wrapper` (the Tauri desktop wrapper, the default daily surface), `packages/gnomon-mod-sdk` (incl. the userland `gnomon-mods` MCP bridge), `packages/gnomon-mods` (first-party mods: `workshop`, `gnome-persona`, `doodle`, `knowledge`, `cowork`), `packages/gnomon-local`, `packages/gnomon-voice`, `packages/gnomon-helper` (signed native helpers), `packages/gnomon-relay` (the self-hosted link relay, zero deps, §3.9). Branch `ideate/paradigm-8` → `feat/gnomon`.

Reads with: `warpline-v3-identity.md`, `warpline-fabric-schema-v2.md`, `warpline-engine.md`, `warpline-forge.md` G1–G5.
Audience: 8.0 is for the founder (dog-fooded on real repos, on macOS AND Windows, iterated live); 8.1 is for the world (P8-D1). "8.1" marks what is deliberately out of this build; §21 holds the team roadmap.

This document is itself a contract source (§7, P8-R2 dog-food): every component, flow, gate, signal and aspect named here as a whole code span with its symbol prefix is a node the compiler (§7.1) must extract, and every node carries an oracle (a test id, or an explicit `exists` declaration). A node without an oracle is a compile refusal. Appendix A is the hand-written expectation; honesty test `G-HON-01` is that the compiler reproduces it.

Vocabulary rule (TD-950 §1): no swarm, hive, queen, consensus or neural anywhere. Collective noun **armada**; a running session is a **session**; a spawned set is a **fleet**. Labels never imply a mechanism the code lacks (law 2).

Platform rule (P8-R4): everything runs on macOS and Windows (Linux follows macOS where POSIX suffices). Every platform-specific mechanism names its Windows counterpart in §16; every `G-` test touching process, file, endpoint, key or sandbox behaviour runs on both OS matrices, and platform-independent tests run on Windows CI from day one (`~windows-parity`). Under P8-D78 the Windows-specific work is built and passed in a later **Windows parity phase** (§19.1); that sequences the platforms and narrows no claim.

---

## 0. Changes in v3.9 (compile: skip)

v3.9 lands in two passes. **Part 1** (the rows up to the first Judgment calls row) folds in what is now built and reviewed in the kernel log (`packages/gnomon/src/kernel/log/`: `kernel-log.ts`, `anchor.ts`, `anchor-policy.ts`, `boot.ts`; commits through `874f6ace`). It also folds in Aegis's S33-10 addendum to research 33, founder rulings **D83–D85** (L-2026-10-17-ascend-191448-001, L-2026-10-17-ascend-210023-001), the S34 review findings, the human-cadence probe, and the first all-green cross-OS CI. **Part 2** adds the T-2026-10-17-004 design (armada review convergence, evidence-driven gate tightening, test/risk/revert fields on briefs and cards) from research 35 (Arky, A35-01..20) and 36 (Aegis, S35-01..11, CB1–CB6), as resolved by founder rulings **D86–D88** (L-2026-10-17-ascend-230742-001); where the two notes differ, the rulings win, then this table. The tables of v3.8 and earlier are kept as §0.1–§0.8 below as history, and this table wins where they differ. Part 1 adds one test id (`G-LOG-11`) and no node. Part 2 adds twenty test ids (`G-STR-01..10`, `G-REV-01..05`, `G-CHG-01..05`), two telemetry ids (`T-REV-01`, `T-STR-01`) and no node.

| Id | What changed |
|---|---|
| P8-D89 (M1.4 slice 3, the one batched `clientApiId` move) | Registers every reason the kernel socket sends (the slice-3 read-token, `gone` and `log.append` ownership reasons, and the slice-2 `log.read` / `ref.resolve` reasons) and the client-local `hello-not-offered`; `key.registry.malformed` may carry `{file, reason}`; adds ENGINE to `log.append`'s refusals; reserves `daemon.log.quarantined` and adds `key.token.revoked`; read tokens become proof of possession (P8-D89a, §4.9); §9.3's limited stub drops `hash`. `clientApiId` moves once, from `sha256:8e30f400…3014` to `sha256:fd576e6d…e0fd`. |
| P8-D90 (link transport: the kernel dials, the child gets the socket) | Probe P14 failed on macOS as written: SBPL compiles only `*` or `localhost` as a `network-outbound` host, so a relay `ip:port` allow-list cannot be expressed; `*:P` admits any host on port P, and `localhost:P` also admitted the Mac's en0 address (research 37). Every other P14 refusal held. Option (b) is adopted: the kernel dials the relay `ip:port` from root-signed config and hands the connected socket to `gnomon-link` over its IPC channel (fd passing, Node's built-in handle passing, zero dependencies). The child profile has no `network-outbound` and no `network-bind`; to redial, the child asks the kernel. Windows passes the socket the same way (libuv duplication), so the child token or AppContainer carries no network capability and the S29-08 allow-list is needed only if P14b-w fails. Probe **P14b** (`link-fdpass`) passed on macOS, so the fallback (f), port-only `*:P` with a §A.5(h) reach residual for founder ratification, is unneeded on macOS and §A.5 is unchanged. `G-LNK-11` becomes the stronger claim: the child opens no network connection, the relay address included. M2.8 absorbs the kernel dialer, the IPC handle pass, the reconnect-request frame on the child channel and the profile-generator change; M1.7's spawner only allows an IPC fd for this child class, and no Move 1 contract changes (§3.9, §15.5, §16, §19.1). |
| M1.8 gnomon-local, zero-dep (ratified by the founder 2026-10-18, P8-D95) | M1.8 slice 1 shipped `gnomon-local` with zero runtime dependencies: it re-derives pi's `--mode rpc` JSONL shape (unverified against pi's `rpc-entry.ts`), the agent loop, argument repair and the text-tool fallback instead of building on `pi-agent-core` + `pi-ai`; the compat allowlist is the local transports only. §13.1 and §18 are amended to match; the founder ratifies or reverts. |
| P8-D92 (track everything, enforce nothing; L-2026-10-18-ascend-041752-001) | Founder ruling: the budget fuse becomes a **meter**. Every `permit.reserve` is granted with a kernel-signed `permit:v1` (law 1 keeps attribution: no spawn without a permit), except law 7: `BILLING_REFUSED` and the per-use api-token card stand exactly as before. Removed: token/USD day caps, compute and concurrency caps, the subscription-window threshold and its 30 % human reserve (incl. the user-rung reserve and armada slot cap of M1.6 round 3), the `quota.error` breaker pause, the armada pause and its `budget` card, the resume allowance. Kept and strengthened: usage per user (P8-D91 one bucket), repo, session, model and backend, local-LLM sessions with model and projects, the 50/75/90/100 % ladder as informational `budget.ladder` rows, subscription-window usage measured and shown in `status`, quota errors recorded. The root-signed config keeps only tracking/display keys; a bad config falls back to the defaults with a logged row. Law 5's opt-in for unattended automations is unchanged (the scheduler still refuses them). §3.4, §10 (laws 1 and 7), §19.1 M1.6 and OPEN 6 are amended. |
| P8-D93 (T-2026-10-18-016, the next batched `clientApiId` move, per P8-D89's batching rule) | Two changes in one move. **(1) hello client nonce** (security gate before M1.3 Reconnect and the wrapper): `hello` params gain a required `clientNonce` (unpadded base64url of 16–64 random bytes; clients send 32), the result echoes it, and `serverSig`'s message becomes `nonce, clientNonce, endpointPath, clientApi, canonical(features)`. The server-signature domain moves to `gnomon:auth-server:v2` (Aegis round 2), so no v1-domain signature verifies under the new message. The auth wire format is a `clientApiId` input (Aegis round 3): the three domains (`gnomon:auth-server:v2`, `gnomon:auth:v1`, `gnomon:auth-token:v1`) and one fixed preimage vector per auth form (server, device, read token) are in the client-API descriptor, so changing a domain or a message layout moves the id; the kernel signer takes its three auth domains from the same contracts constants. Before, `serverSig` covered only a nonce the server chose, so a same-user process could record one genuine hello and replay it from a squatted endpoint. Every client (TS client, CLI `connectGnomon`, GUI) refuses a hello that does not echo its own nonce (`client-nonce-mismatch`, client-local) and verifies the signature over the nonce it sent. The conformance check `hello.server-sig-mismatch` case (c) now replays a real recorded hello, verbatim and with the echo rewritten. The required `clientNonce` is an explicit exception to the §4.9 compatible-additive rule ("never adds a required param"): a pre-release batched `clientApiId` move may add a required v1 param while nothing has shipped against v1. **(2) typed `session.compacted{hsid, turn, reason, summaryHash, tokensBefore, tokensAfter}`**: kernel-owned, `normal`, `summaryHash` a `sensitive` field, `reason` a closed enum (threshold, overflow, manual, unspecified; round 2), token counts safe non-negative integers, payload closed and checked at append; the local runner's successful `compaction_end` maps onto it, and the raw `turn.event` line stays. `clientApiId` `sha256:fd576e6d…e0fd` → `sha256:1faf47f5867f17b95b12922bde7ec21890970f24154ef43e52c06c33e43d8720`; `kitId` and `abiId` (`sha256:18357f44…`) unchanged (§3.2, §4.9, §9.1, §13.1). |
| P8-D94 (hooks inside sessions, founder ruling 2026-10-18) | Sessions load the founder's user-level hooks AND a repo's project/local hooks, but repo hooks are **audited**: the kernel extracts them (the session never reads them raw), shows them on a card one line per hook (install-card rendering, `COMMAND_UNSAFE` refused), and passes only the approved set, pinned by contentHash per repo; a changed set re-raises the card; until a decision exists repo hooks do not run (fail closed). Deny per hook now; edit-in-place deferred. Built in M1.7 slice 2. |
| P8-D95 (founder ratifications, 2026-10-18) | Ratified: P8-D89 (the first batched `clientApiId` move), P8-D89a (read tokens are proof-of-possession; sticky revocations), P8-D89b (`/blob` and `/diag` POST-only), P8-D93 (hello client nonce, `gnomon:auth-server:v2`, typed `session.compacted`, auth wire format as an id input), the §A.7 transport-child residual (narrowed by T-2026-10-18-020), and `gnomon-local` as a zero-dependency re-derivation of pi's rpc shape (revisit before Move 2). |
| P8-D96 (the tool-permission channel, 2026-10-18) | Sessions run `--permission-prompts host --permission-prompt-tool stdio`, not `none`, so Edit, Write and Bash prompts reach the kernel as stream-json `can_use_tool` control requests and become GUI cards (Touch ID, M1.3). Probe p01b (real Claude Code 2.1.302 under the generated session profile, pass) measured the following. `host` alone delivers nothing. No `initialize` handshake is needed. The request shape is `{request_id, request:{subtype, tool_name, display_name, input, description?, permission_suggestions?, decision_reason?, requires_user_interaction?, matched_ask_rule?, tool_use_id}}`. Allow runs the tool with `updatedInput`; deny does not run it and `permission_denials` counts it. With no answer Claude waits (no timeout, no cancel); closing stdin denies. An interrupt while a request is pending sends `control_cancel_request`. Forged lines from a Bash child (stdout, `/dev/fd/1`, `/dev/stdout`, fds 3–9) appear only JSON-escaped inside tool output. An allow rule in any layer and a PreToolUse allow hook both skip the request (residual). Rejected alternative: a kernel `PermissionRequest` hook in `policy.json`, which costs a process per prompt and adds a second channel. The card is the gate (§3.4). No `clientApiId` move: the `permission` card kind, its decisions, the event kinds and `approve.note` already exist. |
| P8-D97 (repo context loads normally; hooks stay audited, founder ruling 2026-10-18) | A session opened in a repo loads that repo's `CLAUDE.md` (with its `@` imports), `.claude/skills`, `.claude/agents` and `.claude/commands` as plain Claude Code would, and every repo hook stays audited per P8-D94: settings hooks and the `hooks:` in skill, agent or command frontmatter. Probe p10m-repo-context (real Claude Code 2.1.303 under the generated session profile, pass) measured four candidates. Loading every setting source with `disableAllHooks` in the kernel `--settings` also silences the kernel's own hooks, and it would load the repo's `permissions.allow` and env, so it is rejected. Copies in an `--add-dir` do not load while the project source is off, so that is rejected too. A kernel-generated `--plugin-dir` loads skills, agents and commands, but an unstripped plugin skill's frontmatter hooks fire, so stripping is load-bearing. `--add-dir <repo>` with `CLAUDE_CODE_ADDITIONAL_DIRECTORIES_CLAUDE_MD=1` loads the memory without the repo's skills or hooks. Built: `audited` keeps `--setting-sources user`, adds `--add-dir` for each memory dir (the cwd and its ancestors up to the git top-level, never the user home) with that env flag, and adds a session-owned plugin named `project` that the kernel copies with frontmatter allow-listed. `hooks`, `allowed-tools`, `permissionMode`, `mcpServers` and unknown keys are dropped. Symlinks and unparseable frontmatter refuse the item, and the copy is capped (1024 files, 16 MiB, 2 MiB per file). Approved repo hooks still ride the kernel `--settings`. Decided (asked to decide and report): the repo's non-hook settings (permissions, env and the rest) are **not** loaded. A repo `allow` rule would skip the P8-D96 card, and a malformed repo rule merged into `policy.json` could make Claude drop the whole kernel policy. They appear as an informational line on each repo-hook card and on `gate-class.detected{what:'repo-context'}`, which carries counts and key names, never values. Frontmatter hooks are stripped and listed, not approvable, in this slice. Residuals: plugin items are namespaced (`project:x`, `/project:z`); the copy is a snapshot taken at open, so mid-session edits do not reload; `CLAUDE.local.md` and nested on-demand `CLAUDE.md` files are not loaded. No `clientApiId` move. Test id `G-SUB-08`. |
| TD-2026-10-19-908 (Orca Adapt item 7, kernel updates; T-2026-10-19-014) | Sessions survive a kernel update by **drain and resume**, built before dogfood GA. The old kernel stops starting turns, drains each session to a turn boundary (interrupting after a deadline), writes `session.drained{uuid, reason:'upgrade'}` and exits on the `gnomon daemon stop` path with reason `upgrade` (no new verb); the new kernel respawns each drained session with `--resume <uuid>` through the respawn path, re-running preflight, `layersHash` and permits. Cached unlocks and voice arms never carry over; permits are re-attributed, never minted (a permit live at the drain gets a fresh window); the old kernel's pending cards expire `cancelled` (TD-2026-10-19-476). Per-client feature gates ride the signed `hello` features; a missing feature refuses, and no feature can switch a check off. Claude sessions first, `gnomon-local` second. §2.4 rule 4 and `G-DMN-04` are amended (a drained session is not an orphan) and rule 11 is added. Live handover stays an unprobed idea after Move 2 (§22 item 25). |
| TD-2026-10-19-648 (Orca Adapt items 8, 10, 11, 12) | **(8)** the `log.read` cursor becomes `{logId, seq}`; a cursor from another log is `BAD_REQUEST{reason:'cursor-epoch'}`; limited views carry `seq` only (§5, §9.3), in the T-2026-10-19-001 move. **(11)** `claimGen` on `contract.claimed` and the `CAS_LOST` reasons `stale-claim`, `claim-expired`, `claim-superseded` are reserved and recorded in the kernel log; built in M2.2 (§7.4). **(12)** each side of a live link adds a kernel-key signature over the Noise handshake hash, verified only against the key ids pinned in `peers.jsonl`; built in M2.8 (§3.9). **(10)** `gnomon guide <topic>`: a CLI-only, pull-only verb whose topics are compiled into the signed binary, with a CI honesty check against the descriptors; built before Move 2 (T-2026-10-19-015, §5). The same move reserves the hello features `session.resume` and `journal.cursor` and the kind `session.drained`. |
| TD-2026-10-19-252 (Orca Adapt item 9, backends) | Claude and local models only. Other agents (opencode, grok, …) get specific support later, each with its own law-7 billing ruling; there is no generic ACP passthrough (§1, §2.2, §22 item 26). |
| TD-2026-10-19-946 (Orca Adapt item 13, approvals away from the Mac) | Approvals happen only on the Mac until enrolled approval devices land, well after the core loop is tested and after item 12. Each device holds its own non-exportable hardware key, is enrolled from the Mac with root presence (a root-signed registry row), takes a fresh biometric check on every decision, has its own signature domain and presence value (never a hand decision) and is revoked by a root-signed row. The bearer-token model (a phone token as approval authority with no biometric) is rejected (§22 items 23, 24). |
| P8-D98 (T-2026-10-19-001, the batched `clientApiId` move for TD-2026-10-19-528, TD-2026-10-19-648 and TD-2026-10-19-908) | One move reserves every wire value the drain-and-resume, cursor, claim and rules work needs, so the slices that build them never move the id again. **Human-device verbs** `gui.open{cwdHint?}`, `rules.revoke{ruleId}` and `daemon.stop{reason?}` (TD-2026-10-19-339: `reason` is `stop`, the default, or `upgrade`, the §2.4 rule 11 drain (built in slice B1); any other value is `BAD_REQUEST`; the verb raises the kernel's signed `daemon-stop` card and returns `{cardId, state}`): callable only by a human principal on a device connection whose `hello` verified the kernel key; every other class, a read token, a peer and a GUI launch-token connection are refused `FORBIDDEN{matrix}`; until a handler lands they refuse `ENGINE{not-implemented}`. They are counted apart from the twelve kernel verbs (law 3 now reads 12 kernel verbs + 3 human-device verbs + 2 client relays), and `abiId` still hashes the twelve. **Kinds** (kernel-owned, `normal`, each with a closed payload check, an extra field refused): `session.drained{uuid, reason}` (reason `upgrade` only), `permit.reattributed{permitId, hsid, drainedEventId}` (the kernel-signed row of §2.4 rule 11 that cites the drained row), `approval.rule.revoked{ruleId, repoKey, tool, connId}` and `approval.effect.failed{cardId, decision, effect, reason}` (kebab-case codes, never free text). **Fields**: `claimGen` on `contract.claimed`; `match` on `approval.rule.learned`, which accepts only `exact`; `approval.expire{why:'session-drained'}`. **Reasons**: `CAS_LOST` `stale-claim`, `claim-expired`, `claim-superseded`; `BAD_REQUEST{reason:'cursor-epoch'}`; `ENGINE{reason:'draining'}` for a turn or open refused during a drain. **Card kind** `daemon-stop`. **hello features** `journal.cursor` and `session.resume` are reserved; the kernel advertises neither until it builds them. The `log.read` cursor type is `{logId, seq}`. **decisionSig v2**: a message whose first line is `gnomon:decision:v2`, then `cardId, decision, cardContentHash, approverPrincipalId, answerDigest`, used when the decision is `answer` (one fixed vector is in the client-API descriptor; the kernel signer adopts it in a later slice). Founder rulings (TD-2026-10-19-528): **F1** `rules.revoke` is a verified-human-device socket verb with no Touch ID, because revoking only narrows; **F2** an always-rule's `match` accepts only `exact`; **F3** `gnomon daemon stop` raises a signed `daemon-stop` card decided with Touch ID; **F4** at a drain, session-bound cards expire with why `session-drained`; cards bound to no session expire `cancelled` at the new kernel's start (amended by TD-2026-10-19-476; an install card is raised again from its staged record). **#trace-capture** (TD-2026-10-19-057, Aegis M5, inside the same unreleased move): `session.sandbox{traced, traceRefused}` reserved, `traceRefused` closed to `trace-unsafe` and `trace-unavailable` (`session.sandbox.traceRefused`). `clientApiId` `sha256:1faf47f5…8720` → `sha256:f27ecb8b3ee5c1a4ad08d4c5eda427a63aebba8ba31963baed3c9d53e3e611a4`; `kitId` and `modAbiId` unchanged (§2.4, §3.4, §5, §7.4, §10). |
| TD-2026-10-19-194 (Gnomon voice slice V0, T-2026-10-19-026) | Contracts and spec only; no kernel behaviour changes and `clientApiId` / `abiId` do not move (a golden test pins both). **(1) Allowlist:** the voice-decidable (card kind, decision) pairs per class are pure data in `gnomon-contracts` (`VOICE_ALLOWLIST`, `voiceMayDecide`), default deny, and are the authoritative hand-only mechanism (§14.7); `classes.ts` says how a hand decision is taken, never whether voice may take it. **(2) Disarm:** any `approval.mode.set`, tighten or loosen, repo or session scope, ends every arm (§14.7). **(3) Drain:** the drain leaves `status.arms` empty and kills the voice child (§2.4 rule 11). **(4) Custody:** no `armCustody: client` fallback on macOS; if the M4.1 TCC probe (P1) fails, Mac voice stays talk-only until the helper gains a peer-PID + code-signature op (§14.7, §16, §19.1, §A.4). **(5) Binary pin:** one signed `gnomon-voice` executable; the pinned hash is a manifest over executable, native addon and model files; spawned only from a kernel-owned 0700 directory and re-hashed before every spawn (§14.7). **(6) Null level:** with no strictness level, voice is toggle 1 at most, no gate arm opens and `enable --gate` is refused (§14.7). **(7) Run modes:** in `manual` voice may only deny; under `auto-edits` a voice-originated turn behaves like a typed turn (founder ruling, no clamp). **(8) Forwarding guards:** a voice `session.send` must cite a logged, enrolled, push-to-talk `voice.turn.user` whose `t0` lies outside agent playback + `echoTailMs`; it rides the attached session's permit; a human attaches voice to a session by a GUI gesture (§14.7). **Aegis fixes and founder rulings (V0 round 2):** `incident` and `daemon-stop` are fully hand-only by voice, deny included (an exception to D66); a disarm withdraws every held card of that arm and the broker re-checks the arm and the effective policy at `commitAt`; `voice-strictness` carries `data.ceiling`; the voice directory is write-denied in every sandbox profile and the child loads models from the bytes the kernel verified. **The one deliberate `clientApiId` move for voice, before any client ships:** the closed `VOICE_DISARM_REASONS` (`approval.voice.disarm.reason`, incl. `daemon-exit`), the `voice-strictness` ceilings `null-level`/`manual`, and the status fields `arms` and `armCustody` are reserved (`clientApiId` sha256:f27ecb8b…11a4 → sha256:99d5c152…97d1; `abiId` unchanged). No new node span and no new test id. |
| TD-2026-10-19-063 (self-update pickup, flow 2; founder ruling "ONE Touch ID", the deliberate `clientApiId` move) | The build writes `dist/build-id.json` and the kernel records what it loaded in `<home>/kernel-build.json` (0600); the kernel polls the stamp on a configured cadence and, on a change, raises its own `daemon-stop{reason:'upgrade'}` card (configured expiry 24 h, at most one per configured cooldown of 1 h) whose first line reads "Gnomon was updated: v→v′ (commit)". A changed helper binary is re-pinned through the existing verify path and its `serve` child respawned with no card; an unsigned or unverifiable one fails closed. Accept takes one Touch ID (F3), drains (§2.4 rule 11), releases the lock and socket, spawns one detached successor gnomond with `--prefer-port <old>` (127.0.0.1 only, exclusive; a taken port falls back and the GUI explains in plain words) and exits; the successor resumes the drained sessions. The new web auth form `{resumeToken}` lets the accepting GUI reattach without a second presence: the old kernel sends `gui.resume{resumeToken, ttlMs, kernelPublicKey}` to that connection alone, hands the successor the token's DIGEST and the successor's kernel key on its stdin pipe (never a file in the home) and its listening socket as an inherited fd, so the port is never unbound; a successor on any other port drops the record, and the GUI first checks the successor signs a fresh nonce with the delivered key; the successor accepts the token once, inside a configured TTL of 60 s, as the bound principal (replay, expiry, another principal or root namespace are refused). It never counts as presence. `clientApiId` moves sha256:99d5c152…97d1 → sha256:fe45ab09…3fb8 (AUTH_WIRE_DESCRIPTOR gains `resume`); `abiId` and `kitId` do not move. When `gnomon` finds the running kernel's build differs from the one on disk and 0 sessions are live, it raises the upgrade card, then opens the GUI. |
| Appendix A (Orca Adapt 7–13) | No new node span and no new test id: the new prose names nodes in plain words and amends `G-DMN-04` in place, so every binding, the count (63) and `orphanTests` (72) are unchanged. Falsifier ids for the built work are assigned in the milestone that builds it. |
| D83 (critical anchors) | §3.2's D82 critical sentence is replaced by the S33-10 spec text, verbatim. A critical append resolves only after commit, file anchor and keystore anchor, in that order, and no effect is released before then. This is an ordering guarantee against a same-user process, not a power-loss guarantee. Critical commits use the bulk durability (`synchronous=FULL`, no `F_FULLFSYNC`), because `F_FULLFSYNC` failed the 25 ms budget (arms G and H1). `append` resolves `anchored: 'both' \| 'file-only'`; `file-only` applies while the keystore anchor is the stub (S34-03). Durability prose now states that surviving a kill comes from write() completing, and that `synchronous=FULL` is what carries a commit through an OS crash (§3.2, §9.1, §15.1 row 10, §16, M1.2). |
| D83 (boot classification) | Every anchor records the boot session (`boot.ts`), normalised on both sides. A keystore anchor ahead of the db is `truncated`, with `cause` `tamper` or `unclean-reboot`, and is never accepted silently. Every non-recovery open re-stamps the keystore boot (S34-01). Recovery is `openForRecovery` plus a presence-gated `acknowledgeGap`, which appends one kernel-signed `daemon.log.gap{lostFrom, lostTo, lostHash, bootPrev, bootNow, fileLag}`; an interrupted acknowledgement is re-anchored on open (§3.2). |
| S34 (faults, reserved kinds, keyless) | Durable `daemon.anchor.failed{seq, kind}` markers: committed immediately in the failure path, recorded again on close, and added by an open-time scan only when a keystore anchor exists. Projections treat a marked seq as not having taken effect (S34-02, S34-17, S34-19). The reserved kinds (`daemon.segment`, `daemon.log.gap`, `daemon.anchor.lag`, `daemon.anchor.failed`) are refused from callers and signature-mandatory, and verify binds them, and every kernel-principal row, to a kernel key (`kernelBinding`, S34-18). Keyless logs are non-rotating and diagnostic-only (`FORBIDDEN{kernel-key-required}`). The keystore write timeout is measured from dequeue (S34-11). A refused open closes its db handle (§3.2, §9.1). |
| D84 (recovery window) | The stub-keystore no-recovery window is accepted **dev-only**. Gnomon is not shippable beyond the founder until T-2026-10-17-009 lands (quarantine, authenticated keystore anchors, seam hardening). This adds §3.2's known-gap bullet, a release-gate paragraph after §19.1's Pass, and the new **§A.6**; §15.1 row 10 names the window. |
| D85 (cross-boot relax) | A file-anchor lag > 63 is relaxed only across a verified boot change, judged on the keystore anchor's boot against the current one (both normalised), and the relax appends a signed `daemon.anchor.lag{rows, which: 'file', bootPrev, bootNow}`. Every other case refuses `anchor-lag` with `data.relax`. S34-24: keystore ahead plus file lag > 63 ⇒ `truncated` with `fileLag`. Evidence first: no open-time anchor write happens before the signed row commits (§3.2). |
| Falsifiers | `G-LOG-04` is re-cut: the bulk clauses are unchanged, and the critical class is measured at 1/s under bulk load with p99 < 25 ms. The unit run asserts it only when n ≥ 100, the ≥ 600-event oracle is the manual probe, and the falsifier runs in an isolated `test:perf` pass, once, never retried. `G-LOG-10` adds "the keystore write starts after COMMIT". **`G-LOG-11` is new**: keystore-ahead classification (a)–(e), the interrupted acknowledgement, boot normalisation and the P8-D85 relax fixtures, plus a manual power cut at release candidate. It binds to `#kernel-log` and `$move-1` (§3.2, M1.2). |
| Probes, CI | §19.1's `p-log-fsync` row carries arms G and H1–H3: `F_FULLFSYNC` fails the 25 ms budget at human cadence, and a plain commit has critical p99 10.2 ms at 10⁶ rows under load; H2 (`F_BARRIERFSYNC`) is unreachable from Node. The M1.0 and M1.2 rows are updated, and the keystore arm still waits on P04. §16 records the first all-green CI on macOS, Ubuntu and Windows (run 39073693925), with marker-enforced exclusions (`G-WIN-05`). |
| Windows residuals (S34-27, S34-28) | §16 states them: renames (head anchor, segment create and seal) are atomic but not durable at return on win32 (no `MOVEFILE_WRITE_THROUGH`); `fsync` needs a writable handle; boot `unknown` ⇒ no cross-boot relax ⇒ every such state fails closed; the D78 helper is to supply a write-through rename and a real boot id. On POSIX, `fsyncDir` now rethrows non-benign errors (T-2026-10-17-013(c)). |
| Conflicts | None with a founder ruling. Amended earlier text: (1) v3.8's "critical kinds always commit with `F_FULLFSYNC`" (D82) is superseded by D83; (2) S33-07's strict file-anchor bound is narrowed by D85 to the same boot; (3) the `kernel-log.ts` header's old claim that a power loss can never leave an anchor ahead of the db is gone from the code and never entered the spec. |
| Judgment calls | **Status column:** the probe table is relabelled `Status (v3.9)`. **S34 citation:** no research file 34 exists; the series is cited through lore and tasks. **Windows residual id:** T-2026-10-17-013(c) cites S34-27, which is used here and, since T-2026-10-17-013, in the `anchor.ts` code comment too (it cited S34-26). **`G-LOG-11` placement:** it sits only in §3.2 and M1.2, so it does not reach `~windows-parity`; its fixtures inject the boot id through the seam and run on every OS. **Release gate:** placed as a §19.1 paragraph after Pass and not in §18's `Deliverable:` line, which lists package-level checks only. **CI ids:** the CI paragraph sits in §16, where `G-WIN-05` already binds, so no binding moves. |
| D86 (part 2: tighten by autonomy) | Tightening spends agent autonomy, never the founder's attention. A tightened repo runs fewer agents at once, stops auto-landing, turns cached-class decisions into fresh-presence ones for that repo, and narrows voice (effective voice classes = grant ∩ strictness); it never adds cards to a decision (B14). Extra verdicts are taken only on gate-class or high-risk nodes. Arky's "every node needs a verdict at L2" (A35-09) is dropped (§3.4). |
| D87 (part 2: start strict, one click) | A repo whose `(repo, mode)` reviewer is not calibrated has a strictness floor of **L1**, so every repo starts at L1 until Move 3's calibration says otherwise. Strictness and the **run mode** (`manual`, `ask`, `auto-edits`, after Claude Code's permission modes) are kernel-owned, per repo and per session, and one click in the GUI: a header control, palette commands and `⇧Tab` in the composer. Tightening is one click with no presence; loosening is one click plus the presence prompt (§3.4, §11.4, §11.5). |
| D88 (part 2: loosen on evidence) | Loosening needs positive, kernel-observed evidence (merged nodes that survived on the watched ref, oracle passes; never quiet time, S35-02) plus an elapsed dwell, goes one level per step, and takes fresh presence each step. Going below what the evidence supports, or below the floor, takes the root key and is logged as an override (§3.4, B12, B13). |
| Vocabulary (part 2) | One vocabulary, chosen here: **strictness levels L0–L3** under the kernel-owned namespace **`approval.strictness.*`** (Arky's name), not Aegis's `gate.breaker.*`. Reasons: `budget.breaker.*` already names the fuse's quota breaker, and `approval.*` already sits under B3 (broker-only, kernel-signed). "Circuit breaker" survives only as a plain-word description. Card kinds: **`reconsider`**, **`strictness`** (absorbs Aegis's `breaker-reset` and Arky's loosening card) and **`incident`** (human-only). Refusal gates: `review` and `strictness`; Aegis's reason `breaker` becomes `strictness-presence`, and its `approval.suspect` reason `breaker-class` becomes `strictness-class`. Config: `strictness:` and `review:` in root-signed `~/.gnomon/config.yaml`. |
| Strictness (part 2) | §3.4 gains the ladder (L0–L3), the effective class (base class ⊓ strictness), the run modes, the kernel-observed signal list (S35-01, S35-03, A35-10), the decayed per-repo score, hysteresis (loosen window ≥ 3× the tighten window, recall to the previous peak, counts keyed by node lineage, S35-06), root-signed thresholds where raising one is a loosening (S35-05), the cached-unlock **remap** instead of a drop (S35-09), and invariants **B11–B16** (= Aegis CB1–CB6). `status.strictness` and `status.unobservedRefWrites` join §5. |
| Convergence (part 2) | §7.3 is amended (A35-01): under the round cap a failing `reviewer` or `test` oracle returns the node to its claimant (`contract.review.returned`) instead of raising a `knot` card. The kernel counts rounds (kernel-observed reviewer results only, keyed by node lineage, S35-10); at 3 rounds the reviewer permit is refused (`review-cap`) and one card is raised: `reconsider`, or `knot` when every other oracle passes and only the reviewer disagrees. From round 2 the reviewer sees only the diff since its last review (A35-04). Findings are `review-finding:v1` with fingerprints, a kernel-set `pre-existing` tag and class-of-bug `instances[]` (A35-05). `reconsider` decisions are `restart`, `extend` (fresh presence, at most 2) and `reject`; none merges, approves or certifies. |
| Change notes (part 2) | `change-note:v1` (A35-16) carries the agent's `agentClaims{testedHow, risk, revert}` as untrusted, sanitized text beside kernel-computed fields: `verified{n, of}` from cited `oracle.result` ids, a kernel risk floor, and a revert action computed from refs. Agent claims are never authority (B16): a claim below the kernel's risk sets `claim-mismatch` (voice-ineligible); `reversible: false` sets `irreversible` (fresh presence, no voice); revert text is never executed (S35-11, §3.4). From Move 2, `built` without a note is refused `note-missing` when the effective tier is medium or above or the repo is at L1 or above. |
| Reservations (part 2) | M1.1 reserves the merged values, listed in §4.11 and M1.1's row, so `clientApiId` does not move in Move 2 (A26-11). No closed enumeration is touched. Move 1 behaviour does not change: Move 1 sessions run as `ask` at L0. |
| Placement (part 2) | **M2.10** review convergence; **M2.11** strictness, run modes, the GUI control, change notes and revert detection; **M3.4** the calibration floor and incident eval set; **M4.5/M4.6** voice respects the effective policy; §20 adds `T-REV-01`, `T-STR-01`, field falsifiers 16 and 17, §15.1 rows 26–29 and §22 OPEN 20–21. |
| Falsifier ids (part 2) | Renumbered so no id collides or means two things (`G-BRK-*` is the broker's existing series, 01–06): Arky's `G-BRK-07` → `G-STR-03`, `G-BRK-08` → `G-STR-01` + `G-STR-04`, `G-BRK-09` → `G-STR-08`; Aegis's `G-CB-01` → `G-STR-01`, `G-CB-02`/`03` → `G-STR-02`, `G-CB-04` → `G-STR-04`, `G-CB-05`/`06` → `G-STR-05`, `G-CB-07` → `G-STR-06`, `G-CB-08` → `G-STR-07`, `G-CB-09` → `G-STR-08`, `G-CB-10` → `G-STR-03`; `G-CNV-01`/`02` → `G-REV-01`, `G-CNV-03` → `G-REV-05`; `G-CLM-01..03` → `G-CHG-01..03`; Arky's unspecified `G-REV-01..05` and `G-CHG-01..03` are defined here as `G-REV-01..05` and `G-CHG-01..05`; telemetry `T-BRK-01` → `T-STR-01`. New: `G-STR-09` (the one-click control) and `G-STR-10` (the calibration floor). |
| Conflicts (part 2) | None with a founder ruling after D86–D88. Resolved between the notes: (1) Arky's L2 "verdict on every node" is dropped (D86). (2) Arky's `reconsider` `accept` (certify despite findings) contradicts Aegis's "no action certifies"; `accept` is removed, and the override path is the existing `knot` card, raised at the cap only when the reviewer alone disagrees, resolved with fresh presence and logged `verdict.founder{override:true}`. (3) Aegis disarms only arms whose class a trip excludes; §14.7 already ends every arm on any policy change, so every level change ends every arm, and the ceiling bounds what a new arm may cover. (4) Arky's Move 1 trip on `approval.suspect` starts counting at M2.11, because Move 1 only reserves. (5) Arky keys rounds by `(contractId, nodeId)`; a spec edit changes `contractId`, so rounds are keyed by node lineage instead (S35-10). Amended earlier text: §7.3's "Disagreement ⇒ `knot`" (A35-01) and §3.4's static decision classes (A35-08). |
| Judgment calls (part 2) | **No new node:** strictness lives in `#approval-broker` and convergence under `^certified`, so the count stays 45. **Run modes** are defined here: `manual` (attended, plan-first, no agent spawn or automation), `ask` (today's behaviour), `auto-edits` (edits inside the session's own worktree pass without a card; gate-class paths, exec and network still ask). There is no bypass mode. **Auto-merge off** is one batched landing `verdict` card per repo per batch window, not a card per node. **`incident`** is raised only by the human's `request.incident`, decided with the cached class (it only tightens) and hand-only. **Critical kinds:** `approval.strictness.raised\|lowered` and `approval.mode.set` join §3.2's as-built list when M2.11 lands; §3.2 is not edited now. **Ids** sit only in sections with a node (§3.4, §7.3, §11.7, §14.7, §19), so `orphanTests` stays at 72; §15.1's new rows cite sections. **`⇧Tab`** cycles the mode as in Claude Code. |
| TD-2026-10-19-063 (the GUI re-pin of the claude binary, flow 1) | A Claude Code self-update no longer strands new sessions behind a stopped daemon. `#session-runner` pre-stages ONE re-pin card per new sha (on an open refused `claude_binary_changed`, whose refusal names the `cardId` and `next: approve the re-pin card`, and on a poll of the resolved source at a 60 s cadence: realpath, size, mtime); the poll never writes a pin. The card is `gate-class`/`allow` (fresh presence, ONE prompt: a root-class decision costs two, the approvals key's and the root key's own `mode:'fresh'` sign), expires into deny after its 24 h TTL, and a human deny suppresses that sha. The signer is checked by `/usr/bin/codesign` on the private copy the kernel will execute (requirement `anchor apple generic and certificate leaf[subject.OU] = "Q6L2SF6YDW"`, Anthropic PBC; notarization not consulted); an unsigned copy, a failed verify or another team sets `claim-mismatch`, a red first line and Deny focused (`#gui`). The `#approval-broker` effect re-hashes the copy and writes the pin with its signer and approval record `{cardId, contentHash, decisionSig}`; live sessions keep their own `claude-<sha>` copy. `gnomon session repin-claude` stays the offline form. No `clientApiId` move. |

### 0.1 Changes in v3.8 (compile: skip)

History. v2's and v3's change tables are in git history (commits `3b352368` and `93957274` on `ideate/paradigm-8`); v3.7's, v3.6's, v3.5's, v3.4's, v3.3's, v3.2's and v3.1's are kept as §0.1–§0.7 below as history, and this table wins where they differ. v3.8 folds in founder rulings **D80–D82** (L-2026-10-17-ascend-172952-001, L-2026-10-17-ascend-183438-001), Aegis's anchor policy (research 33, `S33-01..09`, evidence `packages/gnomon/probes/results/p-log-fsync*.darwin.*`), and the spec questions the builders resolved while landing PR-3 (`gnomon-contracts` core, `4718b7d1`), PR-4 (the kernel log and the symbol-drift CI, `cb56927b`; L-2026-10-17-ascend-180323-001) and PR-5 (the daemon-lifecycle core, `54694cd5`). The builder resolutions are ratified as spec. None contradicts a founder ruling; the two places where one amends earlier spec text are named in the Conflicts row. It adds one test id (`G-LOG-10`) and no node.

| Id | What changed |
|---|---|
| D80 (launchers) | D79's acceptance stands and is **hardened**: the macOS `session` profile denies `process-exec` of `/usr/bin/open`, `/usr/bin/osascript` and `lsregister`, each by realpath, and M1.7's p10m re-run verifies the denies. §A.2(i) is widened. An app that escapes through LaunchServices runs with full same-user rights: it can read the device-key file, write settings and reach the endpoint port. Approvals still need presence. A launcher the session compiles itself remains the stated residual (§11.2 rules 2b and 9, §15.1 row 19, §15.5, §16, §19.1 M1.7 and P10m, §A.2). |
| D82 (anchors) | §3.2's "written after every commit" is replaced by research 33's spec text, verbatim. The file anchor is written within 64 rows or 50 ms. The keystore anchor is written within 1024 rows or 1 s, a cadence that is provisional until P04. A critical kind commits with `F_FULLFSYNC` and writes both anchors before `append` returns and before any effect is released. On open, file-anchor lag > 63 rows, or any lag after a clean close, ⇒ `INTEGRITY_BROKEN{anchor-lag}`. §3.2 gains the critical list, the bulk cadence, bounded lag on open (`daemon.anchor.lag`, kernel-signed) and `status.log.forcedAnchorRate`. §A.1(g) is added verbatim. Threat row 10 and §16's chain-anchor row state the new residual (§3.2, §9.1, §9.6, §15.1, §16, §A.1). |
| D82 (falsifiers, S33-08) | `G-LOG-04` is restated: 10⁶ appends with critical kinds at 1/64, bulk p99 < 5 ms, ≤ 0.1 % over 5 ms, critical p99 < 25 ms; p99.9, max and checkpoint count are reported, not asserted. `G-LOG-06` gains the anchor-lag and keystore-truncation cases. **`G-LOG-10` is new**: truncation right after a critical append ⇒ `truncated`, and no broker effect before both anchor writes. It binds to `#kernel-log` and `$move-1`. M1.2's row carries the new oracles and falsifier. §20 falsifier 9 now reads "bulk append p99 > 5 ms or > 0.1 % over 5 ms at 10⁶ rows" (§3.2, §19.1, §20, Appendix A). |
| D82 (probes, S33-09) | §19.1's probe table gains a `p-log-fsync` row (done, three runs → D82). The M1.0 and M1.2 rows carry the follow-ups: arm G (D+E plus forced `F_FULLFSYNC` + anchor at 1/64) before `G-LOG-04` is re-cut, re-run at 10⁶ rows, and arm H (keystore via the helper) once P04 lands. PR-4 measured p99 7.4 ms at 10⁶ rows under per-commit anchoring, so `G-LOG-04` stays red until the D82 implementation lands. |
| D81 (symbol CI) | A CI check (landed with PR-4) fails on drift between Appendix A and the `.purpose` symbols of implemented Gnomon code. The package-level symbols (#gnomon-contracts, #gnomon-helper, #gnomon-wrapper, #gnomon-probes) and the contract sub-components of #gnomon-contracts (#contract-event-v1, #contract-ids, #contract-principal, #contract-ref-v1, #contract-refusal-v1, #contract-card-v1, #contract-gnomon-rpc-v1, #contract-client-api-id) are listed in §18's Relations as sub-components. They are written without code spans, so they are **not** Appendix A nodes, and they stay allowlisted in `packages/gnomon/test/symbol-allowlist.json`. The node count stays 45 (§18, Appendix A notes). |
| PR-3 (refs, ids) | `turn` is registered as a kernel built-in ref scheme (§3.7, §4.4), so §3.9's `peer:<logId>/turn:…` wraps a registered scheme. Encodings: lowercase Crockford base32 for `ns` and `hsid-short`, uppercase for `hsid:v1d`. Ids are `card:`, `conn:`, `arm:`, `brief:`, `msg:` or `ds:` + ULID, plus `permit:v1:<ULID>`. `rootKeyId8` is the first 8 hex characters of the key digest (§4.2). |
| PR-3 (kinds, labels) | §9.1 splits `permit`/`budget` into two named rows and `tend`/`decay`/`calibration` into three. `credit`, `broke` and `modns` are open namespaces that default to `normal`. Field-level `sensitive` labels are named: `approval.request.tool_input`, `oracle.result.evidence` and `voice.turn.user.audioRef`. `voice.enrol` is `secret`. An unregistered kind is refused at append, with no default (§3.2 L8, §9.1). |
| PR-3 (refusal, enums, clientApiId) | Refusal `data{}` is optional and additive, and `refusalOf()` reads the JSON-RPC `error.data` (§4.6). §4.9 now separates the additive enumerations from four **closed** ones: refusal `code`, `retriable`, `audience` and `sensitivity`. `clientApiId = sha256(canonicalJson(descriptor) + "\n" + kitId)`. `modAbiId` is excluded from it, and the value is pinned in `gnomon-contracts/schemas/client-api.ids.json` (§12.8). |
| PR-5 (daemon) | `GNOMON_HOME` is honoured as a test-only override. The production daemon learns its home from `--home`, and the clean env drops `GNOMON_*`. "Never `/tmp`" means `/tmp`, `/private/tmp`, `/var/tmp` and a world-writable `os.tmpdir()`. The Linux XDG fallback must be owned by the user and 0700, and its socket is `gnomon-<sha256(home)[0..16]>.sock`. macOS refuses a `sun_path` overflow (100 bytes). Clients derive the socket path from the home and never trust the pidfile's `socketPath`. The idle default is 10 min. Until M1.4, `hello` answers `ENGINE` -32000 with reason `not-implemented`. `socket.disconnect` joins §9.1. `maxFrameBytes` bounds inbound frames only, and replies are capped at 4 MiB. Framing is byte-level NDJSON (§2.4, §3.4, §4.9, §9.1, §15.1 row 5, §15.3, M1.9). |
| PR-4 (log) | Storage is `node:sqlite` `kernel.db` plus sealed segments `log/segments/NNNNNN.db`, and the last row of each segment is `daemon.segment` (a log-only kind). Only `daemon.*`, `socket.*` and `schedule.*` may be unsigned. A signature is base64 over `domain + "\n" + hash`. Durability defaults to `process-crash` (WAL, `synchronous=FULL`), with `power-loss` (`F_FULLFSYNC`) opt-in. The appender caps `audience` at `private` in 8.0. Agent and voice readers are `UNSUPPORTED` until their milestones (§3.2, §9.1, §9.3, M1.2). |
| Conflicts | None with a founder ruling. Two amendments of earlier text are recorded here. (1) §3.4 and §15.3 said `GNOMON_HOME` is "a build-time constant … absent from production code" (S16-6, a review finding, not a P8 ruling). The PR-5 code reads it on the client side, so S16-6 is amended: the daemon never reads `GNOMON_*`, and the seam selects a home and never a policy value. Its keystore interaction is OPEN 19. (2) PR-4's "the anchor is not fsynced per commit" is superseded by D82 for critical kinds; bulk rows keep the coalesced, non-forced cadence. |
| Judgment calls | **Placement of D82's falsifiers:** they sit in §3.2's prose and M1.2's row only, so `G-LOG-10` binds to `#kernel-log` and `$move-1`. Threat row 10 names no id, so `orphanTests` stays at 72. **Status line:** it names the kernel log in plain words, because a `#kernel-log` span there would move that node's first appearance to the preamble. **`daemon.anchor.lag`** is kernel-signed even though `daemon.*` may be unsigned (S33-07 says signed). **`lsregister`** is named by its framework directory, not a literal path, and the generator realpaths it. **Idle default:** read as the idle period itself (10 min, root-signed config), beside the existing "no timer due inside 10 min". **Falsifier 9's memory clause** now names §16's per-OS metric instead of RSS, finishing v3.7's change. **OPEN 19** (new): the keystore items are per OS user, not per home, so a non-default `--home` would share them, the head anchor included. The ruling is left to Aegis. **`approval.request`** lists `tool_input` among its fields, so the field-level label has a field to name. |

### 0.2 Changes in v3.7 (compile: skip)

History. v3.7 folds in the **first M1.0 hardware results** (the founder's Mac: macOS 15.7.3 build 24G419, arm64, Node 26.0.0, claude 2.1.302; `packages/gnomon/probes/results/*.darwin.json`; lore L-2026-10-17-ascend-083200-001 and L-2026-10-17-ascend-084150-001) and founder rulings D77–D79 (L-2026-10-17-ascend-084643-001, L-2026-10-17-ascend-084905-001). It adds no node, no test id, no verb and no contract value. Six result files exist (`p-harness`, `p-noise-vectors`, `p01-claude-cli`, `p10m-sbx-deny-mac`, `p10m-claude-compat`, `p11-ws-framing`); every other row of the M1.0 table is still a prediction.

| Id | What changed |
|---|---|
| p10m-sbx-deny-mac (argv leak) | v3.6's process-info rule did **not** hide other processes. `pgrep` carries the entitlement `com.apple.sysmond.client` and gets the whole process table, argv included, from the root `sysmond` daemon over XPC; no `process-info*` or `sysctl-read` rule reaches that path (`kern.procargs`, `kern.procargs2` and `kern.` prefixes all tried). The macOS `session` profile adds `(deny mach-lookup (global-name "com.apple.sysmond"))`; with it `pgrep` fails ("Cannot get process list"), and direct `KERN_PROCARGS2` reads already fail under the full profile (§11.2 rule 2b, §15.1 row 19, §15.5, §16). |
| p10m (escapes) | `tmux send-keys` and `screen -X stuff` to an unsandboxed server both reached the denied port. A `network-outbound` unix-socket deny on the tmux socket directory closed the tmux path. The profile now denies unix-socket connects to the user's tmux (`/private/tmp/tmux-<uid>`) and screen socket directories. `launchctl submit` was already blocked in-session. The endpoint-port deny held for `curl`, Node `net`, WebSocket and `::1`, and port + 1 stayed open (§15.5). |
| p10m (`ps`) | `/bin/ps` is setuid root, and `sandbox-exec` refuses to exec setuid binaries under **any** profile, even `(allow default)`. So `ps` fails in every macOS session, and its failure is not evidence for the process-info rule. Said in §3.3, §15.5 and M1.7. |
| p10m (realpath) | SBPL path filters match **resolved** paths: a rule naming `/var/folders/…` silently does not apply, because the kernel sees `/private/var/folders/…`. The probe hit this, and the kernel's profile generator must realpath every path it writes (§15.5, M1.7). |
| p10m (signals) | No rule covers same-user signals: a session can signal any same-user process, the kernel included. Stated as a residual in §A.2: a session can kill or stop the daemon but cannot become it, and 8.0 has no external supervisor to restart it (launchd activation is 8.1). |
| D79 (`open`) | LaunchServices `open` starts an app **outside** the session's sandbox, and that app reached the denied port. Neither a `launchservicesd` nor a `com.apple.lsd.*` mach-lookup deny stopped it. The founder ACCEPTED it as a stated residual (§A.2): presence (Touch ID on Reconnect/attach and on every gate) is the boundary. v3.6's claim that the session profile denies LaunchServices is withdrawn for macOS (§11.2 rules 2b and 9, §15.1 row 19, §15.5, §16). |
| p10m-claude-compat | **PASS.** Claude Code 2.1.302 works inside the hardened profile (§15.5 plus the `sysmond` and tmux denies). `claude -p` authenticated on the subscription and answered (exit 0, 2 turns). Its Bash tool ran node, git and python, wrote in the workdir, and spawned and killed a child. Meanwhile the stand-in endpoint port was unreachable (curl exit 7, 0 server hits), `pgrep` saw 0 processes (567 in the unsandboxed control), and the denied directory was unwritable. The M1.7 session approach is viable on macOS (§3.3, §15.5, M1.7). |
| p11-ws-framing | Framing passes: 9 API cases plus 27 raw-frame cases mirroring Autobahn §1–7, i.e. 36. 4 MiB frames ran at 620–730 MB/s one way. Peak RSS was 200–248 MB, but macOS RSS counts freed-but-reusable pages, while the charged memory (`phys_footprint`) peaked at 39.8 MB. P11's criterion now names its metric per OS: on macOS `phys_footprint` < 60 MB. P11 passes under it (§19.1). |
| p-noise-vectors | **P13 passes:** 5 published cacophony/snow vectors for `Noise_IK`, `Noise_XX` and `Noise_XXpsk3` (25519, ChaChaPoly, SHA256), using `node:crypto` only, with tampering rejected (§19.1). |
| p01-claude-cli | claude 2.1.302 is at `~/.local/bin/claude`, which is not on a non-login `PATH`. The kernel resolves the `claude` binary by explicit path and never by `PATH` (§3.3, §16). All 12 required flags are present. `--setting-sources` documents only `user`, `project` and `local`. §3.3 states, per p01, that managed settings are always loaded by the CLI and are not controllable by `--setting-sources`; the managed-policy semantics remain a manual check. P01 is partial: hook stdin `session_id`, the `control_request` interrupt and the loopback-override refusal are still manual. |
| D77 | The kernel-linking build (M2.8, M2.9) is deferred until the Gnomon core (Move 1) is ready; no second machine is available for the test. §3.9 stays as written, the M1.1 reservations stay (contract values, the `hsid-short` definition, sensitivity defaults, `rootKeyId` comparison), and OPEN 16 is parked (§19.2, §22). |
| D78 | Move 1's pass is split by platform: the **macOS pass** first, then the **Windows parity pass** when the founder is ready (the helper, Hello/Passport KSP, WFP/AppContainer, pipes, the WebView2 wrapper, the Windows probes). Windows CI keeps running the platform-independent tests. Every Windows-only M1.0 probe and task is marked *Windows parity phase*. This is platform sequencing, not the α/β/γ checkpoints D57 declined: each pass is the whole Move 1 bar on its platform (preamble, §16, §19.1). |
| M1.0 status | §19.1's probe table gains a **Status** column (pass / partial / fail / pending per probe id), and P11's claim names its memory metric. |
| Judgment calls | **`open` residual scope:** the lore says the escaped app "reaches the endpoint port", but such an app runs with the reach of any unsandboxed same-user process. §A.2 therefore states the wider scope and rests the boundary on presence, D68's code-signing check and the layer watch, not on the sandbox. **screen:** only the tmux socket deny was proven; the screen deny is by analogy, and M1.7 re-runs p10m against the generated profile. **Other sockets:** p10m also reached the ssh-agent socket and Chrome's `SingletonSocket`s. They are put in §A.2's residual class rather than given unproven deny rules. **Signals:** written as an availability residual, not as "the kernel survives", because nothing restarts the kernel before the next client in 8.0. **Per-OS memory metric:** private bytes (`PrivateUsage`) on Windows and `Pss` on Linux CI are chosen without a probe, and M1.2's "RSS > 150 MB" falsifier takes the same per-OS metric. **P13:** marked pass on IK/XX/XXpsk3; `Noise_X` and the 1 MiB throughput were not exercised, so they move to M2.8's own tests. **Managed layer:** the wording is neutral, per p01, until the manual check. |

### 0.3 Changes in v3.6 (compile: skip)

History. v3.6 adds **kernel linking** (requirement P8-R9, L-2026-10-17-ascend-080105-001): Arky's design (31-arky-kernel-linking.md: `A31-01..19`), Aegis's threat model (32-aegis-kernel-linking.md: `S32-01..14`) and founder rulings D73–D76 (L-2026-10-17-ascend-081146-001), which win over both reviews. Where the reviews differ and no ruling covers the point, the stricter option is taken unless it conflicts with D74; each such choice is named in the last row. Nothing has been built or run.

| Id | What changed |
|---|---|
| R9 / D73 (handoff = brief) | Handoff is a **handoff brief**: kernel-signed instructions plus attachments (documents, refs, git refs) that let a session on another kernel, or on the same one, continue the work. Nothing is taken over. The source session is unaffected; the receiver's human accepts with fresh presence; then a new session starts, or an existing one continues, on the receiver's own backend, subscription, fuse and law 7. Dropped from 31/32: custody, seal, epochs, CAS, hsid lineage, transcript transplant (`--resume` across machines), permit transfer, the session states `sealed`/`handed-off`, the `xev` ref scheme and `session.stop.handoffTo` (A31-11..13, S32-03; §3.9). |
| D74 / A31-04..06 / S32-01, S32-09 (transport) | Kernels never listen beyond loopback; §11.2 rule 1 keeps its words and gains one sentence saying linking never widens it. A kernel component speaks the closed `gnomon-link:v1` protocol through an outbound-only transport child `gnomon-link` under a new eighth sandbox profile `link`; the kernel holds the keys and runs the handshake. Reachability is `gnomon-relay`, a zero-dependency blind relay the founder hosts on an always-on machine, LAN first; no overlay yet; nevr.land later. Noise `IK` in steady state, `XXpsk3` for pairing, `X` for store-and-forward, all over `node:crypto` (§3.9, §15.5, §18). |
| S32-02 / A31-02 / S32-08 (identity) | `ns` (40 bits) is grindable, so across kernels it is display only: every foreign principal is compared by its full `rootKeyId` and shown as `p:<ns>/<name>@<rootKeyId8>`. One `ns` per root. The founder's own machines link through root-signed `grant.peer{relationship:'self'}` rows: a scoped cross-attestation, never a merged trust set. A peer root never signs a local row (I5 unchanged). Remote kernels are principal kind `peer` (§3.1, §4.1). |
| A31-07 / S32-09 (link key) | New key class **link key**: an X25519 static key in the OS keystore item `gnomon-link-key` behind the helper, with the kernel key's custody rules, bound to the kernel key and the device by a root-signed registry row of kind `link`. It is only the Noise static key and never signs; kernel-key signatures stay the only assertion mechanism. S32-09's Ed25519 link-auth signature is not needed, because Noise authenticates the static key (§3.1, §15.2). |
| A31-08 / S32-04 (pairing) | `$kernel-pairing`: `gnomon link pair` is root-signed and human-only (refused from kernel-spawned shells, never stageable); an out-of-band invite carries a 128-bit PSK; `XXpsk3` runs through the relay; a 6-word SAS over the handshake hash and both sides' root and link keys shows on both screens; fresh root presence on both sides decides a `peer-pair` card that shows the scope and the peer's unverified name; each side then writes `grant.peer` with `until` (30 d default). `^peer-paired` gates every frame after the handshake (§3.9). |
| D75 / A31-14 / S32-05..07 (teammates, co-work) | Teammates may co-work and send and receive briefs. Co-work is messages through the first-party `#cowork-mod` (pull-only inbox; `inject.receipt` by the bridge); the kernel carries `modns.cowork.out` under the first-party-only capability `link:send`, so no verb and no mod RPC is added. Teammate egress is `team`/`org` audience only; anything else crosses only as a projection made on a fresh-presence `declassify` card after a secret scan. Peer content taints the reading session: its cards carry the flag `peer`, are voice-ineligible and refuse `always`, and teammate-tainted `tool.permission` cards need fresh presence (§3.4, §3.9). |
| D75 (§1) | §1's "not a second human" is narrowed: other humans exist only as link peers, never as local principals or approvers; "teleport" stays a non-goal (§1). |
| D76 | Warpline state does not cross kernels until T-2026-10-14-003's bundle sync. Briefs may carry `wl:` refs as text; they resolve only against the receiver's own Warpline (§3.9). |
| S32 §6 (Move 1) | Cheap now, expensive later. Every kind has a default `sensitivity` in §9.1: `turn.event` bodies, `session.inject` bytes, `approval.request.tool_input`, oracle evidence and voice text default to `sensitive` (`G-LOG-09`). Foreign principals are compared by `rootKeyId` (`G-ID-08`). Every id is a ULID or log-qualified, and permits carry their issuing `logId`. `hsid-short` is 80 random bits. The §3.9 values are reserved in M1.1 (§4.2, §4.8, §9.1, §19.1). |
| Contracts | Card kinds `handoff \| declassify \| peer-pair`, flag `peer`, `via: link` (reserved; never valid on a decision), `principal.kind: peer`, `session.inject.origin: peer`, session `taint`, refusal gate `link` and its reasons, the kernel-owned `link.*` namespace, `request.brief.send \| request.cowork.allow \| request.link.revoke`, ref scheme `peer`, `status.links`, redaction row `peer`, registry kind `link`, `grant.peer`, capability `link:send`, `linkApiId`; `G-ABI-02` gains a peer column refused on every verb and relay (§3.4, §4, §5, §9). |
| Falsifiers | `G-LNK-01..14` (merged from both reviews, custody cases dropped), `G-ID-08`, `G-LOG-09`, `T-LNK-01`; threat rows 21–25; §15.5's eighth profile; §16 rows; M1.0 probes P13 (Noise vectors) and P14 (outbound-only `link` profile); Move 2 tasks M2.8 (own machines) and M2.9 (teammates); falsifier 15; §A.5 (§3.9, §15, §16, §19, §20, §A). |
| Judgment calls | **Link key:** X25519 only, no second Ed25519 link key: one design, and Noise authenticates it. **SAS:** 6 words (Arky) over 6 digits (Aegis), because words carry more bits for the same glance. **PSK, not PAKE:** the invite's 128-bit random PSK makes a PAKE unnecessary (a PAKE is for low-entropy codes); the SAS still catches an intercepted invite. **Revocation narrows:** `request.link.revoke` and `gnomon link revoke` need no presence, as voice narrowing does; pairing and renewal need root presence on both sides. **Outbound briefs** always pass a fresh-presence card (`handoff` to self, `declassify` to a teammate), the stricter of 31 (no send card) and 32 (a card for `sensitive` only). **Co-work** needs a per-session human allow (`request.cowork.allow`, fresh presence) before a session's messages leave, for both relationships. **No `PEER_UNREACHABLE` code:** a send queues in an outbox, and delivery is reported by events and `status.links`, so nothing needs a refusal. **No discovery:** the relay address is recorded at pairing; no mDNS (S32-14's hint-only option is dropped with its test). **Store-and-forward** seals with Noise `X` so a brief reaches a sleeping machine: the sender is authenticated, but there is no forward secrecy against later theft of the receiver's link key (§A.5). **Added beyond both reviews:** voice text defaults to `sensitive`; `G-ID-08` and `G-LOG-09` as the Move 1 oracles for S32 §6; quarantine refs for git attachments; the `link:send` capability in place of a mod RPC. |

### 0.4 Changes in v3.5 (compile: skip)

History. v3.5 folds in the **Move 1 kickoff**: Aegis's M1.0 security review (30-aegis-m10-security-probes.md: claims `S29-K1..K8`, probes `S29-01..10`), Arky's kickoff (29-arky-move1-kickoff.md: scaffold, probes `P00..P12`, CI, `PR-0..5`) and founder rulings D68–D72 (L-2026-10-17-ascend-075830-001). Nothing has been run; every probe result is still a prediction.

| Id | What changed |
|---|---|
| D68 / S29-K1 (kernel-key custody) | v3.4's macOS `full` was false: any same-user process could run the helper and ask it for the key. The kernel now ships as a **Developer-ID-signed single executable (Node SEA)**; the macOS helper is an **XPC service under launchd** that answers only a peer meeting that binary's code-signing requirement and refuses to run when sandboxed; macOS `kernelKeyCustody: full` holds only under this design. The §15.5 sentence denying `securityd` lookups per item is removed (SBPL filters `mach-lookup` per service, not per item); the session profile denies `mach-lookup` of the helper's own service instead, as defence in depth (§3.1, §15.2, §15.5, §16, §18, §A.1(e)). |
| S29-K2 (presence phishing) | Presence proves a touch, not what was approved, against a same-user process that can reach the key. macOS closes it with the helper's keychain access group plus D68's caller check; on Windows any same-user process can open an NGC key by name, so the residual stands (§3.2, §3.4, §A.1, §A.3). |
| S29-K3 (WFP) | "WFP filter on the job object" is gone — no WFP condition matches a job object, and adding filters needs BFE rights. The endpoint-port deny on Windows is one of three mechanisms chosen by probe S29-08: a per-session restricting SID matched by `ALE_USER_ID`, an elevated install-time service, or an AppContainer. If all fail: presence only, and §20 kill criterion (e) applies to the Windows browser surface (§11.2 rule 2b, §15.5 note, §16, §20). |
| S29-K4 (Windows key) | Windows root/approvals keys are Passport KSP (`MS_NGC_KEY_STORAGE_PROVIDER`) keys with `NCRYPT_USE_CONTEXT_PROPERTY` for the kernel's text. KeyCredentialManager is dropped because it cannot show text, and so is the Platform Crypto Provider because it prompts for a password, not Hello. A key that `NCRYPT_IMPL_TYPE_PROPERTY` does not report as hardware is refused (§3.1, §3.4, §15.2, §16, §18). |
| S29-K5 (unlock custody) | A cached unlock is an evaluated LAContext (macOS) or an authenticated NCrypt handle (Windows) held **in the helper** per `connId`, and an arm's unlock is held there per `armId`. The kernel keeps only references. A helper crash drops every unlock and arm (`approval.voice.disarm{reason:'helper-exit'}`) (§3.1, §11.5, §14.7, §15.2, §A.1). |
| S29-K6 (pipe PID) | The pipe server-PID check is no longer a security claim: the pidfile is same-user writable and Node cannot read the server PID. `serverSig` is the anchor; the PID comparison stays as a diagnostic in the helper and the Python client (§2.4, §4.9, §15.1 row 7, §16). |
| S29-K7 (token replay) | A second presentation of a launch token revokes (D55) only **inside its 60 s TTL**. After the TTL, a consumed token is a history or session-restore replay: refused and logged `socket.auth.replay`, with no revoke and no card. The session profile also denies reads of browser profiles and the wrapper's WebView data (§9.1, §11.2 rule 2, §15.1 row 19, §15.5, §16). |
| S29-K8 (capture exclusion) | Handles and challenges render in a **wrapper-owned native panel**, because a WebView cannot exclude a region of itself. M1.13 must leave room for it, and the capture-exclusion probe (S29-10) moves into M1.0 (§11.1, §14.7, §16, §19.1). |
| D69 / D70 (M1.0) | §19.1's M1.0 row now points to one merged probe table: Arky P00–P12 and Aegis S29-01..10, deduplicated, both ids kept. Each probe writes `packages/gnomon/probes/results/<id>.<os>.json` (`probeResult:v1`). Hardware split: the founder's Mac, the founder's Win11 PC (TPM 2.0 + Hello, standard-user account; D69), and CI for mechanics only. Notarization is set up in M1.0 through the `gnomon-notary` profile (D70, OPEN 1). |
| D71 | OPEN 3 resolved: the Windows helper is Rust (`windows` crate), sharing the wrapper's pinned toolchain (§18, §22). |
| D72 / Arky 29 (scaffold) | §18's layout gains `src/` and the `test/` and `probes/` dirs. Gnomon packages are private, outside the root build/test until 8.0, and require Node ≥ 24 while the repo stays ≥ 20. Windows code signing is chosen before M1.13 ships, with Azure Trusted Signing preferred. §19.1 notes PR-0..5 (§18, §19.1, OPEN 1). |
| Judgment calls | **K7:** the TTL cut was taken over a rule keyed on the connection's surface, because `hello.surface` is client-declared and a thief could name itself a browser. Inside the TTL, D55's theft signal is unchanged. Residual: a legitimate page arriving after a thief and after the TTL is only refused, and the theft then shows as an extra session in the Reconnect prompt and `status`. **K6:** demoted rather than deleted, as a helper/Python diagnostic. **K3:** the "WFP" cells of the oracle, transport and voice profiles take the same S29-08 mechanism through one §15.5 note rather than three edits. **Added beyond the reviews:** a `mach-lookup` deny of the helper's XPC service in the session profile; macOS custody reports `partial` whenever the kernel does not run as the signed SEA (for example from source); M1.0's optional Mod-socket spike moves to M3.5 (Arky). |

### 0.5 Changes in v3.4 (compile: skip)

History. v3.4 adds **voice approvals**. Ids: `D` founder rulings (D58 in lore L-2026-10-17-ascend-061036-001; D59–D62 in L-2026-10-17-ascend-063541-001, which resolve where the two reviews diverge and win over both; D63, the librarian plan, does not touch this spec), `S26-` Aegis's voice-approval review (26-aegis-voice-approvals.md: `C1..C3 H1..H6 M1..M7 L1..L4`), `A26-` Arky's (27-arky-voice-approvals.md: `A26-01..06, 08..11`; there is no A26-07). Where the reviews still differ on a detail no ruling covers, the stricter (Aegis) option is taken unless it breaks hands-free reversible use; each such choice is named in the last row.

| Id | What changed |
|---|---|
| D58 | Voice may decide cards when a project opts in. **Toggle 1** = reversible (the cached-unlock class: `tool.permission` allow/deny, `ask-user`, `plan`); **toggle 2**, separate and shown with a kernel-composed warning, adds the gate class (fresh-presence `gate-class`, `fallback-cloud`, `resume`). Both off by default; with both off voice opens and never decides (Atelier G1). Phrases are configurable (default prefix `claude admin`). New §14.7 is the authoritative mechanism (§3.1 I3/I4, §3.4, §5, §14.4). |
| D59 (targeting) | Hybrid. Reversible cards are targeted by spoken **ordinal or closed label against the read-back deck** — one step, then hold (A26-05). Gate-class and judgment cards need the kernel's secret per-card **handle** word, shown only to the arming connection under capture exclusion or spoken into headphones, **plus** a 2-word challenge (S26-H3, Aegis §2.3). A free-word target is always two-step (readback + `confirm`). Arky's echo cross-check, phrase mask, half-duplex rule, `voice-phrase` card flag and interception apply to every voice decision (§14.7, §4.10, §12.6, §14.2). |
| D60 (arm windows) | Arm limits are root-signed config. **Gate arm** defaults (Aegis): 5 min hard, ≤ 3 gate decisions, headphones required, 2-word challenge, 5 s hold; configurable ceiling 60 min. **Reversible arm** defaults: 15 min idle, 60 min hard (configurable ≤ 240 min), ≤ 30 decisions; hold 3 s (range 2–10 s, never 0) (§3.1, §14.7). |
| D61 (judgment) | KNOT `resolve` and verdict `accept\|reject` by voice need a separate per-project sub-opt-in — class `judgment` in the grant row — off by default even with toggle 2; when on they follow gate-arm rules plus ≥ 2 s on-screen focus, and carry `via: voice` into Warpline attribution (S26-M7; §3.4, §14.7, §A.4). |
| D62 (custody) | Toggles are root-signed grant rows `grant.voice-approvals{repo, classes}` with `classes` ∈ `[reversible]`, `[reversible, gate]`, `[reversible, gate, judgment]` (A26-02); a set without its prerequisite is refused `BAD_REQUEST`. Phrases, timings, arm limits and the voice binary pin live in root-signed `~/.gnomon/config.yaml` (`voice.approvals`). Widening needs root presence; narrowing needs none. Never in `project.yaml` (ignored, logged `config.ignored`). Hand-only with every toggle on: `install`, `use-api-token`, `always`, `voice-arm`, `grant`, every root-signed verb, `voice-share`, flagged cards (union of S26 §2.6 and A26-04) (§15.4, §15.7, §14.7). |
| S26-C3 / A26-01 / A26-03 (arm) | A decision rests on a **voice arm**: a fresh-presence unlock of the approvals key bound to the pair {human `connId`, voice `connId`}. Separate reversible and gate arms; the gate arm needs a live reversible arm and shows the warning every time. Only the **kernel-supervised voice child** (root-signed binary path + sha, stdio, `voice` profile) can be armed; a socket-connected voice client cannot (`armCustody: supervised \| client` if the M4.1 TCC probe fails, with Aegis's peer check). Drops on idle, hard cap, decision limit, either connection closing, a new voice connection, screen lock / sleep / user switch, any voice-policy or config change, handle or challenge misses, `disarm`, GUI Disarm, an unenrolled decision phrase, daemon exit (§3.1, §14.7, §15.5). |
| A26-04 / S26 §2.2 (no new verb) | The voice principal calls `approve` in an **intent form** — `via: 'voice'`, no `cardId`, no `decisionSig`; the kernel resolves the target. The broker holds the decision, then signs with the approvals key under domain `gnomon:approve-voice:v1` (arm, turn, utterance, handle, challenge, readback and confirm hashes in the preimage); the approver is the arming human, `presence: voice-armed`. B4/B8 amended, B9 new; `G-BRK-06` (§3.1, §3.4, §5). |
| S26-C1 / A26-06 (self-approval) | Agent TTS, gnome lines, readback and card bodies can carry the phrase. Defences: phrase mask on all TTS (`voice.tts.filtered`), approval grammar muted during our playback + tail, half-duplex refusal `voice-echo`, kernel echo cross-check against `textSpoken` of the last 30 s, `voice-phrase` card flag, interception (decision utterances never reach a model), and for gate cards the handle no session, mod or voice process can read (§12.6, §14.2, §14.7, `G-VOX-09`). |
| S26-H1 / H2 / H3 | Replay and injection: handles and challenges single-use and never repeated per arm; `t0` after the handle or deck was issued; the playback gate (no other process rendering audio); whole-utterance anchored grammar (≤ 6 words before an answer's free text); handle hygiene (a live handle seen in any forwarded text rotates it); no batch phrase (`G-VOX-10`, `G-VOX-12`, `G-VOX-13`). |
| S26-H4 / H6 (surface) | Handles and challenges go **only to the arming connection**, rendered under capture exclusion (`NSWindow.sharingType = .none`; `WDA_EXCLUDEFROMCAPTURE`), never to a browser-surface connection; an arm opens only from the wrapper or a native client declaring capture exclusion (§11.5, §16, `G-VOX-11`). |
| S26-H5 | Toggle 2 trades fresh presence per decision for presence per short, counted window; stated plainly (§3.4, §A.4). Companion presence (`kSecAccessControlCompanion`, macOS 15+) is an M4.1 probe and the named replacement at the revisit trigger. |
| S26-M1..M7, L1..L4 | Enrolled speaker required (necessary, not sufficient); phrases validated at sign time; client claims logged `attestedBy: voice`, never a boundary; arms end on screen lock / sleep; the hold with echo, earcon and undo; flagged cards refused; judgment kinds per D61; kernel-owned armed indicator; hashes only in the chain before use, utterance text redacted; forced disarms accepted (fail closed). S26-L3 (deny by voice on every kind under toggle 1) **not adopted** — OPEN 14. |
| A26-05 (grammar) | `<prefix> <cmd> [target] [: text]`; commands `approve deny answer confirm undo cards open arm disarm`; n-best ambiguity refuses; labels from kernel fields only; ordinals against the last deck (≤ 60 s), unchanged by later cards (§14.7). |
| A26-08 (setting) | New kernel-owned `request.*` namespace with a named append exception (human on first-party human-class connections; voice for `request.voice.arm` only); `request.grant` raises a root-signed `grant` card, so the GUI gains a general path to root-signed grants; Settings → Project → Voice; armed indicator, Disarm, stack glyphs, held countdown, Cancel (§5, §9.1, §11.4, §11.5, `G-GUI-13`). |
| A26-09 / A26-10 (contracts) | `card:v1` adds kinds `voice-arm \| grant`, state `held`, decision `withdraw`, flag `voice-phrase`, a kernel-composed `voice{eligible, label, readback, readbackHash, echo}` block, `presence: voice-armed`, `via`, `voiceDecision{…}`; `voice:v1` adds `intercepted`, playback times, kernel-readback sources, `voice.deck`, `voice.focus`, `voice.tts.filtered`; refusal gate `voice` (§3.4, §4.6, §4.10, §9.1–§9.3, §9.6). |
| A26-11 (sequencing) | §4.9's compatible-additive rule covers enum values; M1.1 reserves the D58 values so `clientApiId` does not move in Move 4; M1.6 asserts the voice row refuses; Move 4 gains M4.5 (toggle 1) and M4.6 (toggle 2 + judgment); toggle 2's switch stays disabled until `T-VOX-02` has been run and reported (§19, §20). |
| Unification | One event scheme `approval.voice.*` (`arm disarm intent held withdrawn refused policy.narrowed handle.rotated`); one presence value `voice-armed`; one signature domain; one refusal style — `FORBIDDEN`, gate `voice`, `data.reason` `voice-…`; falsifiers `G-VOX-08..16` (renumbered from both reviews), `G-BRK-06`, `G-GUI-13`, `T-VOX-02`; new nodes `$voice-approval`, `^voice-armed`; seventh sandbox profile `voice`; threat row 20; §A.4; falsifier 14; OPEN 14. |
| Judgment calls | Stricter option taken where no ruling covered: separate gate arm (S26) over one arm with classes (A26); no implicit "the only card" target (S26-H3 over A26-05); the kernel, not the voice child, resolves every target (no `cardId` in the intent); arming only from the wrapper or a capture-excluding native client, so not from the CLI (`gnomon voice disarm` stays); the playback gate applies to both arm classes; handle-miss disarm (3 reversible / 1 gate) and ≤ 2 gate arms per hour per repo; challenge confirm window 10 s, one try; any voice-policy or config change ends all arms. Reconciliations: handles and challenges are rendered or spoken by the **arming connection**, never by the voice child (S26 had the voice client speak them into headphones, which contradicts `G-VOX-12`); deny-direction decisions skip the hold (S26), the rest follow A26's hold; phrase validation compares the distinguishing tails, since a shared prefix leaves one differing word; `disarm` (S26) over `stop` (A26) so the word cannot collide with speech meaning "stop the agent"; the hand-only set also takes Aegis's `daemon stop` / `log segment`. Added beyond both reviews: attach and Reconnect presence are hand-only; a `held` card's timeout is suspended during its hold (B6); the §16 voice-approvals parity row (P8-R4); `voice` in §2.3's profile list. |
| D64–D67 (OPEN 14 resolved) | `maxDecisions` configurable up to `unlimited` (time caps still bind); gate and judgment cards allowed in conversation mode; deny/reject by voice on every card kind under toggle 1, no hold (S26-L3); companion presence an optional per-decision upgrade for gate cards on macOS. Gesture decisions recorded as P8-R8 (later). |
