### 0.6 Changes in v3.3 (compile: skip)

History. Ids: `D` founder rulings (D54–D57, lore L-2026-10-16-ascend-223456-001), `A24-` Arky's v3.2 review (24-arky-v32-review.md: top defects `A24-T1..T3`, numbered parts `A24-1..7`), `S25-` Aegis's v3.2 review (25-aegis-v32-review.md: `C1 C2 H1..H6 M1..M7 L1..L5`).

| Id | What changed |
|---|---|
| D54 (reload) | A GUI connection has no reconnect grace and the page stores nothing secret (no `sessionStorage`, no `localStorage`, no cookie for any token). After a reload the page shows **Reconnect**; the kernel raises a Touch ID / Windows Hello presence prompt naming the `connId` and the count of open GUI sessions, and re-attaches that tab with a fresh GUI token delivered over the WebSocket. The endpoint lingers 2 minutes after the last GUI connection closes so a reload can reach Reconnect, then stops (`socket.web.stop{reason:'no-sessions'}`) — it is not tied to daemon idle exit (§2.4, §4.9, §11.2 rules 2 and 2a, `G-WEB-08`). |
| D55 / S25-C1 (launch) | The launch token is never on any argv: the wrapper receives it on stdin and navigates its WebView in-process; the macOS browser path hands the URL to LaunchServices from the signed helper (Apple Event `GetURL`, token read from stdin); the Windows browser path opens a token-less URL and attaches through the presence prompt, because `ShellExecute` puts the URL on the browser's command line. The `session` profile denies loopback connects to the endpoint port and process information on other processes (`G-SBX-08`). A launch token presented twice is theft: the GUI session that consumed it is revoked, `socket.auth.theft{connId}` is logged and a fresh-presence card is raised. The attach prompt names the `connId` and the count of open GUI sessions (§11.2 rule 2, §15.5, §15.1 row 19, §A.1). |
| D56 / S25-M2 / S25-L1 (wrapper) | The Tauri desktop wrapper (`packages/gnomon-wrapper`) is in Move 1 as the **default daily surface** (M1.13, `G-GUI-12`); it loads the page from `http://127.0.0.1:<port>` so the origin is the same as the browser's, with Tauri IPC disabled for page content, and verifies `serverSig` natively against the kernel public key it received on stdin. The browser surface stays available and carries a visible **extension-risk badge**; `status.guiSessions[].surface` says which is attached. Rule 10 (wrapper origin registration) is gone; the origin lock is one origin (§1, §2.2, §11.1, §11.2, §15.1 row 16, §16, §19.1, OPEN 10 closed). |
| D57 / A24-6 (Move 1) | ONE Move 1 with no α/β/γ checkpoints. The aesthetic session (OPEN 9) completes **before Move 1 starts** and its completion — final themes plus the `G-GUI-10` contrast fixtures — is an explicit Move 1 entry precondition (§19.1 "Entry preconditions", OPEN 9). Arky's test-placement findings are applied: `G-WIN-02` lives in M1.9, `G-WIN-04` in M1.5, `G-WIN-06` in M1.7 and M2.3. |
| S25-C2 | `always` is a **fresh-presence** decision: `approval.rule.learned` is never written under a cached unlock (§3.4, §11.5, `G-BRK-05`). |
| S25-H1 / A24-2 (contradiction) | The cached unlock is **per attached human-class connection** (`connId`) that passed presence — any conformant client, not only the GUI — and no connection uses another's unlock; it drops after 15 idle minutes or when that connection closes (§3.1, §3.4, §11.5, §12.8, §A.1, `G-ID-06`, `G-GUI-06`). The "nothing in the GUI's path is a privilege other clients lack" sentence is now true: the privilege is presence on a human-class connection. |
| S25-H2 / A24-3 (linked mods) | A linked mod runs from an **immutable content-addressed snapshot** `~/.gnomon/mods/<name>/pinned/<packageHash>/` copied at link and at every re-pin (no hash-then-load race); the copier refuses symlinks, junctions, hardlinks (`nlink > 1`), `.node` addons and any path escaping the folder; `.git/**` is never read and the kernel never runs git in a linked folder (S25-L4); the `session` profile denies writes to linked folders; dev mode caps effects at `read`, expires (8 h default), is refused while the folder lies under a live session's `cwd`/worktree, and any **manifest widening** raises a card even in dev mode; `node_modules` needs a lockfile and the card lists every `package@version`; `rollback` re-pins the previous snapshot and never unlinks; `link`/`install` from a kernel-spawned shell stage a card instead of signing (§3.1 I3, §12.4, §12.5, §15.5, `G-MOD-11`). |
| S25-H3 (gnome) | Tier 0 templates ship in the mod package under `packageHash` with validated required slots and are **filled by the host** from kernel fields — `persona.set` cannot touch them; `persona.set` is scoped to the caller's own session; Tier 2 lines carry a distinct `model` label and glyph (§12.6, `G-GNM-03`). |
| S25-H4 / A24-4 (knowledge) | `#knowledge-query` is a first-party **mod** (`packages/gnomon-mods/knowledge`) — one owner surface — whose install card lists the read paths its providers need; providers are **library providers** inside that mod (notebooks, neurons, lore, decisions, Steward memory) or **mod providers** reached through `mod.command` under `knowledge:federate`; `requester` is stamped by the host from the caller's principal, `scope` is clamped to the caller's redaction class, redaction runs **after** providers answer, each provider's tier is capped by its `grant.trust`, the neurons provider is off unless `project.yaml` opts in, providers with `network` are refused; `kind: provider` is a first-party-only manifest kind; `inject.receipt` is written by the userland `gnomon-mods` MCP bridge, never by the kernel (§4.3, §8.10, §12.3, §15.4, §18, OPEN 13, `G-KNW-04`). |
| S25-H5 / S25-M7 (Doodle egress) | `doodle.view` results are tool results — with a Claude backend they leave the machine; they include only **accepted, redacted** blobs, never the transcript window, and the bridge logs `inject.receipt`; the Doodle index stores `doodle:` and `turn:` refs, never transcript text (alignment is a `log.read` join at query time under the requester's class); `G-DDL-02` claims "a pattern the local pass detects", never "never" (§12.7). |
| S25-H6 | The sanitizer renders bidi controls (U+202A–U+202E, U+2066–U+2069, and the marks U+200E, U+200F, U+061C) as visible escape glyphs; strips tag characters (U+E0000–U+E007F), the variation selectors supplement (U+E0100–U+E01EF), zero-width and invisible characters (U+200B–U+200D, U+2060–U+2064, U+FEFF, U+00AD, U+180E, U+034F) and Hangul fillers (U+115F, U+1160, U+3164, U+FFA0); strips misplaced U+FE00–U+FE0F; maps U+2028/U+2029 to `\n`; shows an unterminated ESC as `⟨ESC⟩`; and flags a card body that needed any of it with a visible badge and `approval.request.flags[]`. The exact sets and order are §4.5 and the normative `conformance/display.v1.json` vectors (§4.5, `G-GUI-11`; amended M1.12, Aegis round 2). |
| S25-M1 | M1.0 proves that Windows Hello / NCrypt can show kernel-written prompt text; if it cannot, the kernel's text is shown in a helper-owned window beside the Hello prompt, and `status` reports `presencePromptText: native | helper-window` (§12.8, §16, §19.1). |
| S25-M3 / A24-5 (Excalidraw) | Fonts and assets self-hosted in the bundle; embeddables, iframes and link fields disabled; images travel as `/blob` fetches turned into `blob:` URLs, never `data:`; every placed image is re-encoded to raster and `/blob` never serves SVG; no CSP loosening without a `G-WEB-06` case (§11.2 rule 6, §12.7). |
| S25-M4 | The fragment carries the kernel's Ed25519 public key (`#launch=<token>&k=<spki>`); the page verifies `serverSig` with WebCrypto on every `hello` and pins the key (public material) for Reconnect; a key mismatch refuses with a visible error (§4.9, §11.2 rule 2). |
| S25-M5 | The conformance badge reports **declared** (`hello.features.conformance`), never "passed"; community clients get the redacted class unless the human grants `log.read:sensitive` to that device key (§12.8, §15.4). |
| S25-M6 | Token-exchange and reconnect rate limits are **per connection**; an endpoint-wide cap of 64 connections closes the oldest unauthenticated one, never the authenticated ones (§11.2 rule 8). |
| S25-L2 / L3 / L5 | The page clears the fragment with `history.replaceState` before anything else runs; a non-static HTTP request (`/diag`, `/blob/<sha>`) without `Sec-Fetch-Site` is refused, while a WebSocket upgrade without it is accepted only on the exact `Origin` (Chrome sends no Fetch Metadata on the handshake), and a present `Sec-Fetch-Site` other than `same-origin`/`none` is refused everywhere; there is no reconnect grace, so no builder reaches for `sessionStorage` (§11.2). |
| A24-T1 | New grant-named read class **`turn.text:attached-session`** (capability in §15.4, row in §9.3): the assistant and user text parts of an attached session's `turn.event`, never `tool_input`, never tool results. The gnome Tier 2 line and the Doodle replay alignment read through it; the install card names it. |
| A24-T2 | Doodle exports are **`modns.doodle.export.requested`** events carried out by a userland writer under the `paradigm` grant (the `gnomon learn read` reader), which appends `paradigm.doodle.exported{ds, kind, ref}`; the mod never writes outside its own dir (§8.8, §12.7). |
| A24-T3 | **Kernel key** added to §3.1's key classes: Ed25519, held in the OS keystore item `gnomon-kernel-key` through the signed helper, loaded into kernel memory at start, never a plain file; signs `serverSig`, `card.actions[]`, `oracle.result` and every kernel-appended event; its registry row is root-signed. Same-user forgery scope stated per OS (§3.1, §15.2, §A.1, §A.3). |
| A24-1 (boundary) | HTTP and RFC 6455 parsing move into **`gnomon-web`**, a kernel-supervised transport child under the `transport` profile that passes auth frames through unchanged and never sees a token's meaning; RFC 6455 details named (no `permessage-deflate`, text frames only, no fragmentation, masking required, ping/pong, close codes, UTF-8); `guiBundleHash` pinned in root-signed config; `/blob` is fetched with the header and shown through a `blob:` URL (§2.1, §4.9, §11.2, §15.5, §18, `G-WEB-07`). |
| A24-2 (client API) | `hello{clientApis[], features[]}` version negotiation with compatible-additive rules; `modAbiId` and `clientApiId` decoupled through a shared `kit:v1` hash; `doodle-shape:v1` and `persona:v1` in the published set; unknown `modns.*` kinds render opaquely; law 3 counts "12 kernel verbs + 2 client relays"; `cancel` is in the mod-ABI table (§4.9, §10, §12.3, §12.8, §18, §20 falsifier 12). |
| A24-5 (Doodle) | `by` is stamped by the host (`relayedFor{principal, connId}`); ops are chain events kept forever (only image blobs prune), so `doodle:@seq` refs never break; element↔shape mapping is a pure pair with golden tests; drag coalescing, optimistic render with rollback, undo as inverse ops; `doodle.view`'s PNG comes from the mod's own rasterizer over `doodle-shape:v1` (a different picture from the human's, stated); `kit.Canvas{ds, readOnly?}` everywhere; full `modns.doodle.*` kind names (§12.7, `G-DDL-05`). |
| A24-7 | Body is self-contained; Appendix A regenerated by `compile71.py` (zero unoracled nodes). |

### 0.7 Changes in v3.2 (compile: skip)

History. Ids: `D` founder rulings (D40–D53), `R` requirements (R5–R7), `L06`/`L12` Loid's research (06-loid-learning.md §2, 12-loid-direct.md), `AES` the aesthetic session (`.atelier/sessions/2026-10-15-gnomon-aesthetic/session.yaml`). Rows that v3.3 later changed (the 10-minute reconnect grace, the wrapper after Move 1, wrapper-origin registration, `knowledge-query` as a library behind `paradigm_retrieve`) stand here as history; §0 wins.

| Id | What changed |
|---|---|
| D50 (corrects the shell reading of D26/D32) | Gnomon's shell is a **terminal-like GUI served by the local kernel on localhost and opened in the browser** — not an in-terminal TUI. `#tui` becomes `#gui` (`packages/gnomon-gui`, a browser page that is a pure client of `gnomon-rpc:v1` over a loopback WebSocket); pi-tui is gone from every package, section and test; the kernel gains `#web-endpoint` (loopback HTTP for the static bundle + the WebSocket transport of the ABI, built on `node:http`, zero dependencies). The keyboard-first, prompt-centred, command-palette feel is kept. `G-TUI-01..06` become `G-GUI-01..06` with the same claims measured in a browser; `G-GUI-07..10` are new; `T-TUI-01` becomes `T-GUI-01` (§1, §2, §4.5, §4.9, §11, §16, §18, §19, §20). |
| D50 (localhost security) | New §11.2: bind `127.0.0.1` only; a single-use **launch token** handed to the page by the CLI over the already-authenticated socket, exchanged for a GUI session token held in page memory; **origin lock** (only Gnomon's own page); **DNS-rebinding defence** (`Host` allowlist); **CSRF** closed by having no cookies and checking `Sec-Fetch-Site`; **no CORS header ever**; strict CSP; **WebSocket auth deadline**; rate and path limits; Windows parity. Tests `G-WEB-01..06`; threat rows 16–18 (§15.1). The browser is a client of the same API as every other client. |
| D50 (wrapper) | An optional desktop wrapper (Tauri recommended, macOS + Windows) loads the same page from the same endpoint with the same token; its WebView origin is allowlisted only while it is the launcher. Not in the Move 1 pass; OPEN 10. |
| D51 | The terminal gets the **CLI only** (`gnomon spawn|status|approve|replay|open|mod …`); no in-terminal UI. `gnomon open` is the launcher (device-authenticated, refused from kernel-spawned shells); CLI output is a text projection of the same blocks (§3.1 I3, §9.5, §11.1). |
| D40 / AES | Look: light mode = **papercraft**, dark mode = **phosphor terminal**, optional automatic day/night swap; key edges ≥ 3:1; project hues are fills, never text (`G-GUI-10`, §11.3). |
| D41 / AES | **Workshop** is a first-party, optional mod view (`#workshop-mod`): a needs-you queue of caps and a working zone whose caps bob only while their session streams — an informed override of motion-on-events scoped to that mod; hat shape = work type, filled = Claude, outlined = local, soft hue = project, violet only for KNOT, every attribute from kernel events (`G-WSP-01`, §11.6). |
| D42 / AES | Chrome principle: header, footer, sidebars and mod slots show only what is necessary; everything is reachable from the command palette; layout presets; the Workshop and the Shuttle view can be moved or hidden (`G-GUI-08`, §11.4). |
| D43 / AES | Decisions arrive as a **paper stack** (paper in light, chips in dark) that can be fanned or browsed so a more important decision can be taken first; inside a session only that project's decisions show (`G-GUI-09`, §11.4). |
| D44 / AES | Left sidebar of active and past sessions grouped by project, with new-session creation (§11.4). |
| R5 / D45–D49 / D52 | **Gnome persona** is a first-party mod (`#gnome-persona-mod`, §12.6; concept doc `docs/specs/gnomon-mods/gnome-persona.md`): a dialog voice whose rules follow Simplified Technical English (ASD-STE100) with a length budget; **no restyling pass** — brevity by instruction, so the local-model paraphrase tier of the concept doc is dropped; the relay never rephrases cards, refusals, billing, security or certification text; raw output is always one key away and logged; emotes are driven by kernel events first, phrases second; token claims stay honest; a `persona:v1` manifest is editable live by any agent through the mod ABI. Tests `G-GNM-01..03`, telemetry `T-GNM-01`. |
| R6 | **Doodle** is a first-party mod (`#doodle-mod`, flow `$doodle-session`, §12.7; concept doc `docs/specs/gnomon-mods/doodle.md`): a shared, replayable sheet for the human and the agents. Simpler than the concept doc because the GUI hosts the canvas directly through the new `kit.Canvas` block — there is no separate canvas client, no second loopback listener. Engine: **Excalidraw (MIT)**; tldraw's SDK licence blocks bundling. Doodle sessions, replay by fold, branching, lore compaction, the `doodle:` ref scheme. Tests `G-DDL-01..04`, telemetry `T-DDL-01`. |
| L06 §2 / L12 | **Shared knowledge search**: `#knowledge-query` (§8.10), a userland federated read verb over manifest-registered providers — notebooks, neurons, lore, decisions, Steward memory, doodles — with provenance and trust tiers on every hit, receipts for cross-agent reads, and the scoped phrasing "check the doodles for X". Never a kernel verb (law 3). Manifest field `provides.knowledge`; capability `knowledge:provide`. Tests `G-KNW-01..03`. |
| R7 / D53 | **Linked mods**: `gnomon mod link <folder>` raises an install card listing declared effects, then pins the folder's `packageHash`; any change (an edit or `git pull`) pauses the mod and raises a re-approve card listing the changed files; dev mode re-pins on save and shows a visible `dev` badge (§12.5). Events `mod.linked | paused | repinned | unlinked`; tests `G-MOD-08`, `G-MOD-09`. |
| R7 (community clients) | The **client API is published and versioned**: `gnomon-rpc:v1`, `display-block:v1` (with the kit), `card:v1`, `refusal:v1`, `event:v1` in `@a-company/gnomon-contracts`, hashed as `clientApiId` (reported by `status`, frozen per release) with a conformance suite (`gnomon client conform`), so community GUIs and tools are interchangeable peers of the first-party GUI under the same auth and unspoofable-card rules; the presence prompt text comes from the kernel's card record, never from the client (§12.8). Tests `G-CLI-02`, `G-CLI-03`; threat row 17. |
| Kit (R6, D50) | The mod view kit gains `kit.Image{blob, alt, w, h}` (alt mandatory) and `kit.Canvas{ds}` (a surface the host owns; a mod references it and never ships code to a client); `modAbiId` covers both (`G-MOD-10`, §12.2). The client↔mod relay that v3.1 left implicit is made explicit: `mod.view` and `mod.command` ride the socket, are served by `#mod-host`, are counted in `modAbiId` and are not kernel verbs (§12.3, §5). |
| D36 (one Move 1) | Moves re-planned proper-first: Move 1 gains `#web-endpoint` (M1.4), the GUI (M1.10), the Workshop mod, kit additions, linked mods and the client relays (M1.11), the client API + conformance suite (M1.12); Move 3 gains `#knowledge-query` (M3.7), `#gnome-persona-mod` (M3.8) and `#doodle-mod` (M3.9), which depend only on Move 1 and may start in parallel with Move 2; Move 4 speaks the gnome line (§19). |
| OPEN | OPEN 9 rewritten (aesthetic session paused; open boards); OPEN 10–13 added (wrapper, gnome persona, Doodle, knowledge-provider home) (§22). |

### 0.8 Changes in v3.1 (compile: skip)

History. Ids: `D` founder rulings (D36–D39), `S22-` Aegis v3 review (22-aegis-v3-review.md), `A23-` Arky v3 review (23-arky-v3-review.md). Rows that v3.2 later superseded (pi-tui as the shell renderer, `#tui`, `G-TUI-*`) stand here as history; §0 wins.

| Id | What changed |
|---|---|
| S22-C1 / A23-2 / D37 | The local runner leaves the kernel: `gnomon-local` is a child executable over stdio (pi's rpc mode, `rpc-entry.ts`), one per local session, under the `session` sandbox profile; pi-ai and pi-agent-core never load in the kernel (kernel keeps zero runtime deps); `#local-runner` is now the kernel's stdio driver for that child, in the same shape as `claude -p` (§3.3, §13). `G-LCL-06`: a local session cannot read the kernel's key material. |
| D37 / A23-6 | The Claude-Code-to-local-endpoint probe is dropped; `G-LCL-05` becomes a `G-SUB-01` loopback-override refusal case; recorded for 8.1 (§13.2, OPEN 2 closed). |
| S22-C2 / A23-4 / D39 | Root key = non-exportable hardware P-256 (Secure Enclave on macOS; TPM via Windows Hello `KeyCredentialManager` or NCrypt platform provider on Windows) through small signed native helpers (`packages/gnomon-helper`); registry accepts `gkey-p256:v1` root rows; agent/device keys stay Ed25519 (§3.1). macOS Developer ID signing identity → OPEN 1. |
| D38 / A23-7 | Approvals key = hardware-backed, presence-unlocked once on TUI attach, held in memory, dropped after 15 min idle; fresh prompt always for gate-class, install, KNOT resolve, verdict, cloud fallback, budget resume; cached unlock covers `tool.permission`, `ask-user`, `plan`; `gnomon approve` from the CLI without a TUI prompts every time. The device-auth key (Ed25519, file) is split from the approvals key. No approval key on disk. §A.1 rewritten (§3.1, §3.4, §11, §A). |
| S22-C3 / A23-2 / A23-3 | Default-deny `mod` sandbox profile (writes only `~/.gnomon/mods/<name>/`, egress only through the kernel's host-allowlist proxy to granted hosts, no process exec); `packageHash` over the whole mod tree; mod kinds under `modns.<name>.*` (cannot collide with kernel `mod.*`); `kernel.call` carries the caller hsid and intersects scopes, spend comes from the session's permit; mod tools reach agents as a per-session MCP server run by the host (the ≤12 kernel proxy is untouched); `init`/`shutdown`/`cancel`/feature negotiation; `modAbiId`; `gnomon mod new`, stderr in `inspect`; mod children stop on idle exit (§12). |
| S22-H1 / S22-H8 | `session` profile denies AppleEvents, LaunchServices (`open`), `launchctl`, mic/camera, writes under `~/.gnomon` and to every Claude settings layer; `G-SBX-04..06` run against the session profile; Windows reports `partial` where reads cannot be denied (`WRITE_RESTRICTED` restricts writes only) (§15.5, §A.3). |
| S22-H2 / S22-H3 | Law 7 watches every settings layer mid-session (deny writes via profile + `fs.watch`; change ⇒ kill + `billing.api.refused{where}` + gate-class card); scans any non-default `*_BASE_URL`, `mcp.json` env blocks, and current managed-policy sources (`C:\Program Files\ClaudeCode\managed-settings.json`, `managed-settings.d/`, HKLM/HKCU policy; macOS managed preferences; `C:\ProgramData\ClaudeCode` retired at v2.1.75); only `#claude-adapter` consumes Claude permits (`permit.backend` binding); `gnomon-local` endpoints loopback/LAN only in root-signed config; mod/MCP egress grants refuse Anthropic API hosts; the api-token secret lives in the keystore and is injected into exactly one process; residual (out-of-kernel `claude` + repo `.env`) stated as visibility only (§3.4, §10, §13.2). |
| S22-H4 | Git: `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1 GIT_ATTR_NOSYSTEM=1`; refuse a `.git/config`/`.gitattributes` declaring `filter.*`, `merge.*.driver`, `diff.*.textconv`, `include*`; kernel git always inside the `git` sandbox class (§3.3). |
| S22-H5 | `card.*` slots append-only; kernel card bodies and status indicators unreplaceable; mod content fenced and labelled `mod:<name>`; decision keys reserved; C0/C1/OSC (incl. OSC 52, OSC 8) stripped from every block string and transcript line (§4.5, §11, §12.2). |
| S22-H7 / D39 | Windows named pipe created by the signed helper with a DACL + `FIRST_PIPE_INSTANCE`; clients verify the server PID via `GetNamedPipeServerProcessId` against the pidfile (also peer credentials on Windows) (§2.4, §16). |
| S22-M1..M3 / A23-2 | Voice gets its own principal `p:<ns>/voice` with its own device key (scope `voice`: never `approve`, never root verbs, never a decision by speech); speaker id is not authentication; `voice.*` becomes a userland grant namespace; enrolment = root-signed rows; only the founder's enrolled turns are forwarded by default; voiceprints under `~/.gnomon/voice/prints/` labelled `secret`, deletable (`gnomon voice forget`); voice text as prunable blobs; AEC or headphones required for conversation mode; turns tagged `source: voice`; the `voice` ref scheme dropped (§14). |
| S22-M4 | Reviewer egress through a kernel-run loopback proxy with a hostname allowlist (sandbox allows network only to that port) — hostnames are not enforceable inside `sandbox-exec` (§7.3, §15.5). |
| S22-M5 / S22-L1 / S22-L2 | Mod-principal column in the ABI matrix; `gnomon mod rollback`, `voice enrol|forget` in the human-only verb list; "user-less child" removed (§5, §3.1). |
| A23-1 | `G-LAW-01c` → M1.8, `G-SUB-01` → M1.7; claim expiry defined (`ttl` param, default 2 h, kernel timer); `repo` id = `project.yaml.repoId` when present, else root commit (§4.1, §7.4, §19). |
| A23-2 | Manifest `kind: adapter` is first-party only (§4.3). |
| A23-4 | Interrupt on both OS via the stream-json control message (`control_request interrupt`), not SIGINT/CTRL_C; env allowlist adds `PATHEXT WINDIR SystemDrive ProgramFiles CLAUDE_CODE_GIT_BASH_PATH`; `layersHash` normalizes CRLF and path case (§3.3). |
| A23-5 / D36 | ONE Move 1 kept (founder); inside it: M1.0 probes the Windows root-key type, pipe helper and local capability; Windows CI on platform-independent tests from day one; each test attached to the task that can pass it; `gnomon-oracle` profile moves to M2.3 (§19). |
| A23-7 | Compaction re-derived (not lifted from pi-coding-agent `compaction.ts:129` / `branch-summarization.ts:305`) with an ~8k target; Gnomon departs from `lifecycle.ts:44-50`'s `os.tmpdir()` fallback (stated); suffixed test ranges defined (`G-SBX-04w..06w`); `gnomon-kernel` Warpline token stored at `~/.gnomon/warpline/<repoId>.token` 0600, denied to sessions (§3.2, §6.2, §7.1, §13.1). |
| A23 citations | `agents.ts:456` for the forbidden-flag throw; deepseek-harness paths now cite the implementation (`packages/sandbox/sandbox-windows-acl/src/{index.ts:4,28, ffi.ts:253, runner.ts:4}` @ `86a402200d`); noted that dsh moved to React/Electron even after the cache fix (§11). |

---

## 1. Thesis, positioning, non-goals

**Thesis.** Gnomon is the kernel and the shell beneath the armada: one hash-chained log, one identity, one approval broker with the budget fuse inside, one scheduler, one manifest grammar, a terminal-like GUI served on localhost that drives local models and Claude alike, and — in Warpline projects — every session Warpline-native from birth. Claude Code is the gold-standard coding agent and becomes a **headless backend** driven on the founder's subscription (`claude -p` stream-json, P8-D26/D34/D35); local models are first-class backends for automations (P8-D20: "that's why I wanted my own harness"). The owned kernel is justified by three things Claude Code alone cannot give (lore L-2026-10-16-ascend-065405-001): local-model automations, a cross-session budget and log, and merge adjudication. The founder's tools already form a harness in pieces (04-scout-own-family.md); 8.0 is a deletion project before a build project (07). Positioning (13-fable-names.md): **"ruflo coordinates agents; Gnomon adjudicates their work"** — and owns the seat they are driven from. The market converges on plugins/skills/hooks/worktrees and nobody adjudicates merges (03; dsh refuses, 02); nobody ships local two-way voice with a durable team transcript (20); nobody sandboxes mods (21); nobody gives a human and their agents a shared, replayable sheet whose every mark is a log event (§12.7). Those are Gnomon's slots.

**Non-goals (8.0).**
- NOT the Loom lane: no breadth-first meta-harness, no tool sprawl, no roleplayed distributed protocols, no agent-type catalog (09).
- **We don't re-wrap Task.** No Gnomon tool description steers a model off Claude Code's native Task/Agent tools (09 §4). Gnomon spawns supervised processes with permits, credentials, worktrees and a ledger.
- Not a hosted web app, not Electron, not an in-terminal UI: the shell (`#gui`) is a local web page served by the kernel on `127.0.0.1` (P8-D50), shown by default inside a thin Tauri desktop wrapper (macOS + Windows, the daily surface, P8-D56, §11.1) and available in the user's browser with a visible extension-risk badge; no kernel ever listens beyond loopback — linking to other kernels is outbound only, through the self-hosted `gnomon-relay`, the one off-loopback listener (P8-D74, §3.9); the terminal gets the CLI only (P8-D51). Conductor, constellation, Platform, Atelier Studio, flow's GUI and any community GUI stay as peers and clients of the same published API (§4.9, §12.8). deepseek-harness's move from a terminal renderer to React/Electron (23) is read as "the shell wants a DOM", which the browser already provides without Electron's footprint.
- Not a new knowledge store: neurons vault, notebooks, lore, decisions, `.purpose`, Warpline fabric bytes are never migrated (§17); shared knowledge search (§8.10) reads them in place.
- Not the Warpline server (TD-2026-10-14-551).
- Not cloud voice: only text goes to Claude; raw audio never leaves the box (P8-D29).
- Not containers (sandbox profiles; §A.2).
- Not a generic agent host (TD-2026-10-19-252): the backends are Claude and local models. There is no generic ACP passthrough; another agent (opencode, grok, …) is added only by its own driver and its own law-7 billing ruling (§2.2).
- Not a second local human (P8-D75): other humans exist only as **link peers** (§3.9) — never as principals on the local kernel, never as approvers of a local card. Not teleport: a handoff brief starts or continues a session on the receiver's own backend and subscription, and nothing is transplanted (P8-D73). Not shared fabrics (Warpline state does not cross kernels, P8-D76), ruflo interop, public marketplace (§21).
- Not Rust, not Swift for the kernel (05 §4): TypeScript on Node 24; FluidAudio (Swift) is an optional Mac voice backend behind a Node interface; native helpers (`packages/gnomon-helper`) are small, signed and do one thing each (§3.1, §16); the desktop wrapper (Tauri, Rust shell over the system WebView) is a shell around the page, never a second implementation of it.

---

## 2. Architecture

### 2.1 Kernel vs userland (TD-950 §2, amended by D26/D27/D33/D37/D50)

| Ring | Owns | Never contains |
|---|---|---|
| **Kernel** (`#gnomon-kernel`, one daemon per user, zero runtime npm deps) | `#identity`, `#kernel-log`, `#session-runner` + `#supervisor` over two stdio drivers (`#claude-adapter` → `claude -p`; `#local-runner` → the `gnomon-local` child), `#approval-broker` with `#budget-fuse` inside, `#scheduler`, `#manifest-loader`, `#ref-resolver`, `#socket` (the ABI, §5, over the unix socket / named pipe) and `#web-endpoint` (the same ABI over a loopback WebSocket plus the static GUI bundle served as opaque bytes — the kernel owns the rules and the auth, a sandboxed transport child `gnomon-web` owns the HTTP and RFC 6455 parsing, §11.2), `#oracle-sandbox` (§15.5), `#mod-host` (child per mod, sandboxed, effects enforced, linked snapshots pinned and folders watched, §12), `#peer-link` (pairing, the Noise handshake and the closed `gnomon-link:v1` protocol to paired kernels; the outbound-only transport child `gnomon-link` moves ciphertext and owns no key, §3.9) | the symbol graph, notebooks, calibration, `#contracts-compiler`, the Warpline engine (`warplined`), neurons, lore, the GUI's code, the wrapper, voice, pi-ai/pi-agent-core, React, Excalidraw, any model loop or LLM call of its own |
| **Userland** | `#gui` (the localhost GUI page, a WebSocket client of the ABI, shown in the Tauri wrapper or a browser), mods (manifest packages, including the first-party `#workshop-mod`, `#gnome-persona-mod`, `#doodle-mod`, `#knowledge-query`, the federated knowledge-search mod over library and mod providers, and `#cowork-mod`, the pull-only co-work inbox, §3.9), the `gnomon-mods` MCP bridge (userland, one per session), `gnomon-local` (pi-agent-core + pi-ai, a separate executable), `#gnomon-voice` (its own principal), `#learning-loop`, the compiler, `warplined`, neurons, lore, constellation/flow/Conductor/Platform clients, community GUIs and tools built on the published client API, the optional Claude Code Mod band, and `gnomon-relay` (on the relay host, never inside a kernel) | kernel state (it appends granted event kinds and reads the log) |

Boundary falsifier (05 §6.1, `G-KRN-01`): the kernel boots with zero userland packages and runs one Claude session and one local session end to end; `gnomon replay` reproduces every kernel-controlled input byte-for-byte while recording the hashes of host layers it does not control (§3.3). Any kernel import of `@a-company/premise-core`, any `@a-company/warpline` module, `paradigm-mcp`, `@a-company/gnomon-gui`, `@a-company/gnomon-mods`, `@a-company/gnomon-local`, pi-ai, pi-agent-core, React or Excalidraw is a build failure (`G-KRN-02`); `G-SEC-01` asserts the kernel's `package.json` has no runtime dependencies (pi-ai bundles Anthropic/Bedrock/Google/OpenAI SDKs — verified `packages/ai/package.json`, 23 — which is exactly why it cannot live in the law-7 process). The GUI bundle is served as static files from a root-signed path whose tree hash must equal the root-signed `gui.bundleHash` (§11.2 rule 1a), and RFC 6455 WebSocket server framing is implemented over the `node:http` upgrade event (no dependency; Node 24 ships a WebSocket client, not a server) — both inside `gnomon-web`, a transport child the kernel spawns from its own package under the `transport` profile (§15.5) and talks to over stdio: the child parses bytes into frames and forwards `{connId, headers, frame}` records; every rule of §11.2 (origin, host, token, deadline, limits) and every auth decision is taken by the kernel, which never parses HTTP and never evaluates, imports or inspects GUI code (the isolation test is §11.2 rule 1b's). The kernel never composes prompts of its own beyond the one pointer line (§6.2) and the adapters' system envelope; the local model loop lives in the `gnomon-local` child (§13), outside the kernel.

### 2.2 Topology: the GUI is a client, the kernel owns sessions (OpenCode's split, 21)

- **Clients** speak `gnomon-rpc:v1` (§4.9): `#gui` (primary shell, the page over the loopback WebSocket, inside the Tauri wrapper by default or in a browser), `#gnomon-voice` (its own principal, §14.4), the Claude Code plugin (hooks + ≤12-tool MCP proxy, for interactive Claude Code sessions the founder still opens by hand), the optional Mod band, constellation, Conductor (Swift, M3.6), flow (Python), Platform, Atelier, and any community GUI or tool built from the published client API (§12.8) — all peers under the same auth, the same per-connection presence rule for cached approvals (§3.4) and the same unspoofable-card rules. A client never owns a session; `session.open` is always a kernel act, so a closed browser tab or a crashed client leaves sessions running and re-attachable (`G-GUI-05`).
- **Distros** are manifests (§4.3): `paradigm` (default), `atelier`, `flow`, `constellation`.
- **Backend drivers** (`#backend-adapter` interface): `start(spec) → handle`, `send(handle, turn)`, `steer(handle, text)` (mid-turn queue, QM MessageQueue idea), `stop(handle, {interrupt})` (interrupt = the backend's control message, never a signal), typed events `init | turn.start | partial{delta} | message{usage?} | tool.use | tool.result | permission.request | ask.user{question} | plan.exit{plan} | result{usage,total_cost_usd} | exited | quota.error | model.capabilities`. Two implementations in 8.0, both **stdio children**: `#claude-adapter` (`claude -p`, §3.3) and `#local-runner` (`gnomon-local --mode rpc`, §13). Usage comes only from driver events, never model text (10 §1). Conformance: recorded transcripts replayed per backend version nightly (`G-ADP-01`, `G-ADP-02`). **Backend scope (TD-2026-10-19-252).** For now the interface has exactly these two implementations: Claude and local models. Other coding agents (opencode, grok and the like) get specific, proper support later, each as its own driver with its own law-7 billing ruling (§10), its own argv and env rules and its own recorded transcripts. There is no generic ACP passthrough: no driver forwards an arbitrary agent's protocol to the kernel, because a generic driver would carry no billing ruling and no conformance record.

### 2.3 ASCII

```
            ┌──────────────────────────────── userland ──────────────────────────────────┐
            │  wrapper (Tauri, default) | browser tab (badge): #gui (papercraft | phosphor) │
            │    transcript · decision stack · sessions · Shuttle view · command palette   │
            │    views ← mods (children, default-deny profile): workshop · gnome · doodle  │
            │    knowledge · cowork (mods)  gnomon-mods bridge  gnomon-voice (p:voice)     │
            │  contracts-compiler  learning-loop  warplined  neurons  lore                  │
            │  constellation  flow(py)  Conductor  community GUIs/tools  optional band      │
            └──────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────┘
       ws://127.0.0.1:<port>/rpc (launch token → GUI token)        │ gnomon-rpc:v1 (unix socket | helper-created named pipe)
  ┌──────────────────▼──────────────────────────────────────────────▼─────────────────────┐
  │ gnomon-web (transport child: HTTP + RFC 6455 parsing only) → web-endpoint rules  socket │
  │   (127.0.0.1 only · origin lock · Host allowlist · no cookies · CSP · bundleHash)       │
  │ mutual auth(device key | GUI token | read token) · hello negotiation · 12 verbs + 2     │
  │ relays · status (F4)                                                                    │
  │ approval-broker ── budget-fuse (tokens · USD · compute · subscription windows · law 7)   │
  │ session-runner ── supervisor ── #claude-adapter (claude -p) │ #local-runner (stdio)     │
  │ identity (root P-256 in SE/TPM · approvals key HW-backed · kernel key in keystore ·      │
  │           device keys Ed25519)                                                          │
  │ manifest-loader · mod-host (child per mod, pinned snapshots, folders watched) · refs     │
  │ scheduler · sandbox (oracle · reviewer · session · mod · git · transport · voice · link)│
  │ log · peer-link ── gnomon-link (transport child, outbound only) ──▶ gnomon-relay (LAN)  │
  └────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────┘
                   ▼ (session profile)             ▼ (session profile)
   claude -p --output-format stream-json …    gnomon-local --mode rpc  →  llama.cpp / Ollama / LM Studio / vLLM / mlx_lm.server (loopback/LAN only)
   (subscription only, law 7)                 (pi-agent-core + pi-ai, outside the kernel)
```

### 2.4 Daemon lifecycle (`$daemon-lifecycle`)

1. **One per user.** **Home** is `~/.gnomon` (`%USERPROFILE%\.gnomon` on Windows). `GNOMON_HOME` (an absolute path; anything else is refused) overrides it as a **test-only seam** read by the client-side resolver; the production daemon learns its home only from `--home <path>` on its argv, passed by the activating client, because the clean re-exec env (rule 2) drops every `GNOMON_*`. Lock file `~/.gnomon/gnomon.pid` created `O_EXCL` (`daemonPid:v1 {pid, startedAt, socketPath}`); live holder refuses, dead holder cleaned and re-raced — lifted from `packages/warpline/src/daemon/lifecycle.ts:1-14`. A holder owned by another user (signal 0 answers `EPERM`) is never a kernel of this home and is cleaned like a dead one; a same-user live holder is `already-running` (`superseded`) only when a kernel answers `hello` on the daemon's own socket path within 2 s, else `holder-silent` (`transient`), and its pidfile is left alone because a silent holder may be a racer between its lock and its listen (Aegis L2, T-2026-10-19-011). Endpoint: unix socket `~/.gnomon/gnomon.sock` (0600). **Socket ownership: "can't tell" is never "dead".** The socket's directory must be the user's and 0700, so there is no window between bind and chmod. The kernel binds a private random name in that directory, sets and reads back its mode, then publishes it: by link when `gnomon.sock` is free, otherwise by rename over it only after a connect probe proved the old holder dead (ECONNREFUSED, confirmed by a second probe after a short pause: on Darwin a live listener whose accept queue is full also refuses, so one refusal is strong but not absolute proof). A connect that succeeds is a live holder; a timeout, EPERM or any other error is undecided; both are refused (`endpoint-busy`) and the old socket is never deleted. Node's `server.close()` unlinks the bound name silently, and that is the private name, so closing never removes a successor's socket; the kernel removes `gnomon.sock` itself on close only while it is still the socket its live listener holds. The pidfile code never unlinks the socket. Node has no peer-uid call (getpeereid / SO_PEERCRED), so the 0700 directory, the 0600 socket and the mutual signatures are the boundary. Gnomon deliberately departs from `lifecycle.ts:44-50`'s `os.tmpdir()` fallback: the socket is never under `/tmp`, `/private/tmp`, `/var/tmp` or a world-writable `os.tmpdir()`. On Linux, when `sun_path` overflows, the fallback is `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/gnomon-<sha256(home)[0..16]>.sock`, used only if that directory is owned by the user and mode 0700 (`lifecycle.ts:33-50`); on macOS a socket path over the 100-byte `sun_path` limit is refused, with no fallback; the daemon refuses to start if no path fits. Clients derive the socket path from the home by the same rule and never trust the pidfile's `socketPath` (same-user writable, diagnostic only). Frames are byte-level NDJSON (§4.9). On Windows the endpoint is a named pipe `\\.\pipe\gnomon-<sha256(userSid)[0..16]>` created **by the signed helper** with an owner-only DACL (current user SID only) and `FIRST_PIPE_INSTANCE` (S22-H7; §16); the server is authenticated by `serverSig` (§4.9) — the pidfile is same-user writable and Node's `net` exposes no `GetNamedPipeServerProcessId`, so the PID comparison is a diagnostic in the helper and the Python client only, never a security claim (S29-K6). The loopback web endpoint (`127.0.0.1`, ephemeral port, §11.2) listens only while a GUI connection is wanted — started by the first `gnomon open`, kept for a 2-minute **linger** after the last GUI connection closes so a reloaded page can reach Reconnect (§11.2 rule 2a), then stopped with `socket.web.stop{reason:'no-sessions'}` and its transport child ended; it is never held open by daemon idle time alone.
2. **Client activation.** A client that cannot connect runs `gnomon daemon --detach`; the daemon re-execs itself with a fixed clean env (`PATH` from root-signed config, `HOME`/`USERPROFILE`, locale; `NODE_OPTIONS` and all `GNOMON_*` dropped) so the activator's environment never reaches the key-holding process (S-H3). The re-exec passes exactly one Node flag, `--disable-sigusr1` (Node ≥ 22.14 / ≥ 23.7), and `gnomond --foreground` refuses to start (`sigusr1-enabled`) unless that flag is in its `execArgv`: without it, a same-user `kill -USR1` opens the V8 inspector inside the key-holding process at run time, past every env and flag check. A JS `SIGUSR1` listener does not count (T-2026-10-18-030). A SEA build must bake the flag into its sea-config `execArgv`. Backend binaries (`claude`, `gnomon-local`, llama.cpp, Ollama) and the GUI bundle directory come from root-signed config paths. launchd/Task Scheduler/service activation is 8.1. **Recommendation (Aegis, M1.7; OPEN 22):** gnomond should be launched by launchd or the signed app, not from an interactive terminal, so the responsible process — whose TCC grants (Accessibility / Screen Recording / Input Monitoring) every sandboxed session inherits — holds none (§A.2(v)).
   **Readiness handshake (T-2026-10-19-009).** The activator does not poll the socket. It spawns the kernel with one extra inherited pipe (`--ready-fd 3`) and keeps a bounded tail (8 KiB) of the child's stderr. Once the kernel holds the lock and serves its socket it writes one line on that pipe, `gnomonReady:v1 {pid, startedAt, socketPath, kernelPrincipal}`, closes it and stops writing to the activator's stderr; the activator checks the pid against its child. A child that exits before ready is reported with its stderr tail and its classified exit code. Losing the race is not an error: a child that exits `superseded` (another kernel won the lock) or `transient` makes the activator wait for the winner's socket and report it as attached, exit 0. A live child that closes the pipe without a line (the Windows fallback) gets socket probing bounded by the same global deadline. A probe is a short `hello` RPC, not a bare connect, and no probe outlives the launch's verdict; an answer from the activator's OWN live child (the pidfile names it: it bound the socket but never wrote ready) at the deadline is a timeout, not an attach. The ready line is capped at 4 KiB and must carry the child's pid and the exact socket path the activator expects, else it is rejected. It is an unauthenticated hint: `kernelPrincipal` from it is never pinned; only M1.4 mutual auth authenticates the kernel. The kernel makes the inherited fd close-on-exec before it spawns anything (re-open through `/proc/self/fd` or `/dev/fd`, which Node opens `O_CLOEXEC`; an fd it cannot seal is closed and the activator falls back to the socket probe), so the identity helper and the web transport cannot write a forged line. On Linux, libuv's stdio pipe is an AF_UNIX socketpair that `/proc/self/fd` cannot re-open, so Linux uses a private FIFO instead of an inherited fd (T-2026-10-19-011): the activator creates it `0600` inside a fresh `0700` directory, opens the read end before the spawn and passes only its path (`--ready-fifo`); the kernel opens the write end itself (`O_CLOEXEC`, after checking type, owner, mode and inode) and unlinks the name before it spawns anything; the activator removes the directory when the launch settles. macOS keeps the sealed inherited fd (kqueue does not report a FIFO writer's close to libuv, which would lose the no-ready-line fallback). Residual: Windows handle inheritance (parity phase); the pid + socket-path pin applies on every channel. **Exit codes** are defined in one place (`kernel/lifecycle/exit-codes.ts`), and every lifecycle refusal reason is classified there; a supervisor (launchd at 8.1, Gnomon.app, the activator) restarts only the restartable classes, with backoff, so a configuration fault never loops:

   | Code | Class | Meaning | Restart |
   |---|---|---|---|
   | 0 | ok | clean exit (idle, signal) | no |
   | 4 | superseded | another kernel already serves this home | no (attach to it) |
   | 64 | config | bad argv | no |
   | 70 | internal | unexpected error, crashed interpreter, killed by a signal | yes, with backoff |
   | 75 | transient | lock race, endpoint busy, not ready in time | yes, with backoff |
   | 78 | config | unsafe home, no safe endpoint path, endpoint ACL, SIGUSR1 enabled, dev home, identity cannot be opened, sandbox unavailable, quarantine pending | no |

   The client verbs (`gnomon daemon status`, `stop`) keep their own codes (0, 1 refused, 3 not running). **Note for 8.1 (launchd activation):** launchd's `KeepAlive` cannot choose a restart per exit code (`SuccessfulExit` only tells zero from non-zero), so a plist that relaunches on every non-zero exit would loop on the `config` class. The 8.1 plist needs a `ThrottleInterval`, or a small wrapper that runs gnomond and honours `restartable()` from `exit-codes.ts`.
3. **Idle exit.** No live sessions ∧ no subscribers (an attached GUI connection counts as a subscriber while its WebSocket is open; a closed one counts for nothing, §11.2; a link connection never counts — briefs and messages wait on the relay and are fetched at the next activation, §3.9) ∧ no due timers inside 10 min, held for the idle period (default 10 min, root-signed config) ⇒ mod children and the transport children are stopped (`mod.stopped{reason:'idle'}`, `socket.web.stop`, `link.disconnect{reason:'idle'}`), then `daemon.exit{reason:'idle'}` (A23-2: resident mods never hold the daemon open). Timers are log-derived; a timer due while down fires on the next activation as an attended event.
4. **Crash posture.** Chain write before side effect. On restart, orphans (sessions, local runners, mods) are found by `*.spawned` without `*.exited`; pgid/job probed and stopped with `session.orphaned` (`G-DMN-04`). **A drained session is not an orphan** (TD-2026-10-19-908, rule 11): a session whose last row is a kernel-signed `session.drained{uuid, reason:'upgrade'}` followed by its `session.exited` is not swept; it is handed to the resume path of rule 11. A `session.drained` with no matching exit is still swept as an orphan, because the drain did not finish. `G-DMN-04` covers both cases: a crash orphan is swept and a drained session is resumed, never both. **Stop kills the whole tree.** A group kill misses a tool child that ran `setsid` or daemonized, so stopping a session or local runner first takes an asynchronous process-table snapshot from the kernel, outside any sandbox (`ps -A -o pid=,ppid=,pgid=,lstart=`, TZ=UTC; never a synchronous fork on the event loop), and collects every descendant of the leader and every member of its group with its start-time witness. Out-of-group descendants get SIGTERM with the group; after the graceful window a fresh snapshot is taken and only a pid whose witness still equals the recorded one gets SIGKILL, so a recycled pid is never signalled. `session.exited` carries the verdict `tree: exited | alive | unverifiable`; no snapshot (no `ps`, Windows until the parity phase) or a failed re-check is `unverifiable` and is never reported as either of the others. A descendant already reparented to init before the stop began is out of reach (accepted residual; the witness has one-second resolution). When the leader is already gone at snapshot time and no group member is left, the walk has nothing to start from: the verdict is `unverifiable` with `treeDetail: 'leader-gone-before-stop'`, never `exited`. Further sweep residuals (accepted, T-2026-10-19-011): a descendant forked after the snapshot that calls `setsid` before the group SIGTERM is in neither the group nor the snapshot, so it is missed (a member that leaves the group after the snapshot is still in it and gets the witness-checked SIGKILL, without the SIGTERM); mod children (`#mod-host`) get the group stop but no tree sweep; and a session or local runner that ends on its own (no stop) gets no sweep, so a descendant it left behind is not reaped until an orphan sweep finds its group. One `stopAll` takes one snapshot for all its children.
5. **Degrade path (documented, not built):** daemonless library mode; promotable without ABI change.
6. **Dev launch path (`#dev-launch`, DEV ONLY, until M1.3).** `gnomon dev init [--home]` provisions a separate dev home (default `~/.gnomon-dev`, never `~/.gnomon`) with a software dev-test root, a kernel key and one registered human device, and refuses the production home, a home holding a registry it did not write, and any other non-empty home; `gnomon dev up` runs the kernel in the CLI's own process on that home with the dev-test root (`gnomond` never constructs one), connects as the device and mints the launch token through the in-process `gnomon open` path of §11.2, printing the URL only to an interactive terminal. Approvals stay refused until M1.3. It is removed when the signed helper and the hardware root land.
7. **Real-home launch (`#gnomon-up`).** `gnomon up [--home]` is the production counterpart of `gnomon dev up`: on a home provisioned by `gnomon identity init` (default `~/.gnomon`; like the daemon it never reads `GNOMON_HOME`) it opens the hardware identity exactly as `gnomond` does — root and approvals key behind the pinned signed helper, fail closed on any identity error — runs the kernel in the CLI's own process, connects as the home's registered human device and mints the launch token through the same in-process `gnomon open` path, printing the URL only to an interactive terminal; approvals from that GUI take the helper's presence prompt. It refuses a dev-marked home, an unprovisioned home and a home whose kernel already runs. It is in-process because §5 names no wire verb for `gnomon open`; attaching a GUI to a running `gnomond` waits for that verb. Running in-process departs from rule 2 in two ways. **Environment:** there is no clean re-exec, so the key-holding process is the CLI's own; this is **enforced** — `gnomon up` (and `gnomon dev up`) refuse to start, before any home is read or the helper opens, when `NODE_OPTIONS` is non-empty, when Node was started with any interpreter flag (`--inspect*`, `--require`, `--import`, …) other than exactly `--disable-sigusr1`, or when that flag is missing (the bin's shebang is `#!/usr/bin/env -S node --disable-sigusr1`), rather than inherit code or a debugger into that process. This check (Aegis C1) defends against an **ambient** unsafe runtime; it does not defend against a hostile preload already running as the user, which can scrub env and `execArgv` before the check reads them. **Responsible process:** the kernel is launched from an interactive terminal, not by launchd or the signed app, so its sessions inherit that terminal app's TCC grants (§A.2(v)); this is accepted, not enforced, until launchd activation.
8. **Bare entry (`#bare-entry`).** Typed with no verb in a folder, `gnomon` is `gnomon up` on the default home with a **folder hint**: the nearest ancestor of the working directory holding `.git` (else the working directory itself, and it says so). Every guard of rule 7 runs unchanged and in the same order, and an unprovisioned home gets the same refusal (`next: gnomon identity init`). The hint rides only in the launch URL **fragment**, after the token, as `&cwd=<base64url of the UTF-8 path>` on the same terminal line; it is never on argv, in the environment, in a file, on the wrapper channel or on any other output line, and a path over 1024 bytes is not carried. The GUI treats it as what it is, an **unverified claim from a same-user process**: it opens the new-session form, shows the full path as plain text (control, bidi and zero-width characters, U+FFFD and backslashes escaped; the display capped), prefills the cwd field only when the path is absolute and needed no escaping, and never submits, opens a session, approves a card or skips one; `session.open` still waits for the human's click. A malformed hint drops the hint, never the launch. When a kernel already runs on the home (Gnomon.app, another `gnomon up`, `gnomond`), bare `gnomon` neither joins it nor starts a second one: it refuses `already-running` (exit 3), names the instance from the pidfile pid (diagnostic only) and says how to open this folder in that window. Reaching a running kernel to mint a URL with the hint needs a `gnomon open` wire verb; P8-D98 reserves it as the human-device verb `gui.open` (T-2026-10-19-001), which refuses `ENGINE{not-implemented}` until its handler lands. **`gnomon open` design note (for T-2026-10-19-001).** One new verb `gui.open {cwdHint?: string}` on the socket, callable only by a human-principal device connection whose `hello` verified the kernel key, answers a single-use, 60 s launch grant exactly as the in-process `requestWebLaunch` does (same token rules, the same shared rate cap as the wrapper channel: 8 grants a minute and one in flight in total, of which `gui.open` may hold at most 4, so a socket caller can never lock the terminal or the wrapper out; a socket mint in flight makes the in-process asker wait and go next rather than refusing it; a mint that fails before a token exists refunds its slot; a grant whose caller's connection closed during the mint is withdrawn. The human-device verbs exist on the unix socket / named pipe only: the GUI WebSocket's dispatcher has no port for them, reports them unavailable and refuses them); `cwdHint` is capped at 1024 bytes and returned verbatim inside the grant for the CLI to append as the same fragment parameter — the kernel never reads, resolves or logs it beyond its length. The CLI prints the URL to an interactive terminal only (the rule-7 TTY check before the call). Gnomon.app would gain the same path through its channel as `launch {cwdHint}`. Until then the refusal above is the contract. (Since rule 9 the unprovisioned refusal's `next:` names `gnomon setup`, and on an interactive terminal bare `gnomon` runs the first run instead of refusing.)

9. **First run (`#first-run`, `$first-run`, `#setup-preflight`, `#provisioner`, `^unprovisioned-only`).** `gnomon setup` is a guided first run in plain line prompts (no in-terminal UI, P8-D51), and bare `gnomon` on an interactive terminal whose default home has no identity runs it, then continues into rule 7's launch in the same process with rule 8's folder hint. It explains Gnomon in plain words, shows read-only checks (signed pinned helper, Touch ID — asked of the helper without a prompt (`presence.available`, helper 1.4.0), pass or fail —, macOS, SIP, the claude binary and its pin, API-billing variables, NODE_OPTIONS and node flags, the node floor, leftover Secure Enclave generations, the home's dev marker, symlink or malformed record), each with its fix; the checks are informative and the engine enforces every rule again. It asks for a name (prefilled from the login name, checked against the principal-name pattern by the engine), announces exactly two Touch ID prompts and that a third is an attack, and before each prompt shows the expected digest large and grouped with the instruction that the dialog's last line must show it. The home is `~/.gnomon` of the user's home and never comes from GNOMON_HOME; it is lstat'd and a symlink or non-directory is refused — unlike the raw `gnomon identity init`, which keeps honouring GNOMON_HOME and `--home` as an operator tool. The helper path is the pinned default or the record's, always signature-pinned, never from the working directory or a prompt. Only a home with no identity record is provisioned: an existing one is shown (creation time, root key id) and left alone; a malformed one is shown, never replaced; a generation this run did not create is never adopted, and purging it takes a touch per key. If a commit crashed between the identity record and its registry row, the first run completes it by appending the device row the root already signed during that provisioning, which the record saves together with the device key id; the saved row must verify under the record's root and name the device key on disk and the record's device, and completion makes no new root signature, so it can never be used to have the root sign another key. A record without the saved row is refused and pointed to a rotation. On screens this completion is the **Finish setup** step (`setup.complete`, no prompt). Keys named by a record are never deleted. On screens (§11.2 rule 11): bare `gnomon` and `gnomon setup` on an interactive terminal print a single-use setup URL instead of asking in the terminal (`gnomon setup --cli` keeps the line prompts), and the terminal still prints every expected digest and says it is the surface to trust; Gnomon.app's `up --wrapper` on an unprovisioned home runs the same setup in its window and then continues into the normal launch in the same process.
10. **Auto-open (`#bare-entry`, `#first-run`).** On an interactive terminal, every launch or setup URL that bare `gnomon`, `gnomon up` and `gnomon setup` print (the first one, each Enter fresh URL, each setup re-link after expiry, and the setup → up hand-off) is also handed to the default browser through LaunchServices: the CLI runs `/usr/bin/osascript -` (absolute path, no shell, an empty env) and writes `open location "<url>"` to its **stdin**, so the token is on no argv and no env (`open <url>` is forbidden: its argv is readable by `ps`). The URL is validated first (`^http://127\.0\.0\.1:\d{1,5}/#[A-Za-z0-9_=&%.-]+$`), so no quote, backslash or newline reaches the script; the call times out after 5 s and never fails the launch. The terminal shows `opened in your browser` and keeps the URL below it as the fallback (the TTY is an allowed surface, §11.2 rule 2); on failure or off macOS it prints the URL as before. `--no-open` (all three verbs) or `GNOMON_NO_OPEN=1` only prints. Never without a TTY, and never on the wrapper channel (`up --wrapper`).
11. **Kernel update: drain and resume (TD-2026-10-19-908, T-2026-10-19-014; built before dogfood GA).** Sessions survive a kernel update; they do not survive it by live handover. **Old kernel.** An upgrade rides the existing `gnomon daemon stop` path with reason `upgrade`; there is no new verb. The kernel stops starting new turns, lets each session reach a turn boundary and interrupts a session still mid-turn after a deadline (the backend's interrupt control message, §3.3). For each session it then writes `session.drained{uuid, reason:'upgrade'}` (the backend session uuid, chain write before side effect), stops the child and writes `session.exited`, and exits. **New kernel.** At start, before the orphan sweep of rule 4, it collects the drained sessions and respawns each through the ordinary respawn path with `--resume <uuid>`: the law-7 preflight, `layersHash`, `gitHash` and the permit check all run again exactly as for any respawn (§3.3), and a session that fails any of them stays stopped with the refusal on its card. **What never carries over.** Cached approval unlocks and voice arms belong to the old process and die with it; the human opens them again. The drain leaves `status.arms` empty: before it exits the old kernel ends every arm with `approval.voice.disarm{armId, reason:'daemon-exit'}` (§14.7's daemon-exit trigger; `daemon-exit` is reserved in the closed `VOICE_DISARM_REASONS`) and kills the supervised voice child at the drain, so no voice intent reaches a draining kernel (TD-2026-10-19-194). Learned always-rules are not cached unlocks: they are chain records (`approval.rule.learned`, revoked by `approval.rule.revoked`) that survive any restart by design, and the new kernel rebuilds them from the chain (Aegis Q3). Permits are re-attributed to the resumed session by a kernel-signed row that cites the drained row, and are never minted by the resume. **Permit carry-over (founder ruling).** A permit that was live at the drain (the `session.drained` row's time is at or before the permit's `expiresAt`) is carried over with a fresh expiry window under the same `permitId`; `permit.reattributed{permitId, hsid, drainedEventId, renewedByDrain:true, expiresAt}` records the new expiry, and the law-7 billing scan and the `layersHash` and `gitHash` checks run again. A permit that had already expired before the drain is refused. The permit's principal must be the resumed session's principal. At the drain a pending card bound to a session expires with `approval.expire{why:'session-drained'}` (founder ruling F4, TD-2026-10-19-528); a pending card bound to no session expires at the new kernel's start with `approval.expire{why:'cancelled'}` (TD-2026-10-19-476), before any session resumes; no decision made against the old kernel's card is replayed, and an install card is raised again from its staged record. `gnomon daemon stop`, with any reason, first raises a signed `daemon-stop` card decided with Touch ID (F3). Only a human principal on a verified device connection asks for it; `accept` and `reject` both take fresh presence, so a cached unlock never decides it; reject or expiry keeps the kernel running; an accepted stop exits with `daemon.exit{reason:'stop'}` and exit code 0 (no restart). Status (T-2026-10-19-014 B0, built): the client asks with the human-device verb `daemon.stop{reason?}` (human-device verb 15, ruling TD-2026-10-19-339, inside the unreleased P8-D98 `clientApiId` move), on the unix socket only. `gnomon daemon stop` pins the kernel key from the home, authenticates as the home's human device, calls `daemon.stop{reason:'stop'}`, prints that the card is raised and exits 0 without waiting for the decision; with no kernel it refuses `ENGINE{not-running}`. Status (T-2026-10-19-014 B1, built: the old kernel's drain). `daemon.stop{reason:'upgrade'}` raises the same signed card with lines that name an upgrade (the reason is inside `contentHash`); an accept drains, then exits with `daemon.exit{reason:'upgrade'}`, exit code 0, the no-restart class. From the start of the drain `session.open` and `session.send` are refused `ENGINE{draining}`, for Claude and `gnomon-local` sessions alike. The drain waits for each in-flight Claude turn to reach its result line until the deadline (a built-in default, `DRAIN_DEADLINE_MS`, because no root-signed config value names one yet), then sends the interrupt control message to a session still mid-turn and logs it as `session.inject{channel:'drain'}` with the deadline and its source. For every live Claude session the drain expires the session's pending cards with `session-drained`, writes `session.drained{uuid, reason:'upgrade'}`, stops the child and writes `session.exited` whose `reason` is `drain-clean` (the session was at a turn boundary) or `drain-interrupted` (a turn was cut at the deadline). That `reason` is the record the resume of slice B2 reads, because the `session.drained` payload is closed: a `drain-interrupted` session resumes idle with a visible "turn interrupted by update" note and is never continued, since a turn killed mid-tool re-issues the tool call on resume and continue (probe p-resume, TD-2026-10-19-814 item 2), and a `drain-clean` session resumes normally. After that, every other pending card bound to a session expires `session-drained`; a card bound to no session (install, api-token, fallback) stays pending until the new kernel expires it `cancelled` (TD-2026-10-19-476). `gnomon-local` sessions get no `session.drained` until they can resume (slice B3): their turns are refused and the shutdown stops them. Status (T-2026-10-19-014 B2, built: the new kernel's resume). Before the orphan sweep the new kernel reads its own signed rows: a `session.drained` followed by a `session.exited` whose reason is `drain-clean` or `drain-interrupted`, with no later open or `permit.reattributed` for that session, is resumed; a `session.drained` with no exit is swept and never resumed. The resume runs the ordinary spawn path with `--resume <uuid>` under the same hsid and uuid; `layersHash` and `gitHash` must equal the drained session's, and the permit named by its last sandbox row is re-attributed by `permit.reattributed{permitId, hsid, drainedEventId}` after the same checks as a spawn permit (claude-code, session.spawn, this session, this principal, subscription, this log, live at the drain, law 7) and gets a fresh window (the carry-over ruling above), the row also carrying `renewedByDrain:true` and the new `expiresAt`; the resumed `session.open` carries the drained open's `principalClass` (never assumed). Any failure leaves the session stopped with `session.refused{resume}` (`RESUME_REFUSALS`), and that kernel-signed row is terminal: a later boot never plans the session again. A failed drain-interrupt write is recorded as `session.inject{channel:'drain', failed:true}`. A `daemon.stop` request whose reason differs from the pending daemon-stop card's is refused `BAD_REQUEST{bad-params}` with the pending reason and card id. Nothing is sent to a resumed child; a `drain-interrupted` session also gets the transcript note `session.sandbox{phase:'resume-note', note:'turn interrupted by update'}`. Every card an earlier kernel left pending is closed with `approval.expire{why:'cancelled'}` (TD-2026-10-19-476), FIRST, before any session resumes, instead of being raised from the log, because the log row carries the card but not the detail its effect needs; an install card is raised again from its staged record by the mod pickup. A decision against an old card id is refused `NOT_FOUND`. The kernel advertises the `session.resume` feature, and `session.send` or `session.stop` on a resumed session from a client that did not offer it is refused `FORBIDDEN{reason:'matrix', needs:'session.resume'}`. The GUI drops every card it holds when it reads a new `daemon.start`. **Feature gates.** Resume and the cursor of §5 are per-client features negotiated in the `hello` features, which `serverSig` and `auth` already sign (§4.9). A feature the other side does not offer means the dependent operation is refused, never degraded, and no feature can switch a check off. The names `session.resume` and `journal.cursor`, the kinds `session.drained` and `permit.reattributed`, the refusal reason `draining` and the expiry why `session-drained` are reserved in the T-2026-10-19-001 move (P8-D98). **Order.** Claude sessions first (`--resume` is a Claude Code flag the adapter already drives); `gnomon-local` sessions second, once the local runner can resume from its own transcript. A live handover between kernel processes, with no drain, is an unprobed idea after Move 2 (§22 item 25).

---

## 3. Kernel primitives

### 3.1 `#identity` — principals, key classes, hsid

**Key classes (D38, D39, A23-7, A24-T3).**
```
root key       non-exportable hardware P-256: Secure Enclave (macOS) via the signed helper, in the helper's keychain access group; on Windows a Windows Hello key in
               the Passport KSP (MS_NGC_KEY_STORAGE_PROVIDER) with NCRYPT_USE_CONTEXT_PROPERTY carrying the kernel's prompt text; refused unless NCRYPT_IMPL_TYPE_PROPERTY
               says hardware (TPM) (S29-K4; KeyCredentialManager cannot show kernel text, the Platform Crypto Provider prompts a password, not Hello).
               Presence per signature. Signs registry rows, grants, config, install, api-token decisions. keyId "gkey-p256:v1:<sha256(spki)>".
approvals key  hardware-backed P-256 (same providers), unlocked by presence ONCE PER ATTACHED HUMAN-CLASS CONNECTION (connId) — the GUI page, the wrapper, Conductor or any
               conformant client that authenticates as the human — the unlock is an evaluated LAContext (macOS) or an authenticated NCrypt handle (Windows) held IN THE
               HELPER keyed by that connId (the key never leaves hardware; the kernel holds only the connId → unlock reference, S29-K5), dropped after 15 min idle on that
               connection, 60 min after the presence that opened it or after 64 signatures under it (hard bounds the idle timer cannot slide, enforced in the
               helper AND the broker, T-2026-10-18-028 M2), or when it closes — `unlock.drop` is sent on every close and failure path, and a drop the helper does
               not answer escalates to `unlock.drop-all` and then ends the helper process (M3); a helper exit drops every unlock and every arm; a decision arriving on another connection never uses it (S25-H1). Fresh presence ALWAYS for: gate-class, install, knot resolve, verdict, fallback-cloud,
               budget resume, `always` (S25-C2). Cached unlock covers tool.permission allow|deny, ask-user, plan.
               `gnomon approve` from a CLI prompts every time (a CLI invocation is its own connection and never attaches). Never on disk.
               A VOICE ARM (§14.7, D58–D62) is a further helper-held unlock of this key (its own LAContext / NCrypt handle, keyed by armId), opened only by a fresh-presence `voice-arm` decision on a human-class connection
               whose surface is the wrapper or a native client declaring capture exclusion, bound to the pair {that human connId, the supervised voice child's
               connId}, limited to the classes and repos of `grant.voice-approvals`, and dropped at its idle/hard/count limits (reversible 15 min idle, 60 min hard,
               ≤ 240 configurable, 30 decisions; gate 5 min hard, ≤ 60 configurable, 3 decisions), on screen lock, on either connection closing, or on any narrowing.
               Only the broker signs under it; a decision arriving on any connection other than the paired voice connection never uses it; it never signs
               install, use-api-token, always, voice-arm, grant or any hand-only kind (§14.7).
kernel key     Ed25519 keypair held in the OS keystore item `gnomon-kernel-key` (macOS: Keychain item in the helper's keychain access group; Windows: Credential Manager
               generic credential), written and read only by the signed helper, handed only to the signed kernel binary (P8-D68, below), loaded into kernel memory at daemon start, rotated by the root-signed CLI
               verb `gnomon key rotate kernel`; never a plain file. Its registry row ("gkey:v1:<sha256(spki)>", kind kernel) is root-signed, so a forged kernel key cannot be
               registered. Signs: serverSig in `hello` (§4.9), `card.actions[]` and the presence-prompt record (§3.4), `oracle.result` (§7.3), every event the kernel appends.
               Forgery scope (P8-D68, S29-K1): macOS — the kernel ships as a Developer-ID-signed single executable (Node SEA), and the helper is an XPC service under
               launchd that answers only a peer meeting that binary's code-signing requirement (xpc_connection_set_peer_code_signing_requirement) and refuses to run when
               it is itself sandboxed (sandbox_check on its own pid); a same-user process — `node` running a script included — must defeat that peer check to obtain the key,
               so status.kernelKeyCustody is `full` on macOS ONLY under this design (any other build reports `partial`); Windows — Credential Manager is readable by any
               same-user process, so kernel-signed rows on Windows carry the `partial` fidelity of §A.3 until the helper's protected store is proven (full|partial).
link key       X25519 static key (P8-R9, §3.9) in the OS keystore item `gnomon-link-key` behind the helper, with the kernel key's custody rules (macOS `full` only
               under P8-D68; Windows Credential Manager `partial`) and handed only to the signed kernel binary; bound to kernelKeyId + deviceId by a root-signed registry
               row ("gkey-x25519:v1:<sha256(raw public key)>", kind link); rotated by the root-signed `gnomon key rotate link` (paired peers receive the new row over the
               old session and verify it against the pinned peer root). It is the Noise static key and nothing else: it never signs, so kernel-key signatures stay the
               only assertion mechanism. A teammate link requires `status.linkKeyCustody: full` (S32 §5).
device key     Ed25519, file 0600 (`~/.gnomon/keys/device.key`), authenticates this device's clients on the socket; several devices under one principal (Q1). Cannot approve.
agent keys     Ed25519 files for roster agents; session keys ephemeral; voice principal has its own device key (§14.4); that key never signs a decision —
               voice intents are signed by the broker under a voice arm (§14.7).
```

**Data model (D24/Q1–Q3, D38/D39).**
```
ns            = base32(sha256(rootPublicKey))[0..8]                     — the namespace of this trust set (8.0: one human)
principal:v1  { principalId:"p:<ns>/<name>", kind: human|agent|session|kernel|mod|voice|peer, keys:[keyId…] (device keys), parent?, createdAt, scope }
device:v1     { deviceId:"dev:<ns>/<host>", principalId, keyId:"gkey:v1:<sha256 of normalized spki PEM>", createdAt }   — several devices, one principal
~/.gnomon/keys/registry.jsonl   append-only { row, signer:<rootKeyId>, sig } — PUBLIC material; accepts gkey:v1 (Ed25519) and gkey-p256:v1 rows;
                                a row without a valid signature from a signer in the trust set never resolves; unsigned/malformed rows surfaced (keys.ts:338-360,309-330)
~/.gnomon/keys/device.key       this device's Ed25519 key (0600 / owner-only ACL): authenticates CLI clients (including the GUI launcher `gnomon open`) on the socket; cannot approve
~/.gnomon/keys/<principal>.key  agent keys (roster agents' Gnomon keys), 0600
~/.gnomon/sessions/<hsid>/key   ephemeral session key, deleted on exit
root key / approvals key        hardware (Secure Enclave / TPM) behind `packages/gnomon-helper` — never files, never exportable
kernel key                      OS keystore item `gnomon-kernel-key` behind the helper — never a file; public half in the registry (root-signed row)
link key                        OS keystore item `gnomon-link-key` behind the helper — never a file; public half in the registry (root-signed row, kind link)
~/.gnomon/keys/peers.jsonl      append-only pins of paired peers' PUBLIC material {peerRootKeyId, rootPub, kernelKeyId, kernelPub, linkKeyId, linkPub, relay},
                                written at pairing beside the local root-signed grant.peer row; under keys/**, so the session profile denies reads (S32 §6)
~/.gnomon/grants.jsonl          append-only { grant, signer:<rootKeyId>, sig }
~/.gnomon/keys/read-tokens.jsonl append-only root-signed read-token rows {mint: tokenId, keyId, publicKeyPem, principalId, createdAt,
                                expiresAt ≤ 24 h} | {revoke: tokenId} — PUBLIC keys only; the holder keeps the seed (proof of possession, §4.9, P8-D89a)
```
Generalizes `packages/warpline/src/fabric/keys.ts`: key identity `keys.ts:15-18,126-129`; principal grammar `keys.ts:73-77`; fail-closed loaders `keys.ts:223-239`; registry reader that skips AND surfaces malformed rows `keys.ts:338-360`, closed-kind posture `keys.ts:309-330`; first-row pin `keys.ts:28-32,397-402`. Signature domains: `gnomon:event-sig:v1`, `gnomon:row-sig:v1`, `gnomon:approve:v1\n<cardId>\n<decision>\n<cardContentHash>\n<approverPrincipalId>` (approver in the preimage, Q5), `gnomon:approve-voice:v1\n<cardId>\n<decision>\n<cardContentHash>\n<approverPrincipalId>\n<armId>\n<turnId>\n<utteranceHash>\n<handleHash|->\n<challengeHash|->\n<readbackHash|->\n<confirmTurnId|->` (voice decisions under an arm, §14.7 — a voice decision never verifies as a hand decision, nor the reverse), `gnomon:auth:v1` (pattern `keys.ts:65,143-146`), `gnomon:brief:v1` (a handoff brief's manifest, signed by the source kernel key, §3.9).

**Foreign principals (S32-02, P8-D75).** `ns` is 40 bits and can be ground, so across kernels it identifies nothing: the kernel compares every principal from another trust set by its full `rootKeyId`, displays it as `p:<ns>/<name>@<rootKeyId8>`, and never matches it against the local trust set (I5), whatever its `ns`; the principal parser accepts such ids without treating them as local (`G-ID-08`). A remote kernel is `p:<peerNs>/kernel` with `kind: peer`: it calls no verb (§5), is never an approver, and other humans exist only behind it.

**Invariants.**
- I1 One agent key = one notebook owner = one Warpline signer. **The kernel never mints a Warpline key or daemon token** (P8-D14; `keys.ts:441-443`, `tokens.ts:3-6`). Warpline principals are stable roster agents whose Warpline credentials the founder mints once; sessions borrow them (`session.open{as}`); strand ↔ session link is `causes[]`. The kernel's own Warpline credential (for shadow admissions) is a human-minted agent-class token for `gnomon-kernel` (A17-a), stored at `~/.gnomon/warpline/<repoId>.token` (0600, denied to every sandbox class, A23-7).
- I2 Principal from a key or token, never an env var (TD-735 S1). `WARPLINE_AGENT_ID` stays Warpline's unsigned CLI marker.
- I3 The terminal gets the CLI only (P8-D51): `gnomon spawn | status | approve | replay | open | mod | knowledge | doodle | log …`; there is no in-terminal UI. Human-only CLI verbs fall in two classes: **root-signed** (`gnomon init`, `key mint`, `key rotate kernel|link`, `key device add|revoke`, `link pair|renew`, `trust`, `install`, `uninstall`, `mod link|unlink|dev|rollback`, `gui pin`, `billing api-token`, `voice enrol|forget`, `voice approvals enable`) require hardware presence (Touch ID / Windows Hello) for the root key per signature; **approval-signed** (`approve`, `daemon stop`, `log segment`) use the approvals key with the presence class of §3.4. `voice approvals disable`, `voice disarm` and `link revoke` only narrow, so they are device-authenticated and need no presence (§14.7, §3.9); the same holds for `gnomon strictness` and `gnomon mode` when they tighten and for `gnomon incident`, while loosening through either verb is approval-signed with fresh presence, and with the root key below the evidence level (§3.4).
