The CLI refuses either class from a kernel-spawned shell (`GNOMON_HSID` set, or caller pgid/job = a live session's) with `refusal:v1 {code:'FORBIDDEN'}` and records `cli:{pid, ppid, pgid, tty}` in the resulting event (S16-5) — with one **staging** exception: `gnomon mod install <path|url>` and `gnomon mod link <folder>` from a kernel-spawned shell sign nothing; they validate and test the manifest, copy the snapshot (§12.5) and append `manifest.staged{hash, packageHash, by: hsid}` so that an `install` card appears for the human, who decides it with the root key from a human-class connection. Agents never sign a grant row; they stage a card (A24-3). `link pair` has no staging form: from a kernel-spawned shell it is refused and no card appears, so injected text cannot start a pairing (S32-04). `gnomon open` (the GUI launcher, §11.2) is device-authenticated rather than human-only and is refused from a kernel-spawned shell by the same check. Honest limit: `env -u` + `setsid`/a Terminal TTY defeat the shell check — the presence prompt is the boundary (§A.1).
- I4 Session, mod and voice principals are children: `scope ⊆ parent.scope`; they never widen a permit and never bind to a manifest grant (mods bind only to their own `modns.<name>.*`). Session and mod principals never call `approve`. The voice principal calls `approve` only in its **intent form** (`via:'voice'`, no `cardId`, no `decisionSig`), only as the kernel-supervised voice child, and only inside a live voice arm (§14.7) for cards whose repo and class the project's `grant.voice-approvals` row allows; the broker signs under the arm and the approver is the arming human (`presence: voice-armed`). With the project's toggles off (the default) the intent form is refused `FORBIDDEN` gate `voice`, reason `voice-off`.
- I5 Every registry and grants row names its `signer` and carries its signature; the trust set is the root key (8.0) and, in 8.1, cross-signed roots (§21). A `grant.peer` row is the local root's statement about a peer; a peer root never signs a local row and never joins the trust set, even between the founder's own machines (S32-08). The first root row is pinned forever (`keys.ts:28-32,397-402` pattern).

**Failure modes.** Unknown key/token ⇒ `AUTH`; unsigned/malformed rows never resolve and are surfaced; helper or hardware key unavailable ⇒ root-signed and approval verbs refuse `UNSUPPORTED` (with the helper's error; never a software fallback, never a file); helper exit ⇒ every cached unlock and voice arm is gone (`approval.voice.disarm{reason:'helper-exit'}` per arm) and the next decision on each connection prompts again; presence denied ⇒ `FORBIDDEN` logged `approval.presence.denied`.

**Provisioner.** Every surface that provisions this Mac's hardware identity — the identity verbs, the guided first run and the app's setup phase — drives one engine that renders nothing: it emits step, presence (prompt n of m, its purpose and the 12-hex digest the helper's line will show), done and aborted events, and stops at the next step when its abort signal fires. It accepts a root signature only when the helper's own prompt line, returned with the signature, names the digest the surface showed; it raises at most two root prompts for a first provisioning (three for a rotation, none for a device completion); it holds an exclusive lock file beside the home for the whole run, so a second concurrent run refuses before any key is created; before the identity record is written, a failure restores what it moved and deletes only the keys it created; after it, keys are never deleted, and a missing device row is completed from the signed row saved in the record, with no new signature.

### 3.2 `#kernel-log` — the one hash-chained index

**Data model.** SQLite via `node:sqlite` at `~/.gnomon/kernel.db` (a-flow design `sqlite.py:1-16,185-193`); sealed segments at `~/.gnomon/log/segments/NNNNNN.db` (six-digit ordinal, L5). The last row of every segment is `daemon.segment`, a log-only kind appended by the log itself and by no component:
```sql
CREATE TABLE chain (seq INTEGER PRIMARY KEY, id TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE, kind TEXT NOT NULL, at TEXT NOT NULL, principal TEXT NOT NULL,
                    audience TEXT NOT NULL, sensitivity TEXT NOT NULL, envelope TEXT NOT NULL, prev_hash TEXT NOT NULL, hash TEXT NOT NULL, sig TEXT);
-- seq = MAX(seq)+1 inside the write transaction (sqlite.py:943-951) — never AUTOINCREMENT
-- projections reference chain(seq); BEFORE UPDATE/DELETE triggers RAISE(ABORT) on chain and every projection (sqlite.py:9-11,535-537)
```
`hash = sha256(canonical(envelope minus hash, sig))`, `prev(seq=1) = sha256("gnomon-log-genesis:" + logId)` where `logId` is the genesis ULID (Q2) and `envelope.log = logId`. Canonical = Warpline's serializer (`warpline-fabric-schema-v2.md` §1.2). Decision reads join `chain` on `seq` AND `kind` (`sqlite.py:13-16,542-544`). Chain discipline from a-flow `chain.py:3-4,55-69`.

**Signatures and anchors (S-H1, D12).** `sig` mandatory on `approval.*`, `oracle.result`, `budget.*`, `permit.*`, `key.*`, `manifest.*`, `config.*`, `billing.*`, `verdict.*` and on every event appended by a keyed principal; kernel-appended events are signed by the kernel key (§3.1), root-signed rows by the root key, decisions by the approvals key; only the diagnostics `daemon.*`, `socket.*` and `schedule.*` may be unsigned (any other kind without a valid `sig` is refused at append). The log's own rows are **reserved kinds**: `daemon.segment`, `daemon.log.gap`, `daemon.anchor.lag`, `daemon.anchor.failed` and `daemon.log.quarantined` (P8-D89; only ever a genesis row, seq 1, written by the quarantine command). Any caller that appends one is refused `FORBIDDEN{reserved-kind}`. They are signature-mandatory although they sit in `daemon.*`, and verify binds them to the kernel key (below). `sig` = base64 of the signature over `<domain> + "\n" + hash` (domain `gnomon:event-sig:v1` for events, §3.1). **Durability** defaults to `process-crash`: WAL with `synchronous=FULL`, which fsync(2)s the WAL on every commit. A killed kernel loses no committed row because the commit's write() completed; `synchronous=FULL` is what carries a commit through an OS crash on hardware that honours fsync (S33-10's correction to S33-01). On macOS fsync(2) does not flush the drive cache, so a power loss or panic can drop the last commits and the cache may write back out of order. `power-loss` (`F_FULLFSYNC` on every commit) is opt-in in root-signed config. Critical kinds commit with the same durability as bulk rows (P8-D83, below). Otherwise `F_FULLFSYNC` is used only at genesis, rotation and close.

**Head anchors (P8-D82, amended by P8-D83–D85; research 33 incl. the S33-10 addendum).** The chain head `{log, segment, seq, hash, at, boot, closed?}` is written to `~/.gnomon/head` within 64 rows or 50 ms of a commit, whichever comes first, and to the OS keystore item `gnomon-head` within 1024 rows or 1 s. Both are written durably at genesis, rotation and close; a clean close marks them `closed`. For a critical kind (list), `append` resolves only after the commit, the file anchor and the keystore anchor have completed, in that order, and no effect of the event is released before then. This is an ordering guarantee against a same-user process, not a power-loss guarantee. Each anchor records the boot session. On open, a keystore anchor ahead of the db is `INTEGRITY_BROKEN{truncated}`, with `cause` `tamper` if the boot session is unchanged or the last anchor was a clean close, and `unclean-reboot` otherwise. Neither cause is accepted silently. Recovery requires presence and appends a signed `daemon.log.gap`. On open, a file anchor lagging by more than 63 rows ⇒ `INTEGRITY_BROKEN{anchor-lag}`, unless the lag is relaxed across a verified boot change (P8-D85, below). A file anchor lagging at all after a clean close ⇒ `anchor-lag` too. Only the keystore anchor resists a same-user writer, so only its `boot` and `closed` are authoritative. The file anchor detects accidents.
- **Critical kinds (the list, S33-06):** `approval.decide`, `approval.suspect`, `approval.presence.denied`, `approval.rule.learned`, `approval.rule.revoked` (P8-D98: a lost revoke widens permission), `approval.voice.arm|disarm`, `key.*`, `grant.*`, `config.*`, `manifest.*` (decisions), `billing.*`, `verdict.*`, `link.*` (pair/renew/revoke), `session.inject{origin:'peer'}`, `socket.auth.theft`. As built: the whole `manifest.*` namespace is critical (conservative). `grant.*` is kept as a prefix, although no `grant` kind is registered yet. `link.*` maps to `link.pair.offered`, `link.paired`, `link.renewed` and `link.revoked`. Sequence (P8-D83):
  1. Commit that one transaction with the bulk durability (`synchronous=FULL`, no `PRAGMA fullfsync`). `F_FULLFSYNC` per critical commit failed the 25 ms budget in arms G and H1 (§19.1).
  2. Rename the file anchor in.
  3. Start the keystore write, only after COMMIT has returned, and await it.
  4. Only then resolve `{anchored: 'both'}` and release the effect (run the tool, issue the card result, apply the grant). While the keystore anchor is the stub (until M1.3, P8-D84), the result is `{anchored: 'file-only'}`. Bulk rows resolve `anchored: false`.

  A kernel stopped before step 4 has lost a decision that never took effect. The budget is critical p99 < 25 ms, behind the ≥ 300 ms of Touch ID that already precedes a decision. Measured with plain commits (arm H3): critical p99 0.19 ms over 12 events at 3×10⁵ rows, and 10.2 ms over 94 events at 10⁶ rows under concurrent machine load. The built log's unit run measured 2.1 ms over 60 events, where the p99 index is the max.
- **Bulk cadence (S33-05).** The file anchor is written by tmp + rename (never `pwrite` in place: not atomic, so a torn head would read as a false `INTEGRITY_BROKEN`, and it stalls as badly), when 64 rows or 50 ms have passed since the first un-anchored row, and on idle checkpoint. The keystore anchor (Keychain generic password / Credential Manager generic credential, written by the signed helper, no user presence) is written asynchronously off the append path; its 1024-row / 1 s cadence is provisional until P04 measures the helper's XPC + `SecItemUpdate` cost (probe arm H, §19.1). As built, the triggers fire when the un-anchored count *reaches* 63 and 1023. A crash right after a trigger therefore leaves a lag within the open bound. Keystore writes are serialised, so a slow write can never land after a newer one and roll it back. Each write has a timeout, `keystoreTimeoutMs` (default 5 s), measured from dequeue, not from enqueue (S34-11). While a timed-out write is still unsettled, new keystore writes are refused rather than reordered.
- **Boot session and the truncation cause (S33-10, P8-D83).** Every anchor records `boot`:
  - macOS: `kern.bootsessionuuid`, read with `/usr/sbin/sysctl` by absolute path with an empty environment;
  - Linux: `/proc/sys/kernel/random/boot_id`;
  - Windows: the helper's boot id in the parity phase (P8-D78), `unknown` until then.

  Values are normalised on both sides of the comparison: trimmed, checked as a UUID and upper-cased. Anything else is `unknown` (S34-09, S34-21). Only the keystore anchor's copy is consulted, so a forged `boot` in the file anchor changes nothing.

  A keystore anchor ahead of the db is classified as follows:
  - **`tamper`**: the keystore boot equals this boot, or the keystore anchor is `closed`, or either boot is `unknown` or absent;
  - **`unclean-reboot`**: both boots are known and differ, and the anchor was not a clean close.

  The cause names "power loss or tampering" but never proves either. A truncation made after a reboot, but before the first open in the new boot, reads `unclean-reboot`. To close that window, every non-recovery open re-stamps the keystore anchor with the current boot and gates the first append on that write (S34-01). A truncation after that open reads `tamper`.

  The cause never downgrades the verdict: `open` refuses both causes. `openForRecovery` returns a log that refuses appends until `acknowledgeGap({presenceToken})` runs; until M1.3 both are internal test seams, not public API (§19.1 (c)), and `presenceToken` is the branded `PresenceToken` that only the presence module mints. The acknowledgement is presence-gated and accepted only for `unclean-reboot`. It appends exactly one kernel-signed `daemon.log.gap{lostFrom, lostTo, lostHash, bootPrev, bootNow, fileLag}` and re-anchors, and the chain verifies across the gap row. An acknowledgement can be interrupted after the gap row commits but before the keystore write, leaving the keystore at the lost head that the payload names. The next open re-anchors that case; it does not re-classify it.
- **Bounded lag on open (S33-07, P8-D85, S34-05, S34-24).**
  - Keystore lag above 1023 rows ⇒ the kernel appends a kernel-signed `daemon.anchor.lag{rows, which}`, shown in `status`.
  - A clean-close keystore anchor with any lag ⇒ `anchor-lag` (S34-05).
  - Anchor hash ≠ the row hash at its seq ⇒ `anchor-mismatch`.
  - No anchor on an existing log ⇒ `anchor-missing`.
  - No db while a head anchor, a sealed segment or keys are present ⇒ `INTEGRITY_BROKEN{missing-db}`.
  - A keystore anchor ahead of the db ⇒ `truncated` (above), whatever the file lag (S34-24). The file anchor still gets the hash and clean-close checks; only its > 63 bound is skipped. Its lag is reported as `fileLag`, in the refusal and in `daemon.log.gap`.

  **P8-D85: the cross-boot relax.** File-anchor renames are not fsynced, so they can roll back after a power cut, and a strict > 63 bound would brick a log on a benign power cut. The bound is therefore relaxed across a verified boot change only. After the truncation check, a file-anchor lag > 63 opens only if all of these hold:
  - the keystore anchor is present and not `closed`;
  - it passes the full anchor check (its hash matches the row at its seq, and it is not ahead);
  - it is within its own lag bound, or it yields the keystore lag row;
  - `bootPrev` (the keystore anchor's recorded boot, normalised) and `bootNow` are both known and differ;
  - the file anchor's hash still matches the row at its seq. Only the lag is relaxed; a mismatch still refuses `anchor-mismatch`.

  When the bound is relaxed, the kernel appends a kernel-signed `daemon.anchor.lag{rows, which: 'file', bootPrev, bootNow}`. A keyless log refuses `FORBIDDEN{kernel-key-required}` instead. Evidence comes first: every open-time anchor write (the file rewrite, a rotation, a marker or keystore-lag re-anchor) waits until that signed row has committed.

  Every other case refuses `anchor-lag`, with `data.relax` naming why:
  - the same boot;
  - either boot unknown;
  - the keystore anchor unavailable (the stub; `relax: keystore-unavailable`), absent or malformed;
  - a pre-D83 anchor without `boot` (`relax: boot-unknown`);
  - a `closed` keystore anchor;
  - a `closed` file anchor with any lag.

  The file anchor's own `boot` is never consulted.
- **Anchor faults (S34-02, S34-11, S34-17, S34-19).** Any failure after a critical row's COMMIT rejects that append `ENGINE{anchor-failed}`. The failure can be the file anchor, a rotation, or a keystore reject or timeout. The log then enters an anchor-faulted state. Bulk appends continue. A critical append first re-anchors the current head (file anchor plus an awaited keystore write) and is refused `ENGINE{anchor-faulted}` if that fails.

  Once both anchors succeed, the failure path immediately commits one kernel-signed `daemon.anchor.failed{seq, kind}` marker. Committing needs no keystore, so the marker survives a close or a crash. `close()` records any pending markers before its closing anchors and reports the outcome. **Projections treat a marked seq as not having taken effect**: the row is in the chain, but its effect was never released. The fault clears only when every failed row has its marker and a head at or past every marker is anchored in both anchors.

  On open, a crash between COMMIT and the keystore write can leave critical rows above the keystore anchor's seq with no marker. Each such row gets a marker before the log is usable. This scan runs only when a keystore anchor exists; a stub-backed log has no keystore seq to scan above. A failed open re-stamp (S34-01) enters the fault state with nothing to record.
- **Keyless logs and refused opens.** A log opened without the kernel key (`signer` null) is non-rotating and diagnostic-only. Every row the log writes itself is signature-mandatory. An append that would reach the segment boundary is therefore refused before any commit with `FORBIDDEN{kernel-key-required}`, and so is an open that would have to record a reserved row. An open that refuses closes its database handle before it throws. Windows CI caught the leak; an fd-count regression test covers it.
- **Known gap (P8-D84, accepted dev-only).** Until M1.3 the keystore anchor is a stub that reports `unavailable`, so no authoritative boot exists. Every truncation therefore classifies as `tamper` and cannot be acknowledged: a known no-recovery window. The recovery path is no longer deleting `~/.gnomon`: `gnomon log quarantine` (built, T-2026-10-17-009(a)) moves the whole home aside intact to the sibling `<home>.quarantine-<stamp>` and installs a new home whose log's genesis row (seq 1) is the kernel-signed reserved `daemon.log.quarantined{quarantinedLog, quarantinedHead{seq, hash, segment}, evidencePath, authorizedBy}`. It refuses while the daemon is running, deletes nothing, and builds the new home in a staging directory first: a crash leaves either the old home intact or the evidence plus a complete staging home, which the next quarantine or daemon start installs. Until presence lands (M1.3) it is gated on an interactive terminal and a typed confirmation (`authorizedBy: 'interactive-confirm'`), which any same-user process owning a terminal can pass. Gnomon is not shippable to anyone but the founder until all of T-2026-10-17-009 lands; (b), authenticated keystore anchors, is still open (§19.1 release gate, §A.6).
- **Telemetry.** `status.log.forcedAnchorRate`: bulk p99 stays inside the measured envelope while forced anchors stay at or below 1 per 64 appends (probe arm D's rate); above 1/64 over any 10⁴-row window the log is outside it, and `p-log-fsync` is re-run.

**Verify.** `gnomon log verify` recomputes hashes, verifies signatures against the registry and checks both anchors. It also binds kernel rows to the kernel key in both directions (S34-18):
- a row whose principal is the kernel must carry a kernel-key signature (the current key, or a rotated one listed in `kernelKeyIds`);
- every reserved kind must be appended by the kernel principal with a kernel key.

The report carries `kernelBinding: ok | failed | not-checked`.

Scope stated: a same-user unsandboxed process can stop the kernel and delete the un-anchored tail (§A.1(g)); a same-user attacker who rewrites both anchors and re-signs with the Ed25519 keys read from disk (device, agent) defeats this for events those keys sign (§A.1); root-signed and approval-signed rows cannot be forged without hardware presence, because neither key exists on disk (D38/D39) — though a touch proves presence, not what was approved (§A.1, S29-K2).

**Invariants.** L1 Append-only. L2 Model-visible ⟺ logged for kernel-added bytes (`session.inject{origin:'kernel'}` before the child reads them, `G-LOG-03`); client-origin injects logged uncapped; peer-origin bytes (an accepted handoff brief, §3.9) logged `session.inject{origin:'peer'}` before the child reads them; userland injections logged by userland as `inject.receipt`. L3 Fabric separate; `warpline.*` events carry `ref:"wl:pick/<pickId>"` + `refHash` and `causes[]`; telemetry `T-LOG-05`. L4 Ids never collide (ULIDs; `pev:` wraps 7.x ids). L5 Segment at 10⁶ rows (epoch-anchor pattern, `warpline-v3-identity.md` §5); retention (S22-M2): `session.inject` bytes kept forever; `voice.turn.*` text is stored as blobs retained until the session's lore compaction exists + 90 days unless pinned by the human; `turn.event` bodies, oracle evidence and any opt-in audio pruned at their TTL with `blob.pruned{sha}`. L6 `audience` (`private | team | org`, default `private`; in 8.0 the appender caps it at `private`) and `sensitivity` (`normal | sensitive | secret`) are set at append and never relaxed in place; each kind's default is the §9.1 column, set by the appending component, never by the payload's author (S32 §6, `G-LOG-09`); sharing (8.1) and link egress (§3.9) are projections filtered by them (Q6). L7 A kind in a userland namespace is never written by the kernel and vice versa (ownership table §5). A mod's own `modns.<name>.*` row reaches the log only through the mod host's `kernel.call` (§12.3): it is appended as a userland row under the mod's principal `p:<ns>/mod-<name>`, and the kernel key signs it only as an attestation that the kernel accepted a mod-originated row — readers and verifiers attribute such a row by its `principal`, never by its key id. L8 A kind the registry does not know is refused at append; there is no default kind (§9.1).

**Anchor falsifiers (P8-D82, re-cut by P8-D83 and P8-D85; S33-08, S33-10).**
- **`G-LOG-04`.** The bulk clauses at 10⁶ appends are unchanged: bulk p99 < 5 ms, and at most 0.1 % of appends over 5 ms. The critical class is measured at **human cadence**: one critical append per second of wall time, interleaved with bulk background load, with critical p99 < 25 ms. p99.9, max and checkpoint count are reported, not asserted. The unit run asserts the critical p99 only when n ≥ 100; with fewer events the p99 index is the max, so it is recorded instead. The ≥ 600-event oracle is the manual 10⁶-row `p-log-fsync` human-cadence run (§19.1). The 1/64 sustained mix (arm G) is a reported stress figure, not asserted. The falsifier runs once, in an isolated `npm run test:perf` pass (serial, `GNOMON_PERF=1`), and is never retried until it passes. On Windows its target is excluded by its platform marker; anywhere else a missing target fails loudly.
- **`G-LOG-06`** gains these cases:
  - file-anchor lag above 63 rows in the same boot ⇒ `INTEGRITY_BROKEN{anchor-lag}`;
  - any lag after a clean close ⇒ `anchor-lag`;
  - truncating below the keystore anchor ⇒ `truncated`.
- **`G-LOG-10`.** For each critical kind, stop the kernel at the first instruction after `append` returns, then truncate the db by one row: open must report `truncated`. No broker effect can be observed before both anchor writes complete (a sequencing probe on the tool-release path). The keystore write starts only after COMMIT returns.
- **`G-LOG-11`** (power-loss classification, by fixtures):
  - (a) keystore ahead, same boot ⇒ `truncated/tamper`;
  - (b) ahead, different boot, not closed ⇒ `truncated/unclean-reboot`, with appends refused;
  - (c) ahead, different boot, closed ⇒ `tamper`;
  - (d) a forged `boot` in the file anchor alone changes nothing;
  - (e) an acknowledgement ⇒ exactly one signed `daemon.log.gap`, and the chain verifies across it;
  - an acknowledgement interrupted between the gap row's commit and the keystore write is re-anchored on open (one row lost), not re-classified;
  - boot ids are normalised on both sides (case, whitespace), and a non-UUID seam value is `unknown`;
  - the P8-D85 fixtures: relaxed across a verified boot change with the signed `which: 'file'` row, refused with `data.relax` in every other case, and `truncated` with `fileLag` when the keystore anchor is ahead (S34-24).

  Manual (release candidate): one hard power cut on a desktop Mac at 1/s critical load, recording which cause is reported.

### 3.3 `#session-runner` + `#supervisor` — one supervisor, two stdio drivers

**Responsibilities.** Preflight; mint session principal + hsid; obtain a permit (tokens/USD for Claude, compute for local); route by `model` (§13.3); select the roster credential (Warpline mode); build policy/env/argv; spawn the driver child in its own process group (job object on Windows) under the `session` profile; parse events; meter usage; staged stop; respawn; orphan sweep. Replaces three runners (04): `a-flow/.../headless.py`, `a-constellation/server/agents.ts`, `ClaudeStreamSession.swift` (deleted M3.6).

**Session model.**
```
session:v1 { hsid, principalId, as?, backend:{kind:'claude-code'|'local', uuid?, binarySha256, version, endpoint?, model, compatProfile?}, cwd, worktree?,
             mode: warpline|git|none, declaredModel: local|claude|any, attended: bool, policyHash, mcpHash, layersHash, gitHash, permitId, parent?, taint: none|peer|peer-teammate, briefId?, state, startedAt, lastActiveAt }
             // taint is sticky: set when the session first reads peer bytes (an accepted brief, a co-work inbox read) and never cleared (§3.9)
```

**`#claude-adapter` argv** (shared core `headless.py:133-154` + `agents.ts:433-446`; constellation's extras `agents.ts:447-454`):
```
claude -p --input-format stream-json --output-format stream-json --verbose --replay-user-messages --include-partial-messages
       --permission-mode default --permission-prompts host --permission-prompt-tool stdio (--session-id <uuid> | --resume <uuid>) --settings <policy.json>
       --setting-sources <explicit subset of user,project,local — p01>  --mcp-config <mcp.json> --strict-mcp-config
       [--append-system-prompt <bytes>] [--model <m>]
```
`--include-partial-messages` feeds the GUI's live stream (D34; 21 "Headless loses"). `FORBIDDEN_FLAGS = {--dangerously-skip-permissions, --allowedTools, --allowed-tools, --bare}` checked with a **throw** (`agents.ts:40,456`; not `headless.py:153`'s `assert`). Interrupt = the stream-json control message `control_request{subtype:'interrupt'}` on both OS (A23-4), never SIGINT/`GenerateConsoleCtrlEvent`; Esc in the GUI ⇒ `session.stop{interrupt:true}`. **Permission channel (P8-D96, probe p01b).** `--permission-prompts host` together with `--permission-prompt-tool stdio` (the adapter throws on any other value or a second copy; `host` alone delivers no request, and `none` wins over `stdio`). Claude writes `control_request{request_id, request:{subtype:'can_use_tool', tool_name, input, tool_use_id, …}}` on stdout. The kernel answers exactly once on stdin with `control_response{response:{subtype:'success', request_id, response:{behavior:'allow', updatedInput}}}` or `{behavior:'deny', message}`. `updatedInput` is always the kernel-held input of the request, never a client's. No answer ever carries `updatedPermissions`, and Claude's `permission_suggestions` are never read: every rule lives in the kernel log. `control_cancel_request{request_id}` (an interrupt while the request is pending) expires the card. An unknown `control_request` subtype gets an error response. A malformed request is never answered. Requests come from a tool call (`ExitPlanMode`, `AskUserQuestion`, Write, Edit, Bash and the rest), never from the assistant `tool_use` block. `ExitPlanMode` ⇒ a `plan` card (approve-plan ⇒ allow; reject ⇒ deny, carrying the note) with a `turn.plan` row. `AskUserQuestion` ⇒ an `ask-user` card (cached class, decision `answer` only, 10 min timeout into deny "no answer") with a `turn.ask-user` row: the GUI shows an option picker and a free-text field; `approve{decision:'answer', note}` carries the plain answer for one question or JSON `{answers:{question: answer}}` for several, checked against the kernel-held questions before presence (`bad-params` otherwise); the allow is `updatedInput = {...input, answers}`; `approval.decide` records `noteHash`/`noteBytes`, never the text. The questions travel as structured card detail inside `contentHash` (and as one `{type:'ask-user', detail}` block node the GUI verifies against `contentHash` before it builds the picker — display text is never parsed); an input key outside the closed AskUserQuestion schema is refused `bad-input`. **Accepted risk (Aegis C3, batched into T-2026-10-19-001):** the answer note is bound by neither the `decisionSig` nor the presence binding. `answer` is a cached-class decision, so no per-decision prompt shows it, and the approvals key signs what the kernel received: binding `noteHash` into the message would make the record self-consistent but would not stop a party that rewrites frames on an authenticated GUI connection (the transport child, §11.2 rule 1b) — the same party can already flip a cached `deny` to `allow`. The GUI renderer and transport are therefore trusted for cached-class decisions, answers included; `approval.decide` records `noteHash` so the answer used is auditable.

**`#local-runner` argv:** `gnomon-local --mode rpc --config <root-signed local.yaml> --session <hsid>` — a separate executable (`packages/gnomon-local`, pi's `rpc-entry.ts` JSONL protocol shape, 23) spawned under the `session` profile exactly like `claude -p`; it owns the model loop and the tools; the kernel sees only its JSONL events (§13).

**Preflight** (lift `headless.py:612-618`, `claudeCli.ts:21-97`) — authoritative before every spawn and respawn: the `claude` binary resolved by **explicit path** — the root-signed config path (§2.4), else the known install locations (macOS: `~/.local/bin/claude`, where p01 found 2.1.302), never a `PATH` lookup, because the native installer's directory is not on a non-login `PATH` and the daemon's clean env has no shell profile — then realpath + sha (`headless.py:528-531`, `claudeCli.ts:21-25`; refuse changed binary on respawn; **trust on first use** (M1.7): the first open whose sandboxed preflight passes pins the sha256 in the kernel home and logs it, every later open must match, and after a Claude Code update a human re-pins by approving the re-pin card the kernel pre-stages (TD-2026-10-19-063: one fresh presence, the private copy's publisher signature checked by codesign, `claim-mismatch` when it is not Anthropic PBC's; the poll that stages it never pins), or offline with `gnomon session repin-claude` (an interactive terminal and a typed confirmation, refused while the daemon runs); the kernel executes only a private copy of the pinned bytes, and refuses a script install (`claude_binary_unsupported`)); version ≥ 2.1.288 (`claudeCli.ts:13`); **law 7 scan** (§3.4) over the session env, every settings layer, `mcp.json` env blocks and the current managed-policy sources — macOS `/Library/Application Support/ClaudeCode/managed-settings.json` + managed preferences; Windows `C:\Program Files\ClaudeCode\managed-settings.json`, `managed-settings.d/*.json`, HKLM/HKCU policy keys (`C:\ProgramData\ClaudeCode` retired at v2.1.75); Linux `/etc/claude-code/managed-settings.json` — plus `~/.claude/settings.json`, the session policy and project `.claude/settings*.json`, none of which may set `apiKeyHelper`, `env.ANTHROPIC_*`, `env.CLAUDE_CODE_USE_*` or any non-default `*_BASE_URL` (`headless.py:550-584`, `claudeCli.ts:63-76`); the session env must not carry `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`; `claude auth status` says `authMethod:"claude.ai"`, `apiProvider:"firstParty"` (`headless.py:586-610`, `claudeCli.ts:79-97`); `init.apiKeySource ≠ "none"` kills the group (`headless.py:33-37`). For local sessions: endpoint ∈ loopback/RFC1918 per root-signed `local.yaml`, else `BILLING_REFUSED`. Any failure ⇒ `session.refused{code}` with flow's closed codes preserved (`claude_version | not_subscription_auth | spawn_failed | claude_binary_changed | bad_message | session_busy`) plus `api_billing_refused`.

**Trace capture: a core per-project audit mode (TD-2026-10-19-057, amends TD-2026-10-19-139).** On any home and any build, the human can switch on trace capture for one project folder (`gnomon trace on|off|status`; `gnomon trace path [--folder]` prints where that folder's captures live). The state is stored only in the Gnomon home, in `<home>/trace-folders.json` (0600 in the 0700 home, keyed by the folder's realpath). No project file, project config, env var or flag can switch it on. The kernel reads the mode at every `session.open` and every resume, for the session's cwd; the folder must match exactly. When tracing is on, the supervisor copies every line it writes to claude's stdin, and every stdout line, to `<home>/trace/<project key>/<hsid with ':' as '-'>.jsonl`, one `{t, dir:'in'|'out'|'ctl', line}` record per LF line. The project key is a slug of the folder name (`[a-z0-9-]`, at most 40 characters, `root` if empty) plus `-` and the first 12 hex digits of sha256 of the realpath, for example `a-paradigm-3f9c1e2a07b4`; it has no `:`, so it is a legal Windows name. The key dir holds `project.json` with the realpath; a key dir naming another folder refuses `trace-unsafe`. Nothing is written into the project tree: no `.gnomon/trace`, no `.gitignore`. The session profile read- and write-denies the whole Gnomon home, the directory itself included, so a session can neither read, write, rename nor replace `<home>/trace` or the toggle (runtime falsifier with a fake claude under the darwin profile). No rule in the project tree is added for the trace (Aegis L7: the former literal deny on `<root>/.gnomon` broke git checkouts, and the always-on read deny caused EPERM noise). Dirs are 0700 and files 0600, owner-checked. A trace file is lstat'd and must be a regular file, then opened `O_NOFOLLOW | O_NONBLOCK` (new files `O_EXCL`), so a symlink, FIFO or device is refused without hanging the kernel (Aegis M3). If the trace is refused, the session runs untraced and its row says `session.sandbox{traced:false, traceRefused}` (`trace-unsafe` or `trace-unavailable`, published in `D98_CLOSED_VALUES`; both fields are reserved under P8-D98). A traced session carries `traced: true` on `session.sandbox` and in status. Kernel notes are `ctl` records: the spawn argv (system-prompt text redacted, env names only), dropped lines, the drain interrupt, stdin close, exit, and `prior-close-unclean` when a resume reopens a file whose last record is not `exit` (Aegis L3; a drain also waits up to 2 s for the trace to reach the disk). The trace never blocks a session: one async writer drains a bounded queue (1,024 records, 8 MiB). A full queue drops `out` records first; an `in` or `ctl` record evicts a queued `out` record and has a reserve of the same size. Drops are counted in a `ctl` note. The audit cannot be capped off (Aegis M1): a record over 64 KiB keeps a truncated copy, its sha256 and its full byte length; past the 50 MiB file bound one `capped` note is written and recording goes on, with every `in` and `ctl` record in full and each `out` record as a skeleton (type, subtype, tool_use name and id); past four times the bound, `out` skeletons are only counted (`out-skipped`). Every record is checked for secrets on its decoded JSON value, so JSON escapes cannot hide a match, and only the matched span is replaced with `[redacted:<kind>]`; the record and its structure stay visible (Aegis M2, L4). The shapes are a private-key PEM, `grt:v1:`, `sk-ant-`, `ghp_`, `github_pat_`, `AKIA`, `xox[bp]-` and the value of any secret-named session env var. Residuals: the files hold prompt and tool text in the clear, readable by any process of the user's own account outside a session (`gnomon trace on` says so). A session in `/proj/sub` is untraced when `/proj` is traced (exact-folder match; tracked as a follow-up). `in` and `ctl` records past the hard bound are not bounded. On win32 there are no POSIX modes; sessions there refuse `sandbox-unavailable`, so no trace is opened. Node has no `fcntl`, so `O_NONBLOCK` is left set; it has no effect on a regular file.

**Whose hooks run in a session (T-2026-10-18-010, P8-D94).** The `--setting-sources` list is a root-signed config switch, `config.sessions.hooks`, with four modes. `audited` is the default (P8-D94). It loads only the user layer. The kernel extracts the repo's project and local hooks (`.claude/settings.json` and `.claude/settings.local.json` in the session root) and shows them one gate-class card per hook, in the install card's rendering: a count line first, then the hook on its own line, never cut. A hook with a line separator, format or control character, an unknown key or a non-command type is refused. It is listed by id only and never runs. A symlinked, oversized or unparseable layer, or more than 64 hooks, makes the whole set unreadable, so no repo hook runs. Decisions are allow or deny per hook, pinned to the set's hash per repo. A changed set raises the cards again. Only approved hooks are written into the session's own `policy.json` (`--settings`). Until a decision exists the hook does not run, so the session opens with user hooks only. Approval needs presence (M1.3). Editing a hook in place is deferred; deny it for now. The other modes are `all` (user, project and local, raw: repo hooks run unaudited), `user-only` (only the user layer) and `none` (no layer, only the generated policy). Managed settings load in every mode (p01). Nothing else selects the mode: no env var, project file or CLI flag. A config that has never been written gives `audited`. If a signed config was applied before and the current one cannot be (rollback, missing, unsigned) or names an unknown mode, the mode is `none`: fail closed, logged on `config.loaded`. Every repo-hook card also carries a kernel-written warning: approving trusts the command and every file it runs, and a repo change, a pull or a session edit can change what runs (§A.9). `session.sandbox` rows log the mode and `settingSources`. `gate-class.detected` and `gate-class.decided` rows carry the hook ids only. The command itself is on the card, so it is in the card's `approval.request` row like any card text. Probe p10m-repo-surfaces (real Claude Code 2.1.302 under the generated session profile, 2026-10-18, pass) measured this. With `--setting-sources user`, no repo hook fired: not from the project settings, a skill's frontmatter or an agent's frontmatter. The repo's skills, agents, slash commands and `CLAUDE.md` (with its `@` imports) did not load either. Hooks in the kernel's `--settings` file did fire (`UserPromptSubmit`, `PreToolUse`). `SessionStart` hooks fired in neither arm under `-p` stream-json, including the control arm with every layer loaded. How Claude Code treats an empty `--setting-sources` is still unprobed. **Repo context (P8-D97, probe p10m-repo-context).** `audited` still loads the repo's context through the kernel (`kernel/supervisor/repo-context.ts`). `--add-dir` is passed for the cwd and each ancestor up to the git top-level that holds a `CLAUDE.md`, a `.claude/CLAUDE.md` or `.claude/rules`, never the user home or above, and the child env carries `CLAUDE_CODE_ADDITIONAL_DIRECTORIES_CLAUDE_MD=1`. Claude Code 2.1.303 loads that memory, with its `@` imports, without the project source, and loads none of that dir's skills or hooks. Each `--add-dir` is also a working directory for Claude's own tools: Read, Glob and Grep run there without a card, up to the git top-level when the cwd is a subdirectory, while Edit and Write stay carded (P8-D96 `ask`). The session profile already allows those reads (it denies reads only inside the Gnomon home and the key stores; a supervisor test reads the git top's `CLAUDE.md` from a subdirectory cwd under the real profile). Worktree behaviour, decided: the memory dirs and the plugin come from the session's cwd only, which is the directory Claude runs in. A session's worktree root is a sandbox and hook-extraction root, not a context source. Every memory-chain dir's `CLAUDE*` and `.claude/**` is write-denied in the session profile (`memoryDirs`, Aegis M1), so a session cannot plant steering that a later session loads. The cwd's `.claude/skills`, `.claude/agents` and `.claude/commands` are copied into `<session dir>/context`, a plugin named `project` loaded with `--plugin-dir`. The session profile allows reading that dir and denies writing it. Frontmatter is allow-listed per kind, so `hooks`, `allowed-tools`, `permissionMode`, `mcpServers` and unknown keys are dropped. The probe measured that a plugin skill's frontmatter hooks fire, so stripping is the guard. A tab, a quoted, flow-style, merge or duplicate key, or an unterminated block refuses that file. A symlink refuses the item, and a symlinked `.claude` refuses everything. The copy is bounded at 1024 files, 16 MiB in total and 2 MiB per file. Frontmatter hooks are stripped and listed, never approvable in this slice. The repo's non-hook settings (permissions, env and the rest) are not loaded. Each repo-hook card carries one kernel-written informational line with the counts and key names, never a value, and `gate-class.detected{what:'repo-context'}` logs what loaded, what was refused, what was stripped and what was not loaded. The modes `user-only` and `none` stage nothing, and `all` loads the repo natively. The probe's chosen arm passed every check: the `CLAUDE.md` token was visible; x, y and z were listed and used; no repo hook fired; the kernel hook and an approved repo hook fired; the repo env was absent; `touch` was still asked despite the repo `allow`; and the device key was not read. **Follow-ups (Aegis F1–F3, T-2026-10-19-003).** When the subtree walk meets a symlinked dir it resolves the real path. A target outside the git top-level, or inside any `node_modules`, refuses the memory dir; a target elsewhere in the repo is walked where it lives (F1). The session profile also read-denies every `CLAUDE*.md` and everything under a `.claude` dir inside any `node_modules` below the git top-level, in any letter case. This is the `dep-steering` match: two SBPL regexes on the escaped root, mirrored as Claude Read deny rules (F2). In probe p10m-repo-context, a turn that reads a module file next to such a `CLAUDE.md` succeeds and never sees its text. A control arm without the deny didn't load that file either, so on 2.1.303 F2 is defence in depth. Every in-repo `@` import target the audit resolved, whether or not it exists, is a settings-class write-deny literal in the session profile, so a session cannot plant or rewrite what an approved memory file imports. There is a cap of 4,096 targets per memory dir, and past it the dir is refused. The kernel's card pre-check (`deniedWrite`) also matches the `steering` subtree, so a write there, to a memory dir or to an import target is auto-denied with no card, like any other write-denied path (F3). An import target that SBPL cannot express (a quote or a control character) refuses only its memory dir instead of failing the whole session profile (F3a). The subtree audit collects every `claude*.md` in any letter case, `CLAUDE.local.md` included, which is exactly what the steering deny covers (F4). A dangling symlinked dir is resolved from its link target against the link's own dir, and refuses if it points outside the repo or into `node_modules`; existing outward file links such as `.venv/bin/python` are not refused (F1a). Residual: a carded Bash command can restructure ancestor dirs during a session (`rm -rf docs && ln -s /tmp/evil docs`), so a memory file audited at open could later be read through a new link. The full fix is a session-wide read-deny on steering files the audit did not cover, which is a later slice. **Round 3 (Aegis R1–R4).** Claude Code loads a subdirectory's `CLAUDE.md` lazily, so the audit covers each memory dir's whole subtree (R1). Every `claude.md` at any depth and in any letter case, and every `.md` under a `.claude/rules` dir, gets the symlink and import checks. A symlinked `claude.md` or `.claude` refuses; other symlinks are not followed. Dirs named `.git` and `node_modules` are not walked (residual: a dependency's `CLAUDE.md`). An unreadable dir, or more than 200,000 dirs, refuses the memory dir. The outermost memory dir is walked once and nested dirs reuse that walk; on this repo that takes about 1.6 s. The session profile write-denies every `CLAUDE*.md` and everything under a `.claude` dir anywhere below the git top-level, in any letter case. This is the `steering` match, an SBPL regex, and Claude's own Edit globs mirror it. Other `claude*` names such as `claude-adapter.ts` stay writable. An import outside the repo refuses whether or not the target exists, because the lexical check runs before `lstat`. A `%` in an import token, or an entity-encoded `@` (`&#64;`, `&#x40;`, `&commat;`), also refuses (R2). A file reached at depth 5 that still carries an import refuses instead of stopping silently (R3). In a skill tree, `SKILL.md` is matched in any letter case, and every `.md` goes through the full sanitizer and the shell check; only the sanitized text is written (R4). Before Windows parity, the import resolver must be reviewed again for drive-letter, `\\?\` and UNC forms (a platform-registry row records this). **Round 2 (Aegis H1–H3, M1–M3, Q6).** The kernel audits memory itself (H1). For each memory dir it walks `CLAUDE.md`, `.claude/CLAUDE.md` and `.claude/rules/**`, and it refuses the whole dir if any of them is a symlink. It also follows every `@` import the way Claude does (the same token grammar, depth 5, `~` as the user home, absolute and relative forms; code spans are not skipped, which is stricter than Claude), and it refuses the dir if an import names an existing path outside the git top-level, or a symlink. That refusal is on the repo-hook cards and on the `repo-context` row. Shell preprocessing fails closed (H2): a skill or command containing `` !`…` `` or a fenced `!` block is not loaded, and `policy.json` sets `disableSkillShellExecution`. Every `SKILL.md` at any depth is sanitized, and a nested `.md` whose frontmatter declares hooks refuses its skill (H3). A frontmatter continuation is accepted only after an empty value, a `|` or `>` block scalar or a `- ` list, and C0 and C1 controls, U+0085, U+2028 and U+2029 refuse the file (M3). The adapter's `extra` is an allow-list (`--max-turns`, `--no-session-persistence`), and an arity-aware argv grammar requires each kernel-owned flag exactly once or at most once; `--agents` and `--tools` never appear, and `--add-dir` is one variadic flag (Q6). Dot-leading files such as `.DS_Store` are skipped, and a readdir failure is a refusal, not a failed open. There is no session-dir GC, so `context/` stays after exit beside `policy.json`. Probe p10m-repo-context round 2 (production policy, no allow rules, pass, 12 checks) measured where each outcome came from. A read of another session's file, direct or through a shell variable, was denied by a `policy.json` deny rule, with no request. `osascript` was allowed on its card and then refused by the sandbox (exit 126). Without the setting, a skill's inline `!` shell hits the always-ask Bash rule and fails closed with no `can_use_tool` request; with `disableSkillShellExecution`, both a Skill-tool call and a user-typed `/project:zsh` get "[shell command execution disabled by policy]". The model arms read the founder's `~/.claude` as the user layer, because a scratch HOME needs a `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`, which the session env scrubs in production. Residuals: items are namespaced (`project:x`, `/project:z`); the copy is a snapshot taken at open; `CLAUDE.local.md` and nested on-demand `CLAUDE.md` files are not loaded.

**Settings layers (S-H7, S16-7, S22-H2).** The kernel hashes every layer it can see — managed, user, project, local, worktree `.claude/**`, `CLAUDE.md`, `CLAUDE.local.md`, user-scope skills/plugins listing — into `layersHash`, and `.git/config`, `.git/hooks/**`, `.git/info/**`, `.gitattributes` into `gitHash` (S16-2), both normalized for CRLF and path case (A23-4), at every spawn AND respawn; a change against the previous value or against the base revision is **gate-class** (§15.7) regardless of whether the file is gitignored or appears in a diff. The same layers are **watched mid-session** (`fs.watch`/`ReadDirectoryChangesW`): a change ⇒ `session.stop{interrupt}` + `billing.api.refused{where}` when law-7-relevant, otherwise a `gate-class` card; `session.layers.changed{which}` is logged either way. `--setting-sources` is passed explicitly to limit layers (QM uses `settingSources:[]`, 21); per p01 its documented values are only `user`, `project` and `local`. The managed layer is not among them: per p01 the kernel treats managed settings as always loaded by the CLI and not controllable by `--setting-sources`, so the managed layer is always hashed into `layersHash` and always law-7 scanned. Managed-policy semantics (precedence, which keys apply under `-p`) remain a manual check in P01. `G-KRN-01` claims replay of kernel-controlled inputs plus recorded hashes, not of host context.

**Transcripts and `--resume` (TD-2026-10-19-814 item 1, probe p-resume darwin).** The session profile write-denies the whole user `~/.claude`, so under the stock profile Claude Code saved no transcript and `--resume` had nothing to load (`packages/gnomon/probes/results/p-resume.darwin.json`: `q1StockProfileDeniesTranscriptWrite: true`). A Claude Code session's profile now carves `~/.claude/projects` (subpath, realpath'd) back out with `(allow file-write* (subpath "<home>/.claude/projects"))`, rendered after the `~/.claude` write deny, because in SBPL the last matching rule wins. With that rule the probe's transcript was created and grew on resume. Everything else under `~/.claude` stays write-denied: settings, plugins, skills, agents, commands, hooks and `CLAUDE.md`, and `~/.claude.json` too. The carve-back is policy data (`allowWrite`) derived only from the user Claude dir, behind a supervisor flag (`claudeTranscripts`) and never from an input path, so no project manifest can widen it. Local sessions do not get it. After the allow the profile re-denies (`denyWriteAlways`, plus the T-008 git regexes): the `projects` dir itself as a literal, so a session can neither remove, rename nor replace it with a link (its children match on their own paths, so transcripts still save; the supervisor creates the dir before the spawn); any other write deny inside the carve-back; and, when the memory chain's steering regex covers `projects` (a cwd under the user home), that regex re-rooted at `projects`, so no `CLAUDE*.md` or `.claude` dir can be written inside it (Aegis M1, L3; profile v6). A hardlink to a write-denied file such as `~/.claude/settings.json` or `~/.claude.json` cannot be planted inside `projects`: on macOS 15 the sandbox checks `link(2)` against the source path's write rule, so the link itself is refused (measured; `test/session-sandbox.test.ts`). The allow is withheld, fail closed, if `~/.claude/projects` resolves anywhere but `realpath(~/.claude)/projects` (a symlink) or another write deny covers it (for example a Gnomon home above `~/.claude`). The policy then records `allowWriteRefused` and the supervisor logs `session.sandbox{phase:'transcripts-withheld'}`; that session saves no transcript and cannot be resumed. Claude's own `Edit` deny on `~/.claude/**` in `policy.json` is unchanged, so the model's tools still cannot write there. Residual, accepted by the founder (TD-2026-10-19-814): a session may write anywhere under `~/.claude/projects`, and that reach is wider than its own transcript. (a) It can write the auto-memory (`projects/<enc>/memory/`) and the transcripts of ANY cwd's `projects/<enc>/`, not only its own, and it can delete them (the dir itself stays). (b) It can forge transcript history that a later `--resume` loads as the conversation so far. (c) The readers include the user's own unsandboxed Claude Code, not only later Gnomon sessions. (d) A link it plants inside `projects` (for example `MEMORY.md` → `~/.zshrc`) is followed by a later write from the host's Claude Code, which is not sandboxed, so that write lands outside `projects`. Linux and Windows have no session backend yet, so this is data only there and nothing enforces it.

**Git hardening (S16-2, S22-H4).** Every kernel git invocation (`worktree add`, `merge-tree`, `show <base>:…`, fast-forward) runs with env `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1 GIT_ATTR_NOSYSTEM=1` and flags `-c core.hooksPath=<empty dir> -c core.fsmonitor=false -c core.sshCommand=false -c protocol.allow=never -c safe.directory=<root> -c core.longpaths=true`; a repo whose `.git/config` or `.gitattributes` declares `filter.*`, `merge.*.driver`, `diff.*.textconv`, `include`/`includeIf` is refused until a human decides (`gate-class`); git runs inside the `git` sandbox class (§15.5), never in the daemon process's own context; `gitHash` drift refuses the operation until a human decides.

**Environment.** Allowlist `HOME USER LOGNAME LANG LC_ALL LC_CTYPE TMPDIR` + `PATH SHELL` (`headless.py:97`, `claudeCli.ts:15`; Windows adds `USERPROFILE SYSTEMROOT WINDIR SystemDrive ProgramFiles COMSPEC PATHEXT APPDATA LOCALAPPDATA TEMP CLAUDE_CODE_GIT_BASH_PATH`), scrub `ANTHROPIC_* CLAUDE_CODE_USE_* CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH* CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR NODE_OPTIONS GNOMON_* *_BASE_URL` (`headless.py:98-99`, `claudeCli.ts:16`), add `GNOMON_HSID GNOMON_SOCK GNOMON_SESSION_KEY` and, in Warpline mode, `WARPLINE_AGENT_ID` + `WARPLINE_MCP_TOKEN` (`tokens.ts:295-297`). `envKeys[]` logged. Session-key custody stated honestly: path in env, readable by the session's tool children; blast radius = the session's scope (I4; the key cannot approve); the `session` sandbox denies `~/.gnomon/keys/**`, so it cannot read the device or agent keys, and the approvals key is a hardware handle the kernel alone holds (D38).

**Process.** macOS/Linux: `detached:true`, own pgid, staged stop control message → close stdin → SIGTERM → SIGKILL on the group + sweep (`headless.py:339-351,483-507`, `agents.ts:38,460`). Windows: spawn inside a job object with `KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE` via the signed helper (§16); staged stop = control message → close stdin → `TerminateJobObject`; `taskkill /T /F` fallback logged `partial`. stderr cap 64 KiB; stdout lines cap 4 MiB with `dropped` events (`agents.ts:36,506-513`); leader-exit grace 2 s (`headless.py:389-403`); idle respawn `--resume` charged as a launch (`agents.ts:34,445`); crash cap 3/10 min (`agents.ts:37-39`); busy-elsewhere check (`claudeCli.ts:113-117`). **macOS sandbox facts (p10m).** `ps` cannot run inside any session: it is setuid root, and `sandbox-exec` refuses setuid binaries under every profile, so neither the adapter nor its fixtures depend on `ps`. `pgrep` fails outright once `sysmond` is denied (§15.5). Claude Code 2.1.302 itself works under the hardened profile (p10m-claude-compat: subscription auth, Bash with node/git/python, workdir writes, child spawn and kill).

**Invariants.** S1 No process creation without `permit.grant` (law 1). S2 Every driver event is a `turn.event` before anything acts on it. S3 `turn.usage` (partial/assistant messages) and `turn.result` are the only `budget.usage` sources; mid-turn breach kills the group/job. S4 Children die with the session. S5 Replay per §2.1. S6 `session.*` appended only by the supervisor. S7 A `model: local` session never spawns `claude` without a `fallback-cloud` card decision (§13.3). S8 Only `#claude-adapter` consumes a permit with `backend:'claude-code'` (`permit.backend` binding, S22-H3).

### 3.4 `#approval-broker` + `#budget-fuse`

**Cards (`card:v1`, Q5).**
```
card:v1 { cardId, kind: permission|budget|knot|tend|verdict|merge-conflict|gate-class|install|fallback|ask-user|plan|api-token|voice-share|voice-arm|grant|
                       handoff|declassify|peer-pair|                                     // §3.9; handoff carries direction: out|in
                       reconsider|strictness|incident,                                   // §7.3 convergence cap; strictness and run mode loosening; human-reported incident
          requestedBy: principalId, hsid?, contractId?, nodeId?, attended: bool,          // attended = a human client is subscribed to this session
          approverPolicy: { kind:'self' },                                                // roles/quorum reserved for 8.1 (Q5)
          block: display-block:v1, contentHash, flags?: [hidden-chars|bidi|voice-phrase|peer|irreversible|claim-mismatch], actions:[kernel-signed],   // peer = raised by a peer-tainted session or by a peer's frame (§3.9)
          note?: change-note:v1, strictness?: { level: L0|L1|L2|L3, mode, reason },    // inside contentHash; agentClaims untrusted (§4.11)
          state: pending|held|decided|expired, heldUntil?, heldDecision?,                  // held = a voice decision waiting out its hold (§14.7)
          voice?: { eligible: none|reversible|gate|judgment, reason?: hand-only|class|flagged|voice-phrase|policy,
                    label, readback, readbackHash, echo },                                // kernel-composed, sanitized, secret- and phrase-masked, under the card's kernel signature
          decidedBy?, decidedAt?, decisionSig?, presence?: fresh|cached|cli|voice-armed, via?: gui|wrapper|cli|client|voice|link,   // via: link reserved; no decision ever arrives over a link
          voiceDecision?: { armId, armClass: reversible|gate, humanConnId, voiceConnId, turnId, confirmTurnId?, deckId?, ordinal?, label?,
                            handleHash?, challengeHash?, readbackHash?, speakerId, asrConfidence, mode: ptt|conversation },
          cli?:{pid,ppid,pgid,tty} | {client:<surface>, connId}, timeoutAt }
```
Decisions: `allow | always | deny | resume | resolve | accept | reject | install | answer{text} | approve-plan | fallback-cloud | use-api-token | withdraw | extend | restart` (`extend` and `restart` only on `reconsider`, §7.3; `withdraw` is valid only on a `held` card, from any human-class connection, with no presence, §14.7). `always` appends `approval.rule.learned{tool, matchExact}` (S-L1) and is a standing rule, so it is a **fresh-presence** decision: the broker refuses `always` under a cached unlock with `FORBIDDEN{reason:'presence'}` and raises the presence prompt instead (S25-C2, `G-BRK-05`). **Decision classes (D38, S25-C2):** **fresh-presence** `gate-class`, `install`, `resolve`, `accept|reject` (verdict), `fallback-cloud`, `resume`, `use-api-token` (root key), `always`; **cached-unlock** `allow|deny` on `tool.permission`, `answer` on `ask-user`, `approve-plan` on `plan`. The part-2 kinds (P8-D86–D88): `restart` and `extend` on `reconsider` and `allow` on `strictness` are fresh-presence and hand-only (`allow` on a `strictness` card below the evidence level takes the root key, B12); `reject` on `reconsider` and `deny` on `strictness` are deny-direction and take the cached class; `incident` is raised only by the human's `request.incident` and decided `accept|reject` with the cached class, because it only tightens; a card flagged `irreversible` is fresh-presence whatever its kind. These are **base** classes: the class a decision takes is the base class ⊓ its repo's strictness (below). A cached unlock belongs to one connection: the broker accepts a cached-class decision only on the `connId` whose presence prompt created the unlock (S25-H1). **Voice (D58–D62, §14.7):** with toggle 1 on for the card's repo, cached-unlock-class decisions may arrive as a voice intent inside a reversible arm; with toggle 2, `gate-class`, `fallback-cloud` and `resume` may arrive inside a gate arm with handle and challenge; `resolve` and `accept|reject` only with the further `judgment` sub-opt-in (D61). This is the one named, paired exception to the connection binding: presence per decision becomes presence per short, counted arm (S26-H5). **Linking (P8-R9, §3.9):** `handoff` (out: send a brief to a `self` peer; in: `accept|reject` a received brief), `declassify` (out to a teammate) and `peer-pair` are fresh-presence; `peer-pair` and every `grant.peer` renewal also need the root key; a `reject` on an inbound brief is deny-direction and takes the cached class. A card with the flag `peer` is voice-ineligible (reason `flagged`), refuses `always` (`FORBIDDEN`, gate `link`, reason `peer-always`), and — when the taint is `peer-teammate` — refuses a cached-unlock `allow` on `tool.permission` and raises the presence prompt instead (reason `peer-presence`). No decision arrives over a link: a decision carrying `via: link` is refused. Never by voice: root-key decisions (`install`, `use-api-token`), `always`, `voice-arm` and `grant` cards, `voice-share`, flagged cards and every root-signed act. Every voice decision except a deny-direction one is held before release, and recorded `presence: voice-armed`, `via: voice`, `voiceDecision{…}`. `approval.decide` carries `decisionSig` (approver in the preimage), `presence: fresh|cached|cli|voice-armed`, `connId` and `cli:{pid,ppid,pgid,tty}` provenance; `approval.suspect` flags decisions with no TTY, decisions whose `cli` ancestry contains a live session pgid/job, decisions whose unlock was cached beyond its class, or whose `connId` differs from the unlock's, and voice decisions without a live arm, an enrolled speaker or (gate, judgment) a logged readback (`voice-unarmed | voice-unenrolled | voice-no-readback | voice-echo-overlap | voice-custody-client`), and surfaces them in the GUI inbox (S16-5) — honest scope: a process with its own TTY evades the ancestry check (S22 partial 5). The **presence prompt text** is composed by the kernel from its own card record (kind, requester, `contentHash` summary line, `connId`, open GUI sessions `{n}`) and signed by the kernel key; on Windows it is shown natively when the M1.0 probe (S29-07) proves the Passport KSP's `NCRYPT_USE_CONTEXT_PROPERTY` text appears, else in a helper-owned window beside the Hello prompt (`status.presencePromptText: native | helper-window`, S25-M1). The prompt proves a touch, not what was approved, against a same-user process that can reach the key (S29-K2, §A.1). Unattended sessions (`attended:false`) **wait** on cards; the GUI shows the wait; nothing is auto-decided (D21; QM attended/unattended, 18).

**The card is the gate; a permit never means allow (P8-D96).** A `can_use_tool` request becomes a `permission` card offering `allow | deny | always` (`approve` refuses any other decision with `bad-params`; `always` is fresh presence every time). A standing rule is derived by `#always-rules` from kernel-signed rows only — `approval.request` + `approval.decide{always}` + `approval.rule.learned{cardId, tool, matchExact, presence, repoKey}` — and keyed (repoKey, tool, inputHash); a later exact match is answered allow with no card after `approval.auto{hsid, tool, inputHash, modeRef:'rule:<cardId>'}`, and Claude-side permission rules (`updatedPermissions`) are never written. `#rules-cli` (`gnomon rules list`) reads the same fold from a verified log. Revoke is the human-device verb `rules.revoke{ruleId}` (P8-D98): a verified human device asks for it with no Touch ID, because revoking only narrows (founder ruling F1, TD-2026-10-19-528), and the broker writes `approval.rule.revoked{ruleId, repoKey, tool, connId}`; until its handler lands the verb refuses `ENGINE{not-implemented}`. A learned rule's `match` accepts only `exact` (F2); a rule never widens past an exact match. The card's detail is `{tool, toolUseId, requestId, inputHash, repo, path?}`. Every line is wrapped like an install card's lines and never cut. Every input field is shown. Bash shows the full command, then every other key as `key=value`. Edit shows the path and the old and new text in full. MultiEdit shows the path and every edit's old and new text. NotebookEdit shows the cell source in full. Write shows the path, the byte count, a head excerpt and the `inputHash`, and says that only an excerpt is shown. Write is the only writing tool approved on an excerpt. Any other tool shows its name and every key. These are denied with no card: a write under the session policy's `denyWrite` (case-folded on darwin and win32), Bash with `dangerouslyDisableSandbox` (`sandbox-escape`), a non-text command, content or old/new text (`bad-input`), and a card over 512 KiB (`too-large`). Only an `allow` decision allows. These all deny: no broker, a failed raise, expiry (`approval.expire{into:'deny', why}`), a reused `requestId` (both denied, `approval.suspect{why:'duplicate-request-id'}`), a malformed request body under a valid id, and more than 64 pending requests in one session. The expiry `why` is `timeout` (the sweep), `cancelled` (a `control_cancel_request`), `session-exited` (exit or stop), `duplicate-request-id` (the id was reused while pending), or `answered`: a card whose raise finished after its request had already been answered for another reason. That reason is used instead of `answered` when it is known. While a decision is in flight, an expiry waits; the decision is claimed synchronously after the broker's last check, so an expiry never splits it. A request whose id cannot be answered logs `approval.suspect{why:'malformed-permission-request'}`. A gated tool that ran with no request logs `approval.suspect{why:'ungated-tool-use', tool, toolUseId}` when its `tool_result` arrives. `turn.event` rows for `control_request` lines carry `{lineHash, bytes}` only. Because of P8-D92, the meter grants every `tool.permission` reserve, so a permit is never read as an allow. Each answer is logged `session.inject{origin:'kernel', hash, bytes}` (a byte count, never the text) before it is written, and nothing is written once the session is no longer running. Probe p01b found two paths that bypass the card. An `allow` rule in any loaded settings layer runs the tool with no request, and so does a PreToolUse hook answering `allow`. So the session's `policy.json` carries `permissions.ask` for Bash, Edit, Write, MultiEdit, NotebookEdit, WebFetch and PowerShell; `ask` wins over `allow`, which brings the request back (p01b). A repo-hook card for a PreToolUse or PermissionRequest hook says that the hook can approve tool uses with no card. The `ungated-tool-use` detector reports what still slips through.

**Deny-safe settlement (T-2026-10-19-009).** Three rules hold, each with its own falsifier. An undecodable request is denied: a request whose id is good but whose body cannot be decoded is answered deny with no card, a request whose id cannot be read is never answered, an `approve` whose params cannot be decoded is refused `BAD_REQUEST` and settles nothing, and any decision other than a decoded allow is deny. A cancel from the session settles the card into deny and writes nothing back to the session (the request no longer exists); it never settles to allow, and it settles once. A late answer finds no prompt: an `approve` on a card that is no longer pending is refused before any presence prompt and writes no decision row, and a card the broker never held is `NOT_FOUND`.

**Strictness and run modes (P8-D86–D88; research 35 A35-08..15, research 36 S35-01..09).** Every repo has a **strictness level** L0–L3, owned by the broker and computed from the log alone, so `gnomon replay` reproduces it; every repo and session has a **run mode**. Tightening spends agent autonomy, never the founder's attention (D86): a level takes things away from the agents and never adds a card to a decision (B14). Each level includes everything below it.

| Level | What it takes away |
|---|---|
| L0 | Nothing: the classes above as written. Reached only on evidence (D88), and only once the repo's `(repo, mode)` reviewer is calibrated (D87, §8.6). |
| L1 | Armada concurrency in the repo capped at `strictness.concurrency.L1`; `always` rules for the repo suspended (not deleted); voice narrowed to reversible; gate-class and kernel-`high`-risk nodes need a second reviewer (`reviewer:security`, an extra oracle the `paradigm` distro ships) and a fresh-presence `verdict` before they land — the only extra verdicts any level takes (D86); `built` must carry a change note; mode ceiling `auto-edits`. **The floor of every repo whose reviewer is `uncalibrated`** (D87), which before Move 3 is every repo. |
| L2 | Concurrency 1; **auto-landing off** — certified nodes queue and land through one batched `verdict` card per repo per `strictness.landBatchMinutes`, never a card per node; cached-class decisions in the repo (`allow` on `tool.permission`, `answer`, `approve-plan`) take fresh presence; approving by voice off, denying by voice kept (D66); `unlimited` arm counts (D64) do not apply; mode ceiling `ask`. |
| L3 | No new spawn, automation or unattended turn in the repo (`FORBIDDEN`, gate `strictness`, reason `strictness-paused`); attended human sessions only; mode `manual`. |

**Effective class.** A decision's class is its base class ⊓ its repo's level, the stricter of the two. At L2 a cached-class decision arriving on a cached unlock is refused `FORBIDDEN`, gate `strictness`, reason `strictness-presence`, and the presence prompt is raised for the **same** card; the card is not re-issued and none is added. A trip never drops the helper's unlocks: they are per `connId`, and a global drop would itself be a denial of service, so the broker remaps the class per repo instead (S35-09). A cached decision that lands after a trip is flagged `approval.suspect{reason:'strictness-class'}`. Effective voice classes = grant ∩ narrowings ∩ the level's ceiling (§14.7). The presence prompt and the card's kernel region name the reason for the class (`fresh presence: repo at L2 — 3 reverts / 12 landed`).

**Run modes (D87).** Akin to Claude Code's permission modes, with no bypass mode:
- `manual`: the repo's sessions act only on the human's turn — no agent spawn, automation or unattended session — and writes and exec wait on an approved `plan`.
- `ask`: write and exec tools raise `permission` cards. This is the default, and was Move 1's only behaviour before run modes were built.
- `auto-edits`: file edits inside the session's own worktree pass with no card, recorded by the broker as `approval.auto{hsid, tool, path, inputHash, modeRef}`; exec, network, edits outside the worktree and every gate-class path (§15.7) still ask. **Read-only Bash (TD-2026-10-20-294 part 1).** A `Bash` request whose command the kernel classifier `#shell-readonly` (`supervisor/shell-readonly.ts`) PROVES read-only also passes, recorded `approval.auto{hsid, tool, inputHash, modeRef:'mode:auto-edits:read-only'}` (no path). The classifier parses with a real shell tokenizer for a subset (quotes, escapes, `$(…)`, backticks, `<(…)`, globs, heredocs, `; && || | &`, newlines, redirections, subshells, `for NAME in …; do …; done`) and fails closed: anything unparsed or unproven raises the card. It is read-only only when every simple command, in every pipeline, list, loop body and substitution, is on a tight allowlist with its flag rules (readers with no writing, executing, recursive or symlink-following flag; `sort`, `uniq`, `file`, `date` (display only: no `-f`, `-r`, `-d`, `-s`), `jq`, `find` (no `-L`/`-follow`), `awk` (one `[PATTERN] { print ITEMS }` program only), `sed` restricted; `git` with read subcommands only, no global option, `remote` bare only and `config --get` never on a key that may hold a credential; `rg` is not on the list, it recurses by default); there is no backgrounding, negation or env assignment; output redirections go only to `/dev/null` (or dup 1↔2); `printf` takes no flag (`printf -v HOME …` assigns) and `echo` only `-n`/`-e`/`-E`. **Amendment (Aegis re-check of TD-2026-10-20-294, C1–C2, H1–H4, M1–M3).** (1) *No dynamic paths:* a word holding `$(…)` or a backtick may be only `echo`/`printf` text or a `<<<` word, never a path argument; a `for` loop iterates only literal words and literal globs, every value one safe token, and `$NAME` (an enclosing loop's variable only) is substituted with each value, so every path a reader receives is concrete and checked. (2) *Known cwd:* the cwd is known only after a first command `cd ABS` (absolute or `~/…`, inside a session root, taken at its realpath; a relative `cd` is refused) and only along an unbroken `&&` chain from it; with an unknown cwd any relative path, relative glob, implicit-cwd read (`ls`, `du`, `find` with no operand) or `git` command raises the card, and an all-absolute command still passes. Every operand of a path-taking command counts as a path, patterns included. (3) *Readable roots (an allowlist, replacing the deny-list as the primary rule):* every path, as given and at its realpath (for a path that does not exist, the realpath of its deepest existing ancestor plus the rest), must lie inside a session root or a system read root — `/usr`, `/opt/homebrew`, `/etc`, `/tmp`, `/private/tmp`, `/private/etc` (posix only); everything else raises the card. The deny stays on top: no device path, and nothing inside — or an ancestor of — the Gnomon home or a credential dir (`~/.ssh`, `~/.aws`, `~/.config/gh`, Keychains, …). A repo's own files (its `.env` included) stay readable. (4) *Globs and symlinks:* an unquoted glob is expanded at classification time with a matcher that matches a superset of the shell's, every match is checked at its realpath, and more than 200 matches, or a match starting with `-`, is refused; `diff` takes no directory operand (it would read files inside it that are never operands), a word starting with an unquoted `=` (zsh's `=cmd`, a `$PATH` lookup) is refused, and bash's unary `test -a FILE` is path-checked like `-e`. (5) *Gate-class files:* `.gitconfig`, `.git-credentials`, `.netrc`, curl/pip/conda config, interpreter and toolchain pins, build entry points, shell rc files and `.env`/`.env.*` join the §15.7 gate-class list, so a session cannot plant a file that changes what an allowed tool runs or sends. The founder's loop `for f in $(ls -d 2026-10-*); do …; done` is therefore a card; its safe form `for f in 2026-10-*; do …; done` (after `cd ABS &&`) passes. The request's input may carry only `command`, `description`, `timeout` (never `run_in_background` or a sandbox override), and a flagged card (hidden or bidi characters) is always shown. Never in `ask` or `manual`. Accepted residuals, stated in the module: `git` runs programs the repo's own git config names (`.git/**` is gate-class); a glob is expanded at classification time, so a file planted by a concurrent process between the check and the run is not seen; a planted file NAME inside a session root can still steer which in-root file a literal glob reaches; `du` and `find` walk a directory (names and sizes, never contents) without following symlinks; the system read roots are readable in full (the kernel may narrow them, never widen them); the user's own shell aliases, functions and options are trusted, including zsh `EXTENDED_GLOB`/`KSH_GLOB` patterns (`^x`, `a~b`) the classifier's glob matcher does not model.

A session takes its repo's mode unless one is set for it (`session.open{runMode}` or the control); a mode looser than the level's ceiling is refused `FORBIDDEN`, gate `strictness`, reason `strictness-mode`, and a level step below the evidence without the root key is refused with reason `strictness-evidence`; the effective mode is the stricter of the chosen mode and the level's ceiling, and `status` shows both. **Tightening** — a level up or a stricter mode — is one human gesture with no presence: `request.strictness.set` or `request.mode.set` from a first-party human-class connection (the GUI control, palette or `⇧Tab`, §11.4–§11.5), or `gnomon strictness` / `gnomon mode` on the CLI, device-authenticated like `voice approvals disable`; it takes effect within 1 s. **Loosening** is one gesture plus the presence prompt: the kernel raises a `strictness` card and the GUI decides it in the same gesture, and the prompt names the repo or session, the step and the evidence. A looser mode needs only fresh presence and never exceeds the ceiling; a lower level is governed by D88 below. Mode and level changes are never voice-decidable, never `always`, never decided under a cached unlock or an arm (B8), and are logged `approval.mode.set{scope:{repo, hsid?}, from, to, by, presence}` and `approval.strictness.*`.

**Build status (run modes).** Built on the kernel, the CLI and the GUI. Strictness levels are not built, so no repo has a level and the ceiling permits all three modes. `status.runModes` says so (`strictness: {built: false}`), and `FORBIDDEN{strictness-mode}` is reachable today only through the level seam its tests drive. The modes are folded from kernel-signed `approval.mode.set` and `session.open{runMode}` rows, so a repo's mode persists across sessions and kernel restarts. The repo is the session roots' repo key. `request.mode.set` arrives as `log.append` from the GUI's launch-token connection or from the human device (`gnomon mode [manual|ask|auto-edits] [--session <hsid>] [--repo [<abs>]]`); every other class is refused. A tightening writes `approval.mode.set{…, presence:'none'}` at once; tightening a **repo** is the emergency brake: every live session of that repo whose own mode is looser gets its own `approval.mode.set` down to the repo's new mode (presence none), and a pending card that would loosen such a session is withdrawn (Aegis M1). A loosening raises a `strictness` card (`allow | deny`, timeout 2 min into deny) whose first line names the scope and the step; the GUI decides it `allow` in the same gesture, and its effect checks the ceiling again before it writes `{presence:'fresh'}`. A `session.open{runMode}` no looser than the repo's mode is set by the open. A looser one opens the session at the repo's mode and raises the loosening card (`runModeCard` in the result). `manual` denies Bash, PowerShell, Edit, Write, MultiEdit, NotebookEdit, WebFetch and every `mcp__*` tool with no card until a `plan` card in that session is approved; the approval resets each time manual begins, and every applied mode change is recorded on each live session at once, so leaving manual and coming back between two requests still drops the plan (Aegis L2). A session an agent spawns (when agent spawn exists) inherits its repo's mode like any other session; it never inherits its parent session's own looser mode. Agent spawn, automation and unattended sessions do not exist yet, so manual has nothing else to stop. `auto-edits` passes Edit, Write, MultiEdit and NotebookEdit only when the path is absolute with no `..` segment (a relative path is a card, Aegis M4); the session root it lies under is not the user's home, an ancestor of it, or `/` (Aegis M3; a git work tree is not required, so non-git project folders keep auto-edits; loosening to auto-edits for such a folder is refused `FORBIDDEN{strictness, strictness-mode}` at `session.open` and `request.mode.set`); it lies inside the session's roots with **no symlink at any component below the root**, the final one included, each lstat'd (a dangling `scripts/x -> ../.git/hooks/pre-commit` is a card; an lstat failure other than ENOENT is a card; Aegis H1); it is not a gate-class path (a path-level reading of §15.7, case-insensitive: `.claude`, `.gnomon`, `.git`, `.github` and other CI dirs, `.vscode`, `.idea`, `.cursor`, `.devcontainer`, `.yarn`, `.cargo`, `.paradigm`, `.lefthook`, `claude*.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `GEMINI.md`, `.cursorrules`, `.windsurfrules`, `package.json` and lockfiles, `.pnpmfile.cjs`, test config, lint/format configs (`eslint.config.*`, `.eslintrc*`, `prettier.config.*`, `.prettierrc*`), hook runners (`.pre-commit-config.yaml`, lefthook, lint-staged), task and build files (`Makefile`, `justfile`, `Taskfile.yml`, `setup.py`, `setup.cfg`, `pyproject.toml`, `build.rs`, `Cargo.toml`), env and tool loaders (`.envrc`, `mise.toml`, `.tool-versions`), `.mcp.json`, npm rc files; any non-ASCII name and any win32 alias form (8.3 short name, `:` stream) is a card too; Aegis H2); and the card would carry no hidden or bidi flags. `approval.auto.path` is where the write lands (the root's realpath plus the symlink-free rest). The steering/settings write deny follows a dangling symlink to where the write would land and denies a path whose links cannot be followed (a loop, an unreadable component).
