Open (Aegis M2): the repo key is the session roots' hash, not the git top level, so two sessions in different subfolders of one repo hold separate repo modes. Run modes apply to Claude Code sessions; the local lane keeps `ask`. The GUI shows `mode: <mode> ▾` in the transcript header, takes `⇧Tab` in the composer, carries `mode.*` and `mode.repo.*` in the palette, and shows each pass inline as `auto-approved edit · <path>`. `gnomon trace` prints `approval.auto` rows as `AUTO-APPROVED <tool> <path> (run mode auto-edits, no card)`.

**What moves the level (S35-01, S35-03, A35-10).** Only facts the kernel observed or the broker signed:
- `merge.reverted{landedRef, nodeId, detectedBy: content|warpline-unpick, trailer}` — a kernel-merged node's content restored on a watched ref (git or selvage), however the commit is worded, and whether squashed, fixed forward or renamed;
- `merge.ref.rewritten{ref, kind: non-ff|deleted}` on a watched ref;
- `contract.knot{reopenOf}` on a node that already landed;
- a **bad override**: an `extend`, or a KNOT resolution against the reviewer, on a node that later reverts; and `contract.review.capped`;
- post-land `oracle.result` failures on the integration ref;
- `approval.suspect` (except the TTY-ancestry reasons, which are noisy) ⇒ straight to L2;
- `verdict.incident{severity}`, recorded only by the human (`high` ⇒ L2);
- falsifier tripwires 10, 14 and 15 (§20) ⇒ L3.

Override-class signals count at a capped weight, so friction cannot compound into more friction (S35-07). Never an input: any agent's, session's, mod's, voice turn's or peer's assertion ("reverted", "fixed", "all clear"). A peer frame or a peer-tainted session moves the level in neither direction (extends B10). Move 3's `detector.event`, `broke{}` and calibration rows are shown beside the level but not counted until Loid's liveness data says they can be trusted; a raw override rate is shown and never counted. Git writes outside the kernel are visibility only (`status.unobservedRefWrites{repo, n}`) and block loosening for that repo's window.

**Score and hysteresis (S35-06, A35-11, A35-12; P8-D88).** Per repo the broker keeps a decayed score (each signal's weight decays with time constant `tightenWindow`) whose contributions are keyed by `(repo, node lineage)`, so a respawn, a new hsid, a re-id or a node split inherits them (lineage as in §7.3). Crossing a level's threshold raises the level at once, `approval.strictness.raised{repo, from, to, cause: signal|manual|floor|recall, signals{…}, score, configHash}`; a re-trip within `recallWindow` of a loosening jumps straight back to the previous peak. Loosening is considered only after `dwellDays` at the level, over a `loosenWindow` at least 3× `tightenWindow`, and only on **positive evidence**: at least `evidence.cleanLanded` kernel-merged nodes whose bytes are still on the watched ref after `evidence.survivedDays`, with their oracle passes, a score under the release threshold (below the trip threshold), and zero unobserved ref writes in the window. Quiet time is not evidence; unknown, missing or corrupt data holds the level (`approval.strictness.held{repo, level, reason}`, as B5). When the evidence holds, the broker raises one `strictness` card for one level down (`approval.strictness.proposed{repo, from, to, evidence{landed{n, of}, survived{n, of}, passes{n, of}, dwellDays}}`) and the GUI control shows the step as available; it is decided with fresh presence, one level per decision, and nothing loosens on its own. A step below what the evidence supports, or below the floor, takes the root key and is logged `approval.strictness.lowered{repo, from, to, cardId, override: true}`. Every level change ends every voice arm (§14.7).

**Thresholds (S35-05).** Weights, thresholds, windows, the baseline, the concurrency caps and the review cap live only in root-signed `~/.gnomon/config.yaml`; the starting values are provisional (OPEN 20):

```yaml
strictness:
  weights:    { revert: 1.0, refRewrite: 2.0, knotReopen: 0.5, badOverride: 0.5, reviewCap: 0.25, postLandFail: 0.5, overrideCap: 1.0 }
  thresholds: { L1: 2.0, L2: 5.0, release: 0.5 }   # suspect ⇒ L2, incident high ⇒ L2, tripwires 10/14/15 ⇒ L3
  tightenWindow: 14d   loosenWindow: 42d   recallWindow: 30d   dwellDays: 7          # loosenWindow ≥ 3 × tightenWindow, checked at sign time
  evidence:   { cleanLanded: 10, survivedDays: 3 }
  baseline:   L0                                    # evidence never loosens below max(baseline, floor)
  concurrency: { L1: 2, L2: 1 }
  landBatchMinutes: 60
review: { maxRounds: 3, diffOnlyAfterRound: 1, maxExtends: 2 }      # maxRounds 1..5 (§7.3)
```

Every edit is a root-signed act. Raising a threshold or a cap, lowering a weight, or shortening a window is a loosening and is shown as one on the root prompt. A `strictness` or `review` key in `.gnomon/project.yaml` is ignored and logged `config.ignored{field, source:'project.yaml'}`; no env var, flag or CLI value changes them (as the fuse). Tightening without root presence is the one-click level raise, not a config edit.

**Change notes on cards (A35-16..18, S35-11).** A card may carry `note: change-note:v1` (§4.11). The agent's part, `agentClaims{testedHow{summary, refs[]}, risk{tier, why, blast[]}, revert{how, steps, reversible}, draftedBy}`, is untrusted: sanitized under §4.5, stored apart from the kernel's fields, and rendered below the kernel-owned region in a fenced block labelled `agent-claimed`. The kernel's part, `kernel{verified{n, of}, risk{floor, effective, reasons[]}, revert{how: auto|none, ref?}}`, is a three-line strip in the kernel-owned region: what was tested (`2/3 verified`, each uncited claim marked "unverified"), the effective risk with the kernel's floor beside the agent's tier, and the kernel's revert action. `verified` counts only refs that resolve to a kernel-signed `oracle.result`. The floor is `high` for gate-class paths (§15.7), portal-gated symbols, symbols carrying a security aspect, or a diff over the size limit, and `low` for docs-only changes; the effective tier is the higher of the agent's and the floor, so an agent can raise its tier and never lower it. A claim below the kernel's tier sets the flag `claim-mismatch` (voice-ineligible, reason `flagged`); `revert.reversible: false` sets `irreversible` (fresh presence, never voice). The kernel never executes `revert.steps` and never offers them as a control; its own revert action is computed from refs (`git revert` of the landed sha, or the Warpline unpick). The note is inside `contentHash`, so the decision signature covers what was shown. Notes ride `contract.claim{op:'built', note}` into `contract.built` and onto `brief:v1`, where a peer's note carries the `peer` taint; the reviewer model receives agent claims as data.

**Strictness and change-note falsifiers** (both OS): `G-STR-01` agent, session, mod, voice and peer principals cannot append `approval.strictness.*`, `approval.mode.set`, `approval.auto`, `verdict.incident` or the `request.strictness.set`, `request.mode.set` and `request.incident` kinds; a peer frame or a peer-tainted session moves the level in neither direction · `G-STR-02` a revert hidden by squash, fix-forward or rename is counted; a non-fast-forward or deleted watched ref trips · `G-STR-03` a revert fixture crossing L2 refuses a cached `allow` with `strictness-presence` and raises the presence prompt on the same card; a cached decision landing after the trip is flagged `strictness-class`; `gnomon replay` reproduces the level · `G-STR-04` a quiet window with no survival evidence never loosens; unknown data or unobserved ref writes hold the level; no loosening without fresh presence; one level per step; a step below the evidence or the floor only with the root key and `override: true` · `G-STR-05` a `project.yaml` threshold key is ignored and logged; a root-config threshold raise without root presence is refused; a `loosenWindow` under 3× `tightenWindow` is refused at sign time · `G-STR-06` sawtooth — behave for a full window, then re-trip ⇒ the previous peak; signals kept just under a count threshold still trip on the decayed score; a respawn or a node split inherits the score · `G-STR-07` 50 forced reverts: cards per decision stay flat, concurrency drops, override weight stays capped · `G-CHG-01` `risk: low` on a gate-class diff ⇒ `claim-mismatch`, with class, voice eligibility and strictness weight unchanged · `G-CHG-02` hidden or bidi characters in a claim field are handled and flagged as in §4.5 · `G-CHG-03` a `testedHow` without `oracle.result` ids renders "unverified", and `verified` counts only kernel-signed results · `G-CHG-04` revert text is never executed or offered as a control; `reversible: false` ⇒ `irreversible`, fresh presence, voice refused · `G-CHG-05` from Move 2, `built` without a note at an effective tier of medium or above, or at L1 or above, is refused `BAD_REQUEST` with reason `note-missing`.

**The fuse is a meter (P8-D92, "track everything, enforce nothing").** It enforces only law 7 (below); every other `permit.reserve` is granted with a kernel-signed `permit:v1`, for attribution, never for rationing. It tracks, per UTC day: tokens and notional USD per user (an agent's spend counts in the bucket of the human it runs for, P8-D91), repo/project, loop, session, model and backend (`claude-code` vs `local`), and local-LLM sessions with their model and project(s) (D22's compute is recorded, not capped); the subscription's rolling usage window (D35) from `turn.usage`/`turn.result` and the adapter's rate-limit headers/`quota.error`, measured and shown in `status` (OPEN 6), never reserved; quota errors as recorded events, never a pause. Ladder user → repo → loop → session, rungs 50/75/90/100 % of reference amounts announced once per scope per day as informational `budget.ladder` rows; nothing denies, pauses or raises a card on them. Day windows computed from the chain's last event time (S-L2). Usage enters only from stream events; a malformed one is logged and dropped. The reference amounts and the window's display shape are tunable only in root-signed `~/.gnomon/config.yaml` (with a version high-water mark; a bad config falls back to the defaults, logged); **no env var, project file or CLI flag changes them or law 7**; `GNOMON_HOME` selects only which home is read, as a test seam that the daemon never sees (§2.4); it names no fuse value, and the fuse reads only that home's root-signed config (S16-6, amended in v3.8).

**Law 7 inside the broker (D35, S22-H2/H3).** `permit.reserve{kind:'session.spawn'|'turn'}` for `backend:'claude-code'` is refused `BILLING_REFUSED` when the scan finds an API-billing path in env, any settings layer, `mcp.json` env blocks, or any non-default `*_BASE_URL`; the scan list is versioned per `claude` version (`G-SUB-01` fixtures) and covers managed sources: macOS `/Library/Application Support/ClaudeCode/managed-settings.json` + managed preferences; Windows `C:\Program Files\ClaudeCode\managed-settings.json`, `managed-settings.d/*.json`, HKLM/HKCU policy keys (`C:\ProgramData\ClaudeCode` retired at v2.1.75); Linux `/etc/claude-code/`. A `gnomon-local` endpoint outside loopback/RFC1918 ⇒ `BILLING_REFUSED` (pi-ai's cloud providers are compiled out of `gnomon-local`'s compat allowlist, §13.1). Egress grants for mods/MCP servers refuse Anthropic API hosts. The only lawful API path is the `billing api-token` card: human-only, root-signed decision, **per use** (one session or one named automation run), carrying the reason, never a default, never a fallback target, never agent-requestable; the token is held in the keystore and injected into exactly one child's env; while active the GUI's Shuttle view shows a persistent `API CREDITS ACTIVE` indicator and `billing.api.active{hsid, until}` is logged every turn; expiry logs `billing.api.ended`. Residual stated: an out-of-kernel `claude` or a repo `.env` is visibility only (`status.unknownClaudeProcesses`).

**Honest fuse coverage.** Metered: kernel-spawned sessions (Claude + local), interactive Claude Code sessions registered by hooks (usage only). Not metered: a `claude -p` an agent runs from Bash outside the kernel (advisory policy deny; `status.unknownClaudeProcesses{n}` from `ps`/`tasklist` makes it visible). Task fan-out inside a turn is caught by per-message usage.

**Invariants.** B1 Nothing spends by default. B2 ULID permit ids. B3 Only the broker appends `approval.*`/`budget.*`/`permit.*`/`billing.*`, kernel-signed. B4 Agent/session/mod kinds never decide cards or Warpline overrides (`protocol.ts:148-177`); the voice principal never decides — it submits an intent the broker signs under a human-opened arm (§14.7). B5 Corrupt state ⇒ deny. B6 An unattended card never expires into `allow`; `tool.permission` cards expire into `deny` (`agents.ts:721`); `fallback` and `api-token` cards never expire — they wait; a `held` card's timeout is suspended until its hold ends. B7 No decision is ever signed by a key on disk. B8 No standing rule (`always`, `install`, `grant.*`) is ever created under a cached unlock, nor under a voice arm. B9 A voice decision is committed only after its hold, only by the broker under domain `gnomon:approve-voice:v1`, and only if the arm, class, repo, target, echo and speaker checks of §14.7 pass and — for gate and judgment cards — its handle and challenge match live, unused values bound to the card's current `contentHash` and a kernel readback of that card was logged (`G-BRK-06`). B10 A peer never decides a card, never reserves or draws a permit and never raises a presence prompt; a peer's frame can at most queue a `handoff` card, and peer-caused cards are rate-limited per peer (§3.9). B11 Only the broker appends `approval.strictness.*`, `approval.mode.set` and `approval.auto`, kernel-signed; no agent, session, mod, voice or peer assertion is ever a strictness input (CB1). B12 No principal lowers a level faster than the evidence schedule allows; root presence can, and is logged as an override (CB2). B13 Absence of signals never loosens; unknown data holds the level (CB3). B14 A trip never increases the number of cards per decision: it removes autonomy (concurrency, auto-landing, cached class, voice), and the cards of a tightened repo are batched in the decision stack (CB4). B15 Effective voice classes = grant ∩ narrowings ∩ the level's ceiling, and loosening never re-arms (CB5). B16 Agent claims never change a decision's class, voice eligibility or strictness weight (CB6).

**Failure modes.** Broker unreachable ⇒ kernel-spawned sessions deny (`headless.py:54-58`); 7.x interactive sessions unaffected.

### 3.5 `#scheduler`

Log-derived timers only (`timer:v1`), recomputed on start (flow `timers` cache, `sqlite.py:21-22`); one daemon ⇒ one timer per key; a timer fires an event; whatever spends needs a permit; loop policies are userland over `loop:v1`; `loop.paused` is kernel-written (A17-3); claim expiry timers (§7.4) are scheduler timers. Automations (constellation/flow routines) register `automation:v1 {id, trigger, model: local|claude|any, attended:false}`; the scheduler fires `automation.due` and the runner routes per §13.3.

### 3.6 `#manifest-loader`

Validates `manifest:v1` (§4.3) including the mod ABI fields and `packageHash` over the whole mod tree (S22-H6); accepts `kind: adapter` and `kind: provider` only from the first-party trust tier (A23-2, S25-H4); refuses a mod whose `name` collides with a kernel namespace token; checks capabilities/egress against signed grants (`registry.py:9-24` refusals; egress grants naming Anthropic API hosts refused; a manifest with both `provides.knowledge` and an egress list is refused); generates `plugin.json`, `.mcp.json`, `hooks.json`, tool-family listing (`families.ts:28-53` tiers, `:55-92` table), per-session policy; hot-reloads mods by `packageHash` from pinned snapshots only; accepts linked-folder sources (`gnomon mod link`, §12.5) as content-addressed snapshots, refusing symlinks, junctions, hardlinks, `.node` addons and paths escaping the folder at copy time (`manifest.refuse{code:'SNAPSHOT'}`), requiring a lockfile when `node_modules` is present, and pauses them on any change until re-carded; detects **manifest widening** between two snapshots (any addition to effects, egress, capabilities, event kinds, tools, views, commands, keybindings or knowledge providers) and always raises a card for it; validates `provides.knowledge` (§8.10); `install` card for anything agent-written or linked with pinned hash and one-command rollback to a previous snapshot (`gnomon mod rollback <name> [<packageHash>]`, human-only); no `postinstall` anywhere; generated files carry markers; `gnomon uninstall` removes exactly those (law 4). `generate-hooks.mjs` subsumed (JSON out, scripts stay in `src/commands/hooks/scripts/`).

### 3.7 `#ref-resolver`

Grammar in the kernel (§4.4); resolvers userland and read-only; built-in schemes `ev hsid turn cc gkey dev permit card contract node pev peer` (`turn:<hsid>/<n>`, §4.2) (`peer:<logId>/<ref>` names an object on a paired kernel; it never dereferences a local object, §3.9); `sym wl n s td l t spec doodle` registered by their packages (`doodle` by the Doodle mod, §12.7; the `voice` scheme is dropped, A23-2).

### 3.8 `#mod-host` (D33, S22-C3, A23-3)

Spawns one child per installed mod (`node <entry>` from the mod's pinned snapshot `~/.gnomon/mods/<name>/pinned/<packageHash>/`, with the allowlisted env) under the **`mod` sandbox profile** (default-deny: reads of the kit SDK + its own pinned snapshot + `~/.gnomon/mods/<name>/state/` + the read paths its `knowledge:provide` grant lists, writes only `~/.gnomon/mods/<name>/state/` and `blobs/`, network only to the kernel's loopback egress proxy for hosts in the mod's grant, no process exec), speaks `mod-abi:v1` over stdio JSON-RPC (§12.3), enforces declared effects at the RPC boundary AND by profile (a `tool.call` whose declared effects are `read` cannot open a write verb; `network` cannot be used without an egress grant; `spend` is drawn from the calling session's permit, never a mod's own; a dev-mode mod is capped at `read`), supervises health (`mod.health`), restarts on crash (cap 3/10 min), stops all children at idle exit (`mod.stopped`), forwards `view.render` results to clients as `display-block:v1` built from the closed kit through the block sanitizer (§4.5), fills Tier 0 persona templates from kernel fields itself (§12.6), serves the two client relays `mod.view`/`mod.command` (§12.3) and stamps `relayedFor{principal, connId}` on every relayed input so a mod can never assert who acted, and watches every linked mod folder — any change pauses that child and raises the re-approve card; the running snapshot is immutable, so a change in the folder can never reach a running child (§12.5). Mods never receive the socket credential; the host proxies `kernel.call` on their behalf under the mod's own principal `p:<ns>/mod-<name>` with the **caller's hsid attached and scopes intersected** (confused-deputy guard, A23-3). Mod tools reach agents through the **`gnomon-mods` MCP bridge**, a userland child the host spawns per session under the `session` profile (`packages/gnomon-mod-sdk`, principal `p:<ns>/mods-mcp`, bound by the `paradigm` distro's grant to `inject.receipt`): the bridge talks only to the host over stdio, serves the session's `mcp.json` entry, and logs `inject.receipt{hsid, turn, requester, owner, refs[], shas[]}` for every tool result it hands a model — so the kernel never writes a userland kind (L7) and every model-visible mod byte is logged (L2).

### 3.9 `#peer-link` — kernel linking (`$kernel-pairing`, `$handoff-brief`, `^peer-paired`, P8-R9, P8-D73–D76)

`#peer-link` links this kernel to paired kernels — the founder's other machines (relationship `self`) and teammates' kernels (`teammate`) — for two things only: **handoff briefs** and **co-work messages** (P8-D73, D75). It is a peer protocol, not a wider endpoint: a peer is never a `gnomon-rpc:v1` client, calls none of the 12 verbs or 2 relays, reads no log and decides nothing (S32-01; the peer column of §5's matrix). Other humans exist on this kernel only as peers behind a link (D75). There is no takeover: no custody, seal, epoch, transcript transplant or permit transfer (D73).

**Architecture (D74, A31-04/05, S32-09, P8-D90).** No kernel listens beyond loopback (§11.2 rule 1). The kernel spawns the transport child `gnomon-link` from its own package under the `link` profile (§15.5): a connected TCP socket the kernel dialed to a relay address in root-signed config and handed over (fd passing), plus stdio and an IPC channel to the kernel, nothing else — no listen, no network connection of its own, no keys, no home, no exec. It moves length-prefixed ciphertext and never sees plaintext; the kernel runs every handshake and parses every plaintext frame, so keys and plaintext never sit in the network-facing process. Reachability is **`gnomon-relay`** (`packages/gnomon-relay`, zero dependencies), a blind relay the founder runs on an always-on machine (the home server), listening on a LAN address configured on that host only. It forwards opaque frames between members and keeps a size-capped store-and-forward queue (1 GiB, 7-day expiry) so a brief reaches a sleeping machine. It admits a connection only after a Noise `IK` handshake against its own static key (pinned in every invite) from a link key named in a root-signed `grant.peer` row that a member kernel submitted (the relay owner's root is the first member, pinned at relay setup); a pairing handshake is admitted once on the invite's single-use ticket. The relay is **untrusted** for confidentiality and integrity: it sees link-key hashes, sizes and timing, never content (S32 §3). LAN only until an overlay or the nevr.land relay (the same binary) exists; no direct listener, hole punching, WebRTC, QUIC or mDNS (A31-05). A link connection never holds the daemon open (§2.4).

**Cryptography (A31-06, S32-09).** End to end, kernel to kernel, inside the relay connection: `Noise_IK_25519_ChaChaPoly_SHA256` for live sessions (mutual authentication against pinned static keys, forward secrecy, per-direction counters); `Noise_XXpsk3_25519_ChaChaPoly_SHA256` for pairing; `Noise_X_25519_ChaChaPoly_SHA256` to seal a store-and-forward item to the receiver's static key (sender authenticated; no forward secrecy against later theft of the receiver's link key, §A.5). Prologue `gnomon-link:v1` ‖ `linkApiId`. Everything is built from `node:crypto` (`x25519`, `chacha20-poly1305`, `sha256`, HMAC-based HKDF), so the kernel keeps zero dependencies (law 6), and it must pass the published Noise test vectors (`G-LNK-13`, probe P13). A key mismatch, a failed tag or an out-of-order counter drops the connection before any plaintext frame is parsed; there is no unauthenticated fallback.

**Kernel-key binding (TD-2026-10-19-648 item 12; built in M2.8).** The Noise static key proves possession of the link key only. So that a live link is also bound to the paired kernel, each side adds, inside the encrypted handshake payload, a signature by its kernel key over the Noise handshake hash. The receiver verifies it only against the `peerKernelKeyId` pinned for that peer in `peers.jsonl` (and the link key against the pinned `peerLinkKeyId`), exactly: a key id the peer presents, a registry row it carries or a newer row it claims is never substituted for the pin. A missing, malformed or non-verifying signature drops the connection before any plaintext frame is parsed, the same as a failed tag. A peer whose kernel key changed is therefore refused until it is paired again, and re-pairing takes the whole ceremony.The static key is the link key (§3.1); assertions that cross a link (a brief's manifest) are signed by the kernel key.

**Pairing (`$kernel-pairing`, `^peer-paired`; A31-08, S32-04, S32-10).**
1. **Invite.** On kernel A the human runs `gnomon link pair --as self|teammate --scope brief.send,brief.receive,cowork` (root-signed, human-only, refused from a kernel-spawned shell with no staging form, §3.1 I3). It mints a single-use `invite:v1 {rootKeyId, rootPub, kernelKeyId, linkPub, relay:{addr, staticPub, ticket}, psk (128 random bits), as, scopes, expiresAt (10 min)}`, printed as text and QR and handed to B out of band.
2. **Handshake.** `gnomon link pair --accept <invite>` on B runs `XXpsk3` with A through the relay. Each side sends its root-signed `kernel` and `link` registry rows inside the encrypted payload and verifies the other's against the peer's root public key. Identity is the full `rootKeyId`; a ground `ns` collision changes nothing (S32-02).
3. **Confirm.** Each kernel raises a `peer-pair` card: a 6-word SAS (2048-word list, 66 bits) derived from the handshake hash and both sides' `rootKeyId` and link keys, the relationship, the scopes, the expiry, and the peer's self-asserted name marked *unverified*. The human compares the words on both screens and decides with fresh presence and the root key, on **both** sides. A mismatch is `reject`; nothing is written.
4. **Grant.** Each side appends a root-signed `grant.peer{peerRootKeyId, peerKernelKeyId, peerLinkKeyId, peerNs, relationship: self|teammate, scopes: [brief.send|brief.receive|cowork], maxAudience, relay, until}` — `maxAudience` is `private` for `self` and `team` or `org` for a teammate; `until` defaults to 30 days and is renewed by `gnomon link renew` with root presence — pins the peer's public material in `peers.jsonl` and logs `link.paired`. A kernel whose `linkKeyCustody` is `partial` may hold `self` grants only (S32 §5).

**Revocation and the gate.** `request.link.revoke{peer}` or `gnomon link revoke <peer>` (device-authenticated, no presence: it only narrows) appends the kernel-signed `link.revoked{peerRootKeyId, by, connId}`, drops the connection, purges the outbox to that peer, and sends `revoke` to the peer and the relay best effort; expiry acts the same. The effective peer set is the latest `grant.peer` per peer minus later revocations and expiries; re-pairing takes the whole ceremony. `^peer-paired` is the gate: a frame other than `noise` is parsed only on a link whose static key is pinned under a live `grant.peer`, and only within that grant's scopes.

**Protocol (`gnomon-link:v1`, A31-15, S32-01).** Noise transport messages of ≤ 4 MiB; each plaintext is one JSON frame from a closed set of 10, hashed with its schemas into `linkApiId` (frozen per release like `modAbiId`, reported by `status`, never part of `clientApiId`):

| Frame | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `noise` | handshake messages (`IK`, `XXpsk3`); the only frame parsed before authentication |
| `caps` | `{linkApis[], features[], maxFrame}`; the highest shared version wins; none ⇒ `bye` |
| `msg` | one co-work message (`cowork.msg:v1`) |
| `brief.offer` | a brief's signed manifest (`brief:v1`) |
| `brief.chunk` | `{briefId, n, of, sha256, bytes}`, 1 MiB, resumable by hash |
| `brief.decline` | `{briefId, reason: rejected \| expired \| limit \| refused}` from the receiver |
| `ack` | `{ref: msgId \| briefId, state: received \| accepted \| read}` |
| `ping` | liveness every 30 s; two misses close |
| `bye` | orderly close with a reason |
| `revoke` | the sender revoked this link; the receiver logs `link.revoked{by:'peer'}` and stops |

An unknown or malformed frame, a frame outside the grant's scopes, or any frame on an unpaired link ⇒ `link.frame.refused{frame, reason}` and the connection closes. A peer has no request it can make of this kernel: no RPC, no query, no custody frame.

**Handoff brief (`$handoff-brief`, P8-D73).**
```
brief:v1 { briefId (ULID), from:{rootKeyId, kernelKeyId, ns, hsid?, repo?}, to:{peerRootKeyId}, title, instructions (≤ 64 KiB UTF-8),
           attachments:[ {kind: document, name, sha256, bytes, mediaType, audience, sensitivity}
                       | {kind: ref, ref, note?}                                       // sym: td: l: t: n: wl: doodle: …, carried as text
                       | {kind: git, repoId, ref, commit, base?, bundle?:{sha256, bytes}} ],   // a bundle only of commits the receiver lacks
           createdAt, expiresAt (default 7 d), manifestHash }   — signed by the source kernel key, domain gnomon:brief:v1
```
1. **Draft.** The human writes a brief (GUI "Hand off…", `gnomon brief new`), or a session drafts one with the `#cowork-mod` tool `brief.draft`; a draft is staged (`link.brief.drafted{briefId, by}`) and an agent never sends one.
2. **Send.** The human sends it with `request.brief.send{briefId, peer}` (first-party human-class connection, §5) or `gnomon brief send`. The kernel composes the manifest, applies the egress rules and the secret scan below to every item, and raises one outbound card listing each item with its labels and scan result — `handoff{direction:'out'}` to a `self` peer, `declassify` to a teammate — decided with fresh presence. On `allow` it signs `brief:v1`, logs `link.brief.sent{briefId, to, manifestHash}`, and sends `brief.offer` and the `brief.chunk`s live, or sealed to the relay's queue. **The source session is unaffected**: nothing is stopped, sealed or transferred.
3. **Receive.** The receiver checks the Noise sender against the pinned link key, the kernel signature, `to` = itself, `expiresAt`, the limits, and that `briefId` was never consumed (replay, S32-03); stores the brief under `~/.gnomon/link/inbox/<briefId>/` (denied to sessions); logs `link.brief.received`; and raises one `handoff{direction:'in'}` card flagged `peer` showing the peer's name (unverified), `rootKeyId8` and relationship, the instructions in the sanitized kernel region, every attachment with its labels, and the git refs. Nothing else happens until a human decides.
4. **Accept.** `accept` with fresh presence, then either a **new session** — `session.open{…, brief}` with the receiver's own backend and model choice, under its own permit, fuse and law 7 — or **continue**: `session.send{hsid, content:[{brief}]}` to a session the human picks. The kernel adds the brief through a fixed `peer-envelope:v1` (a provenance header naming peer, relationship and `briefId`, with the instructions fenced as untrusted data — a versioned template like the adapters' system envelope, not a composed prompt) and logs `session.inject{origin:'peer', peerRootKeyId, briefId, hash, bytes}` before the child reads it (L2); the session's `taint` becomes `peer` or `peer-teammate`. Documents are materialized read-only in the session's worktree under `.gnomon/briefs/<briefId>/` (ignored by git). A git attachment is fetched by `commit` from a shared remote, or imported from its bundle into the quarantine ref `refs/gnomon/peer/<peerNs>-<rootKeyId8>/<briefId>/<name>` under the hardened git of §3.3 — never into a checked-out branch — and a new session may start its worktree from it. `reject` sends `brief.decline`. Either way the source gets an `ack` and logs `link.brief.accepted|declined`.
5. **Expire.** An undecided brief expires at `expiresAt` (`link.brief.expired`) and its blobs are pruned.

**On one kernel** a brief needs no link (D73: "another session, on another kernel or the same one"): the human takes a draft straight into a new or existing session with the same `brief` param or content part; it is a client-origin inject (`session.inject{origin:'client'}`), with no egress card, no taint and no `peer` flag, because no peer wrote it — an agent-drafted brief still reaches another session only by that human act.

Refs to the source kernel's objects (`ev:`, `hsid:`, `turn:`, `card:`) are rewritten at receipt to `peer:<sourceLogId>/<ref>`; `ref.resolve` returns the copy the brief carried, else `{target: null, peer, unresolved: true}` — never a local object (S32-13). Turn ids never cross. **Warpline (D76):** a `wl:` ref travels as text and resolves only against the receiver's own Warpline; Warpline objects, tokens and fabric bytes never travel (`UNSUPPORTED`, reason `warpline-sync-absent`) until T-2026-10-14-003's bundle sync.

**Co-work (`#cowork-mod`, A31-14, P8-D75).** A first-party mod on `mod-abi:v1` exposing two tools through the per-session bridge: `cowork.send{to:{peer, hsid | topic}, kind: note|request|result|ref, text ≤ 16 KiB, refs[], attachments?}` and `cowork.inbox{since?}`. Messages are `cowork.msg:v1 {msgId (ULID), threadId, inReplyTo?, from:{rootKeyId, hsid}, to:{hsid | topic}, kind, text, refs[], attachments[], audience, sensitivity}`.
- **Out.** A session's messages leave only after the human allowed co-work for that session and peer: `request.cowork.allow{hsid, peer}` raises a fresh-presence card (`handoff{direction:'out'}` for `self`, `declassify` for a teammate) naming the session, the peer and the labels, valid for the session's life. The mod appends `modns.cowork.out{msgId, to, …}`; the kernel carries it under the first-party-only capability `link:send` after the egress filter and the secret scan. A scan hit holds the message as a card; an attachment always takes its own card. The mod never holds the link or a key.
- **In.** The kernel logs `link.msg.in{msgId, from, to, hash, bytes, audience, sensitivity}`, stores the body as a blob and passes it to the mod's event feed. The inbox is **pull only**: a message reaches a model only when that model calls `cowork.inbox`, inside a turn its own permit already pays for. The bridge wraps each message in `peer-envelope:v1` and logs `inject.receipt{requester, owner: p:<peerNs>/…@<rootKeyId8>, refs:[peer:…], shas[]}` (L2, L7); the reading session is tainted. A peer message never starts a turn, spends, or raises a card by itself; a human may forward one as a `session.send` (client origin).
- Delivery is at-least-once, deduplicated by `msgId`, ordered per (from, to); the outbox keeps 24 h or 1 000 messages. Same-repo co-work that writes `.paradigm/` on two machines waits for T-2026-10-14-001(d) (OPEN 17).

**Egress (L6; S32-07, A31-16).** One filter in the kernel, applied to every item before the Noise seal, reading only labels set at append (§9.1):

| Relationship | May cross | Fresh-presence card |
|---|---|---|
| every link | never `secret`; never an item of the never-cross list | — |
| `self` | audience `private`, `team` or `org` at `normal` or `sensitive` | every brief (`handoff` out); a session's co-work (`request.cowork.allow`) |
| `teammate` | audience `team` or `org` at `normal` only | every brief and every attachment (`declassify`); a session's co-work (`request.cowork.allow`) |

L6 forbids relaxing a label in place, so `declassify` makes a **projection**: a new blob labelled `team`/`normal`, redactions recorded in `link.declassified{from, to, redactions[]}`, the original untouched. The secret scan runs the Doodle pattern list (§12.7) over every text, document and bundled file; it is best effort, and a hit blocks the item until the human redacts or removes it. `status`, session lists, card stacks and presence are never shared; a peer sees no metadata beyond what the relay already sees.

**What never crosses (S32 §2, A31-12).** `approve` in every form and every decision; root-signed acts, grants and grant rows; every key and token (root, approvals, kernel, link, device, agent and session keys, the api-token, Warpline tokens, `~/.claude` credentials); permits, budget and fuse state; cached unlocks, voice arms, handles and challenges; presence prompts; `always` rules; `secret` events and blobs; `log.read`, `status`, session lists and card stacks; `mod.view`, `mod.command` and knowledge queries; the chain head anchor; Warpline state (D76); and a session itself — a transcript travels only as a labelled document a human attached.

**Limits (S32-11).** Pre-auth: ≤ 10 handshakes per minute per source at the relay and per peer key at the kernel; ≤ 32 connections per relay member; a flood never evicts an authenticated link. Per link: 60 messages per minute per session pair, 600 per hour per peer, 256 MiB per day, 6 brief offers per hour, 10 peer-caused cards per hour; frames ≤ 4 MiB; a brief ≤ 64 KiB of instructions, ≤ 32 attachments, ≤ 256 MiB. A limit pauses the link (`link.paused{reason}`) and raises a card; nothing is auto-decided.

**Honest scope.** A paired peer is a trust relationship, not a sandbox. A teammate can lie, and injection carried across kernels (brief instructions, co-work text, attachments a peer session was itself injected with) rests on the reading model (threat 4): provenance envelopes, receipts, taint and the `peer` flag make it visible and keep it away from voice, `always` and cached teammate approvals; they do not prevent it. Data sent belongs to the recipient; revocation is not retroactive, and the receiver's redaction is the receiver's kernel's honesty. The SAS rests on the human comparing it. The relay sees who talks to whom, when and how much. Store-and-forward items lack forward secrecy against later theft of the receiver's link key. Residuals and the revisit trigger: §A.5.

**Falsifiers** (both OS unless stated): `G-LNK-01` no kernel binds a non-loopback address with linking on; `gnomon-relay` on the relay host is the only off-loopback listener · `G-LNK-02` handshake — an unpaired key, a swapped key, a peer whose root differs from the pinned one (including a root ground to the same `ns`) and a key retired by rotation all fail before any plaintext frame is parsed; a foreign principal never matches a local principal · `G-LNK-03` pairing — no `grant.peer` without fresh root presence on both sides; `gnomon link pair` from a kernel-spawned shell is refused and leaves no card; session text asking to pair produces no card; a MITM'd handshake shows different SAS words and writes nothing; an expired or reused invite is refused · `G-LNK-04` no ABI for peers — every verb and both relays are refused for a peer principal; a frame outside the closed set, outside the grant's scopes or on an unpaired link closes the connection; a decision carrying `via: link` is refused · `G-LNK-05` briefs — a replayed, expired or misaddressed brief is refused; a brief is accepted at most once; nothing is spawned, injected or spent before the fresh-presence `accept`; sending leaves the source session's state, permit and transcript unchanged · `G-LNK-06` ingress — every peer byte a model sees has a prior `session.inject{origin:'peer'}` (brief) or `inject.receipt` (co-work); the session is tainted; its cards carry `peer`, are voice-ineligible and refuse `always`; a `peer-teammate` `tool.permission` allow is refused under a cached unlock · `G-LNK-07` no peer-caused spend or prompt — a peer cannot cause a permit grant, a spawn, a turn, a presence prompt, or an `api-token`, `fallback` or `budget` card; inbound co-work is pull only · `G-LNK-08` egress — a fixture with `secret`, `private` and `sensitive` items addressed to a teammate, `secret` items to self, and every never-cross item yields zero bytes on the wire; seeded secrets in an attachment block its card until redacted or removed; a declassified item is a new projection and the original's labels are unchanged · `G-LNK-09` revocation and expiry — the next frame is refused within 1 s, the outbox to that peer is purged, and a connected peer receives `revoke` · `G-LNK-10` relay — tampered, reordered, replayed or dropped frames are detected (tags, counters, acks) and drop the link; the relay's queue holds ciphertext only; a relay with a different static key is refused · `G-LNK-11` the `link` profile cannot read keys, home or the link store, exec, listen, or open any network connection; it uses only the socket the kernel handed it · `G-LNK-12` a limit pauses the link with a card, nothing is auto-decided, and a pre-auth flood never evicts an authenticated link · `G-LNK-13` the Noise implementation over `node:crypto` passes the published vectors for `IK`, `XXpsk3` and `X` · `G-LNK-14` Warpline — no Warpline object, token or fabric byte crosses, and a `wl:` ref in a brief resolves only locally · `T-LNK-01` briefs sent, accepted, rejected and expired per week, and co-work messages sent and read per week, each `{n, of}` (§20).

---

## 4. Hard contracts (versioned; additive or a version bump)

### 4.1 Event envelope (`event:v1`)

```jsonc
{ "schemaVersion": "event:v1", "id": "ev:01J9Z6…",
  "log": "log:01J9…",                          // genesis ULID logId (Q2)
  "seq": 48213, "at": "…",
  "principal": "p:a1b2c3d4/ascend", "keyId": "gkey:v1:…", "deviceId": "dev:a1b2c3d4/macbook",   // server-stamped; principal.kind ∈ human|agent|session|kernel|mod|voice|peer (peer: link events only, §3.9)
  "connId": "conn:…", "idem": "sha256:…",
  "session": "hsid:v1:…",
  "scope": { "org": null, "team": null,        // reserved (Q4)
             "repo": "repo:<sha256(project.yaml.repoId)[0..16]>",   // repoId committed at `gnomon init`; else the root-commit sha (A23-1)
             "loop": "loop:…", "contract": "contract:v1:…" },
  "audience": "private", "sensitivity": "normal",   // Q6
  "kind": "session.spawned", "causes": ["ev:…", "wl:pick/pick:v3:…"],
  "payload": { … }, "ref": "wl:pick/…", "refHash": "sha256:…",
  "prev": "sha256:…", "hash": "sha256:…", "sig": "…" }
```

### 4.2 hsid + aliases

`hsid:v1:<ULID>` kernel-issued; `hsid:v1d:<base32(sha256("gnomon:hsid:v1d\n" + principalId + "\n" + "cc:" + uuid))[0..26]>` derived for interactive Claude Code sessions, keyed on the **namespaced** principalId (Q7), accepted only from that principal's device key, first registration wins (`session.alias.rejected` on squat). Hooks read `session_id` from stdin JSON (fallback `$CLAUDE_SESSION_ID`; probe in M1.0, P8-D18). Aliases `cc: wl:session const: flow:` (the `voice:` alias went with the dropped scheme, A23-2). **`hsid-short`** (branch `gnomon/<hsid-short>`, worktree paths, session principal names) = lowercase base32 of the ULID's 80 random bits (16 characters), never its time bits, checked for uniqueness against live and past session branches and worktrees at spawn (A31-10). **Turn ids** `turn:<hsid>/<n>`: `n` from 1, monotonic per hsid, kernel-local; another kernel names them only as `peer:<logId>/turn:…` (§3.9). **Ids that cross a link** (S32-13): every id is a ULID (`connId`, `cardId`, `permitId`, `armId`, `briefId`, `msgId`) or log-qualified; a permit names its issuing `logId` (§4.8) and never verifies on another log. **Id prefixes and encodings (PR-3):** `card:`, `conn:`, `arm:`, `brief:`, `msg:` and `ds:` + ULID; `permit:v1:<ULID>`; `ns` and `hsid-short` are **lowercase** Crockford base32; `hsid:v1d` is **uppercase** Crockford base32; `rootKeyId8` (the display suffix of a foreign principal, §3.1) = the first 8 hex characters of the root key's digest (the sha256 in its keyId).

### 4.3 Manifest (`manifest:v1`, extended per 21 §5 / D33, S22-H5/H6, A23-2)

```yaml
manifest: 1
kind: distro | mod | provider | adapter | skill-pack     # adapter and provider: accepted from the first-party trust tier only (A23-2, S25-H4); provider = a manifest that ships only provides.knowledge and runs as a library provider inside the knowledge mod (§8.10)
name: diff-lens                  version: 1.2.0
abi: "mod-abi:v1"                # required for kind: mod; the kernel refuses an unknown abi
compat: { kernel: ">=8.0.0 <9", claudeCode: ">=2.1.288" }
provides:
  mcp: [...]   skills: [...]   agents: [...]   hooks: [{ event: <string>, command }]   bands: [...]   steps: [...]   resolvers: [...]   blocks: [...]   oracles: [...]
  tools:   [{ name: lens.diff, schema: {...}, effects: [read] }]                       # effects ⊆ read | write | network | spend ; enforced by #mod-host
  views:   [{ id: diff.pane, slot: panel, placement: append, fixtures: [fixtures/diff-pane.json] }]
           # slots: sidebar | panel | status.mod | composer.footer | tool.view:<tool> | card.detail:<kind>
           # placement: append | replace for sidebar/panel/composer.footer/tool.view; card.* and status.mod accept append ONLY (never replace, S22-H5)
  events:  [modns.diff-lens.*]                                                         # mod event kinds live under modns.<name>.* (A23-1)
  commands: [{ id: lens.toggle, title: "Toggle diff lens", args: {...} }]
  keybindings: [{ key: "ctrl+shift+d", command: lens.toggle }]                           # bind to command ids only, never to code; reserved decision keys refused (§12.2)
  knowledge: [{ name: diff-lens, scheme: "lens:", query: lens.search, maxTier: T3 }]    # knowledge provider (§8.10); needs capability knowledge:provide; egress must be none
requires: { capabilities: [...], egress: none | [{host, purpose}] }                     # Anthropic API hosts are refused in egress; capabilities may name turn.text:attached-session (§15.4)
config_schema: {...}
```
No `trust:` field (grant table only); no `source:` field either — package vs linked folder is an install-time fact recorded on the `grant.install` row (§12.5). `manifestHash = sha256(canonical(manifest))`; `packageHash = sha256(sorted file tree of the whole mod package)` sits beside it and pins installs, hot reloads and `grant.install` rows (S22-H6).

### 4.4 Ref grammar (`ref:v1`)

`ref = scheme ":" id ["@" rev]`; registered schemes `ev hsid turn cc gkey dev permit card contract node pev peer spec sym wl n s td l t knot doodle` (`doodle` per §12.7; `peer:<logId>/<ref>` per §3.9, resolved only to a copy a brief carried; `turn` is a kernel built-in for the turn ids of §4.2, so §3.9's `peer:<logId>/turn:…` rewrite wraps a registered scheme); aliases `# $ ^ ! ~ → sym:`, `TD- → td:`, `L- → l:`, `T- → t:`. The `voice` scheme is dropped (A23-2).

### 4.5 display-block (`display-block:v1`), the closed kit, and the sanitizer

```jsonc
{ "schemaVersion": "display-block:v1",
  "block": "kit.Box" | "kit.Text" | "kit.List" | "kit.Table" | "kit.Markdown" | "kit.Diff" | "kit.Progress" | "kit.Button" | "kit.Image" | "kit.Canvas" | "inbox.card" | "decision.stack" | "session.live" | "contract.progress" | "budget.meter" | "knot.split" | "ask-user" | "plan" | "voice.turn",
  "data": { … }, "children": [ … ], "refs": [...], "causes": [...], "at": "…" }
```
Kit `Box Text List Table Markdown Diff Progress Button Image Canvas` (published as `kit:v1`, hashed as `kitId`, shared by `modAbiId` and `clientApiId`, §12.8); `kit.Button` carries only `{command}` (a command id from a manifest); `kit.Image` requires `alt`; `kit.Canvas{ds, readOnly?}` names a doodle session and is rendered only by a host-owned surface (§12.2); a block carries **no actions**. Decision controls exist only on broker-authored `card:v1.actions[]` signed by the kernel key (S-M4). Agent-authored text is data (`descriptors.ts:73-74`). **Sanitizer** (S22-H5, S25-H6; exact sets amended M1.12, Aegis round 2): every string in a block, card or transcript line is sanitized before rendering or logging, in this order, with **no Unicode normalisation** (no NFC/NFKC): lone surrogates become U+FFFD; **terminated** terminal escape sequences are neutralised — a string sequence, OSC (including OSC 52 clipboard and OSC 8 hyperlinks), DCS, SOS, PM and APC in 7-bit or 8-bit form, terminated by BEL, ESC \ or 8-bit ST with no 8-bit string introducer in the body (an in-body introducer ends the outer candidate, so the scan is linear), is rendered with its introducer and terminator as visible glyphs and its payload **visible** between them (`⟨OSC⟩payload⟨ST⟩`, `⟨DCS⟩…`, `⟨SOS⟩…`, `⟨PM⟩…`, `⟨APC⟩…`; the payload passes the remaining rules), so requester text can never hide inside one (amended M1.12 slice 2, Aegis M2); CSI and every other ESC sequence carry no text and are removed whole — while an ESC that starts no terminated sequence is shown as the visible glyph `⟨ESC⟩` and its tail stays visible (never swallowed to the end of the text); **Unicode** handling: tag characters (U+E0000–U+E007F), the variation selectors supplement (U+E0100–U+E01EF), zero-width and invisible characters (U+200B–U+200D, U+2060–U+2064, U+FEFF, U+00AD, U+180E, U+034F) and the Hangul fillers (U+115F, U+1160, U+3164, U+FFA0) are stripped (invisible to people, read by models); variation selectors U+FE00–U+FE0F are kept after a base that can take one, decided on the output (the last emitted character is a letter, number, symbol or punctuation — never a mark or another VS; VS16 after an emoji base is never stripped; only U+FE0E/U+FE0F are kept without a flag, a kept U+FE00–U+FE0D sets `hidden-chars`, so an unflagged selector carries at most one bit — amended M1.12 slice 2) and stripped at the start of a string, after any other character, or as the second and later of a run; U+2028/U+2029 become `\n`; bidi controls (U+202A–U+202E, U+2066–U+2069) and the marks U+200E, U+200F, U+061C are not stripped but rendered as visible escape glyphs (`⟨RLO⟩`, `⟨LRI⟩`, `⟨LRM⟩`, …) so no text can be reversed on screen (LRM/RLM in Arabic or Hebrew text therefore badge — accepted); every remaining C0/C1 control and DEL is removed except `\n` and `\t`, and CR is removed without a flag. Any other removal, replacement or glyph sets the flag (`hidden-chars`, or `bidi` for the bidi set). `@a-company/gnomon-contracts` `conformance/display.v1.json` is **normative**: a conformant client reproduces its vectors byte for byte; a card body that contained any of them is flagged `card.flags: [hidden-chars|bidi]`, shown as a visible badge on the card and in the presence prompt, and logged on `approval.request` (`G-GUI-11`); in the GUI every string becomes a DOM text node and Markdown renders with raw HTML disabled, so markup inside a string is text, never code (§11.1). **Kernel-owned regions**: card bodies (`inbox.card`, `ask-user`, `plan`, `knot.split`, `budget.meter`), the status indicators (`API CREDITS ACTIVE`, mic state, the voice-arm indicator, budget rung) and the `contentHash` summary line are rendered by the GUI core (and by every conformant client, §12.8) and cannot be replaced or overlaid; mod content in `card.detail:<kind>` is appended below, fenced and labelled `mod:<name>`. The same kit renders in the GUI, in the CLI as a text projection (`kit.Image` → its `alt`, `kit.Canvas` → the mark list), in the optional Claude Code Mod band (mirroring its Pane kit shape, 21), in constellation, Conductor and any conformant community client (§12.8).

### 4.6 refusal (`refusal:v1`, Gnomon superset)

Shape and semantics lifted from `packages/warpline/src/fabric/refusal.ts:47-197` (schema id, `MAX_CONTESTED` 32, `RefusalGate`, `Retriability`, `RefusalNextStep{verb,params,requires,principal}`, `Refusal`), the single constructor `refuse()` (`:418-434`) and accessor `refusalOf()` (`:291-301`), with two schema differences so no engine type enters kernel contracts (A-boundary-4): `verdict` is `string | null` (Warpline's `AdmitStatus` values are legal strings) and `contested[].rule` is `string | null` (Warpline's `KnotRule`). Gnomon's `RefusalCode` is Warpline's twelve (`refusal.ts:76-88`) plus `BUDGET_DENIED` (gate `budget`, `retry-with-override`, `next:[{verb:'approve', principal:'human'}]`, exit 6), `PERMIT_REQUIRED` (gate `budget`, `retry-corrected`, `next:[{verb:'permit.reserve', principal:'agent'}]`, exit 2), `CAS_LOST` (gate `claim`, `retry-corrected`, `next:[{verb:'status'}]`, exit **7**, A17-5), `BILLING_REFUSED` (gate `billing`, retriable `never`, `next:[{verb:'approve', principal:'human'}]` pointing at an `api-token` card, exit 8), `MODEL_UNAVAILABLE` (gate `model`, retriable `retry-with-override`, `next:[{verb:'approve', principal:'human'}]` pointing at a `fallback` card, exit 9); gates add `budget`, `claim`, `billing`, `model`, `voice` (voice-approval refusals: always `FORBIDDEN`, `data.reason` a `voice-…` string from §14.7, `next:[{verb:'approve', principal:'human'}]` pointing at the card for a hand decision), `link` (§3.9: `FORBIDDEN` with `data.reason` ∈ `link-unpaired link-scope link-expired link-revoked link-paused link-rate audience sensitivity secret-found brief-replayed brief-expired brief-misaddressed peer-always peer-presence peer-via`; `UNSUPPORTED` with `warpline-sync-absent`). No new code: an unreachable peer is not a refusal — a send queues in the outbox and delivery shows in `status.links` and `link.*` events. Exit codes otherwise follow Warpline's table (`refusal.ts:213-226`). `data` is an optional object and additive: a refusal without it is valid, and a new `data` field is a compatible addition (§4.9). Over `gnomon-rpc:v1`, `refusalOf()` reads the refusal from the JSON-RPC `error.data`. Warpline's schema is a strict subset; Warpline keeps its own file, no re-export (A-OPEN-13 answered); `G-CON-02` asserts subset-compatibility (every Warpline refusal validates against Gnomon's schema).

### 4.7 contract (`contract:v1`)

```jsonc
{ "schemaVersion": "contract:v1",
  "contractId": "contract:v1:<sha256(canonical({specRef, mode, nodes:[{ref, kind, oracles}] sorted by ref}))>",   // ordinal-free, id-free (A17-b)
  "specRef": "spec:docs/specs/gnomon.md@<blobsha>", "repo": "repo:…", "mode": "warpline" | "git",
  "compiler": { "name": "paradigm-contracts", "version": "8.0.0", "deterministic": true },
  "nodes": [ { "ref": "sym:#approval-broker", "kind": "symbol", "section": "2.1", "oracles": [...], "deps": [] } ],
  "orphanTests": { "ref": "doc:release-gate", "ids": [...] },
  "certification": { "required": ["test", "admission" | "merge-ci", "reviewer"] }, "compiledAt": "…" }
```
`nodeId` in events = the node's `ref`, always paired with `contractId` (`{contractId, nodeId:"sym:#socket"}`). A node with zero oracles ⇒ compile refusal `BAD_REQUEST gate:usage` naming the node.

### 4.8 Budget permit (`permit:v1`)

```jsonc
{ "schemaVersion": "permit:v1", "permitId": "permit:v1:<ulid>", "log": "log:…", "principal": "p:…", "hsid": "…",   // log = issuing logId; never valid on another log (S32-06)
  "scope": { "org": null, "team": null, "user": "p:…", "repo": "repo:…", "loop": "loop:…", "session": "hsid:v1:…", "machine": "dev:…" },
  "kind": "session.spawn" | "turn" | "tool.permission" | "tend.llm" | "oracle.reviewer" | "teacher.draft" | "mod.spend",
  "backend": "claude-code" | "local", "model": "…", "billing": "subscription" | "api-token:<cardId>" | "local",   // backend is BINDING: only #claude-adapter may consume claude-code (S8)
  "limits": { "tokens": 400000, "usd": 4.0, "computeSeconds": 600, "concurrent": 2 },
  "issuedAt": "…", "expiresAt": "…", "ladder": { "user": 0.41, "repo": 0.12, "loop": null, "session": 0, "machine": 0.5, "subscriptionWindow": 0.62 },
  "decision": "allow" | "deny", "sig": "…" }
```

### 4.9 Socket protocol (`gnomon-rpc:v1`)

JSON-RPC 2.0, newline-delimited, over the unix socket or named pipe; every frame carries `"rpc":"gnomon-rpc:v1"`. **Hello and version negotiation** (A24-2): the client's `hello{clientApis:['gnomon-rpc:v1', …], features:[…], clientNonce, client:{name, version, surface?: browser|wrapper|cli|conductor|…, conformance?: {suite, version, reportHash}}}` names every client API it speaks and carries a fresh `clientNonce` (unpadded base64url of 16–64 random bytes; clients send 32, P8-D93); the kernel answers `{clientApi, features, nonce, clientNonce, serverKeyId, serverSig, clientApiId, kitId}` choosing the highest it shares and echoing `clientNonce`; a client naming none the kernel speaks is refused `UNSUPPORTED{data: {offered[]}}`. **Compatible-additive rule**: within `gnomon-rpc:v1` the kernel may add optional params, result fields, event kinds and refusal `data` fields; it never removes or retypes one, never changes a verb's meaning and never adds a required param — anything else is `gnomon-rpc:v2` offered beside v1. **Additive enumerations** inside v1 schemas (`card.kind`, `card.state`, `card.flags`, `presence`, `via`, `principal.kind`, `session.inject.origin`, decision values, refusal gates and reasons) may gain values; a client renders an unknown value opaquely and treats a card in an unknown `state` as not decidable. Four enumerations are **closed** and never gain a value inside v1: the refusal `code`, `retriable`, `audience` and `sensitivity`; a new value in any of them is `gnomon-rpc:v2`. Values the next release needs are reserved in `gnomon-contracts` one release before (A26-11; the D58 and P8-R9 values are reserved in M1.1). **Peers are not clients** (S32-01): a paired kernel speaks `gnomon-link:v1` to the link component only (§3.9) and has no `gnomon-rpc:v1` connection, socket credential or token; an `auth` naming a principal of kind `peer` is refused `AUTH`. Clients render an unknown `modns.*` kind opaquely as `{kind, principal, at}`, never drop it. **Mutual auth** (S-H4): `hello{clientNonce}` → `{nonce, clientNonce, serverKeyId, serverSig over 'gnomon:auth-server:v2\n'+nonce+'\n'+clientNonce+'\n'+endpointPath+'\n'+clientApi+'\n'+canonical(features)}` → `auth {principal, deviceId, sig over 'gnomon:auth:v1\n'+nonce+'\n'+serverKeyId+'\n'+principalId+'\n'+deviceId+'\n'+endpointPath}` or `{tokenId, sig}` (a read token, below) → `{principal, connId}`. Every auth signature also binds the negotiated hello: the messages are `nonce, clientNonce, endpointPath, clientApi, canonical(features)` for `serverSig` and `nonce, serverKeyId, principalId, deviceId, endpointPath, clientApi, canonical(features)` for device auth (`gnomon-contracts` `authServerMessage` / `authClientMessage`), so a relay that rewrites the offer cannot pick a version no signature covers. The client verifies `serverSig` against the registry's kernel key (§3.1) before sending `auth` (a relay that cannot sign as the kernel is detected); it refuses a hello whose `clientNonce` is not the one it sent (`client-nonce-mismatch`, client-local) and verifies the signature over its own nonce, never the echoed field, so a genuine hello recorded on another connection and replayed from a squatted endpoint fails (P8-D93). **Residual (P8-D93, Aegis):** clientNonce closes replay of a recorded hello. It does not bind the transport. A same-uid or root process that can rename the kernel's unix socket and listen at its path can relay a live hello+auth and hold a connection authenticated as the victim (frames carry no MAC). The web port is narrower: the relay must present the identical host:port. Tracked: T-2026-10-18-024. (§A.8); `serverSig` is the server's only authentication on both OS — the Windows pipe-PID comparison is a helper/Python-client diagnostic, not a claim (§2.4, S29-K6). Nonce single-use, 30 s; failed auth closes after one `AUTH` refusal, and so does any other method sent before auth, known or unknown: before auth every name except `hello` and `auth` gets the same `AUTH` answer, so an unauthenticated caller cannot enumerate method names (`UNKNOWN_VERB` is answered only after auth). **Read tokens** (P8-D89a) are proof of possession, never bearer secrets. Issuance is a root-signed act: a library-only mint appends a root-signed row with the token's Ed25519 **public** key, `tokenId`, principal, `createdAt` and `expiresAt` to `~/.gnomon/keys/read-tokens.jsonl`. No verb and no argv carries a token, and the holder keeps the seed. Lifetime defaults to 1 h and is at most 24 h; a token is not valid before `createdAt` minus 60 s. `auth {tokenId, sig}` signs `gnomon:auth-token:v1` over `nonce, serverKeyId, tokenId, endpointPath, clientApi, canonical(features)`, so a relaying impostor receives nothing it can replay. One live connection per token. The connection's class is the read-token column of §5 whatever the token's principal. Revocation is a root-signed row. The kernel records each revocation it sees as `key.token.revoked`, honours it for good (seeded from the chain at start), and treats a token file that shrinks or loses a seen line as untrusted: nothing resolves. A live connection is re-checked on every call and every 5 s. Identity bound per connection (`connId` on every event). Calls → `{result}` or `{error:{code, message, data: refusal:v1}}`; total code map: AUTH -32001, FORBIDDEN -32002, NOT_FOUND -32004, UNSUPPORTED -32005, BAD_REQUEST -32600, UNKNOWN_VERB -32601, ENGINE -32000, BUDGET_DENIED -32010, PERMIT_REQUIRED -32011, CAS_LOST -32012, INTEGRITY_BROKEN -32013, GATE_REFUSED -32020, CLAIM_BREACH -32021, TRUST_HELD -32022, STALE_BASE -32023, BILLING_REFUSED -32030, MODEL_UNAVAILABLE -32031 (verdict-class codes normally ride inside results). Subscription `log.read{follow:true}` returns the backlog then streams `event` notifications; backpressure > 10 000 undelivered frames ⇒ drop + `socket.subscription.dropped{connId, lag}`; the client re-subscribes with `since`. Limits: `maxFrameBytes` (4 MiB) bounds **inbound** frames, and a longer line is discarded to its newline and answered `BAD_REQUEST`; the kernel caps its own reply frames at 4 MiB. Framing is byte-level NDJSON: a frame ends only at a `\n` byte, so a UTF-8 character split across reads is never corrupted (Warpline's split-UTF-8 bug is not lifted). Until M1.4 lands, the daemon answers `hello` with `ENGINE` (-32000), `data.reason: 'not-implemented'`. Redaction per §9.3. Node has no peer-credential API on unix sockets or pipes; endpoint ACL + mutual signatures are the boundary.

**Loopback WebSocket transport (`#web-endpoint`, §11.2).** The same frames ride RFC 6455 text frames (one JSON-RPC message per frame) on `ws://127.0.0.1:<port>/rpc`, parsed by the `gnomon-web` transport child and forwarded to the kernel as `{connId, headers, frame}` records — the child never interprets a frame, never holds a token's meaning and never answers on its own (A24-1). **RFC 6455 as implemented:** text frames only (a binary frame ⇒ close `1003`); no fragmentation (a frame with `FIN=0` or a continuation opcode ⇒ close `1009`; one JSON-RPC message per unfragmented frame, ≤ 4 MiB); `permessage-deflate` and every other extension refused at the upgrade (no `Sec-WebSocket-Extensions` in the response; a client that sends compressed frames is closed `1002`); client frames must be masked (`1002` otherwise); invalid UTF-8 ⇒ close `1007`; the child pings every 30 s and closes after two missed pongs; a close handshake is answered with the mirrored code; oversize ⇒ `1009` (`G-WEB-07`). `hello` is as above with `surface: browser | wrapper`; `auth` accepts two more forms — `{launchToken}` on first use, answered `{guiToken, principal, connId}`, and `{reconnect: true}`, which raises a Touch ID / Windows Hello presence prompt naming the `connId` and the count of open GUI sessions and, on approval, answers `{guiToken, principal, connId}` (D54) — and the connection then acts as the launching device's human principal with `client:'gui'` and `surface` stamped on every event it causes. The page **verifies `serverSig` with WebCrypto** against the kernel's Ed25519 public key carried in the launch fragment (`#launch=<token>&k=<base64url spki>`), pins that public key in `localStorage` (public material, not a secret — the only thing the page ever stores) and verifies every later `hello`, including Reconnect, against the pin; a mismatch refuses with a visible "kernel key changed — run `gnomon open`" and sends nothing (S25-M4). A fragment key that differs from the pin arrived through the authenticated `gnomon open` channel, so the page verifies against it and **re-pins visibly**: a sticky, kernel-owned notice naming the old and new keyIds (shortened) that only a user click clears, and a `/diag` line recording the re-pin (Aegis M1.10 C2) — never a silent replacement. The wrapper verifies the same signature natively with the key it received on stdin. Every other rule of this section (nonce, code map, subscription, backpressure, limits, redaction) applies unchanged to the WebSocket transport.

### 4.10 Voice events (`voice:v1`, D30/V, S22-M1/M2)

```
voice.session.start { voiceSessionId, hsid, mode: ptt|conversation, engines:{vad, stt, tts, diarization}, device:{mic, aec:bool}, audioRetention: none|ttl:<s> }
voice.turn.user     { turnId, speakerId: "spk:<ns>/<name>"|"spk:unknown-2", speakerEnrolled: bool, forwarded: bool, intercepted?: decision|control, t0, t1, text, asrModel, confidence, audioRef? }
                                                            — an intercepted decision turn stores its text redacted (`claude admin approve ⟨handle⟩`)
voice.turn.agent    { turnId, hsid, textPlanned, textSpoken, interruptedAtMs|null, ttsVoice, t0, t1,
                      source: local-ack|claude|local|kernel-readback|kernel-echo|kernel-deck, cardId?, readbackHash? }   — t0/t1 = playback wall times
voice.deck          { deckId, cardIds[], textHash }         — the deck the kernel's readback last spoke (§14.7)
voice.focus         { cardId }                              — "open": focus a card in the GUI (G1); decides nothing
voice.tts.filtered  { turnId }                              — the phrase mask withheld an approval-grammar match
voice.bargein       { turnId, atMs, heardText }
voice.session.end   { voiceSessionId, turns, compactedTo: "l:L-…" }
voice.share         { loreId, audience: team, redactions:[…], consent:[speakerId…] }
voice.enrol         { speakerId, consent, printRef }        — root-signed row (CLI `gnomon voice enrol`)
voice.forget        { speakerId }                           — root-signed row (CLI `gnomon voice forget`)
```
`textSpoken` is the truth after a barge-in; `textPlanned` is what the model intended (20). Every `turn.sent` that originated from speech carries `source: voice`. The namespace `voice.*` is a **userland grant** to `p:<ns>/voice` (A23-2); the kernel validates shape only (speakerId known or `unknown-N`, turn ids monotone, text ≤ 16 KiB) — except for the `voice.turn.user`, `voice.turn.agent` and `voice.deck` events a voice intent references (§14.7), whose text, timing and speaker the broker validates against the decision grammar and the echo cross-check.

### 4.11 Review findings, change notes and strictness (`review-finding:v1`, `change-note:v1`; P8-D86–D88, reserved in M1.1)

```jsonc
review-finding:v1 { fid, round, class, severity: "important" | "nit" | "pre-existing",
                    locus: { path, lines?, symbol? }, instances: [ { path, lines?, symbol? } ], claim,
                    fingerprint }        // sha256(class ‖ (symbol ?? path) ‖ overlapping line range); severity pre-existing is set by the kernel (§7.3)
change-note:v1    { agentClaims: { testedHow: { summary /* ≤ 1 KiB */, refs: [ref] },
                                   risk: { tier: "low" | "medium" | "high", why /* ≤ 512 B */, blast: ["sym:…"] },
                                   revert: { how: "auto" | "command" | "manual", steps /* ≤ 1 KiB, never executed */, reversible: bool },
                                   draftedBy },                                     // untrusted, sanitized (§4.5), rendered as agent-claimed
                    kernel:      { verified: { n, of }, risk: { floor, effective, reasons: [] },
                                   revert: { how: "auto" | "none", ref? } } }        // computed by the kernel; never written by the agent
```

**Reserved in M1.1** (one release before use, A26-11), so `clientApiId` does not move in Move 2; every value lands in an additive enumeration or is a new kind, field or schema, and none of the four closed enumerations of §4.9 changes:
- **Card kinds** `reconsider`, `strictness`, `incident`; **decisions** `extend`, `restart`; **flags** `irreversible`, `claim-mismatch`; **card fields** `note?: change-note:v1`, `strictness?{level, mode, reason}`.
- **Refusals**: gate `review` with reason `review-cap`; gate `strictness` with reasons `strictness-presence`, `strictness-paused`, `strictness-mode`, `strictness-evidence`; reason `voice-strictness` on gate `voice` (Arky's `strictness-voice` is folded into it, because voice refusals always use gate `voice`, §4.6); reason `note-missing` on `BAD_REQUEST`; `approval.suspect` reason `strictness-class`.
- **Schemas** `review-finding:v1`, `change-note:v1`; **verb params** `contract.claim` gains `note` and `responses: [{fid, response: fixed|wrong|intentional|immaterial, note?}]`.
- **Event kinds** `approval.strictness.raised|proposed|lowered|held`, `approval.mode.set`, `approval.auto`, `contract.review.returned|capped|extended|restarted`, `merge.reverted`, `merge.ref.rewritten`, `verdict.incident`, `request.strictness.set`, `request.mode.set`, `request.incident`.
- **Additive fields** `oracle.result` gains `round`, `range{from, to}`, `findings[]`; `contract.knot` gains `reopenOf`; `verdict.founder` gains `override`; `session.open` gains `runMode?`.
- **Status** `status.strictness`, `status.review`, `status.unobservedRefWrites` (§5); **root config** `strictness:` and `review:` (§3.4).

---

## 5. Kernel ABI — twelve verbs (law 3)

`status` is the man page (F4, TD-2026-07-21-766): principal, legal next verbs, verb→tool map, `abiId` (hashed as `descriptors.ts:383-393`), `modAbiId`, `clientApiId` and `kitId` (§12.8), the negotiated `clientApi`, the web endpoint (`listening`, `port`, `bundleHash`, `guiSessions{n}` with each session's `connId`, `surface` and unlock state), connected clients with their declared conformance, linked and dev mods, `kernelKeyCustody`, `presencePromptText`, voice approvals (`voice.approvals{projects:[{repo, classes}], armed:{armId, class, repos, until, idleLeftMs, decisions{n, of}} | null, armCustody: supervised|client}`), backends and their health, subscription window, billing mode, sandbox fidelity per profile, inbox by kind, unknown Claude processes, links (`links{peers:[{peerRootKeyId8, ns, relationship, scopes, until, state: connected|offline|paused|revoked, outbox{n}, lastSeenAt}], relay:{addr, reachable}, linkApiId, linkKeyCustody: full|partial}`, §3.9), strictness and run modes (`strictness{repos:[{repo, level, floor, ceiling, mode, since, signals{…}, score, revertRate{n, of}, loosenEligibleAt, evidence{landed{n, of}, survived{n, of}}}], sessions:[{hsid, runMode, effectiveMode}]}`, §3.4), review convergence (`review{nodes:[{contractId, nodeId, round, of, openFindings{n, of}, extends{n, of}}]}`, §7.3) and `unobservedRefWrites{repo, n}`. The MCP proxy registers ≤ 12 tools (`descriptors.ts:269-271` naming law). Everything else is CLI-only (§3.1 I3) or a mod command (§12).

| # | Verb | Params | Result | Refusals | Human | Agent (roster) | Session | Mod (`p:<ns>/mod-*`) | Voice (`p:<ns>/voice`) | Read token | Peer (`p:<peerNs>/*`, §3.9) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `status` | `{}` | self-description | AUTH | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ (own view) | ✓ | ✓ | ✗ |
| 2 | `session.open` | `{backend?: claude-code\|local, model: local\|claude\|any, cwd, mode?, runMode?: manual\|ask\|auto-edits, as?, goal?, contract?, alias?, derived?, permitId?, systemPrompt?, attended, brief?}` (`brief`: human only; a peer's brief only after its `handoff` accept, §3.9) | `{hsid, principalId, backend, state}` | PERMIT_REQUIRED, BUDGET_DENIED, BILLING_REFUSED, MODEL_UNAVAILABLE, UNSUPPORTED, FORBIDDEN | ✓ | ✓ permit | ✓ permit | ✗ | ✗ | ✗ | ✗ |
| 3 | `session.send` (incl. `steer`, `source: voice`) | `{hsid, content[], permitId, steer?: bool, source?: voice}` (content part `{brief}`: human only, §3.9; `steer: true` joins the IN-FLIGHT turn under that turn's live permit and takes no `permitId` — one supplied with a steer is refused `BAD_REQUEST`, an expired head permit `PERMIT_REQUIRED`; with no turn in flight a steer is an ordinary send under a new permit; served for local sessions only in M1.8, a Claude session refuses it `BAD_REQUEST`) | `{turn}` | PERMIT_REQUIRED, BUDGET_DENIED, NOT_FOUND, BAD_REQUEST | ✓ | own subtree | own subtree | via `kernel.call` with caller hsid, same scope | ✓ attached session, `source: voice` forced | ✗ | ✗ |
| 4 | `session.stop` | `{hsid, interrupt?}` | `{signals[], exited}` | NOT_FOUND, FORBIDDEN | ✓ | own subtree | own subtree | ✗ | interrupt only | ✗ | ✗ |
| 5 | `log.append` | `{kind, payload?, ref?, causes?, scope?, idem?, audience?, sensitivity?}` | `{id, seq, hash}` | FORBIDDEN (kernel-owned or ungranted kind), BAD_REQUEST, ENGINE (a cell whose milestone has not landed) | granted kinds + `request.*` (first-party human-class connections) | granted kinds | **none** | `modns.<name>.*` only | `voice.*` + `request.voice.arm` | ✗ | ✗ |
| 6 | `log.read` | `{since?, kinds?, scope?, follow?}` | backlog + stream | AUTH, BAD_REQUEST | full | redacted | own scope | granted kinds, own `modns` | own session + `voice.*` | redacted | ✗ |
| 7 | `permit.reserve` / `release` | `{kind, scope, backend?, model?, limits?, tool?, input?}` / `{op:'release', permitId}` | `permit:v1` | BUDGET_DENIED, BILLING_REFUSED, FORBIDDEN | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ own scope | ✗ (spend comes from the caller's permit) | ✗ | ✗ | ✗ |
| 8 | `approve` | `{cardId, decision, note?, decisionSig}`; voice intent form `{via:'voice', decision, voice:{armId, turnId, target, challenge?, confirmTurnId?, readbackHash?, answerText?, attest}}` | `card:v1` | FORBIDDEN (incl. gate `voice`), NOT_FOUND, AUTH | ✓ (presence per class; root key for `use-api-token`, `install`; `withdraw` on `held` cards with no presence) | ✗ | ✗ | ✗ | intent form only, as the supervised child inside a live arm, for classes and repos the grant allows; no `cardId`, no `decisionSig`; hand-only kinds never (§14.7) | ✗ | ✗ |
| 9 | `contract.register` | `{contract}` | `{contractId, nodes}` | BAD_REQUEST, FORBIDDEN | ✓ | ✓ | ✗ (A17-5) | ✗ | ✗ | ✗ | ✗ |
| 10 | `contract.claim` (`claim\|built\|release`, `ttl`) | `{contractId, nodeId, op: claim\|built\|release, ttl?, expect?:{status, seq}, note?, responses?}` (`note`: `change-note:v1`; `responses`: answers to open findings, §7.3) | `{nodeId, status, by, seq, expiresAt?}` | CAS_LOST, NOT_FOUND, BAD_REQUEST (`note-missing`) | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✗ | ✗ | ✗ | ✗ |
| 11 | `oracle.run` | `{contractId, nodeId, oracle}` | `oracle:v1 {pass, evidence, ranBy:'p:<ns>/kernel', reviewer?, round?, findings?}` | PERMIT_REQUIRED, UNSUPPORTED, ENGINE, FORBIDDEN (`review-cap`) | ✓ | ✓ requests | ✓ requests | ✗ | ✗ | ✗ | ✗ |
| 12 | `ref.resolve` | `{ref}` | `{target, hash?}` | BAD_REQUEST, UNSUPPORTED, NOT_FOUND | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✗ |

**Client relays.** `mod.view` and `mod.command` (§12.3) ride the same socket but are served by the mod host (§3.8) under the caller's principal, stamped `relayedFor{principal, connId}`, and counted in `modAbiId`; they are not among the twelve — the client-callable surface is **12 kernel verbs + 2 relays** — and `status` lists them separately. A mod holding `knowledge:federate` (the first-party knowledge mod only, §8.10) may call `mod.command` on another mod through `kernel.call`, under the original requester's principal.

**Namespace ownership (A17-3, A23-1).** Default: **every kind not listed below is kernel-owned** and appendable by no principal through `log.append`. Userland namespaces, appendable only by a principal bound to a manifest grant listing the kind (`grant.bind`): `paradigm.* inject.* credit.* broke.* detector.* loop.row loop.open loop.closed tend.* decay.* calibration.* flow.* neurons.* voice.* modns.<name>.*`. Explicitly kernel-owned (named because reviewers asked): `verdict.* gate-class.* blob.pruned digest.consumed loop.paused contract.* billing.* mod.* approval.* permit.* budget.* session.* turn.* key.* config.* manifest.* oracle.* warpline.* merge.* liveness.* request.* link.*` — `request.*` carries one named append exception (A26-08): `request.grant`, `request.voice.narrow`, `request.brief.send`, `request.cowork.allow`, `request.link.revoke` (§3.9), `request.strictness.set`, `request.mode.set` and `request.incident` (§3.4) by the human principal on a first-party human-class connection, `request.voice.arm` by the human or the voice principal; the kernel answers each with a card or a narrowing, never with a grant row of its own — `mod.*` (kernel: `installed | rolled-back | health | effect.refused | view.rendered | stopped | sandbox | linked | paused | repinned | unlinked`) and `modns.*` (mods) cannot collide.

**Matrix invariants (`G-ABI-02`).** `approve` is the only HUMAN_ONLY verb: every decision's approver is the human; `decisionSig` by the approvals key (hardware, presence class per §3.4) over `(cardId, decision, contentHash, approverPrincipalId)`; the voice principal's intent form is decided under the human's arm and signed by the broker (domain `gnomon:approve-voice:v1`, §3.1); `use-api-token` and `install` decisions additionally require the root key (presence). Read tokens: `status`, `log.read`, `ref.resolve` only (`protocol.ts:138-144`, `server.ts:206-212` pattern). `G-ABI-02` enumerates every principal class above (human, agent, session, mod, voice, read token, peer) against every verb and both relays — the peer column is refused everywhere, because a peer has no `gnomon-rpc:v1` connection at all (§3.9, S32-01) — with the voice-intent row: toggles off, unarmed, wrong connection, unsupervised client, class or repo outside the grant, hand-only kind ⇒ refused. `status` < 50 ms (`G-ABI-03`). Next-legal-verbs table hashed into `abiId` (`descriptors.ts:304-347` pattern): `{budgetPaused} → [status, log.read]` · `{cardsPendingForYou>0, human} → [approve]` · `{human, heldCards>0} → [approve decision:withdraw]` · `{voice, armed, voiceEligibleCards>0} → [approve]` · `{voice, !armed} → [status, log.read, session.send, session.stop]` · `{repoStrictness: L3, agent|session} → [status, log.read]` · `{reviewReturned} → [contract.claim op:built]` · `{reviewCapped, agent|session} → [status, log.read]` · `{noSession} → [session.open]` · `{noPermit} → [permit.reserve]` · `{hasContract, unclaimed>0} → [contract.claim]` · `{claimed, built:false} → [contract.claim op:built]` · `{built} → [oracle.run]` · `{} → [status]`.

**Per-method access tiers, default deny (T-2026-10-19-009).** The table above is enforced through one method registry in the kernel socket (`kernel/socket/methods.ts`). Every method, verb or relay, is registered with a tier for every column: `false` (refused) or a non-empty access string. Registering a method with any tier missing, undefined or empty throws, and so does a duplicate or a late registration; the registry is built from the frozen descriptors when the module loads and then sealed, so a descriptor with a missing tier stops the daemon from starting rather than failing on a call. The check made on each call fails closed: an unknown method, an unknown class or any stored tier that is not `false` or a non-empty string answers `false` (`FORBIDDEN{matrix}`), never "no restriction". The relays carry `caller principal` in every column except read tokens and peers. `hello` and `auth` are not in the registry: they are the pre-auth handshake, outside the tier registry, handled before any registry lookup (no matrix class exists until `auth` names a principal); before auth every other name is refused `AUTH` (§4.9).

**`log.read` cursor (TD-2026-10-19-648 item 8; reserved in the T-2026-10-19-001 move, P8-D98).** The cursor is `{logId, seq}`, not a bare seq: every streamed `event` and every backlog page carries it, and `since` takes it back. A cursor whose `logId` is not this kernel's current log (another kernel's log, or a log that a quarantine replaced, §19.1) is refused `BAD_REQUEST{reason:'cursor-epoch'}`, so a client re-reads from the start of the current log instead of silently resuming at a seq that names a different event. Limited views (read token, community client, mod, §9.3) carry `seq` only, with no `hash` or `prev`. The same batched `clientApiId` move (one move, per P8-D89's batching rule; P8-D98) also reserves the `hello` feature names `session.resume` and `journal.cursor`, which the kernel advertises only once it builds them, the kinds `session.drained` and `permit.reattributed` (§2.4 rule 11), and the contract values of §7.4 (`claimGen`; the `CAS_LOST` reasons `stale-claim`, `claim-expired`, `claim-superseded`). The cursor type `{logId, seq}` is published now; `since` keeps taking a bare seq until the feature is built.

**Human-device verbs (P8-D98).** Two socket methods sit beside the twelve verbs and the two relays: `gui.open{cwdHint?}` (§2.4 rule 8) and `rules.revoke{ruleId}` (§3.4).
