Only a human principal on a device connection whose `hello` verified the kernel key may call them; agent, session, mod, voice, read-token and peer callers and a GUI launch-token connection are refused `FORBIDDEN{matrix}`. Each handler is its own module; until it lands the call refuses `ENGINE{not-implemented}`. They are not kernel verbs for law 3, and `status` lists them separately.

**`gnomon guide <topic>` (TD-2026-10-19-648 item 10; T-2026-10-19-015, built before Move 2).** A CLI-only verb that prints how-to text for a topic. It is not a socket method and not one of the twelve verbs, so `clientApiId` and `abiId` do not move. Topics are compiled into the signed binary; there is no topic file on disk, in the home or in a repo, so nothing a session or a repo writes can change what a guide says. It is **pull-only**: the text reaches a human or a model only when one runs the verb, and the kernel never injects a guide into a session, a prompt, an envelope or a card. A CI honesty check (law 2, §9.7) fails the build if a guide names a verb, flag or refusal reason that the frozen descriptors lack. `status` stays the man page (F4); a guide explains, it never grants or decides anything.

**`gnomon trace` (dogfood audit, CLI-only, read-only).** A CLI-only verb, not a socket method and not one of the twelve verbs, so `clientApiId` and `abiId` do not move. It prints one session's audit timeline: the kernel-log rows of the session (open, sandbox, turns sent, usage and cost, cards and decisions, permits, drain and resume, exit, and the daemon start and exit around it), the Claude Code transcript of the session's uuid, and the raw stream capture of a folder whose trace mode a human switched on (read from `<home>/trace/<project key>/`, TD-2026-10-19-057), in time order. Each turn sent is checked against the transcript prompt by its content hash, computed by the same function the supervisor logs with. The reader writes nothing and never connects to the kernel: it opens the log files so that nothing is created, checks the hash chain, and leaves signatures to `gnomon replay`. Its output contains prompt text. `gnomon trace on | off | status` switches and lists per-folder trace mode; that state file in the home is the verb's only write.

---

## 6. Spawn (`$spawn`)

### 6.1 Generic path

1. `permit.reserve{kind:'session.spawn', backend, model}` — refused ⇒ nothing else happens (law 1); interactive `derived:true` registrations skip this.
2. Route by `model` (§13.3): `local` ⇒ `#local-runner` or a `fallback` card; `claude` ⇒ `#claude-adapter` under law 7; `any` ⇒ local if a capable local model is up, else Claude. Routing never considers Claude Code against a local endpoint (D37).
3. `#identity` mints `p:<ns>/s-<hsid-short>` with an ephemeral key; Warpline mode binds `as`.
4. `session.open`; hsid issued.
5. Preflight (§3.3) incl. law-7 scan and `layersHash`/`gitHash`; a gate-class difference ⇒ card before spawn.
6. `#manifest-loader` writes `policy.json` (permissions, hooks → `gnomon hook …`, denies on `~/.gnomon/keys/**`, `~/.gnomon/warpline/**`, `.warpline/keys/**`, `.warpline/daemon-tokens.jsonl`, `.warpline/daemon/mcp-tokens.json`, `.warpline/daemon/mcp.token`, and writes to every Claude settings layer — on EVERY mode, S16-1/S22-H2) and `mcp.json` (including the per-session `gnomon-mods` server when mod tools are granted, §12.3).
7. `session.inject` for every added byte (`origin: kernel | client`).
8. Spawn the driver child (`claude -p` or `gnomon-local --mode rpc`) inside the **`session` sandbox profile** (§15.5) — the profile denies reads of `~/.gnomon/keys`, the Warpline token store and the OS keystore items, writes under `~/.gnomon/**`, to every settings layer and to every linked mod folder, launcher escapes, loopback connects to the web endpoint's port, and process information on other processes (D55); it does not confine the worktree, tools or other network (sessions need them). The `gnomon-mods` MCP bridge (§3.8) is spawned beside it under the same profile when mod tools are granted.
9. Turns with `turn` permits; `turn.usage`/`turn.result` → `budget.usage`. Every `can_use_tool` request becomes a card and gets one kernel answer (P8-D96, §3.3/§3.4): `permission` (allow | deny), `plan` for ExitPlanMode, with AskUserQuestion an `ask-user` card answered from the GUI picker. Exit or stop expires every pending card into deny.
10. Stop/exit; worktree left in place.

### 6.2 Warpline-native path (base revision declares `mode: warpline`)

Preconditions: `.gnomon/project.yaml` at the base revision says `mode: warpline` (§15.7); the roster principal named by `as` has a Warpline key file and an agent-class daemon token minted by the human (`warpline key mint`, `warpline daemon token mint … --kind agent`); `warplined` is reachable or activatable for the root (`lifecycle.ts:44-50`). The kernel is a **re-derived `warplined:v1` NDJSON client** (`protocol.ts:31,60-110`; the engine is never imported), may start `warplined` for a root (`warpline daemon start`), never mints. Four kernel duties (05; TD-950 §3; D14/A17-a): (1) **select** the roster credential (`WARPLINE_AGENT_ID=<as>`, `WARPLINE_MCP_TOKEN` from the keyed store, `tokens.ts:205-232,295-320`; refuse `UNSUPPORTED` if absent, never a kernel mint); (2) **env** as §3.3; (3) **deny** Warpline human-class verbs and override flags on every skin — `resolve`, `stake`, `stake.recover`, `backup` (`protocol.ts:115-120`), `--accept-breach/--accept-risk` (`:148-151`), `branch --protect/--unprotect` (`:161-164`), `merge --confirm` (`:175-177`) — plus the key/token paths (TD-2026-08-23-136 M3-Q5; S-H5); these denies are **advisory string matches** (bypassable by `python -c`, scripts on disk, `env -u WARPLINE_AGENT_ID`); the enforced boundary is `warplined`'s verb × principal matrix for keyed callers (`server.ts:215-233`) and, once P8-D16 ships, `resolve` served only to keyed principals; (4) **attach** worktree + `fork` the scratch ref through the daemon (`forkNative`, `native.ts:260`) + per-session line `refs/heads/gnomon/<hsid-short>`; selvage read-only to agents; the kernel watches `.warpline/refs/heads/selvage` with `fs.watch` (no polling) and appends `warpline.selvage.moved{ref}` so sibling sessions re-predict, until Warpline's `refs.watch` replaces the fs watch (P8-D16). **The one kernel pointer line** (F4): `--append-system-prompt "Warpline project. Run \`warpline status\` (or the warpline_status tool) before your first write."` — exactly one kernel-added line, logged as `session.inject{origin:'kernel', kind:'warpline.pointer'}`; the skill is installed and discoverable, never pushed into turn one (10 §5.2); teaching is carried by Warpline's `status` result and `refusal:v1.next[]` (`descriptors.ts:383-393`). `G-SPN-05` asserts kernel-origin injected bytes equal that line byte-for-byte; client-origin bytes are logged separately and uncapped. **Sealing and KNOTs.** `PostToolUse` does not seal on every edit (41 s absorb, `hook.ts:30-34`); the session calls `propose` when it claims a node built; admission runs as the **shadow** oracle (§7.3), presented by the kernel with its own human-minted agent-class token `gnomon-kernel` from `~/.gnomon/warpline/<repoId>.token` (A17-a, A23-7); a shadow KNOT ⇒ `warpline.knot{payloadId}` + `knot` card → human `approve{resolve}` (fresh presence) → human runs `warpline resolve` on the CLI (HUMAN_ONLY, FG-1) — the kernel never calls `resolve`. `G-SPN-06` (restated, S-H5): an agent-class `resolve` through the daemon or MCP skin is `FORBIDDEN` and logged as a W3 attempt; unmarked CLI shells are outside this measurement and said so in the result (`skinScoped:true`). Revisit trigger for the per-user-kernel/per-repo-warplined split: > 3 concurrently active Warpline repos or daemon RSS over budget (A-warplined).

### 6.3 Git-mode path

`git worktree add .gnomon/worktrees/<hsid-short> -b gnomon/<hsid-short> <base>` with the hardening env + flags and inside the `git` sandbox class (§3.3); landing = `merge-ci` oracle; conflicts ⇒ `merge-conflict` card without semantic judgment; clean ⇒ fast-forward onto `gnomon/integration` after the base revision's `ci` command is green inside the sandbox; `main` never written by the kernel. On Windows worktrees live under the same path (long-path aware, `core.longpaths=true` in the hardening set).

---

## 7. Spec-as-contract (P8-R2)

### 7.1 The compiler (`#contracts-compiler`, userland, deterministic)

Lives in `packages/paradigm/src/core/contracts/` as the `plan` stage of `paradigm pipeline` (`pipeline/index.ts:131-162`; `STAGE_ORDER` at `core/pipeline-types.ts:67`, imported `pipeline/index.ts:16`); the `paradigm_pipeline` MCP family runs the compiler locally and calls `contract.register` (A17-5). Rules:
1. Sections open at NUMBERED ATX headings (`## 3.`, `### 3.2`, `## A.`, `### A.1`); unnumbered headings do not change the section; text before the first numbered heading is `preamble`. Fenced code blocks are never scanned. A heading of ANY kind ending `(compile: skip)` skips everything until the next heading of equal or higher level (A17-2).
2. A **node candidate** is a whole inline code span matching `^[#$^!~][a-z][a-z0-9-]*$` in headings, prose or table cells.
3. Node identity = `sym:` + span; kind from prefix; `section` = first appearance; one node per ref.
4. A **test id** matches `\bG-[A-Z]{2,4}-\d{2}[a-z]?\b`; a range is written `<prefix>-NN<sfx>..MM<sfx>` where `<sfx>` is an optional single lowercase letter that must be identical on both ends, and it expands with its suffix (a range written with the suffix `w` on both ends expands to the ordinals `04w 05w 06w`; a mismatched suffix is a compile refusal, A23-7). An id in a table row binds to the nodes mentioned in that row plus the section heading's nodes; a table row that mentions no node under a heading that mentions none skips its own section and takes the ancestor path below. An id outside a table binds to every node mentioned in the section; otherwise to the nearest ancestor section's nodes; otherwise to the `doc` node declared by the single `Deliverable: <kebab>` line (`orphanTests`).
5. `deps` only from an explicit `Depends on:` line in a single-node section. This spec declares none.
6. Oracles: `exists` on every node; `test` per bound id; gates get `gate-check`; flows get `ripple-clean`; every node gets the mode's admission oracle and `reviewer`; no bound test and no `Oracle: exists` line ⇒ refusal.
7. Canonicalize (nodes sorted by ref), hash, emit; byte-identical for identical input (`G-CTR-01`).

### 7.2 Node kinds

`symbol · gate · flow · signal · aspect · test · doc`.

### 7.3 Oracles and certification

| Oracle | Runs | Deterministic |
|---|---|---|
| `exists` | `ref.resolve(sym:…)` via premise-core resolver | yes |
| `test` | repo test command filtered to the id, on a worktree COPY, inside `#oracle-sandbox` profile `gnomon-oracle` (default-deny, §15.5), under `p:<ns>/kernel` | yes |
| `gate-check` / `ripple-clean` | portal.yaml / `paradigm ripple` | yes |
| `admission` (Warpline) | `warplined admit {shadow:true}` (`protocol.ts:43`) presented with the `gnomon-kernel` token; real admit only after `^certified` | yes |
| `merge-ci` (git) | `git merge-tree` clean onto base AND the base revision's `ci` green in the sandbox | yes |
| `reviewer` | one `claude -p` turn on the **subscription** (D15, law 7) under an `oracle.reviewer` permit, as roster principal `p:<ns>/reviewer` ≠ claimant and ancestors, **no tools**, ≤ 1 turn, diff enveloped untrusted, spec section from the base revision, strict `{pass, findings: review-finding:v1[]}` schema (§4.11; widened from `{pass, reasons[], riskyUnits[]}`, A35-05), from round 2 on the diff since its last review, inside profile `gnomon-reviewer` (S16-4: Keychain/Credential Manager item for Claude's OAuth reachable, `~/.gnomon`/`~/.ssh` denied; weaker than `gnomon-oracle`, stated) whose egress is a **kernel-run loopback proxy** carrying the Anthropic-hosts allowlist (P8-D17) — the profile allows network only to that loopback port, since hostnames are not expressible in `sandbox-exec` (S22-M4) | no |

**Certification** (`^certified`): `exists ∧ test ∧ (admission | merge-ci) ∧ reviewer`, each `oracle.result` kernel-signed (an agent claims, only the kernel certifies, 10 §4). The gate's own tests are `G-CTR-04`, `G-CTR-05`, `G-CTR-06`, `G-CTR-08`, `G-CTR-09` (§19.2). Disagreement at admission or merge level ⇒ `contract.knot` + `knot` card (Warpline KNOT payload `knot-payload.ts:56` attached when the shadow admission knotted); resolution human with fresh presence, logged `verdict.founder`. **Amended in v3.9 (A35-01):** a failing `reviewer` or `test` oracle under the round cap is not a disagreement for the human; it returns the node to its claimant (below). Reviewer calibration is mandatory (§8.6).

**Review convergence (P8-D86; A35-01..07, S35-10).** A **round** is one kernel-observed review outcome on a node after a `contract.built`: a reviewer `oracle.result`, or a human `verdict` reject. An agent never declares one. Rounds are keyed by node **lineage**, `(repo, nodeRef)` across contract versions, so a release and reclaim, a respawn, a new hsid or a recompiled contract keeps the count; a node that first appears in a registration which, within 24 h and in the same section, drops a node with open findings inherits that node's rounds and findings (a split or a re-id). Only a human `restart` resets the count.
- **Return, not knot.** While the count is under `review.maxRounds` (default 3, root-signed, range 1..5, §3.4), a failing `reviewer` or `test` oracle hands the node back to its claimant: `contract.review.returned{nodeId, round, openFindings{n, of}}`. The claim stays and no card is raised. A `knot` card is raised for disagreements at admission or merge level (a Warpline KNOT, or `merge-ci` failing where the reviewer passed) and at the cap as below.
- **The cap.** At `maxRounds` the broker refuses the next `oracle.reviewer` permit with `FORBIDDEN`, gate `review`, reason `review-cap`, `next:[{verb:'approve', principal:'human'}]`, appends `contract.review.capped`, suspends the claim's TTL timer while the card is pending (as for `held` cards), and raises **one** card. When every other required oracle passes and only the reviewer disagrees, it is a `knot` card: certifying over the reviewer is the founder's judgment, with fresh presence, logged `verdict.founder{accepted, override: true}`. Otherwise it is a `reconsider` card.
- **`reconsider`.** The body is kernel-composed from the round history (`loop.row`, `oracle.result`), the open findings grouped by class and the claimant's change note; the agent's proposed alternative is appended as fenced, untrusted text. Decisions: `restart` releases the claim and resets the count, and the next `built` must carry a new note (fresh presence, hand-only); `extend` grants one more round (fresh presence, voice-ineligible, counted as an override signal, at most `review.maxExtends` = 2 per lineage); `reject` abandons the node (deny-direction, cached class, voice-decidable under D66). No decision on the card merges, approves or certifies. The kernel logs `contract.review.extended` and `contract.review.restarted`.
- **Diff-only after round 1.** From round 2 the reviewer's input is the diff from the commit it last reviewed to the new head, plus the findings ledger; the full text of the touched files is context only. A new finding outside the new hunks is dropped, counted `outOfScope{n, of}` and filed `pre-existing`, which does not block. Residual: a change in a new hunk can break unchanged code, and only the tests, or a `regression`-class finding anchored to a new hunk, catch it.
- **Findings** are `review-finding:v1` (§4.11). The kernel tags a finding `pre-existing` when its locus lies outside the base..head diff. In round 1 the reviewer lists every instance of a class in `instances[]`. A finding whose `fingerprint` matches the ledger is dropped and counted `repeated{n, of}`; a paraphrase under another class passes this check (a stated residual, OPEN 21). The kernel's `pass` is the reviewer's `pass` with zero open `important` findings. The claimant answers on `built` with `responses[]` (`fixed`, `wrong`, `intentional`, `immaterial`), not through a new verb (law 3); the mix is `T-REV-01`'s reviewer-noise measure.
- **Kernel and userland.** The kernel owns counting, the diff range, dedupe, the out-of-scope drop, the cap, the card and the `contract.review.*` kinds; `oracle.result` carries `round`, `range{from, to}` and `findings[]`. Userland owns the reviewer's prompt and class vocabulary (`provides.oracles` in the `paradigm` distro manifest); §3.4's L1 second reviewer (`reviewer:security`) is the same mechanism as one more oracle.

Convergence falsifiers: `G-REV-01` an agent-declared round is not counted; a release and reclaim, a respawn, a recompiled contract and a node split each keep the count · `G-REV-02` under the cap a failing reviewer or test returns the node and raises no card; at the cap the reviewer permit is refused `review-cap`, the TTL is suspended and exactly one card is raised (`knot` when only the reviewer disagrees, else `reconsider`) · `G-REV-03` from round 2 the reviewer's input is the last-reviewed..head diff plus the ledger; a finding outside the new hunks is dropped as `outOfScope` and filed `pre-existing`; a locus outside base..head is tagged `pre-existing` by the kernel · `G-REV-04` a repeated fingerprint is dropped and counted; `pass` is false while an `important` finding is open; `responses[]` are recorded on `contract.built` · `G-REV-05` no `reconsider` decision merges, approves or certifies; `extend` needs fresh presence, is refused by voice and is refused after two per lineage; `reject` takes the cached class.

### 7.4 CAS claims and the node lifecycle (A17-1, A23-1)

`contract.claim{op:'claim', ttl?, expect:{status:'open', seq}}` ⇒ `contract.claimed{expiresAt}` or `CAS_LOST`, where `expiresAt = now + ttl` (default 2 h) and a kernel timer (§3.5) releases the claim at expiry with `contract.released{reason:'expired'}` unless `built` arrived; `{op:'built', note?, responses?}` by the claimant ⇒ `contract.built` carrying the change note (the oracle trigger; from Move 2 the note is required at an effective risk tier of medium or above or a repo level of L1 or above, else `BAD_REQUEST{reason:'note-missing'}`, §3.4); `{op:'release'}` ⇒ `contract.released`. All `contract.*` kinds are kernel-owned and written by the verb handlers.

**Claim generations and lost-CAS reasons (TD-2026-10-19-648 item 11; reserved in the T-2026-10-19-001 move, built in M2.2).** `contract.claimed` carries `claimGen`, a per-node generation the kernel increments on every successful claim, so a later `built` or `release` names the claim it belongs to. A `CAS_LOST` refusal carries one of three reasons: `stale-claim` (the caller's expectation names a state or generation that is no longer current), `claim-expired` (the caller's claim lapsed at its TTL before it acted) and `claim-superseded` (the node was claimed again under a newer generation). Both are recorded in the kernel log: `claimGen` as a field of the kernel-written `contract.claimed` row, and each lost CAS with its reason as a kernel-written row beside the refusal, so a contested node's history is in the chain and not only in a caller's error.

### 7.5 Progress = exists ∧ passes

`contract.progress` projection `{exists, passes:{oracle}, certified, claimedBy}`; aggregate `{certified, total}`; rendered as the `contract.progress` block in the GUI; moves only on events.

### 7.6 Git-mode variant and KNOT routing

Identical compile; `certification.required` swaps `admission` for `merge-ci`. Merge conflicts → `merge-conflict` card, no KNOT. Oracle disagreement → `knot` card in both modes, except that a reviewer or test fail under the round cap returns the node to its claimant (§7.3) (KNOT = "oracles disagree"; a Warpline KNOT is the meaning-level special case). Calibration keyed `(repo, mode)` keeps them apart (§8.6).

---

## 8. Learning loop (TD-950 §8; `#learning-loop`, userland)

The loop reads only the kernel log — never the Shuttle view, never notebook YAML counters (12 §1). `#learning-loop` is userland, bound to the `paradigm` manifest's grants for `inject.* credit.* broke.* detector.* loop.row|open|closed tend.* decay.* calibration.*` (`loop.paused` is kernel-written, A17-3).

### 8.1 Credit, defined

**Credit** = an entry that was *injected* into a turn whose *outcome was accepted*. Three counts, each with its denominator:
- `injected` — `inject.receipt{hsid, turn, entries:[{ref, tier, source_hash}], frozenSliceHash}` logged for the turn by the injecting userland (orchestration path that today calls `recordNotebookReference`, `orchestration.ts:866-876`; the `orchestrationId` becomes `turn`) and, for every mod tool result a model sees (knowledge hits, `doodle.view` sheets and snapshots), by the `gnomon-mods` MCP bridge (§3.8). MCP notebook enrichment is thereby logged (S-M7).
- `credited` — `credit{turn, outcome:'accepted'}`: the turn's node certified, its PICK admitted, or `verdict.founder{accepted}` — never "the turn finished".
- `broke` — `detector.event{turn, class}` (§8.4) or later overturn (`grade.report` survival, `descriptors.ts:243-249`).
Today's `appliedCount` increments on INJECTION (`orchestration.ts:878-885` → `incrementApplied`, `notebook-loader.ts:426-442`) and the loader ranks by it (`notebook-loader.ts:148`) — a popularity loop (06 §1). 8.0 freezes `appliedCount` as a deprecated display field and ranks by `credited − broke`.

### 8.2 Re-enabling injection with credited ranking

`gnomon learn export` writes `.paradigm/learned/credit.jsonl` (a projection; A-OPEN-7 answered: file) with `{ref, injected, credited, broke, lastCredited, decayed, tier}`; `notebook-loader.ts` sorts by `credited − broke`, tie-break recency. One exploration slot in five is reserved for never-injected entries **restricted to T0/T1 or human-reviewed entries** (S-M7) so ranking cannot ossify on the seeded 28 (06 §6) without guaranteeing exposure to unreviewed material.

### 8.3 Decay write-back

60-day half-life after a 7-day grace, reset on credit; `confidence < 0.3 ⇒ query-only` (06 §5.2). Computed by `tend`, logged `decay.writeback{ref, from, to}`, and written to the entry's YAML `confidence` through the latest-wins path `reviseDown` uses (`notebook-loader.ts:465-515`).

### 8.4 Detectors and the teacher→skill flywheel (D23)

Detector classes (deterministic): `tests-fail-after-edit`, `tsc-fail-after-edit`, `warpline-knot`, `warpline-refusal`, `reviewer-dismissed`, `loop-thrash` (same files/symbols rewritten k=3 rounds with flat score, or a hunk reverted and re-applied — 06 §4), `repeat-symbol-failure`, and `local-model-failed` (a `model: local` session hit tool-call parse failure after repair, exceeded the compute permit, produced a non-passing oracle, or triggered `fallback`). The LLM contradiction judge and teacher drafting take `tend.llm`/`teacher.draft` permits; denials are learning signal. **Flywheel**: `detector.event{class:'local-model-failed'}` ⇒ a `teacher.draft` permit (Claude on the subscription, law 7, under the fuse) ⇒ the teacher writes the fix AND a candidate `SKILL.md` scoped to symbol/ecosystem (01 "Steal 1"; 06 §3) ⇒ staged at T3 ⇒ Classroom certifies (replay the saved `.scenario`, nearest-scenario regression, peer refutation, founder sign-off; first field break auto-overturns) ⇒ lands as notebook knowledge or `.claude/skills/<name>` through an `install` card (S-M6). Counts: `{drafted, certified, broke}` per class, with denominators. Failure to certify is itself logged (`tend.proposal.fate{dropped}`), so "5/5 survived" cannot hide (06 §3).

### 8.5 Loops and the loop ledger

Per round `loop.row{loopId, repo, iter, goal_ref, tests_passing, tsc_errors, diff_lines, files_touched, symbols_touched, tokens, verdict}` (06 §4). Progress = monotone improvement on something that cannot lie; past the thrash threshold the kernel pauses (`loop.paused`) and emits a detector event. Ship k=3 + budget cap.

### 8.6 Reviewer calibration, keyed by (repo, mode) — never pooled

For each `oracle.result{oracle:'reviewer', pass:true}` the loop checks survival (overturned PICK, detector on its symbols, founder reversal) and appends `calibration.reviewer{repo, mode, passes, laterBroke, window}` weekly; passes are weighted `1 − laterBroke/passes` when ranking contested nodes. ≥ 20 passes with 0 later-broke alongside a known field break ⇒ `uncalibrated` flag in the Shuttle view (12 §3). Git-mode and Warpline-mode rows are never pooled (12 §6). **Calibration and strictness (P8-D87, A35-13).** A `(repo, mode)` reviewer is **calibrated** once it has ≥ 20 passes in the window with `laterBroke` measured and the `uncalibrated` flag clear; until then — so for every repo before Move 3 — the repo's strictness floor is L1 (§3.4), and evidence alone never loosens below it. Thresholds carry between repos; evidence does not. Nodes reverted after landing (`merge.reverted`) and nodes named by a `verdict.incident` count as `laterBroke` and join the reviewer's daily eval set (n:incidents-as-reviewer-eval). `T-REV-01` (§20) is reported beside `laterBroke/passes`, which serves as its recall, and `T-STR-01` beside both. `G-STR-10`: a repo with an uncalibrated row has floor L1; a step below it needs the root key and logs `override: true`; once the row calibrates, the floor drops to the root-signed baseline and the level still loosens only on D88 evidence.

### 8.7 `tend` passes (law 5)

Trigger: ≥ 5 new learning events AND ≥ 10 min quiet, cancelled if the founder is active, ≤ once per hour (06 §2). Deterministic passes need no permit: dedupe (≥ 0.9 → propose merge), source-hash validation (drift ⇒ `stale` + ripple re-check), decay, calibration aggregate, nomination surfacing. LLM passes (contradiction judge, pairs sharing a symbol at ≥ 0.75) only under `tend.llm`. Output: `tend.digest{proposals}` + one `tend` card per proposal needing a human; T0/T1 never mutated without a card.

### 8.8 Liveness gates and the reader

`≥ 1 verdict.founder per working day`, `≥ 1 tend.digest per week`; violations append `liveness.violation` and light the Shuttle view, never block. **The reader** (`gnomon learn read`, scheduler-triggered) auto-applies deterministic proposals, turns the rest into cards, and logs `digest.consumed{digestId, applied, carded, dropped}`; a digest unconsumed for 7 days is a liveness violation — "nobody consumes the outputs" (12 §7) becomes measurable. The reader is also the **export writer** for sandboxed mods (A24-T2): it consumes `modns.doodle.export.requested{ds, kind, target, payload}` events, writes the lore entry, decision, task, contract-node evidence or Atelier finding under the `paradigm` grant, and appends `paradigm.doodle.exported{ds, kind, ref, requestedSeq}`; a request unconsumed for 24 h is a liveness violation.

### 8.9 Week-4 falsifiers

Credited reuse of loop-born entries ≥ 25 % of credited injections by week 4 and rising; repeat-failure rate per (symbol, class) halves after certification; certifications stop surviving at 100 % (06 §6; §20).

### 8.10 Shared knowledge search (`#knowledge-query`, a first-party mod; Loid 06 §2, 12; S25-H4, A24-4)

One **userland** read verb — never a kernel verb (law 3) — `knowledge.query({q, scope?: {repo, hsid, since}, minTier?, k?, providers?})`, served by the first-party **knowledge mod** (`packages/gnomon-mods/knowledge`, `#knowledge-query`, the one owner of the verb and of the provider registry): a mod on `mod-abi:v1` under the `mod` profile whose install card lists every read path its providers need (the notebook directories, the neurons vault, `.paradigm/lore/**`, `.paradigm/decisions/**`, the Steward memory store, `~/.gnomon/mods/doodle/index.db`), granted as `grant.capability{knowledge:provide, read:[paths]}` and added to its profile by the host — the only way a mod ever reads outside its own dir, and a human sees the list before it exists. **Provider runtime.** Two kinds, both without network (a provider manifest with an egress list is refused): **library providers** (`kind: provider`, first-party only — notebooks, neurons `n:`, lore `l:`, decisions `td:`, Steward memory) run inside the knowledge mod's child and read the granted paths; **mod providers** (`provides.knowledge` on any mod, e.g. Doodle's `doodle:`) are invoked by the knowledge mod through `kernel.call{verb:'mod.command'}`, which the host serves only to a mod holding `knowledge:federate` (the knowledge mod alone) and always under the original requester's principal, so a provider sees exactly what the requester could. Each provider answers with hits in one envelope: `{ref, owner, provenance: {source: authored | certified | observed | external, by}, trust: T0 | T1 | T2 | T3, confidence_live, last_validated, applied, broke, source_hash, why (the matched text, quoted), snapshot?, replay?, links[]}`. Trust tiers: T0 founder-confirmed or gate-certified; T1 peer-certified; T2 pipeline auto-promote; T3 external, forage or observed — always injected as untrusted; a doodle fact is `observed`/T3 evidence until a class promotes it; Steward memory enters at T2 at most. **What the federator enforces, in order** (`G-KNW-04`): (1) `requester` is stamped by the host from the caller's principal and hsid — a caller-supplied `requester` is `BAD_REQUEST`; (2) `scope` is clamped to the requester's redaction class (§9.3): a session queries only its own repo and hsid subtree, an agent its own subtree, the human everything; (3) every hit's `trust` is capped at the provider's `grant.trust` tier and at the provider's declared `maxTier` (a provider never self-describes its tier; clamps are counted `{clamped, of}`); (4) **redaction runs after providers answer**: a hit whose source event or file carries `sensitivity: secret`, or an `audience` beyond the requester's, or text from a `turn.event` the requester may not read, is dropped — the provider's own view is never the last word; (5) a hit missing provenance, tier or `source_hash` is dropped and counted `{dropped, of}` (`G-KNW-01`). The **neurons provider is off** unless the project's `.gnomon/project.yaml` lists it under `knowledge.providers` (the vault is the human's, not the repo's). Cross-agent reads reach a model only through the `gnomon-mods` MCP bridge, which logs `inject.receipt{requester, owner, refs[]}` so a later failure attributes across agents (`G-KNW-02`); the GUI palette and the CLI, which inject nothing into a model, log `modns.knowledge.query{requester, providers[], hits{n, of}}`; subagents get a frozen slice at spawn; model-visible ⟺ logged (§3.2 L2). The scoped phrasing **"check the doodles for X"** is `providers: ['doodle']`, and the answer is moments — `doodle:` refs whose `ref.resolve` hash matches the fold, with the mark's author named (`G-KNW-03`). Surfaces, all thin callers of the same mod command: the per-session `gnomon-mods` bridge exposes `knowledge.query` as a tool (the kernel's ≤ 12 proxy tools are untouched), `paradigm_retrieve` calls it over the session's socket, the GUI palette has `/knowledge <q>` and `/doodles <q>` (`mod.command`), the CLI has `gnomon knowledge <q>`. The query never writes to any store; ranking inside the learning loop stays `credited − broke` (§8.2); `tend` (§8.7) runs its deterministic passes over every provider; the `tend` digest names the provider of every proposal.

---

## 9. Observability (`~ample-internal-logs`)

### 9.1 Event taxonomy (closed per namespace; new kinds additive; default owner = kernel)

| Namespace | Kinds | Join keys | Owner | Default sensitivity (S32 §6) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `daemon` | start, exit{reason}, lock.refused, segment (log-only: the last row of each sealed segment, §3.2), reexec, anchor.lag{rows, which, bootPrev?, bootNow?}, anchor.failed{seq, kind}, log.gap{lostFrom, lostTo, lostHash, bootPrev, bootNow, fileLag}, log.quarantined{quarantinedLog, quarantinedHead{seq, hash, segment}, evidencePath, authorizedBy} (reserved, P8-D89; `evidencePath` a sensitive field) (the last four, with segment, are the reserved kinds: written only by the log, kernel-signed, §3.2) | — | kernel | `normal` |
| `socket` | connect, disconnect{reason}, hello{clientApi, surface}, auth.ok, auth.fail{reason: key\|token\|origin\|host\|deadline\|fetch-site\|rate}, auth.theft{connId, revokedConnId}, auth.replay{connId, consumedBy}, auth.reconnect.requested{connId, openGuiSessions}, web.listen{port, bundleHash}, web.bundle.mismatch{expected, found}, web.linger, web.stop{reason}, web.transport.exited{code}, gui.token.issued{connId, surface}, gui.session.ended{reason}, subscription.dropped{lag}, frame.dropped, frame.refused{code} | connId | kernel | `normal` |
| `key` | mint, rotate, registry.malformed{line} (or {file, reason} for the read-token file, P8-D89), registry.unsigned{line}, device.added, device.revoked, keystore.unavailable, token.revoked{tokenId} (a read-token revocation the kernel observed, P8-D89) | keyId | kernel (CLI) | `normal` |
| `config` | loaded{hash,signed}, unsigned, prefs{device, theme, layout}, ignored{field, source} (e.g. `voice.approvals`, `strictness` or `review` in `project.yaml`) | — | kernel | `normal` |
| `session` | open, alias, alias.rejected, refused{code}, spawned{…}, inject{origin: kernel\|client\|peer, hash, bytes, peerRootKeyId?, briefId?}, tainted{taint, by}, idle, respawn, exited, orphaned, routed{declared, chosen, reason}, sandbox{full\|partial}, layers.changed{which}, compacted{hsid, turn, reason, summaryHash, tokensBefore, tokensAfter} (P8-D93: payload closed, checked at append; `reason` ∈ threshold\|overflow\|manual\|unspecified, a runner value outside it recorded as unspecified; `summaryHash` a `sensitive` field) | hsid, cc alias | supervisor | `inject` bytes `sensitive`; else `normal` |
| `turn` | sent{permitId,contentHash,source?}, event{type,seq}, usage{tokens}, result{usage,cost,permission_denials}, partial{bytes}, steer, ask-user, plan | hsid, turn | supervisor | `event` bodies `sensitive`; else `normal` |
| `permit` | reserve, grant, deny, release | permitId, hsid | broker (signed) | `normal` |
| `budget` | usage{permitId, hsid, source, tokens, usd, user, repo, model, backend} \| usage{malformed, permitId, why} \| usage{overPermit, permitId, hsid, used, limits}, ladder{scope, id, dimension, rung, day, informational}, window{quotaError, hsid, used, of}; reserved and unused since P8-D92 (the meter writes none of them): pause, resume{by}, breaker.open, breaker.close, compute{machine, seconds}, window's `reservedForHuman` | permitId, hsid | broker (signed) | `normal` |
| `billing` | api.card.requested, api.active{hsid, until}, api.ended, api.refused{where} | cardId, hsid | broker (signed) | `normal` |
| `approval` | request{card, flags[], tool_input}, decide{decision,by,decisionSig,presence:fresh\|cached\|cli\|voice-armed,via,connId,cli{pid,ppid,pgid,tty},voiceDecision?}, unlock{connId}, unlock.dropped{connId, reason}, presence.denied, expire, rule.learned{tool,matchExact} (fresh presence only), suspect{reason}; voice (§14.7): voice.arm{armId, class, repos[], humanConnId, voiceConnId, custody, expiresAt, idleMs, maxDecisions, phrasesHash}, voice.disarm{armId, reason}, voice.intent{armId, cardId?, decision, turnId, handleHash?, challengeHash?, speakerId, speakerScore, asrConfidence, attest{ttsActive, otherRender, route}, attestedBy:'voice'}, voice.held{cardId, decision, armId, commitAt}, voice.withdrawn{cardId, by, connId}, voice.refused{reason, turnId, cardId?}, voice.policy.narrowed{repo?, hsid?, classes, by, connId}, voice.handle.rotated{cardId, reason}; strictness and run modes (§3.4, P8-D86–D88): strictness.raised{repo, from, to, cause, signals, score, configHash}, strictness.proposed{repo, from, to, evidence}, strictness.lowered{repo, from, to, cardId, override}, strictness.held{repo, level, reason}, mode.set{scope, from, to, by, presence}, auto{hsid, tool, path, inputHash, modeRef}; suspect reason `strictness-class` | cardId, hsid, connId, armId, repo | broker (signed) | field `request.tool_input` `sensitive`; else `normal` |
| `request` | grant{grant}, voice.arm{voiceConnId?}, voice.narrow{repo?, hsid?, classes}, brief.send{briefId, peer}, cowork.allow{hsid, peer}, link.revoke{peer}, strictness.set{repo, to}, mode.set{repo?, hsid?, to}, incident{refs[], severity} | connId | kernel (append exception: human on first-party human-class connections; voice for `request.voice.arm` only, §5) | `normal` |
| `link` (P8-R9, §3.9) | pair.offered{inviteHash, as, scopes}, paired{peerRootKeyId, relationship, scopes, until}, renewed{peerRootKeyId, until}, revoked{peerRootKeyId, by, connId?}, expired{peerRootKeyId}, connect, disconnect{reason}, auth.fail{reason}, frame.refused{frame, reason}, paused{reason}, rate{dim, n, of}, relay.unreachable{addr}, msg.in\|msg.out{msgId, from, to, hash, bytes, audience, sensitivity}, brief.drafted{briefId, by}, brief.sent{briefId, to, manifestHash}, brief.received{briefId, from, manifestHash}, brief.accepted{briefId, cardId, hsid}, brief.declined{briefId, reason}, brief.expired{briefId}, declassified{from, to, redactions[]}, scan.hit{ref, patterns{n, of}}, egress.refused{ref, reason} | peerRootKeyId, briefId, msgId, hsid, cardId | kernel (the link component; signed) | `normal` (bodies are blobs carrying their own item's labels) |
| `manifest` | load{hash,packageHash}, refuse{code: …\|SNAPSHOT\|LOCKFILE\|WIDENED}, widened{name, fields[]}, generate{files[]}, staged{hash,packageHash,by}, installed{hash,cardId}, uninstall{diff} | manifestHash | loader | `normal` |
| `schedule` | due{key}, dropped, tend.start/end, automation.due | key | scheduler | `normal` |
| `model` | capabilities{backend, model, tools, ctx}, probe{endpoint, ok}, repair{argument}, fallback.requested, fallback.decided | hsid | runner | `normal` |
| `mod` | installed{hash,cardId}, rolled-back{name, toPackageHash}, health{name, ok}, effect.refused{name, effect}, view.rendered{name, slot, ms}, stopped{reason}, sandbox{profile, full\|partial}, linked{name,path,packageHash}, snapshot.refused{name, reason, path}, paused{name,reason,files[]}, repinned{name,packageHash,dev}, dev.expired{name}, unlinked{name} | manifestHash, packageHash | mod-host | `normal` |
| `modns.<name>` | the kinds a mod declares in its manifest (first-party: `modns.workshop.*`, `modns.gnome.*`, `modns.doodle.*` incl. `export.requested`, `modns.knowledge.*`, `modns.cowork.*` incl. `out`, carried by the kernel under `link:send`, §3.9) | per mod | that mod's grant | as the manifest declares per kind; else `normal` |
| `voice` | §4.10: session.start, turn.user, turn.agent, deck, focus, tts.filtered, bargein, session.end, share, enrol, forget (enrol and forget as root-signed rows) | voiceSessionId, hsid, turnId | `p:<ns>/voice` grant (kernel validates shape only) | `turn.user` and `turn.agent` `sensitive`, and field `turn.user.audioRef` `sensitive`; `enrol` `secret` (it carries the voiceprint reference, §14.4); else `normal` |
| `contract` | registered{contractId}, claim, claimed{expiresAt}, released{reason?}, built{note?, responses?}, knot{nodeId,oracles,reopenOf?}, progress, review.returned{nodeId, round, openFindings}, review.capped{nodeId, rounds}, review.extended{nodeId, cardId}, review.restarted{nodeId, cardId} | contractId, nodeId, hsid | kernel | `normal` |
| `oracle` | run{oracle}, result{pass,evidence,ranBy,reviewer?,round?,range?,findings?} (signed) | nodeId, hsid | kernel | field `result.evidence` `sensitive`; else `normal` |
| `warpline` | pick, admit{status,shadow}, knot{payloadId}, resolve, selvage.moved — `ref:` wl | pickId, hsid, turn, nodeId | kernel | `normal` |
| `merge` | tree{clean}, conflict{paths}, ci{exit}, landed, reverted{landedRef, nodeId, detectedBy, trailer}, ref.rewritten{ref, kind} | hsid, nodeId | kernel | `normal` |
| `gate-class` | detected{paths}, decided | hsid, cardId | kernel | `normal` |
| `inject`/`credit`/`broke` | `inject`: receipt{entries,tier,source_hash,frozenSliceHash} (also written by the `gnomon-mods` bridge for mod tool results); `credit` (e.g. credit{outcome}) and `broke` (e.g. broke{class}) are **open** namespaces: any well-formed kind under them is valid | hsid, turn, contractId, entry ref | paradigm grant (bound to the learning loop and to `p:<ns>/mods-mcp`) | `normal` |
| `paradigm` (exports) | doodle.exported{ds, kind, ref, requestedSeq} | ds, ref | paradigm grant (the reader, §8.8) | `normal` |
| `detector` | event{class,evidence} incl. class `local-model-failed` | hsid, turn, symbols | paradigm grant | `normal` |
| `loop` | open, row{…}, closed (grant); paused{reason} (kernel) | loopId, hsid | paradigm grant (except `loop.paused`: kernel) | `normal` |
| `tend` | digest, proposal.fate, teacher.drafted | digestId, entry ref | paradigm grant | `normal` |
| `decay` | writeback | entry ref | paradigm grant | `normal` |
| `calibration` | contested, reviewer{repo,mode,passes,laterBroke} | entry ref, repo+mode | paradigm grant | `normal` |
| `verdict` | founder{cardId,accepted,note?,override?}, incident{cardId, refs[], severity} | cardId, nodeId, turn | broker (signed) | `normal` |
| `liveness` | violation{gate,lastSeenAt}, digest.consumed | digestId | scheduler | `normal` |
| `paradigm`/`flow`/`neurons` | event (bridged, `ref:`) | nativeId | their grants | `normal` |

Every number ships as `{n, of}`; a bare count in a result or block fails CI (`G-HON-03`). **Kind registry (PR-3).** The table is the registry: a kind it does not list is refused at append (there is no default kind), except under the three **open** namespaces `credit`, `broke` and `modns`, where any well-formed kind is valid and defaults to `normal` (a `modns.<name>.*` kind takes the label its manifest declares). **Default labels (S32 §6).** Every kind is appended with `audience: private` and the default sensitivity in the last column, set by the appending component, never by the payload's author; a kind missing from the table fails CI. A **field-level** label (`approval.request.tool_input`, `oracle.result.evidence`, `voice.turn.user.audioRef`) marks that field only: the row keeps its own label, and readers below the field's class receive the row with the field projected per §9.3. An all-`normal` log would make every egress filter leak (§3.9), so these defaults hold from Move 1 on.

### 9.2 Join keys

`hsid · turn (turn:<hsid>/<n>) · contractId · nodeId · permitId · cardId · keyId · deviceId · connId · entry ref · pickId · digestId · loopId · voiceSessionId · turnId · armId · deckId · manifestHash · packageHash · ds (doodle session) · peerRootKeyId · briefId · msgId`. Day-one learning rows are joinable on `hsid + turn + contractId + entry ref` (session.yaml rule 2).

### 9.3 Redaction matrix (one, total, tested — `G-ABI-05`)

| Principal class | `session.inject.bytes` | `turn.event` bodies | `approval.request.tool_input` | `oracle.result.evidence` | `voice.turn.*.audioRef` | everything else |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| human (full) | full | full | full | full | full | full |
| agent (roster) | own subtree | own subtree | own subtree | own nodes | never | full |
| session | own hsid | own hsid | own hsid | own nodes | never | own scope |
| mod (`p:<ns>/mod-*`) | never | never | never | never | never | own `modns.<name>.*` + the kinds its grant lists |
| mod with `turn.text:attached-session` (grant-named, install card lists it) | never | attached sessions' assistant and user **text parts only** — never `tool_input`, never tool results, never `session.inject` bytes | never | never | never | as mod |
| voice (`p:<ns>/voice`) | never | attached session only | never (its cards' kernel `voice.{label, readback, echo}` lines only, for repos whose grant is on) | never | own | attached session's events + `voice.*` + `approval.request` / `approval.voice.*` of enabled repos, minus live handles and challenges |
| read token | `{kind, hsid, at}` | same | same | same | never | full minus `secret` |
| community client (device key without `log.read:sensitive`) | `{kind, hsid, at}` | same | same | same | never | full minus `sensitive` and `secret` |
| peer (`p:<peerNs>/*`, §3.9) | never | never | never | never | never | never — a peer has no read path; it receives only the egress projection of what a human sent |

First-party surface (dogfood note, Aegis): a client's `surface` in `hello` is self-declared, so a CLI that names itself `cli` is still a community client on the socket and reads the community-client row above. A self-declared CLI surface counts as first-party only after M1.3 helper attestation. Until then the one first-party human surface is the GUI session that consumed a launch token (§11.2 rule 2). The DEV-ONLY dogfood launch path of §2.4 rule 6 changes nothing here: its CLI device connection is a community client.

Limited views (read token, community client, mod) carry no `hash`, `sig`, `prev`, `idem` or `refHash`, and a redacted field is `{redacted: true}`. The envelope `logId` (`log:<ULID>`, the genesis ULID of §3.2 and the `log.read` cursor of TD-2026-10-19-648) is shown to limited readers too; its ULID timestamp reveals when the log was created. This disclosure is intended: the creation time is not secret, and a cursor that hid it could not be resumed (Aegis L5). A digest over hidden content would confirm a dictionary guess (Aegis, M1.4). `audience`/`sensitivity` restrict reads further (a `secret` event is never streamed to a read token; `team`/`org` audiences are 8.1 projections). A human-class connection gets the full row only on a first-party surface (GUI page, wrapper, CLI) or a device key the human granted `log.read:sensitive` (S25-M5). `G-ABI-05` enumerates every (class, kind) pair. Card provenance in limited views (no `hash`/`sig`): see the card-provenance proposal in §12.8, pending founder ratification. Until their milestones the read projection refuses the **agent** class (Move 2) and the **voice** class (Move 4) with `UNSUPPORTED` rather than guess a row. **Voice handles and challenges** (§14.7) are in no row: before use the chain holds only `handleHash` / `challengeHash`, and the plaintext goes to exactly one reader — the arming human connection, rendered under capture exclusion — and appears in `approval.decide` only after use; every other class, the voice principal and browser-surface human connections included, never receives a live one.

### 9.4 Retention

Chain forever, segmented at 10⁶ rows; `session.inject` bytes forever; `voice.turn.*` text as blobs until the session's lore compaction exists + 90 days unless pinned by the human (S22-M2); `turn.event` bodies and oracle evidence 90 days, pruned with `blob.pruned{sha}` (A-OPEN-6); opt-in audio at its TTL only. Transcripts stay in `~/.claude/projects/<cwd>/<uuid>.jsonl`, referenced by `cc:`.

### 9.5 Library logging

Paradigm logger everywhere (`packages/paradigm-mcp/src/utils/mcp-logger.ts:3` `export const log = new ParadigmLogger(...)`; flow's Python port `headless.py:84`), never `console.log`. Operational logs (`~/.gnomon/logs/`) are diagnostic, not the kernel log; GUI diagnostics reach `~/.gnomon/logs/gui.log` through the web endpoint's size-capped `/diag` route and are never left to the browser console alone; CLI output is plain text through the `cli-output` helpers.

### 9.6 Metrics in `status`

`appendP99Ms`, `rssBytes`, `openSockets`, `liveSessions`, `unknownClaudeProcesses`, `permitsOpen`, `cardsPending`, `chainSeq`, `headAnchorOk`, `log{forcedAnchorRate{n, of}, fileAnchorLag, keystoreAnchorLag}` (§3.2), `lastSegmentAt`, `subscriptionDrops{n, of}`, `renderP99Ms` (GUI keystroke-to-paint, reported by the client), `guiSessions{n, bySurface:{wrapper, browser}}`, `unlocks{n}` (connections holding a cached unlock), `voiceArmed{class, until} | null`, `voiceDecisions{n, of}` (voice decisions of all decisions), `voiceArmCustody: supervised|client`, `linkedMods{n, dev}`, `webEndpoint{listening, port, bundleHash, lingering}`, `links{n, connected, paused}`, `linkOutbox{n}`, `linkKeyCustody: full|partial`, `clients{n, declaredConformant}`, `kernelKeyCustody: full|partial`, `presencePromptText: native|helper-window`, `localTtftMs`, `voiceToVoiceMs{p50, p95}`, `subscriptionWindow{used, of}`, `apiCreditsActive`, `modChildren{n}`, `sandbox{profile: full|partial}`.

**As shipped (amended M1.12 slice 3).** The flat names above are the design names; the shipped `status` nests them by owner, and the nested path is normative for clients: `chainSeq` is `log.seq` (with `log.segment`, `log.hash`); `cardsPending` is `cards.n`, with `cards.pendingForYou`, `cards.held` and `cards.byKind.*` as counts of part of `cards.n`; `liveSessions` is `sessions.n`; `permitsOpen` is `budget.permits.n`; `openSockets` is `daemon.connections`; `subscriptionWindow{used, of}` is `budget.subscriptionWindow.used{n, of}`; `guiSessions{n, …}` is `web.guiSessions.n`; and subscriptions report `subscriptions{n, onThisConnection, perConnectionMax, backlogCap}`. Every numeric leaf of `status` is declared in the kernel's status-number table (`packages/gnomon/src/kernel/socket/status-numbers.ts`) as `{n, of}`, a declared gauge, a part of a named denominator, an ordinal, a configured limit, a duration or a latency (which must name its method and machine); an undeclared number fails the honesty CI. The remaining metrics of the list above are not shipped yet.

### 9.7 Honesty CI (law 2)

`G-HON-01` compiler reproduces Appendix A · `G-HON-02` no fabricated mechanisms (grep for `Math.random` outside ids/tests, `simulated`, fake `sleep(`, hardcoded gains, forbidden vocabulary) · `G-HON-03` denominators · `G-HON-04` manifest fixed point + reversible install · `G-HON-05` no Task-steering descriptions · `G-HON-06` guard names state scope · `G-HON-07` every latency number in docs or `status` names its measurement method and machine. The `gnomon guide` check (§5, TD-2026-10-19-648 item 10) joins this set when T-2026-10-19-015 builds it: a guide that names a verb, flag or refusal reason the frozen descriptors lack fails the build; its test id is assigned then.

---

## 10. The seven laws as testable invariants

| # | Law | Invariant | Falsifier test (scope stated) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | No spawn without a permit | every `session.spawned`/`respawn`/`turn.sent` has an earlier unconsumed `permit.grant`; spawn takes `permit:v1` as a required argument; local sessions consume compute permits | `G-LAW-01` (5-session fan-out ⇒ 5 grants, each permit attributed; P8-D92), `G-LAW-01b` (no env/config/project/CLI setting changes the meter or bypasses law 7 for kernel-spawned sessions), `G-LAW-01c` (6 local sessions ⇒ 6 compute permits, each recorded with model and project; P8-D92) |
| 2 | Honesty is CI | §9.7 in `npm test` and the release gate | `G-HON-01..07` |
| 3 | Tiny ABI | 12 kernel verbs + 3 human-device verbs (P8-D98) + 2 client relays (the whole client-callable surface); ≤ 12 proxy tools; mod ABI ≤ 8 RPCs + lifecycle (`init`, `shutdown`, `cancel`) + the same 2 relays; `gnomon-link:v1` closed at 10 frames and callable by no client (§3.9); `abiId`, `modAbiId`, `clientApiId`, `kitId` and `linkApiId` frozen per release and published, each moving only when its own schema moves | `G-ABI-01`, `G-ABI-04`, `G-MOD-01`, `G-CLI-02` |
| 4 | Reversible by construction | one repo dir (`.gnomon/`, `project.yaml` committed, `worktrees/` + `sessions/` ignored) + one home dir; markers; uninstall exact; learned state exported or intact | `G-LAW-04` on macOS and Windows |
| 5 | Nothing spends by default | fresh install, 24 h, zero `permit.grant`; spending scheduler jobs are manifest `opt-in`; local automations spend compute only after a human marks them `attended:false` + `model`; a link peer causes no spend — inbound co-work is pull-only and a brief spends only after its human accept (§3.9) | `G-LAW-05`, `G-LAW-05b` |
| 6 | Trust posture | no install scripts in `@a-company/gnomon*`; exact pins; `npm pack` lists only `dist`; signatures verified (helpers by checksum + code signature); `alpha` never `latest`; mods never load without a grant row; the GUI bundle fetches nothing at runtime | `G-LAW-06` |
| 7 | **Subscription only** (`^subscription-only`, D35) | no Claude process starts if any env/settings layer would bill API credits; the only API path is a per-use root-signed `api-token` card with a persistent indicator and `billing.api.active` events; the meter records subscription-window usage (P8-D92: no human share is reserved); settings layers are watched mid-session; `gnomon-local` endpoints are loopback/LAN only | `G-SUB-01` (each of `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, `apiKeyHelper`, `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, any `*_BASE_URL`, `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_BEDROCK\|VERTEX\|FOUNDRY`, an `mcp.json` env block, a managed-policy source, AND a loopback base-URL override — the former local-endpoint probe case (D37) — in each layer ⇒ `api_billing_refused`), `G-SUB-02` (agent/mod/voice cannot request an `api-token` card), `G-SUB-03` (indicator + events present for the card's whole validity), `G-SUB-04` (subscription-window usage measured and shown in `status`; no reserve and no pause, P8-D92), `G-SUB-05` (a settings layer written mid-session ⇒ the session is stopped within 2 s and `billing.api.refused` logged), `G-SUB-06` (`gnomon-local` configured with a non-loopback/LAN endpoint ⇒ `BILLING_REFUSED`) |

---

## 11. The GUI (`#gui`) — Gnomon's shell on localhost (P8-D50, D40–D44, D51)

### 11.1 Role and topology

**Role.** The primary shell for local and Claude sessions: a terminal-like web page — keyboard-first, prompt-centred, a command palette that reaches everything — served by the kernel's web endpoint (§11.2) on `127.0.0.1` and opened by `gnomon open` in the **desktop wrapper by default** (P8-D56) or, with `gnomon open --browser`, in the user's default browser. It is a pure client of `gnomon-rpc:v1` over a loopback WebSocket: the kernel owns sessions; the GUI renders, composes, decides (through the broker, §3.4) and never spawns a process itself. Several GUI connections (the wrapper, a tab, constellation) attach to the same session concurrently; `turn.partial` streams to all; closing the window or tab leaves every session running and re-attachable (`G-GUI-05`). The terminal keeps the CLI only (`gnomon spawn | status | approve | replay | open | mod | knowledge | doodle …`, P8-D51): there is no in-terminal UI, and CLI output is plain text projected from the same blocks (`kit.Image` prints its `alt`, `kit.Canvas` prints the mark list).

**Desktop wrapper (`packages/gnomon-wrapper`, Move 1, the default daily surface — P8-D56, S25-M2).** A thin Tauri shell (Rust over the system WebView: WKWebView on macOS, WebView2 on Windows; small footprint; signed with OPEN 1's identity) that `gnomon open` spawns as a child, handing it `{port, launchToken, kernelPublicKey, bundleHash}` **on stdin** — nothing on argv, nothing in the environment. The wrapper navigates its WebView in-process to `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/#launch=<token>&k=<spki>`, so the page's origin is exactly the browser's origin and rule 3 has one origin to lock; **Tauri IPC is disabled for page content** (no global `__TAURI__`, an empty capability set for the page's window, no `invoke` reachable, remote-domain IPC off) so the page has nothing the browser page lacks and a compromised page has no native surface; the wrapper verifies `serverSig` natively with the key from stdin and refuses to render on a mismatch (closing the port-reuse-after-restart hole for the daily surface, S25-M4); it adds a dock/taskbar presence, native notifications, the Touch ID / Windows Hello Reconnect flow and — reserved for M4.6 — a **wrapper-owned native panel** for the handle and challenge region (a WKWebView/WebView2 cannot exclude part of itself from capture, S29-K8), and nothing else; the page never knows which host it runs in beyond `hello.surface`. Electron is not used (law 6 footprint). Store distribution is 8.1 (§21); the Tauri version and its WebView origin behaviour are pinned and confirmed in M1.0. `G-GUI-12`: the wrapper passes `G-WEB-01..06`, page content cannot reach any Tauri command, and a forged `serverSig` leaves the window blank with the error shown. Stated residual (M1.13, Aegis C1): the native `serverSig` check defends against port squatting and a replayed hello; the node binary and the kernel tree are pinned by hash at build time inside the signed app and re-hashed before every spawn, which defends against a modified checkout or node only up to that spawn — a same-user process can still swap the files between the check and node's reads, the same-user-writer posture of §15.1.

**Browser surface (available, badged).** `gnomon open --browser` opens the same page in the default browser (per-OS launch in §11.2 rule 2); the page shows a persistent **extension-risk badge** in the header: a browser extension with all-sites access can read page memory and the fragment, send trusted-looking events and use this connection's cached unlock (S25-L1; threat row 16). The badge is kernel-owned chrome (§4.5) and cannot be hidden by a layout preset; `status.guiSessions[].surface: browser` names such sessions. The browser path is removed, not weakened, if `G-WEB-01..06` cannot pass on both OS (§20 kill criterion e).

**Renderer.** A single-page app in `packages/gnomon-gui` (`@a-company/gnomon-gui`; React, because the Doodle canvas engine requires it, §12.7; every dependency pinned exact, law 6; nothing fetched from any CDN at runtime — fonts, icons and the canvas engine with its fonts and assets are bundled), with: a **virtualized transcript** (only visible rows mount; row heights cached per width — deepseek-harness's long-session lesson, commit `10bb9cbf4a`, 21, carried into the DOM), streaming Markdown through a sanitizing renderer with raw HTML disabled, per-hunk diffs (`kit.Diff`), the composer with history, kill ring, undo and paste collapse. Every string reaches the DOM as a text node, never as markup (`G-GUI-07`); bidi controls render as visible glyphs and hidden characters are stripped and badged (`G-GUI-11`). The page holds the GUI token and the kernel public key in JavaScript memory and writes **nothing secret to any storage** (D54); the only stored item is the kernel public key pin. Browser matrix: Safari, Chrome and Firefox on macOS; Edge, Chrome and Firefox on Windows — no feature requires a particular browser; WebCrypto Ed25519 is a matrix minimum, with a bundled, pinned pure-JS verifier as the fallback (`G-GUI-03`). Diagnostics are posted to the endpoint's size-capped `/diag` route and land in `~/.gnomon/logs/gui.log`; the browser console is never the only record.

First run on screens (T-2026-10-19-005 MVP-b): a setup fragment `#setup=<token>&k=<setup key>[&cwd=…]` makes the pinned bundle show the setup screens instead of the app — welcome, read-only checks with their fixes and a purge button per leftover generation, the name (checked by the kernel), the two identity prompts announced up front as exactly two, each with the expected digest shown large and grouped and the instruction that the Touch ID dialog's last line must begin with `Gnomon Helper: root key signs 1: <digest>`, the result (root key id, principal, device, file locations) and the hand-off to the first session (the terminal prints the launch URL; the app continues over its channel). Every string is set as text; a click only requests the next step; the page sends only the name and a generation the kernel listed. Inside the app window the digest is trustworthy because the wrapper verified the bundle pin and the setup key natively before loading the page; in a browser started from a terminal, the terminal is the surface to trust.

### 11.2 Localhost web security model (`#web-endpoint`)

Threat: a page in another tab of the same browser, a same-user process using the browser, or a **sandboxed session** on this machine tries to reach the kernel through the GUI's endpoint or to become the human principal at attach time (S25 verdict). Every rule below is a kernel rule enforced in `#web-endpoint` — decided in the kernel on records the `gnomon-web` transport child forwards (§2.1, A24-1) — and every one has a test that runs on both OS.

1. **Bind.** `127.0.0.1` only, IPv4 literal, ephemeral port chosen by the OS when the kernel listens; the transport child never listens (§15.5) — the kernel accepts each connection and hands the connected socket, unread, to the child; the root-signed config has no field for an address or interface, so no setting, env var or flag can widen it; IPv6 loopback is not bound and the page is always opened by IP literal, never by the name `localhost` (`G-WEB-01`). Kernel linking (§3.9) never widens this endpoint: it is a separate, outbound-only transport child under the `link` profile, and a peer never reaches this endpoint or the socket.
1a. **Bundle pin.** The GUI bundle directory and its tree hash `gui.bundleHash` are root-signed config (`gnomon gui pin`, run by the installer under the root key and again at every upgrade); the transport child hashes the directory at start and the kernel refuses to open the endpoint on a mismatch (`socket.web.bundle.mismatch`) — a same-user write into `dist/` can therefore not replace the human-class GUI without a root-signed re-pin (A24-1, `G-WEB-08`).
1b. **Transport child.** HTTP parsing, the upgrade, RFC 6455 framing (details in §4.9) and static-file serving run in `gnomon-web`, spawned from the kernel's own package under the `transport` profile (§15.5: no socket of its own — it serves the connected sockets the kernel hands it over its IPC channel; read the pinned bundle, stdio to the kernel, nothing else — no keys, no home, no exec); it forwards `{connId, method, path, headers}` for HTTP and `{connId, frame}` for WebSocket text frames, and writes back only what the kernel tells it; a crash or a malformed-input exploit in the child reaches no kernel memory and no key; the kernel restarts it (cap 3/10 min) and every open GUI connection sees a Reconnect (`G-WEB-07`). It does relay every GUI frame, so a compromised child is a compromised set of GUI sessions — human-class connections, bounded by the transport profile, the 12 h session cap and `gnomon open --revoke-all` (§A.7); the kernel validates every record it sends and kills it on the first one out of shape.
2. **Launch token (D55).** `gnomon open` (device-key-authenticated on the unix socket / named pipe) asks the kernel for `{port, launchToken, kernelPublicKey}`; the kernel starts the endpoint if it is not listening, mints a 32-byte random **single-use** token with a 60 s TTL bound to the asking connection's `deviceId`. The token is **never on any argv, environment or file**. Until §5 names a wire verb for this request, the production launcher is `gnomon up` (§2.4 rule 7), which runs the kernel in its own process and asks the in-process path directly, so the socket ABI does not move. The private channel is per surface and OS:
   - **Wrapper (default, both OS):** the CLI spawns `gnomon-wrapper` and writes `{port, launchToken, kernelPublicKey, bundleHash}` to its stdin; the wrapper navigates its WebView in-process to `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/#launch=<token>&k=<spki>`.
   - **Browser, macOS:** the CLI pipes the token to the signed helper on stdin; the helper builds the URL and hands it to LaunchServices (`LSOpenCFURLRef` / `NSWorkspace.open`), which delivers it to the default browser as a `GetURL` Apple Event — not as a process argument, even when the browser must first be launched. M1.0 verifies that no process on the box observes the URL on a command line during this hand-off. Until that helper verb lands, the terminal launchers use the same LaunchServices hand-off through `/usr/bin/osascript -` with `open location "<url>"` on its **stdin** (§2.4 rule 10): the URL rides osascript's stdin, never its argv or env, and LaunchServices delivers it as the same `GetURL` Apple Event.
   - **Browser, Windows:** `ShellExecute` puts a URL on the browser's command line, so the helper opens the **token-less** URL `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/`; the page shows **Connect**, and the attach runs through the presence prompt of rule 2a (one Windows Hello touch at `gnomon open --browser`). The kernel public key reaches the page in the Reconnect answer after presence and is pinned then. M1.0 probes whether WebView2 or Edge offer a private URL hand-off; until one is proven, presence is the Windows browser path.
   The fragment is read once and **cleared with `history.replaceState`** before any other script runs (S25-L2), so it appears in no server log, no `Referer`, no history entry and no page memory beyond the two values held in JavaScript. The page exchanges the token in the WebSocket `auth` frame for a **GUI session token** (32 bytes, JavaScript memory only — never a cookie, never `sessionStorage`, never `localStorage`); the exchange consumes the launch token. **A launch token presented a second time inside its 60 s TTL is theft** (D55): the kernel refuses `AUTH`, **revokes the GUI session that consumed the token** (`gui.session.ended{reason:'theft'}`), logs `socket.auth.theft{connId, revokedConnId}`, and raises a fresh-presence `gate-class` card telling the human which connection was revoked and why (`G-WEB-05`). **After the TTL** a consumed token presented again is a replay — browser history and session-restore files can keep the fragment (S29-K7) — and is refused `AUTH` and logged `socket.auth.replay{connId, consumedBy}` only: no revoke, no card. The TTL is the cut because the attach race of rule 2b can only happen inside it (an unconsumed token dies at its TTL), so D55's theft signal is kept whole, while a stale replay can no longer revoke the legitimate session or raise cards on demand; a rule keyed on `hello.surface` was rejected because the surface is client-declared. Sessions are also denied reads of every browser profile and the wrapper's WebView data directory (§15.5), so a session cannot harvest the fragment from those files. The GUI session authenticates as the human principal on the launching device with `client:'gui'` and `surface` stamped on every event it causes; it lives exactly as long as its WebSocket — it ends when the socket closes (no reconnect grace, D54), on `gnomon open --revoke-all`, on daemon exit, or after 12 h; `status.guiSessions{n}` lists them with `connId` and `surface`. The page verifies `serverSig` against the kernel public key from the fragment (§4.9, S25-M4); a fragment key that differs from the page's pin arrived through the authenticated `gnomon open` channel and re-pins visibly (§4.9: sticky kernel-owned notice with both keyIds, a `/diag` line); its second anchor is that the port and the token come from the kernel itself over the already-authenticated socket, so a same-user listener squatting a loopback port before the kernel never receives the token. Stated residual: a same-user **unsandboxed** process that reads the launching CLI's memory, or the browser's profile files, within 60 s can take the token — that actor already owns the device key (§A.1); a sandboxed session cannot, because the `session` profile denies it both the endpoint port and other processes' information (rule 2b). On macOS, an app a session launches through LaunchServices `open` runs unsandboxed; that is the accepted residual of §A.2 (P8-D79), and it still meets this rule's single-use token and rule 2a's presence prompt.
2a. **Reconnect (D54).** A page without a GUI token (after a reload, a transport-child restart, a daemon restart, or the Windows browser launch) shows **Reconnect** and nothing else; on the user's click it opens the WebSocket, verifies `hello` against the pinned kernel public key where it has one, and sends `auth{reconnect:true}`; the kernel raises a Touch ID / Windows Hello presence prompt whose text, composed from the kernel's own record, names the new `connId`, the surface and the count of open GUI sessions (`approval.request` of kind `permission` with `requestedBy: p:<ns>/kernel`); on presence the kernel answers `{guiToken, principal, connId, kernelPublicKey}` and logs `socket.auth.reconnect.requested{connId}` then `socket.gui.token.issued`; a denial logs `approval.presence.denied` and closes the socket. Reconnect is rate-limited per connection (rule 8). Nothing the page stored made this possible: the boundary is the human's touch (`G-WEB-08`).
2b. **Sessions cannot race the attach (D55, S25-C1).** The `session` profile (§15.5) denies every kernel-spawned session and its tool children (a) outbound loopback connections to the web endpoint's port (`sandbox-exec` `network-outbound` remote-port deny on macOS; on Windows a WFP `FWPM_LAYER_ALE_AUTH_CONNECT` filter on the port whose key is chosen by probe S29-08 — no WFP condition matches a job object (S29-K3) — see §16) (b) information about other processes, and (c) unix-socket connects to the user's tmux and screen servers, which would run a command outside the sandbox. On macOS (b) is `process-info*` denied except `self`, the `kern.proc` sysctls denied, **and `mach-lookup` of `com.apple.sysmond` denied**: entitled tools such as `pgrep` read the whole process table, argv included, from the root `sysmond` daemon over XPC, and no process-info or sysctl rule reaches that path (p10m). (c) is a `network-outbound` unix-socket deny on `/private/tmp/tmux-<uid>` and the screen socket directory, with every path realpath'd. On Windows, (b) is `tasklist`/WMI/`NtQuerySystemInformation` denied through the restricted token and the helper, reported `partial` where a read cannot be denied (Windows parity phase, P8-D78). Claude Code works under this profile (p10m-claude-compat). On macOS the profile also denies `process-exec` of `/usr/bin/open`, `/usr/bin/osascript` and `lsregister` (in `LaunchServices.framework`'s `Support/` directory), every path realpath'd (P8-D80). It also denies (T-2026-10-18-028 M5) `process-exec` of any `GnomonHelper.app` bundle and any file named `gnomon-helper`, and `file-read*` of the bundle, by regex on the resolved path, so a session can neither run the signed helper nor copy it out; a bare copy of the executable is killed by the OS, and the helper itself refuses to serve under any sandbox. Not covered on macOS, and stated in §A.2: a launcher the session builds itself that calls LaunchServices directly (D79 accepted, D80 hardened) and same-user signals. A session that connects to the endpoint is refused at the socket layer and logged `socket.auth.fail{reason:'sandbox'}` when the kernel can attribute it (peer pid in the same job or pgid as a live session, A.1 scope). `G-SBX-08` runs on both OS (macOS in the macOS pass, Windows in the parity phase, P8-D78). It asserts the port deny, that `pgrep` sees no other process, the tmux/screen socket denies and a symlinked-path fixture (realpath); it never counts a failing `ps` as evidence.
3. **Origin lock.** Every WebSocket upgrade and every non-static HTTP request must carry `Origin: http://127.0.0.1:<port>` exactly — the one origin, for the wrapper and the browser alike (D56, S25-M2); a missing or different `Origin` ⇒ `403` with no body and `socket.auth.fail{reason:'origin'}` (`G-WEB-02`). Static files are public bytes of a public npm package and are served without an Origin check, with the headers of rule 6.
4. **DNS rebinding.** `Host` must equal `127.0.0.1:<port>`; anything else — `attacker.example:<port>` resolved to 127.0.0.1, `localhost:<port>`, a bare IP — ⇒ `421 Misdirected Request` and `socket.auth.fail{reason:'host'}` before any routing (`G-WEB-03`).
5. **CSRF.** The endpoint sets no cookie and reads none; the only credential is the GUI token carried in the WebSocket `auth` frame and, on the two non-static HTTP routes (`/diag`, `/blob/<sha>` for canvas images), in the `X-Gnomon-Token` header — so an ambient-credential request from another origin carries nothing; `<img src>` cannot send that header, so both routes are `POST` — a same-origin `GET` carries no `Origin`, so rule 3 could not hold on it — sent with `referrerPolicy: 'same-origin'` (under the page's `no-referrer` policy a `POST` would carry `Origin: null`), and `GET`/`HEAD` on them is refused `405`; the page fetches a blob with a `POST` `fetch` + the header and shows it through a `blob:` object URL, revoked when the block unmounts (A24-1); additionally, where `Sec-Fetch-Site` is present it must equal `same-origin` or `none` on every non-static request and on the WebSocket upgrade, else `403` + `socket.auth.fail{reason:'sec-fetch-site'}`; on the two non-static HTTP routes the header must be present — a request without it is refused `403` (S25-L3); on the WebSocket upgrade a missing header is accepted, because Chrome sends no Fetch Metadata on the handshake (first dogfood run, 2026-10-19) and every browser sends `Origin` there, which rule 3 already locks to `http://127.0.0.1:<port>` exactly (`G-WEB-04`). A cross-site form POST, `fetch` or WebSocket from another page performs no state change and is logged (`G-WEB-04`). Every kernel verb travels on the authenticated WebSocket only; there is no HTTP RPC.
6. **Headers and CORS.** No `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` on any response, ever (there is no cross-origin consumer); `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; script-src 'self'; style-src 'self'; img-src 'self' blob:; font-src 'self'; connect-src ws://127.0.0.1:<port> http://127.0.0.1:<port>; worker-src 'self'; frame-src 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'none'`; `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin`, `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-origin`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, `Cache-Control: no-store` on non-static routes; the page has no inline script and no `eval`; `data:` is not in `img-src`, so canvas images travel as blobs (§12.7); the CSP is never loosened without a `G-WEB-06` case for the loosening, and the Excalidraw page load under this CSP is one of its fixtures (S25-M3); `/blob` serves `image/png`, `image/jpeg` and `image/webp` only, never SVG; the static route path-normalizes and confines to the pinned bundle directory (`G-WEB-06`, including a traversal case and a script-in-block-string case shared with `G-GUI-07`).
7. **WebSocket auth deadline.** After the upgrade the first frame must be `hello` and the second `auth`, both within 5 s, or the socket closes; any other first or second frame closes the socket (`1008`) without being dispatched; the `gnomon-rpc:v1` frame rules apply unchanged (4 MiB, one JSON-RPC message per text frame, backpressure, redaction by principal class — the GUI is the human class, §9.3). Approvals from the GUI go through the broker: `approve` is signed by the hardware approvals key the kernel holds (presence per class, cached unlock per connection, §3.4); the page holds no key, ever.
8. **Rate and size (S25-M6).** Token exchange and Reconnect ≤ 5 attempts per minute **per connection**, after which that connection is closed; the endpoint holds at most 64 connections and, at the cap, closes the oldest **unauthenticated** one — never an authenticated GUI connection — so no local process can lock the real exchange out; 4 MiB frame cap; `/blob/<sha>` serves only blobs the GUI session's principal may read (§9.3) and only by content hash.
9. **Windows parity.** Identical code path (`node:http` in the transport child on `127.0.0.1`); browsers Edge/Chrome/Firefox; the wrapper is WebView2; the browser launch is the token-less URL through the signed helper (`ShellExecute`) followed by Reconnect — the CLI is not sandboxed, and the `session` profile's `ShellExecute` deny stays for sessions (on macOS the profile denies exec of the stock launchers `open`, `osascript` and `lsregister`, but no rule stops a self-built launcher calling LaunchServices: an accepted residual, §A.2, P8-D79/D80); `G-WEB-01..08` run on both OS.
10. **One origin.** The wrapper loads the page from `http://127.0.0.1:<port>` like the browser does and registers nothing; there is no second origin, no `tauri://` scheme in the lock, and `status.guiSessions[].surface` is the only way the kernel tells them apart (D56, S25-M2).
11. **Setup mode.** An unprovisioned home is set up through a separate loopback endpoint with the same transport child, rules 1 and 3–8, CSP and pinned bundle, whose dispatcher speaks only the closed `gnomon-setup:v1` protocol (`hello`, `auth{setupToken}`, `setup.preflight`, `setup.name{name}`, `setup.begin`, `setup.purge{gen}`, `setup.complete`, `setup.finish`; events `setup.state` and `setup.presence{n,of,purpose,digest12}`), with its own `setupApiId`; no `gnomon-rpc:v1` verb is reachable and `clientApiId` does not move. An ephemeral Ed25519 setup key signs `serverSig`, and the page never stores it as the kernel pin. The setup token is 32 bytes, lives 60 s, is single use and is carried only in the URL fragment on the launch surface (the TTY line, or the wrapper's channel line `{t:'setup',port,setupToken,setupPublicKey,bundleHash,expiresAt}`), never in argv, env, a file or a log; a reuse inside the TTL aborts the provisioning and writes an identity-journal line. At most one setup connection exists; its close aborts the provisioning at the next step boundary, which cleans up. Every params object (the hello's too) has an exact key set, so the page carries only the name and a server-listed gen, and a row or digest from the page is refused; the provisioner composes every row and digest, and the same inputs commit the same artifacts as the terminal path. The endpoint never hangs: it settles on `setup.finish` (after a commit, a completion, or on a home that is already fully set up), on the token expiring unused (the terminal then prints a fresh link), on an idle cap after auth, on the socket closing, on the transport stopping and on a token reuse; a refused purge (a generation another home named by GNOMON_HOME lives on is never purged) or a failed begin returns to the checks, naming a generation it left. After `setup.finish` the endpoint stops and the normal launch (rule 2) follows; the wrapper navigates only after a native preflight of the setup key and bundle pin, locked to that one port, and the page reaches no wrapper command.
12. **Backpressure watermarks and delivery unknown (T-2026-10-19-009).** Every outbound stream has a high and a low watermark on its unsent bytes. On the GUI WebSocket the transport child reports `pressure` above the high mark (1 MiB) and `drain` only once the connection is back at or below the low mark (256 KiB), and still closes it 1008 past 8 MiB. The unix socket pauses reading above its high mark (1 MiB) and resumes at or below its low mark (256 KiB). The transcript stream (`log.read{follow}`) stops at the high mark and resumes only at or below the low mark, so a reader hovering between the two never makes it flap. When a connection drops after a request was written and before its answer, the client call layers (the GUI page, the TypeScript client and the kernel's own CLI client) report a distinct `delivery-unknown`: the kernel may or may not have acted, so the caller re-reads state before it retries. It is never reported as success, and never as `connection-closed`, which stays the answer for a call made after the close and so never sent. Both reasons are client-local and never cross the wire. A client-side timeout is exactly as delivery-ambiguous as a dropped connection: the frame may have reached the kernel and been acted on, and bytes still buffered when the timer fires keep flushing afterwards, so no client can prove a timed-out call unsent. Clients report it as a timeout (`ETIMEDOUT`, GUI `TIMEOUT{call-timeout}`), and a caller treats it like `delivery-unknown`: it re-reads state before retrying a non-idempotent verb (every write: `approve`, `permit.reserve`, `session.open`, `session.send`, `session.stop`, `log.append`, `contract.register`, …).
