**Residual: `gui.open` over the on-disk device key (Aegis M1, T-2026-10-19-001 A3).** Until M1.3 custody puts the device key in hardware, the device key is a file under `<home>/keys/`. Any same-user process that can read it can authenticate a device connection on the unix socket and call `gui.open`, so it can mint a launch grant and attach a GUI session as the human principal, without presence. The boundary is the generated `session` profile (§15.5): it read-denies `<home>/keys/**` (`denyRead` and again in `denyReadAlways`, after every allow), so no kernel-spawned session or its tool children can read the key or the identity record (falsified in `test/session-sandbox.test.ts`, `G-SBX-08`). The transport child's profile forbids the same tree. A same-user process **outside** every Gnomon sandbox (an app the human runs, or one escaped through LaunchServices, §A.2 D79/D80) can read the file and is not stopped by this rule; presence on gated actions (§3.4) still holds there. The rule-8 cap share bounds how many grants such a caller can mint.

The page is a client of the same API as every other client; nothing in the GUI's path is a privilege the Python or Swift clients lack — a cached unlock belongs to any human-class connection that passed presence, whatever the client (§3.4).

### 11.3 Look: papercraft by day, phosphor by night (D40)

Light mode = **papercraft** (Paper atelier: cut-paper sheets with grain, ink lines, folded paper caps, pressable ink keys); dark mode = **phosphor terminal** (mono, glow, bracketed keys, glyph caps); optional **automatic day/night swap** following the OS `prefers-color-scheme` or a schedule. The user's choice is stored by the kernel per device (`config`, root-signed is not required for preferences — they are `config.prefs` rows signed by the device key), not in the browser, so every tab and the wrapper agree. Rules measured in the aesthetic session: every key edge ≥ 3:1 (the Shadow-instrument candidate's edges measured under 3:1 and was superseded); project hues are fills only, never text (sage measured 3.96:1 on paper) — `G-GUI-10` is a fixture contrast test on both themes (fixtures: `docs/specs/gnomon-fixtures/contrast.json`, checker `check-contrast.mjs`; tokens from `.atelier/design-system.yaml` `gnomon.colour`). Colour is never the sole signal; `prefers-reduced-motion` freezes every decorative motion (breeze, paper fly-in) and makes emotes instant pose changes. Fonts are bundled (law 6). The session's open boards — colour (soft project hues + a failure colour), type (papercraft serif vs all-mono), motion specs including the paper fly-in, CLI voice, and the decision-stack + sidebar render — are OPEN 9 and close **before Move 1 starts**: the final themes and the `G-GUI-10` contrast fixtures are a Move 1 entry precondition (P8-D57, §19.1). Reference renders: `.atelier/sessions/2026-10-15-gnomon-aesthetic/refs/papercraft.html`, `styles.html`, `workshop-mod.html`, `walkthrough.html`.

### 11.4 Chrome, navigation, decisions (D42, D43, D44)

**Chrome principle (D42).** Header, footer, sidebars and mod slots show only what is necessary; everything is reachable from the **command palette** (`⌘K` / `Ctrl+K`), which lists every kernel verb the principal may call, every GUI command and every mod command by id, with its keybinding (`G-GUI-08`); **layout presets** (named arrangements of panes and slots) are saved per device through the kernel and round-trip; the user can move or hide the Workshop (§11.6), the Shuttle view and any mod view; nothing a mod places can pin itself visible (§12.2). The palette carries the strictness and run-mode commands (`strictness: tighten`, `strictness: loosen`, `mode: manual|ask|auto-edits`, each for the focused repo or, with `--session`, the focused session) and `incident: report`.

**Strictness and run-mode control (P8-D86–D88).** A kernel-owned control in the header shows the focused session's repo level and effective mode (`L1 · auto-edits`), and is as quick as Claude Code's mode switch or quicker. One click opens a segmented control with the four levels and the three modes, per repo and per session. A tightening choice applies on that click with no presence. A loosening choice raises the presence prompt in the same gesture, naming the step and the evidence; no second card or inbox trip is needed. A choice the evidence or the level's ceiling does not allow is shown disabled, with its reason (`needs 4 more clean landed`, `ceiling ask at L2`), and a root-key override sits behind a separate, labelled action. The control's state comes only from `status.strictness` and `approval.strictness.*` / `approval.mode.set` events, never from a mod, and no preset or mod can hide or replace it.

**Left sidebar (D44).** Active and past sessions grouped by project (repo scope), each row with its backend glyph (filled = Claude, outlined = local), state and pending-card count `{n, of}`, and each project heading with a strictness glyph (none at L0, the level number from L1, the warning colour at L3); a **new session** control opens `session.open` with the project's defaults (`model`, `mode`, `cwd`); search across sessions; a row is the entry to its transcript.

**Decision stack (D43).** Pending cards arrive as pieces of paper (papercraft) or chips (phosphor) that fly in and pile into a **stack**; the stack can be fanned or browsed so a more important decision can be taken first — deciding a later card leaves earlier ones pending and untouched (`G-GUI-09`); inside a session the stack shows only cards whose `scope.repo` is that session's project, and the global inbox in the header shows all. The card body is kernel-owned and verbatim (§4.5): the stack moves the paper, never the words. A card's fly-in is motion on an event (`approval.request`) — the one decorative motion outside the Workshop. In a repo with voice approvals on (§14.7), a voice-eligible card carries a mic glyph with its class and its current deck ordinal; an ineligible one says why (`hand-only`, `voice-phrase`, `class`); on the arming connection a gate or judgment card shows its handle word in a capture-excluded, kernel-owned region; a `held` card shows its countdown and a **Cancel** control (`approve{decision:'withdraw'}`, no presence); voice-decided cards carry a `voice` badge in history. Every card's kernel region names the reason for its class when strictness changed it, and shows the change-note strip (§3.4) with the agent's claims fenced below as `agent-claimed`; `irreversible` and `claim-mismatch` show as badges beside the decision keys. A tightened repo's cards are batched in the stack (B14), and a `reconsider` card shows its round history and open findings by class.

**Panes.** Transcript, inbox/stack, sessions, contract progress, Shuttle view and mod panels in resizable splits; everything the GUI draws beyond the transcript is a `display-block:v1` tree: kernel cards (`inbox.card`, `ask-user`, `plan`, `knot.split`, `budget.meter`, `contract.progress`, `session.live`, `decision.stack`) and mod views placed by slot (§12.2); kernel-owned regions and the sanitizer (§4.5) apply to every pane. The **Shuttle view** (status slot) shows live children `{n, of}` with spend, inbox pending by kind, hsid + mode + backend, budget rung, subscription window, `API CREDITS ACTIVE` when law 7's card is in force, mic state when voice is on, `VOICE APPROVALS ARMED · reversible | GATE · <mm:ss> left` with **Disarm** while an arm is live (kernel-owned, unhideable by any preset or mod, `GATE` in the warning colour, earcons on arm and disarm; also a wrapper dock/taskbar badge), sandbox fidelity, linked and `dev` mods, and `certified/total` in a contract session, and the **strictness indicator** for every repo above L0 — `STRICT L2 · 3 reverts / 12 landed · since <date>`, with the time and evidence until the next eligible loosening — kernel-owned and unhideable like the voice-arm indicator. Motion-on-events: the GUI repaints only on events or input — no timers, no polling; socket and input silent for 60 s ⇒ zero DOM mutations and zero running animations (`G-GUI-02`); the Workshop's bob is the one scoped override (§11.6).

### 11.5 Composer and decisions (D38)

**Composer.** Local or Claude per session; `/model local|claude|any` per turn is refused unless the session's `declaredModel` is `any`; mid-turn steering (`session.send{steer:true}`) queues text the backend reads at its next step (QM MessageQueue, 21); `Esc` interrupts (control message, §3.3); slash commands are palette commands; `⇧Tab` cycles the session's run mode as in Claude Code (`manual → ask → auto-edits → manual`), applying a tightening step at once and raising the presence prompt for a loosening step; the composer footer shows the effective mode; dropping or pasting an image into the composer offers "attach to turn" and "open in Doodle" (§12.7).

**Decisions.** The GUI shows cards and takes the decision keystroke or click; the decision goes through the broker with the hardware approvals key — one presence prompt when this connection attaches, cached on **this `connId` only** for `tool.permission` allow/deny, `ask-user` and `plan`; fresh presence for gate-class, install (including a linked mod's re-approve and any manifest widening), `always`, KNOT resolve, verdict, cloud fallback, budget resume (root key for `use-api-token`/`install`); dropped after 15 min idle on this connection or when the connection closes — a reload therefore costs one touch (D54) and a second tab has its own unlock (`G-GUI-06`). The GUI never holds any key — the helper holds the unlocked handle for this `connId` (S29-K5); a decision made from the GUI records `presence`, `connId` and `cli:{client:<surface>, connId}` so §3.4's `approval.suspect` rule can see it; the GUI never calls a write verb without a user gesture (`G-GUI-04`); a card carrying `flags` (hidden or bidi characters, §4.5) shows the badge beside its decision keys. Reserved decision keys (`y n a d Enter Esc` and digits while a card is focused) belong to the GUI core, never to a mod (§12.2).

**Voice approvals (D58–D62).** Settings → Project → Voice shows the switches — "Voice may approve reversible decisions", "Voice may also approve gate-class decisions" (disabled until the first is on, and until `T-VOX-02` has been reported once), "Voice may also resolve KNOTs and decide verdicts" (disabled until the second is on); turning one on appends `request.grant` and raises a `grant` card decided with the root key, whose body — and presence prompt — carries the kernel's warning (§14.7); turning one off is immediate (`request.voice.narrow`, no presence). Arming is a `voice-arm` card decided with fresh presence, and a connection may open an arm only from the wrapper or a native client declaring capture exclusion: the browser surface cannot arm and never receives handles or challenges. `Esc` on the arming connection during a hold withdraws the decision.

### 11.6 Workshop (`#workshop-mod`, first-party, D41)

A minimal optional mod view (vertical sidebar or horizontal footer, movable and hideable per D42) with two zones: **needs you** — caps lined up with their question kind, one per pending card, equal to the inbox's `{n, of}`; **working** — caps that bob only while their session streams (`turn.partial` events) and stand still otherwise. Hat shape = work type (builder, reviewer, teacher, tender), filled = Claude, outlined = local, soft hue = project (never text), violet only for KNOT; every attribute derives from kernel events (`session.*`, `turn.*`, `approval.*`, `warpline.knot`), never from model text (`G-WSP-01`). The bob is an **informed override** of motion-on-events, scoped to this mod and to sessions actively streaming (recorded in the aesthetic session's `overrides`); reduced motion makes it a static "working" pose with a text label. Ships in `packages/gnomon-mods/workshop` on `mod-abi:v1` with fixtures and golden renders on both OS; events `modns.workshop.*`; declared effects `read` only; trust tier `first-party`.

### 11.7 Falsifiers

`G-GUI-01` a 200k-event session: keystroke-to-paint < 50 ms and attach < 2 s, measured in the wrapper and in Chromium, Safari/Edge on the founder's machines with method stated (`G-HON-07`) · `G-GUI-02` 60 s of socket and input silence ⇒ zero DOM mutations and no running animation · `G-GUI-03` the browser matrix and both WebViews render every fixture; no feature requires a particular browser · `G-GUI-04` no write verb without a user gesture · `G-GUI-05` close the window or tab mid-turn, reopen, Reconnect, no event lost · `G-GUI-06` the cached unlock expires after 15 idle minutes, dies with its connection, and a second connection cannot use it · `G-GUI-07` a block string containing HTML or script markup renders as text and never executes · `G-GUI-08` every command id is reachable from the palette; presets round-trip; Workshop and Shuttle view movable and hideable; the extension-risk badge is not · `G-GUI-09` decision stack: order independence and per-project filtering · `G-GUI-10` theme contrast fixtures (key edges ≥ 3:1; project hue never as text) · `G-GUI-11` bidi controls render as visible glyphs, tag and zero-width characters are stripped, and a card containing them carries the badge and `flags[]` · `G-GUI-12` the wrapper: passes `G-WEB-01..06`, page content reaches no Tauri command, a forged `serverSig` renders nothing but the error, and the token arrives on stdin only · `G-STR-09` strictness and run-mode control: a tightening click, palette command or `⇧Tab` step takes effect within 1 s with no presence prompt; a loosening one raises exactly one presence prompt and no other card; a choice above the ceiling or below the evidence is disabled with its reason; `auto-edits` never passes an edit outside the session's worktree or on a gate-class path; the header control, the strictness indicator and the project glyph cannot be hidden or replaced by any preset or mod · `G-GUI-13` voice approvals: the armed indicator and Disarm cannot be hidden by any preset or mod; a held card shows its countdown and Cancel, and Cancel withdraws with no `approval.decide`; a handle or challenge never renders on a browser-surface connection and is absent from a screen capture of the arming window; turning a toggle off needs no presence and drops affected arms within 1 s · `G-WEB-01..08` per §11.2, both OS · `T-GUI-01` the founder's daily driver is the GUI after two weeks (≥ 70 % of Gnomon-managed sessions opened from it, wrapper or browser).

---

## 12. Mods — day one, out of process, agent-maintainable, linkable (D27, D33, R7, D53)

### 12.1 Model

A mod is a `manifest:v1 {kind: mod, abi: "mod-abi:v1"}` package: tools (with declared effects), views (slot + placement + fixtures), commands, keybindings, event kinds under `modns.<name>.*`, optional knowledge providers (§8.10), optional MCP/skills/hooks. Its source is either an installed package (`gnomon mod install <path|url>`) or a **linked folder on disk** (`gnomon mod link <folder>`, §12.5) — a cloned repo is the sharing unit. Either way the mod **runs from an immutable, content-addressed snapshot** `~/.gnomon/mods/<name>/pinned/<packageHash>/`, never from the source folder. `#mod-host` runs each mod as one child process over stdio JSON-RPC under the `mod` profile; the mod never touches the GUI process, the kernel process, the socket credential or another mod. Hot reload by `packageHash` (sha fingerprint over the whole snapshot, OpenCode pattern, 21). First-party mods ship in `packages/gnomon-mods` under the same ABI and the same sandbox as any other mod: `workshop` (§11.6), `gnome-persona` (§12.6), `doodle` (§12.7), `knowledge` (§8.10); first-party means the trust tier, not a wider ABI — the knowledge mod's wider read paths are an install-card grant, not an ABI exception.

### 12.2 Closed view kit and slots

Views are pure `render(input) → display-block:v1` over the kit `Box Text List Table Markdown Diff Progress Button Image Canvas` (`kit:v1`); `Button` carries only a command id; `Image{blob, alt, w, h}` requires `alt` and is served to the GUI only through the endpoint's `/blob/<sha>` route (fetched with the token header and shown through a `blob:` URL, raster formats only, §11.2 rules 5–6) under the viewer's redaction class (§9.3) and to the CLI as its `alt`; `Canvas{ds, readOnly?}` names a doodle session and is a **surface the host owns** — the GUI's first-party canvas component binds to it, a mod only references it and can never ship executable content into any client (`G-MOD-10`). Every input a view or command receives from a client carries the host-stamped `relayedFor{principal, connId}`; a mod cannot name who acted. No raw strings reach any client: every string passes the sanitizer (§4.5) and the GUI renders strings as DOM text nodes (`G-GUI-07`); a block exceeding 256 KiB or 2 000 nodes is refused `BAD_REQUEST`. `card.*` and `status.mod` slots are **append-only**; kernel card bodies, status indicators and the `contentHash` summary are unreplaceable; mod output is fenced + labelled `mod:<name>` and shows a `dev` badge when the mod is dev-linked (§12.5); a mod view can be moved or hidden by the user and can never pin itself visible. Reserved decision keys (`y n a d Enter Esc` and the digits while a card is focused) are never bindable by a mod (`G-MOD-06`). Fixtures ship with every view; `gnomon mod test` renders fixtures to golden blocks on both OS, including the CLI text projection.

### 12.3 `mod-abi:v1` (≤ 8 RPCs + lifecycle + 2 client relays, law 3)

| RPC | Direction | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `init{abi, features}` → `{abi, features, modAbiId}` · `shutdown` · `cancel{cancelToken}` | host → mod | lifecycle + feature negotiation; `cancel` names an in-flight `view.render`/`tool.call` token and must be honoured within its timeout or the child is restarted |
| `health` | host → mod | liveness + abi echo |
| `view.render{viewId, input, width, prefs:{motion, theme}, cancelToken}` | host → mod | pure render |
| `command.run{commandId, args}` | host → mod | user command |
| `tool.call{name, input, cancelToken}` | host → mod | agent tool; effects enforced by the host |
| `event{event:v1}` | host → mod | filtered feed (grant-bound kinds) |
| `state.get{key}` / `state.set{key, value}` | mod → host | per-mod KV, `~/.gnomon/mods/<name>/state.db`, size-capped |
| `kernel.call{verb, params}` | mod → host | proxied kernel verb under the mod's principal with the caller's hsid attached and scopes intersected; `approve` is never proxied; `spend` from the caller's permit; `mod.command` through `kernel.call` only for a mod holding `knowledge:federate` (§8.10) |
| `mod.view{name, viewId, input}` · `mod.command{name, commandId, args}` | client → host (→ mod) | the **client relays**: a client asks the host to render a mod view or run a mod command; the host forwards as `view.render` / `command.run` with `relayedFor{principal, connId}` stamped under the client's principal, returns the sanitized block or the command result; `mod.command` from a GUI requires a user gesture (§11.5); both ride `gnomon-rpc:v1`, are served by the mod host, are hashed into `modAbiId`, and are **not** kernel verbs — the client-callable surface is 12 kernel verbs + these 2 relays (§5), and the mod-ABI count (§10 law 3) includes them |

Every host→mod call carries a timeout; `cancel` is honoured or the child is restarted. `modAbiId` = sha256 of the mod-ABI schema (the eight RPCs, the lifecycle incl. `cancel`, the two client relays) ‖ `kitId`; `clientApiId` = sha256 of the client schemas (§12.8) ‖ `kitId`; `kitId` = sha256 of `kit:v1` — so a kit change moves both ids, a mod-RPC change moves only `modAbiId`, and a verb or event change moves only `clientApiId` (A24-2); `status` reports all three. Effects: `read` (kernel reads, state.get, view inputs) · `write` (state.set, `log.append` of `modns.<name>.*`) · `network` (only with an egress grant, through the kernel's loopback proxy) · `spend` (drawn from the calling session's permit; the broker grants only if the human's grant row allows that mod to spend). A call outside the declared set ⇒ `FORBIDDEN` + `mod.effect.refused`; a mod in dev mode is capped at `read` whatever it declares (§12.5). Every `view.render`, `command.run` and `tool.call` carries a host-minted `callId` that the mod echoes on its mod→host requests; a request naming a call that is not in force is refused, one naming none is judged as idle (`read`). A mod's `log.append` keeps only `kind`, `payload` and the two labels (tighten-only), is appended as a userland row under the mod's principal and signed by the kernel key as an attestation (§3.2 L7), and is budgeted per mod (rows per second, bytes per call). Relays admit only installed, unpaused mods that still have restarts left; a relay-started child counts against the restart cap. Refusal rows a mod or a client can trigger (`mod.effect.refused`, relay misses) are folded like the pre-auth socket rows: the first 8 per 10 s window per mod are written individually, the rest are counted into one coalesced row of bounded size (at most 16 keys plus an overflow count and a distinct count). A kernel-side refusal reaches a mod as its code and reason only. **Mod tools reach agents** through the per-session `gnomon-mods` MCP bridge (§3.8: a userland child the host spawns under the `session` profile, listed in the session's `mcp.json`, talking only to the host), so the kernel's ≤12 proxy tools are untouched (A23-3) and the bridge — not the kernel — logs `inject.receipt` for every tool result; knowledge queries (§8.10) and the Doodle tools (§12.7) travel the same way.

### 12.4 Tooling for agents (`gnomon mod …`)

`new <name>` (scaffold with fixtures), `types` (generated `.d.ts` + JSON Schema for the ABI, `modAbiId`-stamped), `test` (fixtures → golden renders + contract tests, both OS), `inspect <name>` (manifest, grants, health, source `package | linked:<path>`, pinned `packageHash` and the snapshot list, dev state and its expiry, last renders, **stderr tail**), `install <path|url>` (validate → test → snapshot → `install` card with pinned `packageHash`; from a kernel-spawned shell it stages the card and signs nothing, §3.1 I3), `link <folder>` / `unlink <name>` / `dev <name> on|off` / `diff <name>` (§12.5), `rollback <name> [<packageHash>]` (human-only; re-pins a previous snapshot, never unlinks). The example mod and docs are referenced from `status` so an agent can write a mod from the man page (F4 for mods: `G-MOD-04`, a cold agent builds and stages a passing view with the docs only, and the human installs it from the card).

**Trust.** Tiers in the grant table (`first-party | signed:<gkey> | local`), judged on provenance + hash, never self-description; a linked folder is `local` unless its manifest is signed by a trusted key; a mod's manifest cannot request `approve`, `band:write`, `knowledge:federate` (first-party only) or kernel-owned kinds. Falsifiers: `G-MOD-02` a mod declaring `read` cannot `state.set`/`log.append`/write outside its dir/reach an ungranted host (profile + boundary); `G-MOD-03` a crashing mod never takes the GUI or kernel down; `G-MOD-05` any edited file in an installed or linked mod's source stops the next load until re-carded (`packageHash`), and the running snapshot is unchanged by the edit; `G-MOD-07` a mod view cannot overlay a card body or the status indicators; `G-MOD-11` the snapshot copier refuses a symlink, a junction, a hardlink, a `.node` addon and a path escaping the folder, a session cannot write into a linked folder, a dev-mode mod cannot `state.set` or reach the network, dev mode expires on schedule, and a manifest widening in dev mode raises a card before the next load.

### 12.5 Linked folders — pin, pause, re-approve (R7, D53)

Sharing a mod is cloning its repo and linking the folder. `gnomon mod link <folder>` validates and tests the manifest, **copies the folder into a snapshot** `~/.gnomon/mods/<name>/pinned/<packageHash>/` (owner-only, read-only after the copy; `packageHash` = sha256 over the sorted tree of the copy, which excludes `.git/**`), and raises an **install card** (fresh presence, root key) that shows the mod's name, source path, trust tier, declared effects, egress hosts, event kinds, tools and views, the read paths it asks for, and — when `node_modules` is present — every `package@version` from the lockfile (a `node_modules` without a lockfile is refused `manifest.refuse{code:'LOCKFILE'}`). **The copier refuses** (`mod.snapshot.refused{name, reason, path}`, no card raised): a symbolic link, a junction or reparse point, a hardlinked file (`nlink > 1`), a `.node` native addon, and any path that resolves outside the folder (S25-H2, A24-3). The kernel never runs git in a linked folder (hashing is a file read; `.git/**` is skipped unread, S25-L4). On `install` the kernel appends `grant.install{manifestHash, packageHash, cardId, source:'linked:<path>'}` and `mod.linked{name, path, packageHash}`, and `#mod-host` starts the child **from the snapshot only** — a lazy `import` inside the mod can read nothing but the approved bytes, so there is no window between hashing and loading. `#mod-host` then **watches the source folder** (`fs.watch` on macOS, `ReadDirectoryChangesW` on Windows, 500 ms debounce, with a full rehash on every start and on every `gnomon mod inspect`): any change — an edit, a `git pull`, a dependency install — is copied to a candidate snapshot, pauses the child before its next load (`mod.paused{name, reason:'changed', files[]}`), marks every view of that mod paused in the GUI, and raises a short **re-approve card** (kind `install`, fresh presence) listing exactly the added, changed and removed paths with old and new hashes and any **manifest widening** by field (`gnomon mod diff <name>` prints the same list); `install` re-pins (`grant.install` with the new `packageHash`; the old snapshot is kept for rollback), `deny` keeps the mod paused on its current snapshot, `rollback` re-pins the previous snapshot (`mod.rolled-back{name, toPackageHash}`) and keeps the link. Nothing from the changed tree is loaded before the decision (`G-MOD-08`). The `session` profile denies writes under every linked folder (§15.5), so a session cannot even cause a card. **Dev mode** is for mods the user is authoring: `gnomon mod dev <name> on` is itself a card decision (fresh presence, root key) available only for tier `local`, refused while the folder lies under any live session's `cwd` or worktree (and a later `session.open` whose `cwd` contains a dev-mode folder turns dev mode off first, with a card), and **expiring** after 8 h by default (`mod.dev.expired{name}`, re-card to extend); while on, every save re-pins a new snapshot without a card (`mod.repinned{name, packageHash, dev:true}`) **unless the manifest widened** — any addition to effects, egress, capabilities, event kinds, tools, views, commands, keybindings or knowledge providers pauses the mod and raises the re-approve card even in dev mode (`manifest.widened{name, fields[]}`); the mod's effects are **capped at `read`** for the whole dev period whatever it declares; every view of that mod carries a visible `dev` badge, the Shuttle view lists `linkedMods{n, dev}`, and `status` names the folder and the expiry; `gnomon mod dev <name> off` returns the mod to the pin-and-card rule (`G-MOD-09`). A mod that was dev-linked never silently becomes trusted: the badge and the `dev:true` rows are the record. `link` is a root-signed human verb (§3.1 I3); an agent runs `gnomon mod link <folder>` from its session and thereby **stages** the snapshot and the card (`manifest.staged{…, by: hsid}`) without signing anything; only a human decides the card (`G-MOD-11`).

### 12.6 Gnome persona (`#gnome-persona-mod`, first-party, R5, D45–D49, D52)

Concept doc: `docs/specs/gnomon-mods/gnome-persona.md`. What this spec commits to for 8.0:

- **A dialog voice over the transcript, never instead of it.** Each session may wear a persona; its line is a short game-dialog box above the composer — a `say` sentence or two plus up to four choices — derived from kernel events and from the model's own closing words. The raw completion, every tool call and every kernel event stay where they are (the hash-chained log, the transcript pane), **one key away** (`r` opens the source turn, `R` the log slice); every line is appended as `modns.gnome.line{turn, mode: status | quote, spanRefs[], personaHash}` so the dialog itself is in the log (model-visible ⟺ logged, §3.2 L2).
- **Two tiers, no restyling pass (D52, S25-H3).** Tier 0: deterministic status templates whose slots are kernel fields only (`approval.request{kind}` → "A {kind} card is waiting."; `permit.deny` → "Blocked: {dimension} at {n} of {of}."; `oracle.result{pass:true}` → "Certified {nodeId} ({n} of {of})."). The templates ship in the mod package tree under `packageHash` (changing one is an install card), each declaring its required slots; at `init` the host reads them from the pinned snapshot, validates that every required slot is present, and thereafter **fills Tier 0 lines itself** from the kernel event (`kit.Text{role:'status', template:<id>, slots:{…}}` is what the mod emits; the host substitutes the slots from its own fields and refuses a block whose text differs from the pinned template) — no `persona.set`, no model and no mod code can change what a Tier 0 line says. Tier 2: the session model writes the line itself, instructed by a mod-shipped skill to end each turn with a fenced `<gnome say="…" choices="…" />` tail and to keep its closing prose brief — **brevity by instruction**; a Tier 2 line is rendered with a distinct `model` label and glyph so it is never mistaken for a kernel-event line, and a `modns.gnome.line` records `tier: 0 | 2`. There is no second model call that paraphrases a completion: the concept doc's local-model restyle tier is dropped; a turn without a tail shows the raw's first sentence as a `quote` (labelled `model`).
- **Voice rules = Simplified Technical English (ASD-STE100, D52)** with a length budget: one topic per sentence, ≤ 20 words per sentence, ≤ 2 sentences per line, active voice, present or simple past, one approved word per meaning (no synonyms for the same thing), numbers as digits, no idiom; the forbidden-vocabulary defaults (`understand think feel know believe sure confident realize happy-to obviously`) cannot be removed by any persona. `understood` means acknowledged receipt ("Noted."), never "I understand" (D47); `sorry` pairs courtesy with the fact and the limit ("Sorry — stopped: tsc exit 1.").
- **Never rephrased (D46).** Card bodies, `refusal:v1` text, billing (`API CREDITS ACTIVE`, `api-token`), security and approval text, certification results: the relay may announce their existence by kind and `{n, of}` and must step aside while a card is focused; no choice is ever an approval and no card is read back in the gnome's words. The TTS channel never speaks an approval-grammar match: the phrase mask (§14.7) replaces it with "approval phrase withheld" and logs `voice.tts.filtered`; kernel readback, deck and echo lines are spoken verbatim in the fixed **system voice**, which no persona may use.
- **Emotes from kernel events first (D48).** `turn.partial` → walk; `turn.sent` → acknowledged; `approval.decide{allow | always | approve-plan | answer}` → yes; `approval.decide{deny | reject}` and `oracle.result{pass:false}` → no; `permit.deny`, `session.refused`, `refusal` → sorry; `oracle.result{pass:true}` → certified. Phrase triggers from the model's own prose (never tool results, never card text) may fire only the expressive subset (`wave tilt bow blink bounce`), never `yes`, `no` or `certified`.
- **`persona:v1` (D49, S25-H3)** — a small YAML document per gnome (binds by role/backend; voice register, length, vocabulary within the STE validator; emote phrases; body shape/height/hue/texture; hat style within the Workshop's semantics — hat shape, fill and violet are facts, the body is yours; TTS voice; motion — never Tier 0 text) — stored in the mod's state, not its package tree, so `persona.get | persona.set | persona.preview` on the per-session `gnomon-mods` bridge hot-apply ("make my reviewer gnome terse and blue" is a tool call, not a reinstall). `persona.set` from a session is **scoped to that session's own gnome**: the bridge stamps the caller's hsid and the mod refuses a `personaId` bound to another session or to the default set (`FORBIDDEN`); cross-session and default-persona changes are palette commands on a human-class connection. Every change logs `modns.gnome.persona.changed{id, hash, by: relayedFor.principal, hsid}` and rollback is `persona.set` with the previous hash. The schema is published as `persona:v1` in `@a-company/gnomon-contracts`.
- **Rendering** follows §11.3: papercraft bodies with the breeze skew, a top-left → bottom-right shadow and a grain layer in light mode; glyph gnomes with glow in dark mode; reduced motion freezes the breeze and makes emotes instant; every emote has a text label.
- **Honest token claims (D45).** Restyling never cuts generation tokens; what saves is brevity by instruction, choices that avoid clarifying turns, and Tier 0 costing nothing; every budget sentence in the UI follows `G-HON-*` wording and the mod measures before it claims (`T-GNM-01`: choice-picked vs typed follow-ups, `raw` open rate, closing-prose token delta with and without the skill, reported with method).
- **Falsifiers.** `G-GNM-01` no `modns.gnome.line` contains a number, path or identifier absent from its source turn or kernel event · `G-GNM-02` on a fixture set the rate of omitted "but/however/except" clauses between the raw closing prose and the `say` line is reported with method, never claimed · `G-GNM-03` the relay never alters a kernel-owned region, `yes`/`no`/`certified` fire only on kernel events, never on phrases, a Tier 0 line cannot be changed by `persona.set` or by the mod's own code, a session's `persona.set` cannot reach another session's gnome, and every Tier 2 line carries the `model` label.

Effects declared: `read` (event feed) plus the grant-named capability **`turn.text:attached-session`** (§9.3, §15.4: the assistant and user text parts of the sessions the gnome is attached to, never `tool_input` or tool results — the install card names it), `write` (personas, `modns.gnome.*`), `spend` (choices that send a turn draw on the attached session's permit). No `network`. Default posture, one mod or two with the Workshop, and the Tier 2 skill route versus an envelope-contribution RPC in 8.1 are OPEN 11. The voice client speaks the gnome line as `textPlanned` when the persona is on (§14.2).

### 12.7 Doodle (`#doodle-mod`, `$doodle-session`, first-party, R6)

Concept doc: `docs/specs/gnomon-mods/doodle.md`. What this spec commits to for 8.0:

- **A shared, replayable sheet.** A doodle session (`ds:<ULID>`, client-minted; the kernel validates shape only) is attached to one `hsid`, inherits its `scope.repo`, and records every mark as a kernel event appended by `p:<ns>/mod-doodle`: `modns.doodle.session.start{ds, hsid, title, by, seed?}`, `modns.doodle.op{ds, op, by, relayedFor}` (one kind, discriminated by `op.type`: `add | update | delete | move | lock | unlock | clear`), `modns.doodle.branch{ds, from: seq, child}`, `modns.doodle.export.requested{ds, kind, target, payload}` (A24-T2; the write itself is the reader's, §8.8), `modns.doodle.view.served{ds, requester, shas[]}`, `modns.doodle.session.end{ds, ops, images, branches, compactedTo: "l:L-…"}`, with `causes[]` naming the chat or voice turn live when the mark was made. **`by` is never asserted by the mod**: the host stamps `relayedFor{principal, connId}` on every `mod.command` and every bridge tool call, and `by` is copied from it (A24-5). Shape vocabulary `doodle-shape:v1` (closed, small, published in `@a-company/gnomon-contracts`): `stroke rect ellipse arrow text image redline point group`, each with `id`, `by`, `z`; colour is a role (`ink | accent | project | knot | agent-<n>`) so both themes render it; human marks and agent marks are never indistinguishable (agent ink tinted, cap on hover).
- **The GUI hosts the canvas directly.** The sheet is a `kit.Canvas{ds, readOnly?}` block rendered by the GUI's first-party canvas component (§12.2) — no separate canvas client, no second loopback listener, nothing beyond the web endpoint's rules (§11.2); the canvas engine is **Excalidraw (MIT; LICENSE verified 2026-10-16)**, bundled, with its own history disabled in favour of the log: element changes become `modns.doodle.op`s through `mod.command{name:'doodle', commandId:'doodle.op'}` (§12.3), the fold of ops is the only truth. **Engine hygiene under law 6 and the CSP (S25-M3):** fonts and every asset self-hosted in the bundle (`EXCALIDRAW_ASSET_PATH` → the bundle; no CDN); embeddables, iframes, link fields and the library panel disabled; images never travel as `data:` URLs — a placed image is re-encoded to raster by the GUI, uploaded as a blob and shown through a `blob:` URL from `/blob/<sha>`; `/blob` never serves SVG; the Excalidraw page load under the unchanged CSP is one of §11.2 rule 6's fixtures. **Edits as ops:** pointer drags are coalesced into one `move`/`update` op at pointer-up (intermediate positions are never logged); the GUI renders optimistically and rolls the element back when the op is refused or its `modns.doodle.op` event does not arrive; **undo is an inverse op** appended to the log, never a deletion of history; the Excalidraw-element ↔ `doodle-shape:v1` mapping is a **pure pair** (forward and reverse) with golden tests in both directions so the engine can be swapped. tldraw is held as a reference, never a dependency: its SDK licence is source-available, requires a licence key or a watermark and phones home on trial, which conflicts with shipping inside `@a-company/paradigm` under law 6. Images are content-addressed blobs under `~/.gnomon/mods/doodle/blobs/` (the mod's own dir) served to the GUI through `/blob/<sha>` (§11.2).
- **Agents draw through two tools** on the per-session `gnomon-mods` bridge: `doodle.view{ds?, bounds?}` → `{sheet: simplified JSON, snapshot: PNG, legend}` (effect `read`) and `doodle.draw{ds, ops[]}` → `{applied, refused[]}` (effect `write`; validated against the vocabulary and ownership, a bad shape refused by name). **What `doodle.view` sends, stated (S25-H5):** the result is a tool result — with a Claude backend it leaves the machine over the subscription; it contains the sheet JSON (text shapes, redline notes, geometry), the mod's own PNG, and **only images whose blobs the human accepted after the redaction pass** — an image still in the proposed tray or never redacted is replaced by `{image: sha, state:'pending-redaction'}`; it never contains the aligned transcript window; the bridge logs `inject.receipt{hsid, turn, refs:[doodle:…], shas[]}` and the mod logs `modns.doodle.view.served`. **The PNG is the mod's own rasterizer** over `doodle-shape:v1` (a pure function in the mod, no DOM, no Excalidraw), with a coordinate grid and legend — the model sees a different picture from the human's Excalidraw render, and the sheet JSON is the shared truth; the rasterizer has golden tests on both OS (`G-DDL-05`). Agents never delete or move a human's marks, never open a sheet without a human keystroke (an agent's `doodle.open` is a proposal the human accepts), never place an image the human has not seen (a "proposed" tray), and are budgeted to 64 ops per turn (`G-DDL-03`).
- **Time-reversal is replay.** Pick a `seq`; apply ops `≤ seq` from the nearest keyframe (a snapshot every N ops or 60 s of activity, derived, recomputable by `gnomon doodle fsck`); the GUI's scrub rail aligns three tracks — ops, chat turns, voice turns (`textSpoken`) — on the same `seq`/`at` axis, the chat track read by the GUI (human class) and, for the mod's own replay line, through `turn.text:attached-session` at query time; `gnomon doodle replay ds:…@<seq|iso>` prints the sheet as JSON (`G-DDL-01`: scrub to any seq of a 10k-op session in < 100 ms on the founder's Mac, method stated; `G-DDL-04`: the fold's hash is reproducible from ops alone). **Ops are chain events and are kept forever** (§3.2 L5 prunes blobs, never chain rows), so a `doodle:<ds>@<seq>` ref never dangles; only image blobs prune (below). Branching (`modns.doodle.branch`) seeds a child from a moment; `accept` replays the branch's ops onto the parent as new ops; no three-way merge in 8.0.
- **Lore compaction and exports (A24-T2).** The mod can write only its own dir, so every export is an event it requests and a userland writer carries out: at `modns.doodle.session.end` the mod appends `modns.doodle.export.requested{kind:'lore', payload}` with the compaction (title, participants, counts by kind `{n, of}`, every `text` and `redline.note`, rule ids cited, images by sha with redaction state, decisions and tasks exported, `sym:` refs that resolved, `doodle:` refs to pivotal moments); the reader (`gnomon learn read`, §8.8, under the `paradigm` grant) writes the `.lore` record and appends `paradigm.doodle.exported{ds, kind, ref}`, which the mod links as `compactedTo`. Image blobs are retained until that record exists + 90 days unless pinned by the human or referenced by a `doodle:` ref from a lore or decision record (`ref.resolve` of a pruned blob returns `{sha, pruned:true}`). Export kinds, all via the same request event: lore entry, decision (`doodle:` refs as evidence), task or contract-node evidence, Atelier critique finding (`redline` with `rules[]` → `{rule, where, note, severity, evidence}`), Atelier animation (ops' time axis → layer deltas), PNG of a moment with agent tint preserved (SVG export is written by the reader to a file, never served from `/blob`).
- **Privacy.** A screenshot is the most likely image and the most likely leak: on placement the GUI runs a local pass (secret patterns from the redaction matrix plus local OCR — engine OPEN 12) and shows proposed redaction boxes before the image is accepted; redaction produces a new blob and the original is pruned or kept `sensitivity: secret`; **a pattern the local pass detects** never reaches `session.send` or `doodle.view` unredacted (`G-DDL-02`) — a pattern it does not detect is the stated residual, and the pass's pattern list is versioned in the fixtures. Only what the human sends or accepted leaves the box (a snapshot in `session.send{content:[image]}` is a logged `session.inject` under law 7 and the fuse; a `doodle.view` result is a logged `inject.receipt`); sheet text and OCR text are data, fenced and labelled, through the sanitizer; the mod has no `network`; `audience: private` on every event in 8.0.
- **Refs and knowledge.** The mod registers the `doodle:` scheme (§4.4): `doodle:<ds>`, `doodle:<ds>@<seq|iso>`, `doodle:<ds>#<shapeId>[@<seq>]`, `doodle:<ds>/<childDs>`; `ref.resolve` returns `{target: {ds, seq, hsid, repo, lore?}, hash: sha256(fold at seq)}`. A local FTS index (`~/.gnomon/mods/doodle/index.db`, rebuildable by `gnomon doodle reindex`) over text shapes, redline notes with rule ids, OCR text after redaction and the compaction makes Doodle a knowledge provider (`provides.knowledge: [{name: doodle, scheme: "doodle:", maxTier: T3}]`, §8.10) with `provenance: observed`, `trust: T3`, a redacted thumbnail and a replay line per hit. **The index never copies transcript text** (S25-M7): for the aligned chat and voice turns it stores only `turn:` and `voice.turn` ids beside the `doodle:` ref, and a hit's transcript line is resolved at query time through `log.read` under the requester's redaction class — so pruning (§3.2 L5) and the redaction matrix (§9.3) apply to it as to any other read.
- **Telemetry.** `T-DDL-01` doodles opened per week, ops per doodle, exports per doodle — if exports stay near zero the connection thesis is false.

Effects declared: `read` plus `turn.text:attached-session` (replay alignment only, never stored), `write`, `spend` (an agent-proposed export that drafts text spends from the human's permit, never by default — law 5). No `network`. Windows parity: identical mod and GUI code; blobs under the owner-only ACL'd home. Open items (OCR engine and default, Excalidraw's bundle cost in the GUI, branch merge, the Atelier `findings.yaml` owner, agent-opened sheets as proposals) are OPEN 12.

### 12.8 Published client API — community GUIs and tools (R7)

`@a-company/gnomon-contracts` publishes the whole client surface as versioned schemas with TypeScript types: `gnomon-rpc:v1` (verbs, params, results, refusal codes, auth forms including the launch and GUI tokens and Reconnect, the `hello` negotiation, the two client relays), `display-block:v1`, `kit:v1` (the kit, hashed as `kitId`), `card:v1`, `refusal:v1`, `event:v1` and the kind registry, `doodle-shape:v1` and `persona:v1` (so a community client can render a sheet or a gnome from the package alone); `clientApiId = sha256(canonicalJson(descriptor) + "\n" + kitId)`, where the descriptor lists the client schemas and their versions and `modAbiId` is excluded, is reported by `status`, pinned in `gnomon-contracts/schemas/client-api.ids.json` (CI fails when the computed value and the pin differ), frozen per release and moves with `gnomon-contracts` semver; `modAbiId` moves independently (§12.3), so a mod-ABI change never churns clients (A24-2, §20 falsifier 12). The **compatible-additive rule** of §4.9 governs every `v1` schema: additions are optional fields, new kinds and new refusal data; a client renders unknown kinds opaquely and ignores unknown fields; removals, retypings and new required fields are a `v2`. `docs/specs/gnomon-client-api.md` is generated from the schemas (never hand-maintained, `G-HON-04`'s fixed-point rule). A **conformance suite** (`gnomon client conform <ws-url | socket>` against recorded fixtures) is the definition of a conformant client: negotiates `hello` and verifies `serverSig` (WebCrypto in a page, the registry or the stdin key elsewhere); authenticates (device key, launch token → GUI token, Reconnect, or read token); subscribes with `since` and survives a `subscription.dropped`; renders card bodies **verbatim from kernel-signed `approval.request` events** and never renders a client- or mod-authored block as a card; shows a card's `flags` badge; sends `approve` only on a user gesture; passes every string through the sanitizer including the Unicode rules; shows every number as `{n, of}`; renders `kit.Image` with its `alt`, `kit.Canvas` either as a surface or as the mark list, and an unknown `modns.*` kind as an opaque row. A third-party client built from the published package alone must pass it (`G-CLI-02`), and a forged `approval.request` without a valid kernel signature must not appear as a card (`G-CLI-03`). **Proposal (Aegis, M1.12 round 2; pending founder ratification) for `G-CLI-03` in limited views:** limited views keep dropping `hash` and `sig`; a limited-view client takes the kernel origin of an `approval.request` from the verified channel — the `hello` `serverSig` plus the kernel-owned kind, which no other principal can append — and never builds a card from mod or kit output; an optional future per-connection projection signature `viewSig = Sign(kernelKey, canonical({connId, seq, projectedRow}))` covers only the visible bytes; `card.actions[]` signatures never cover a `contentHash` over a redacted `tool_input`, and the `contentHash` summary line goes only to full views. Community GUIs and tools are therefore interchangeable peers of the first-party GUI: the same auth, the same per-connection presence and cached unlock (§3.4), the same unspoofable cards — `card.actions[]` are signed by the kernel key, `approve` is signed by the hardware approvals key after presence, and the **presence prompt text is composed by the kernel from its own card record** (kind, requester, the `contentHash` summary line, `connId`, open GUI sessions) and shown natively or in a helper-owned window on Windows (S25-M1), never by the client, so a misleading client cannot hide what a fresh-presence decision approves. **Redaction class (S25-M5):** a community client authenticating with a device key receives the `community client` row of §9.3 — `sensitive` and `secret` fields withheld — unless the human has granted that device key `log.read:sensitive` (a root-signed `grant.capability` row); the first-party page, wrapper and CLI are the full human row. Honest limit (threat row 17): the kernel cannot force a client to draw honestly, and it cannot verify a conformance run it did not perform — the badge in `status.clients[]` reports what each client **declared** in `hello` (`conformance: {suite, version, reportHash}`) and is labelled `declared`, never `passed`; a non-conformant client can mislead its own user within its own cached-unlock window (§A.1).

---

## 13. The local lane (`#local-runner`, D20–D23, D37)

### 13.1 `gnomon-local` — the child

`packages/gnomon-local` is a **separate executable** (userland dependency of `@a-company/paradigm`, never of the kernel) with **zero runtime dependencies** that **re-derives** pi's `--mode rpc` JSONL shape (`rpc-entry.ts`, 23; unverified against pi's `rpc-entry.ts`) as its stdio protocol — not built on `pi-agent-core` + `pi-ai` (M1.8 slice 1; ratified 2026-10-18, P8-D95). The kernel's `#local-runner` is a stdio driver for it, symmetric with `#claude-adapter`. Inside the child: agent loop with parallel tools, steering/follow-up queues, JSONL session tree; **compaction re-derived** (not lifted from pi-coding-agent `compaction.ts:129` / `branch-summarization.ts:305`, 23) with an ~8k-token working target, fixed headings Goal/Constraints/Progress/Decisions/Next/Critical and one compact-and-retry (each successful `compaction_end` is logged twice: the raw line as `turn.event` and the typed `session.compacted{hsid, turn, reason, summaryHash, tokensBefore, tokensAfter}`, P8-D93; a start, a failed end or an end missing its numbers is only the `turn.event`); transport = OpenAI-compatible completions to llama.cpp server, Ollama, LM Studio, vLLM or `mlx_lm.server` (Mac boost), seeded with pi-ai's **llama.cpp compat preset** (no developer role, no `store`, `max_tokens`, qwen chat-template thinking); **pi-ai's cloud providers are excluded from the build** (the compat allowlist contains only OpenAI-compatible local transports); endpoints must be loopback or RFC1918 per root-signed `local.yaml` (law 7, `G-SUB-06`; wire form `~/.gnomon/local.signed.json`, schema `gnomon-root-local:v1`, version high-water mark from a sidecar and the chain's signed `config.loaded{file}` rows — anything unsigned, tampered, invalid or older is no local config at all, logged; it names the endpoints, the `gnomon-local` runner by a MANDATORY pin (node + its sha256, the runner package dir + a tree hash, the entry; the kernel copies the tree into `~/.gnomon/runners/<treeSha256>/`, hashes the copy, verifies node, and execs only the copy — round 2, Aegis H1; residual: node itself is verified and then exec'd in place), the ctx floor and the compaction target; M1.8 slice 2). Per-task endpoint choice: the first endpoint in signed order that is capable for the task's floor (slice 2 reading; `session.open` takes no endpoint param). Per-backend **compat profile** + **tool-capability probe** at `model.capabilities` (tools off unless detected; `n_ctx` from llama.cpp `/slots`), **argument repair** (OpenCode tool-input-repair idea, re-derived), **text-tool fallback** and per-model tool surface `none | compact | full` (Odysseus ideas, re-derived — AGPL, no code), ~8k context targets for coding. Windows: llama.cpp (CUDA/Vulkan) and Ollama are first-class; MLX is Mac-only and optional (§16). The child runs under the `session` profile; `G-LCL-06` asserts it cannot read `~/.gnomon/keys`, the Warpline token store or the approvals handle.

### 13.2 Probe (M1.0)

`gnomon probe local` records `model.probe` + `model.capabilities` for each configured endpoint on both OS. The Claude-Code-to-local-endpoint probe is **dropped** (D37; A23-6: it needs `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, exposes the OAuth token to whatever listens, and Claude Code's prompt + tools overflow ~8k contexts); its loopback override is a `G-SUB-01` refusal case; revisit in 8.1 only if Claude Code gains a first-party local-model mode.

### 13.3 Routing and fallback

Every task/automation/session declares `model: local | claude | any` (`automation:v1`, `session.open`, contract nodes may declare per node). `local` ⇒ a capable local model (probe says tools ok, ctx ≥ task floor) or a **`fallback` card** (`MODEL_UNAVAILABLE` to the caller; the card names the task, the reason, the estimated Claude cost; unattended work **waits**; never auto-falls back — D21). `claude` ⇒ Claude under law 7. `any` ⇒ local if capable, else Claude, logged `session.routed{reason}`. Falsifiers: `G-LCL-01` a `model: local` automation completes end to end on llama.cpp with no Claude process started; `G-LCL-02` an incapable local endpoint yields a `fallback` card and no spawn; `G-LCL-03` the card's `fallback-cloud` decision (fresh presence) spawns Claude under the subscription; `G-LCL-04` compute permits cap concurrent local sessions per machine.

### 13.4 Flywheel

§8.4: local failures are supply for the teacher→skill loop; `{drafted, certified, broke}` denominators; teacher drafts land only via Classroom + `install` card.

---

## 14. Voice (`#gnomon-voice`, P8-R3, D28–D31)

### 14.1 Shape

A client of the kernel (`gnomon-voice`, Node + sherpa-onnx bindings) running as its own principal (§14.4), never part of the kernel; the kernel stays text-native. Engines via sherpa-onnx (ONNX, cross-platform): Silero VAD; Parakeet TDT 0.6B (English default; Whisper via sherpa for multilingual); speaker segmentation + embeddings for live clustering; Kokoro-82M TTS streamed per sentence; optional Smart Turn v3 end-of-turn model. **FluidAudio** (Swift, CoreML/ANE) is an optional macOS backend behind the same `VoiceEngine` interface (`vad | stt | diarize | tts`), selected by capability probe, never required (D31). Audio I/O through a cross-platform capture library (CoreAudio / WASAPI; pick in M4.1, OPEN 4) with the platform AEC on, or headphones.

### 14.2 Interaction (`$voice-turn`)

Push-to-talk by default; **conversation mode** opt-in per session and **only with platform AEC or headphones** — the client refuses to enter it otherwise and says why (S22-M3): VAD-gated listening while the agent speaks; **barge-in** = ≥ 250–400 ms of speech while TTS plays ⇒ stop playback, cancel pending TTS and the LLM continuation, truncate the agent turn to what was heard, log `voice.bargein` and `voice.turn.agent{textSpoken, interruptedAtMs}` (20; `G-VOX-02`). Mic state always visible in the GUI's Shuttle view (D28). Every spoken turn enters the session as `session.send{source:'voice'}` and the model sees the `source: voice` tag — except an utterance matching the decision grammar (§14.7), which the voice child intercepts (`voice.turn.user{intercepted:'decision'|'control', forwarded:false}`), never forwards to any model, and routes to the voice-approval flow (§14.7) when armed or to a spoken refusal when not. All TTS text (agent, gnome, kernel lines) passes the phrase mask first; while TTS plays and for 750 ms after, the decision grammar is muted (barge-in still works for speech). Latency budget end-of-speech → first audio < 800 ms local (VAD 30 ms, end-of-turn ~200 ms, Parakeet 100–200 ms, local TTFT 150–300 ms, Kokoro first chunk 100–150 ms); Claude's TTFT (400–900 ms) is bridged by a **local-model acknowledgement** (a one-line spoken ack generated by `#local-runner` or a cached filler) logged as `voice.turn.agent{source:'local-ack'}`. Every figure is measured on the founder's machines and reported with method (`G-HON-07`). The agent speaks only **speakable content**: the TTS channel carries a spoken summary distinct from the full text answer (diffs/tool output summarized) — when the gnome persona mod is on, its dialog line is that summary (§12.6); `textPlanned` vs `textSpoken` keeps context honest.

### 14.3 Team chat log (D30)

Every turn is a kernel event (§4.10). At `voice.session.end` the client compacts turns into a `.lore`-style record (speakers, decisions, tasks, symbol refs, links to raw `voice.turn.*` ids) written through the `paradigm` grant and linked by `compactedTo`; voice text blobs are retained until that compaction exists + 90 days unless pinned (§3.2 L5). Speaker labels: live clusters → enrolled local voiceprints confirmed in the GUI; unknown = "Speaker 2". Sharing (8.1 transport, 8.0 export): text-only, redacted, **per-speaker consent** recorded in `voice.share.consent[]`; raw audio, voiceprints and wake-word buffers never leave the box; audio not kept by default (opt-in TTL).

### 14.4 Trust (S22-M1/M2, A23-2)

The voice client runs as its own principal `p:<ns>/voice` with its own Ed25519 device key: scope `voice` can `session.send{source:'voice'}` to the session it is attached to, `session.stop{interrupt}`, append `voice.*` and `request.voice.arm`, read its session — and never runs root verbs, reserves permits or requests any card except `voice-arm`. With both of a project's voice-approval toggles off (the default) it decides nothing by speech: it may read the deck back and open (focus) a card in the GUI (`voice.focus{cardId}`) — voice opens, never decides (Atelier G1). With a toggle on and a live arm it submits decision intents per §14.7; its device key never signs one. **Speaker id is not authentication**: diarization and labelling happen in the client; the kernel validates shape only (speakerId known or `unknown-N`, turn ids monotone, text ≤ 16 KiB); enrolment (`voice.enrol`) and deletion (`voice.forget`) are root-signed CLI acts with recorded consent; voiceprints live under `~/.gnomon/voice/prints/` with `sensitivity: secret`, never leave the box, and are deleted by `forget`. **By default only the founder's enrolled turns are forwarded** to the session; other speakers are logged (`speakerEnrolled:false`) and shown in the GUI but not sent unless the human toggles it per session. Only text ever reaches Claude (D29); a cloud STT/TTS or speech-to-speech path is 8.1 and would require a per-session visible flag + logged event (20). Falsifiers: `G-VOX-04` the voice principal's root-verb and `permit.reserve` attempts are always `FORBIDDEN`, and its `approve` is `FORBIDDEN` with both toggles off, without an arm, from an unsupervised client, for a class or repo outside the grant, and for hand-only kinds with every toggle on; `G-VOX-06` unenrolled speech is logged but not forwarded by default; `G-VOX-07` conversation mode refuses without AEC/headphones.

#### 14.4.1 The kernel-spawned client (voice slice V2e, T-2026-10-19-026, TD-2026-10-19-194/-298/-063)

**Spawned over a pre-authenticated fd.** The daemon spawns `@a-company/gnomon-voice` itself (`node --disable-sigusr1 <cli.js> run --fd 3 --home <home> --io <bin>`) with fd 3 one end of a socketpair; the kernel serves its end without the listener and authenticates it as `p:<ns>/voice`, principalKind `voice`, credential `device` (`socket.auth.ok{via:'spawn'}`), with no hello and no auth. The child holds no key, no socket path and no kernel pin; its env is an allow-list (fixed PATH, HOME, USER, LOGNAME, LANG, LC_*, TMPDIR, GNOMON_HOME). The adopted connection has the kernel's hello features (`journal.cursor`); a voice `log.read{follow}` with no `since` starts at the released head. `status.voice.principal` names the principal to the voice connection. The child is restarted with exponential backoff (1 s doubling to 60 s, reset after 60 s of uptime) and dies with the kernel on every exit, an upgrade drain included: it never carries over (TD-2026-10-19-908). Before each spawn the kernel copies `GnomonVoiceIO.app` into `<home>/bin/voice/` (0700, ditto), verifies the copy with `codesign` against team `SW4BMQ8PNL` and identifier `gnomon.voice-io` (a dev build runs unsigned only with the daemon's dev flag, shown loud), and fetches the pinned models (`models fetch --json`). The client itself never runs from the (session-writable) workspace, and is never trusted on first use: the `gnomon-voice` build writes `dist/voice-client.manifest.json` (sha256 of every dist file and of the runtime root file `voice-models.manifest.json`), and the `gnomon` build, which runs after it (root `build:gnomon`), records that manifest's sha256 in its build stamp (`build-id.json` `voiceClientManifest`). At daemon start the kernel refuses voice unless the workspace matches the manifest exactly and the manifest matches the stamp it loaded; it then copies all of `dist`, the root file, a module `package.json` and the optional native `sherpa-onnx-node` (with its platform packages; outside the manifest, so pinned at copy) into `<home>/bin/voice/client/` (0700 directories, 0600 files), pins a sha256 of every copied file in its own memory, runs the model fetch and every spawn from that copy, and re-verifies the pin immediately before each spawn; any refusal is phase `unavailable` with the reason (Aegis V2e M2). The client in turn spawns `gnomon-voice-io` with an explicit `ignore` on fd 3, so the kernel link never reaches the io binary (M1). **Trust assumptions.** (L1) Sessions must not be able to write `<home>/bin/voice`: the session sandbox denies the Gnomon home, and the pin catches a write the sandbox missed only between copies. (L4) The client records the voiceprint after the human allows the card: it is a trusted, kernel-verified component (the pinned copy above), and the GUI says who must speak (`Recording <name> — only <name> should speak`) and shows done only once `status.voice.enrolled` lists the name. (L5) Voice confirms nothing by speech today; before any spoken confirmation is added, a kernel-voiced earcon (a sound only the kernel can play, not synthesizable through the client's TTS path) is required, so a played-back or synthesized voice cannot pass as the kernel. macOS only; every other OS reports it unavailable. Status: `status.voice.clientConnected` (an explicit boolean) and `status.voice.client{phase: stopped|preparing|installing|downloading-models|starting|running|backoff|failed, wanted, restarts, downloadMb?, devUnsigned, lastError?, unavailable?}`.

**Dispatcher-only requests (no §9.1 kind, never written as a row, `clientApiId` unchanged).** Like `request.mode.set` they ride `log.append{kind, payload}`, but they are not in the registry: adding them would move `clientApiId`. `request.voice.client{op: start|stop}` (human, GUI or device) → `{voiceClient: status.voice.client}`. `request.voice.enrol{name, consent}` (human) → `{pending: true, cardId}`; it raises a fresh-presence `strictness` card with `detail{op:'voice.enrol', name, speakerId, consent, connId}` (never by voice: the voice allowlist approves no strictness card) whose allow effect writes the same root-signed enrolment row as `gnomon voice enrol` and sets `status.voice.enrolment{name, phase:'requested'}` for the client to record the phrases. `request.voice.forget{name}` (human) → `{forgotten: true, speakerId}` with no card (a forget narrows, D62). `request.voice.report{enrolment?:{name, phase: ready|recording|recorded|done|failed|cancelled, phrase?, of?, error?}, notice?:{text, code?}}` (voice principal) → `{ok: true}`; the kernel keeps the latest of each in memory and shows it in `status.voice.enrolment` / `status.voice.notice{text, code?, at}` (kernel-stamped `at`, dropped after a ten-minute TTL; text bounded and stripped of control and bidi characters). `status.voice.enrolled` lists the enrolled speaker names. The client reads its principal from `status.voice.principal`, polls status while it runs and, on `requested`, records the three enrolment phrases (a list it shares with the GUI dialog; the report carries no phrase text), stores the print and reports `ready → recording{phrase, of} → recorded → done` (or `failed{error}` when the mic is off and cannot be turned on, or no speech is heard). The GUI asks for the Touch ID card first, then follows `status.voice.enrolment`; its notices are `status.voice.notice` only (no client notice row).

**Enrolment is the kernel's call.** A `voice.turn.user` claim `speakerEnrolled: true` counts only when its `speakerId` is in the root-signed consented registry; any other speaker (an `spk:unknown-N` label, a forgotten or never-enrolled name, a row another root signed) is stored as an unknown speaker: hash only, no text, `speakerEnrolled: false`, refused `voice-speaker`, never forwardable.

**The speakable reply row.** The kernel watches its own commits for the attached session's `turn.event`, `turn.ask-user` and `turn.result` rows and appends, as the kernel principal (a userland kind, kernel-attested), `voice.turn.agent{source:'kernel-speakable', turnSeq, items:[{kind: reply|ask|edits, text}]}` into that session: the reply's `gnome:` line, else its first prose paragraph (code, diffs and tool text stripped); every ask-user question; "Edited N files." at turn end. Never a diff, tool input or tool output. The voice principal cannot write `source:'kernel-speakable'`.

**The voice reader, narrowed.** The `voice` projection shows the attached session's `voice.*` rows only, the kernel's speakable row in full; never `turn.*`, session, approval or secret rows.

### 14.5 Lift (file:line)

constellation: `ui/src/audio/capture.ts:1-8` (AudioWorklet 16 kHz downsample, energy segmenter with adaptive floor, Me/Them capture), `server/audio.ts:1-8` + `server/hear.ts:1-8` (hallucination filter, normalise, WAV parse), `server/voice.ts:1-2` (engine process manager pattern; whisper-server replaced by sherpa-onnx), `server/commands.ts:1-8` (addressing grammar incl. mishearings), `server/bridge.ts:15-25,211-215` (gap/cap batcher 1 500/8 000 ms + wait contract), `shared/types.ts:177-191` (`TranscriptChunk` chunkId/speaker/startMs/endMs/revisionOf/command) and `shared/graph.ts:319-321`, `shared/api.ts:28` (`source.chunkIds` provenance from lines to ops). a-aloud: `packages/core/src/types/tts.ts:2,11-37,57` (`VoiceQualityTier`, `TTSVoice`, `TTSBoundaryEvent`, `TTSProvider`), the broker's `/page` + `/await_reply` speak-and-listen loop (`packages/broker`, `portal.yaml:12`). Re-derive, not lift: Conductor's WhisperKit push-to-talk state machine (Swift).

### 14.6 Falsifiers

`G-VOX-01` PTT turn round-trips text to a local session on macOS and Windows · `G-VOX-02` barge-in truncates `textSpoken` and cancels TTS within 300 ms · `G-VOX-03` session end produces a lore record whose links resolve to every raw turn · `G-VOX-04` with both toggles off (the default) every voice `approve` form is refused `voice-off`; root verbs and `permit.reserve` are `FORBIDDEN` regardless; an intent without an arm, from an unsupervised client, or outside the grant's classes and repos is refused · `G-VOX-05` no audio file exists after a default session · `G-VOX-06` unenrolled speech logged, not forwarded · `G-VOX-07` conversation mode refused without AEC/headphones · `G-VOX-08..16` voice approvals, defined in §14.7 · `T-VOX-01` voice-to-voice p95 < 800 ms local on the founder's Mac, reported with method (telemetry) · `T-VOX-02` decision-phrase false accept / false reject (§14.7).

### 14.7 Voice approvals (`$voice-approval`, `^voice-armed`, P8-D58–D62)

`#gnomon-voice` reports what it heard; `#approval-broker` decides whether that is a decision and signs it. The voice client never holds decision authority: a voice decision is signed by the hardware approvals key, inside an arm that a fresh Touch ID / Windows Hello prompt opened, and attributed to the human. With both toggles off (the default) nothing below applies except the spoken refusal "Voice approvals are off for this project."

**Toggles (D58, D61, D62).** Per project, root-signed `grant.voice-approvals{repo, classes}` rows in `~/.gnomon/grants.jsonl`: toggle 1 `[reversible]` (`tool.permission` allow/deny, `ask-user` answer, `plan` approve-plan); toggle 2 `[reversible, gate]` (adds `gate-class`, `fallback-cloud`, `resume`); sub-opt-in `[reversible, gate, judgment]` (adds KNOT `resolve` and verdict `accept|reject`, D61). A set without its prerequisite is refused `BAD_REQUEST`. **Widening** is a `grant` card (from `request.grant` or `gnomon voice approvals <repo> enable [--gate] [--judgment]`) decided with the root key; its body and presence prompt carry the kernel's warning, verbatim:

```
Warning. With this on, voice can approve gate-class decisions in <repo> while a gate arm is open:
changes to CLAUDE.md, .claude/, CI, test and package scripts; cloud fallback; budget resume.
One touch opens a gate arm for up to <n> minutes and <k> decisions.
Anyone who can make your microphone hear your voice and can read your screen or your headphones in that window can approve them.
Speaker recognition is not authentication. Install, API tokens, "always" and voice settings stay hand-only.
[judgment adds:] KNOT resolutions and verdicts are judgments on a diff. A spoken approval proves who spoke, not that you read the diff.
```

**Narrowing** needs no presence — `request.voice.narrow` from any human-class connection, `gnomon voice approvals <repo> disable [--gate|--judgment]`, or the `disarm` phrase for the current arm — and appends the kernel-signed `approval.voice.policy.narrowed{repo?, hsid?, classes, by, connId}`; a session may be narrowed alone ("no voice decisions in this session"). Effective policy = latest root grant minus later narrowings, cached and recomputed on any `grant.*`, `config.*` or narrowing append; **any such change ends every arm**. `.gnomon/project.yaml` never carries voice-approval settings: the key is ignored and logged `config.ignored{field:'voice.approvals', source:'project.yaml'}`.

**Strictness overlay (P8-D86; S35-08, A35-09).** The repo's strictness level (§3.4) is an overlay on the grant, never a grant: effective classes = (latest root grant minus later narrowings) ∩ the level's ceiling — L0 the grant, L1 `reversible` at most, L2 and L3 no approving class (deny and reject by voice stay, D66). Every level change appends `approval.voice.policy.narrowed{repo, classes, by:'kernel'}` when it narrows and, like any policy change, **ends every arm**. Loosening restores the overlay but never re-arms: a new arm needs a fresh-presence `voice-arm` card, which opens only for classes the overlay allows. An arm opened while the repo is at L1 or above ignores `unlimited` (D64) and takes the default counts. An approving intent the overlay excludes is refused `FORBIDDEN`, gate `voice`, reason `voice-strictness`.

**Configuration** (root-signed `~/.gnomon/config.yaml`; values outside a range are refused at sign time):

```yaml
voice:
  approvals:
    prefix: "claude admin"                               # every phrase = prefix + command word
    phrases: { approve: approve, deny: deny, answer: answer, confirm: confirm, undo: undo, cards: cards, open: open, arm: arm, disarm: disarm }
    perRepo: { "repo:<id>": { prefix?, phrases? } }      # same validation
    reversible: { idleMinutes: 15, hardMinutes: 60, maxDecisions: 30, holdMs: 3000, minGapMs: 2000 }    # hardMinutes ≤ 240; maxDecisions ≥ 1 or `unlimited` (D64); holdMs 2000..10000, never 0
    gate:       { hardMinutes: 5, maxDecisions: 3, holdMs: 5000, minGapMs: 10000, armsPerHour: 2,
                  requireHeadphones: true, confirmWindowMs: 10000 }                                   # hardMinutes ≤ 60; maxDecisions ≥ 1 or `unlimited` (D64); holdMs ≥ 5000; never refreshed
    echoTailMs: 750                                      # grammar muted during our playback and this tail
    deckMaxAgeMs: 60000
    minAsrConfidence: 0.90
    speakerThreshold: <set from T-VOX-02>
    binary: { path, sha256 }                             # the supervised voice child; sha256 = the manifest hash (executable + native addon + model files), TD-2026-10-19-194
```

Phrases are not secrets — security never rests on them — but are validated at sign time: ≥ 3 words; one shared prefix; command tails pairwise at Double-Metaphone distance ≥ 2 (`approve`↔`deny`, `confirm`↔`undo` checked explicitly); no phrase a substring of another; distinct from the wake/open phrase, persona emote phrases and persona voice names; no phrase found in the English n-gram reject list.

**Arms (D60; `^voice-armed`).** An arm's record lives in kernel memory only: `{armId, class: reversible|gate, repos[], humanConnId, voiceConnId, custody, openedAt, idleMs, hardMs, maxDecisions}`; its unlock — an evaluated LAContext / authenticated NCrypt handle for the approvals key — lives in the helper keyed by `armId`, never in the kernel (S29-K5). It opens when a `voice-arm` card — raised by `request.voice.arm` from the GUI palette or from the voice `arm` phrase (≤ 1 per 5 min, never while armed) — is decided `allow` with **fresh presence** on a human-class connection whose surface is the wrapper or a native client declaring capture exclusion (a browser surface is refused `voice-surface`); that connection becomes the **arming connection**. The prompt text is kernel-composed: repos, class, the voice binary's sha8, both `connId`s, expiry, decision count, "drops when the screen locks", "Speaker recognition is not authentication." A **gate arm** is a separate `voice-arm` card that needs a live reversible arm on the same pair, shows the toggle-2 warning every time, never refreshes on activity, and is limited to `armsPerHour` per repo. Only the **kernel-supervised voice child** can be armed: the kernel spawns `gnomon-voice` from `binary.path`, checks `sha256`, talks to it over inherited stdio (no socket credential) and runs it under the `voice` profile (§15.5); a voice client on the socket keeps every G1 capability but arming refuses it (`voice-custody`). **Custody (TD-2026-10-19-194).** There is no client-custody fallback on macOS: if the M4.1 TCC probe (P1) shows mic attribution fails for a daemon-spawned child, Mac voice stays **talk-only** (every G1 capability, no arm, so no voice decision of any kind) until the helper gains an operation that returns a socket peer's PID together with its code-signature identity; only then may a socket client be armed there. On Windows the fallback stands: a socket client whose arm binds its `connId` and, by the peer PID (`GetNamedPipeClientProcessId`), requires the signed `gnomon-voice` binary (`voice-peer`); `status` says `armCustody: client`. **Binary pin (TD-2026-10-19-194).** `gnomon-voice` ships as one signed executable. `binary.sha256` pins a manifest whose hash covers the executable, its native addon and every model file it loads; the kernel installs them into a kernel-owned 0700 directory under the home, spawns only from there, and re-hashes the whole manifest before every spawn (a mismatch refuses the spawn and arms nothing). That directory is in the write-deny set of every sandbox profile (§15.5), and the child loads its models from the bytes the kernel verified (handed over by the kernel, not reopened by path). **Disarm** (`approval.voice.disarm{armId, reason}`) on: idle or hard expiry; decision limit; either connection closing; a second voice connection; screen lock, display sleep, user switch or system sleep (observed by the helper; within 1 s); any policy or config change; any `approval.mode.set` (tighten or loosen, repo or session scope; TD-2026-10-19-194); the `disarm` phrase; GUI or CLI Disarm; a decision phrase from an unenrolled speaker; daemon exit; helper exit (its unlocks are gone); on a gate arm, one handle or challenge miss; on a reversible arm, 3 consecutive refused intents. Gate-only triggers end the gate arm; every other trigger ends both. An arm is never re-opened automatically. The `reason` is closed (`VOICE_DISARM_REASONS`, TD-2026-10-19-194): `idle` `hard` `limit` `conn-closed` `voice-conn` `screen-lock` `display-sleep` `user-switch` `system-sleep` `policy` `config` `mode-set` `phrase` `gui` `cli` `unenrolled` `daemon-exit` `helper-exit` `handle-miss` `challenge-miss` `refused-streak`. A disarm withdraws every card held under that arm (`approval.voice.withdrawn{cardId, by:'kernel', connId}`); nothing was signed, so nothing is reversed.

**Helper arm custody (helper 1.6.0, T-2026-10-19-026, Aegis V5).** The helper enforces its own bounds on every arm as defence in depth: `arm.open` requires `hardSeconds` (≤ 240 min) and `maxSignatures`, and caps `maxSignatures` at 1024. D64's `unlimited` therefore means 1024 signatures in the helper; mapping it, and enforcing the configured counts, stays the kernel's job. The helper holds at most 8 arms, apart from its 32 cached unlocks. It signs `gnomon:approve-voice:v1` only under a live arm, and only when the preimage (`gnomon-contracts` `voice-decision.ts`, frozen) names that arm and its `{humanConnId, voiceConnId}` pair. It refuses to open an arm, and closes every arm instead of signing, when the console session is locked, switched out or unknown. Its own prompt line (arm, both `connId`s, bounds) leads the Touch ID dialog, and the kernel's text after it is capped at 120 characters. A session push maps to `screen-lock` (also for a screensaver), `display-sleep`, `system-sleep` or `user-switch`, and the row carries the helper's `cause`.

**Grammar and targeting (D59).** Matched on normalized ASR text, n-best checked, anchored to the whole utterance (≤ 6 words before any `:` text):

```
utterance := prefix SP cmd [SP target] [SP ":" SP text]
cmd       := approve | deny | answer | confirm | undo | cards | open | arm | disarm
target    := "card" SP ordinal          ; against the last deck (≤ deckMaxAgeMs), reversible cards
           | "the" SP label SP "card"   ; closed kernel vocabulary, must match exactly one card in that deck
           | handle                     ; gate and judgment cards: the kernel's secret word for that card
           | "the" SP word SP "card"    ; free word against eligible readback lines — always two-step
ordinal   := one .. nine
label     := permission | bash | edit | write | web | tool | question | plan | gate | fallback | budget | knot | verdict
text      := ask-user answer text only
```

`cards` speaks the kernel's deck — eligible cards with ordinal, label and readback line — in the system voice and logs `voice.deck{deckId, cardIds}`; new cards never shift a spoken ordinal. Labels come from kernel fields only (card kind; tool name only for Claude Code's built-in tools, else `tool`), never agent text. There is no implicit target, no batch phrase, no "approve all", no "same again". A target matching 0 or > 1 cards refuses, and the system voice says "<n> cards match. Say claude admin cards." `open` focuses a card (`voice.focus`) and works with every toggle off.

| Card | Target | Steps |
|---|---|---|
| reversible | ordinal or closed label | one step: phrase → echo → hold → commit |
| reversible | free word | readback of that card → `confirm` within `confirmWindowMs` → hold → commit |
| gate, judgment | handle | phrase → readback + 2-word challenge → `confirm <w1> <w2>` within 10 s, one try → hold ≥ 5 s → commit; judgment cards also need ≥ 2 s on-screen focus on the arming connection |

**Handles and challenges.** While a gate arm is open, each pending gate or judgment card in its repos gets one **handle** word drawn without replacement per arm from a fixed, public, phonetically spaced 256-word list, bound to `{cardId, contentHash, armId}`; a changed or superseded card gets a new one. The **challenge** is two words from a 2048-word list (about 22 bits), issued after `approve <handle>` and bound the same way.
