Both are single-use and delivered **only to the arming connection**: in a capture-excluded, kernel-owned region — in the wrapper a native panel the wrapper owns, outside the WebView (`NSWindow.sharingType = .none`; `SetWindowDisplayAffinity(WDA_EXCLUDEFROMCAPTURE)`; whether ScreenCaptureKit honours `sharingType` on macOS 15+ is probe S29-10, run in M1.0, S29-K8) — or spoken by that connection when its route is headphones — never by the voice child, never to a browser surface, never in the chain before use (`handleHash`/`challengeHash` only; plaintext in `approval.decide` after use). The voice child knows only the public lists. A live handle or challenge word seen in any `session.send`, turn text, `textPlanned` or `textSpoken` rotates it (`approval.voice.handle.rotated`). The gate route must be headphones while `requireHeadphones` is on (`voice-route`).

**Hold, echo, undo.** An accepted intent logs `approval.voice.held{cardId, decision, armId, commitAt}`; the kernel-composed echo is spoken verbatim in the system voice ("Allowing bash permission, session three, in 3. Say claude admin undo."), shown in the card's kernel region and marked by an earcon. During the hold, `undo` on the same arm, `Esc` on the arming connection, or **Cancel** from any human-class connection (no presence — it narrows) logs `approval.voice.withdrawn{cardId, by, connId}` and returns the card to `pending`; nothing was signed, so nothing is reversed. At `commitAt` the broker re-checks that the arm is still live and re-reads the effective policy (a card the policy no longer admits is withdrawn `by:'kernel'`), then signs and commits. Deny-direction decisions (`deny`, `reject`) skip the hold. A `withdraw` requires the card to be `held`, held under the same `armId`, and is never blocked by card flags: undo is never narrower than the hold (TD-2026-10-19-194).

**Broker checks, in order** (each failure is `FORBIDDEN`, gate `voice`, logged `approval.voice.refused{reason, turnId, cardId?}`): (1) the caller is the paired voice connection of a live arm of the right custody; (2) the card's repo and class are in the effective policy and the arm; (3) the target resolves to exactly one card (deck age, ordinal, label, handle); (4) the card is `pending`, not hand-only, not flagged; (5) the referenced `voice.turn.user` is logged, intercepted, from an enrolled speaker at or above `speakerThreshold`, parses to this command and target, meets `minAsrConfidence`, has no other command in its n-best, and starts after the deck or handle was issued; (6) echo cross-check: its `t0` lies outside every `voice.turn.agent` playback `[t0, t1 + echoTailMs]`, its text is absent from the `textSpoken` of the last 30 s, and the client attests `ttsActive:false`, `otherRender:false`; (7) gate and judgment: a logged kernel readback of that card (`voice.turn.agent{source:'kernel-readback', readbackHash}`), a matching challenge, `confirm` inside the window, the headphone route, judgment focus; (8) rate: `minGapMs`, `maxDecisions`. Then the hold, then the signature under `gnomon:approve-voice:v1` (§3.1); `approval.decide{presence:'voice-armed', via:'voice', voiceDecision{…}}` names the arming human as approver. Judgment decisions carry `via: voice` on `verdict.founder` and on the KNOT card's decision, which Warpline's attribution cites; Warpline's own `resolve` stays a human CLI act (§6.2).

Refusal reasons: `voice-off` `voice-not-armed` `voice-custody` `voice-peer` `voice-surface` `voice-conn` `voice-project` `voice-class` `voice-hand-only` `voice-ineligible` `voice-target` `voice-ambiguous` `voice-handle` `voice-stale-utterance` `voice-grammar` `voice-speaker` `voice-echo` `voice-playback` `voice-readback-missing` `voice-challenge` `voice-confirm-expired` `voice-route` `voice-focus` `voice-rate` `voice-strictness` `held`.

**Self-approval and replay guards.** Agent TTS, gnome lines, readback and card bodies can all carry a phrase, and speakers plus a mic will leak it. So: the voice child masks every phrase and its phonetic neighbours in all TTS input (`voice.tts.filtered`); the decision grammar is muted during our playback and `echoTailMs`; the kernel computes the `voice-phrase` flag at card creation with the same matcher, making the card voice-ineligible; decision utterances are intercepted and never reach a model, so no model sees phrases in its transcript; the echo cross-check above runs on logged timing and text; the client refuses while another process renders audio (Core Audio process objects, macOS 14.2+; `IAudioSessionManager2` sessions on Windows) and reports concurrent mic use on Windows (CapabilityAccessManager); handles and challenges never repeat within an arm. Speaker score, playback state, TTS state, route and ASR confidence are claims of the voice client, logged on `approval.voice.intent` with `attestedBy: voice` — they stop accidents, not a compromised client, and no sentence of this spec calls them a boundary.

**Never by voice, with every toggle on.** `install` (incl. a linked mod's re-approve, manifest widening and `mod dev`) · `use-api-token` · `always` / `approval.rule.learned` · `voice-arm` and `grant` cards · every root-signed verb (`key *`, `trust`, `grant.*`, `mod link|unlink|dev|rollback`, `gui pin`, `billing api-token`, `voice enrol|forget`, `voice approvals enable`) · `daemon stop`, `log segment` · attach and Reconnect presence · `voice-share` (consent belongs to each speaker) · any card with `flags` (`hidden-chars`, `bidi`, `voice-phrase`, `irreversible`, `claim-mismatch`, `peer`) · `strictness` cards and every run-mode or level change, `incident` cards, and `restart` or `extend` on a `reconsider` card (§3.4, §7.3) · judgment kinds without the sub-opt-in. **Exception (D66, S26-L3):** with toggle 1 on, `deny` and `reject` by voice are accepted on every card kind above, inside a reversible arm, with no hold — a deny fails closed, so the worst case is a re-request; approving those kinds stays hand-only. **Except** `incident` and `daemon-stop`: fully hand-only by voice, deny and reject included (founder ruling, TD-2026-10-19-677). **And** the claude-binary re-pin (a `gate-class` card whose detail `target` is `claude-binary`, TD-2026-10-19-063): fully hand-only by voice, deny included; `#approval-broker` refuses it `FORBIDDEN{reason:'voice-hand-only'}` before the voice-off check, because the allowlist sees only (kind, decision) and not the detail (no `clientApiId` move).

**The allowlist (TD-2026-10-19-194).** What voice may decide is pure data in `gnomon-contracts` (`VOICE_ALLOWLIST`, `voiceMayDecide(class, kind, decision, flags)`), and that list is the authoritative hand-only mechanism: anything it does not name is hand-only (default deny), so a new card kind or decision is hand-only until a row names it. Classes are cumulative, as the grant sets are (D62). `reversible`: `permission` `allow|deny`, `ask-user` `answer`, `plan` `approve-plan`. `gate` adds `gate-class` `allow`, `fallback` `fallback-cloud`, `budget` `resume`. `judgment` adds `knot` `resolve` and `verdict` `accept|reject` (D61). The **deny row** (D66): with any class, each kind's deny-direction decision (`deny` or `reject`) by voice, with no hold — except `incident` and `daemon-stop`, whose deny rows are empty. `withdraw` (`undo` during the hold) is admitted on any kind with an approving row, never blocked by flags; the broker also requires state `held` under the same `armId`. Any card flag makes every other decision on that card hand-only, deny included (`voice-ineligible`). `flags` is a required argument. Broker check (4) reads this list; `#approval-broker`'s presence classes (`classes.ts`) say how a hand decision is taken, never whether voice may take it.

**No level, run modes (TD-2026-10-19-194).** While a repo has no strictness level (levels unbuilt, `status.runModes` `strictness: {built: false}`), the overlay's ceiling is `reversible`: voice decides toggle 1 at most, no gate arm opens, and `gnomon voice approvals <repo> enable --gate` (or `--judgment`) is refused `FORBIDDEN`, gate `voice`, reason `voice-strictness`, `data.ceiling:'null-level'`, while the level is null. In `manual` run mode voice may only deny: an approving intent on a card of a `manual` session is refused with the same reason and `data.ceiling:'manual'`, and the deny row stands. Under `auto-edits` a voice-originated turn behaves exactly like a typed turn: the mode's auto-passed edits pass with no card, and the kernel adds no clamp for voice origin (founder ruling).

**Forwarding guards (TD-2026-10-19-194).** A `session.send` from the voice connection is refused unless it cites a logged `voice.turn.user` from an enrolled speaker in push-to-talk mode whose `t0` lies outside every `voice.turn.agent` playback `[t0, t1 + echoTailMs]`; a voice send rides the permit of the session voice is attached to and never reserves its own; and voice is attached to a session only by a human gesture in the GUI, never by the voice child or an agent. The kernel stamps its own receipt time on every `voice.turn.user` and `voice.turn.agent` row and judges on its clock as well as the client's: a turn is forwardable for at most 30 s after the kernel received it (configurable; voice slice V1), never if its receipt or its `t0` precedes the attachment, and the echo window is also checked on kernel receipt times. Push-to-talk is a **client claim**: each `voice.turn.user` carries the client's `mode`, which the kernel cross-checks against the mode the human chose when attaching (`voice-off`, `data.mode`); it is not a kernel-enforced boundary (§A.4).

**Ceiling and ambient guards (TD-2026-10-19-298, voice slice V2a).** The mode the human chose when attaching is a **ceiling**, not an equality: a `ptt` turn is forwardable under any ceiling, a `conversation` (ambient) turn only under a `conversation` ceiling; a turn above the ceiling is refused `voice-off`, `data{mode, ceiling, cause:'ceiling'}`. Re-issuing `voice.session.start{hsid, mode, continues?}` from a first-party human ends the old attachment and starts a new one (`continues` must name the human's own prior `voiceSessionId`, else `voice-target`); every turn logged under the old attachment is `voice-stale-utterance`. **Widening** to `conversation` takes one fresh presence (a `strictness` card, `detail.op:'voice.widen'`, decided `allow` with Touch ID / Windows Hello: the run-mode loosening path, never voice-decidable) once per first-party human connection — one GUI login; a closed connection loses the grant. Narrowing, re-attaching at `ptt` and ending take no presence. The voice principal can never start an attachment or set its mode. Ambient turns are forwardable (no wake word, no ask clamp; under `auto-edits` they behave like typed turns, founder rulings). Under a `conversation` ceiling **every** turn, whatever mode it claims, is forwarded only with an `aec` claim (`vpio` | `headphones`) on the `voice.turn.user`, else `voice-off` `data.cause:'aec'` (the refusal reason occupies `data.reason`), from an enrolled speaker, and while the attached session is **not busy by the kernel's own clock**: from its `turn.sent` until its result, and through `echoTailMs` after, the turn is refused `voice-playback` `data.clock:'kernel'` even when the client logs no playback. Idle fails closed: once `idleAt` passes, every send is refused `voice-off` `data.cause:'idle'` even before the kernel's end row lands; a failed end row is counted (`status.voice.idleEndFailures`) and retried, never dropped. A pending widening card is reused only while it is pending and for the same session; a denied or expired one is dropped and a new one raised. **Strictness cards carry an op (rule):** a `strictness` card is raised for `detail.op:'mode.loosen'` (run modes) and `detail.op:'voice.widen'` (voice); every consumer of strictness cards must branch on `detail.op`, and `voice.widen` is not a strictness or run-mode change. `asrModel` (at most 64 of `[A-Za-z0-9_.-]`) and `confidence` (a number in 0..1) are bounded on every `voice.turn.user`. A conversation ceiling with no forwarded turn for `voice.ambientIdleMs` (default 10 minutes, clamped to at most 60 minutes, never unbounded) is ended by the kernel: `voice.session.end{reason:'idle'}` under the kernel principal, which every subscriber reads. **Privacy:** a turn the kernel refuses at receipt (unknown `spk:unknown-N` speaker, over the ceiling, no `aec`, busy, echo, no attachment) is stored without its text: a per-boot keyed text hash (`hmac-sha256:`), its duration and the refusal reason; it is never forwardable. Forwarded turns keep their full text, `sensitive`. `status.voice` carries the kernel-owned live-mic indicator `{attached, hsid, ceiling, since, idleAt}` (ISO timestamps) plus `voiceSessionId`, `turns{n}`, `echoTailMs`, `ambientIdleMs`; it is an unreserved status field, so `clientApiId` does not move. **Residual risk:** `aec` and the per-turn `mode` stay client claims; the kernel busy window covers model streaming plus `echoTailMs` only, so client TTS that plays longer than that, a client that logs no `voice.turn.agent`, and a turn claiming a duration of 0 can each get an echoed turn past it (the client playback rows and the AEC claim are the rest of the defence); a refusal decided only at send time (stale age, text mismatch) leaves the text the row already carried; the ambient microphone hears every enrolled-sounding voice in the room, and speaker recognition is not authentication.

**Dictation (TD-2026-10-20-262, T-2026-10-19-026).** Dictated text goes into the GUI **composer** and the GUI sends it after a countdown; the voice client does not send it. The kernel-spawned client runs with `delivery: 'composer'` (the default); it never calls `session.send` and appends every `voice.turn.user` with `forwarded:false`. `gnomon-voice run --delivery direct` keeps the old forward for headless use. **Forwardable verdict:** an accepted `voice.turn.user` (one the kernel did not refuse at receipt) carries a kernel-stamped `forwardable:true` (`false` on an intercepted turn); a client-sent `forwardable` is refused `BAD_REQUEST{bad-params}`, like `receivedAt`. A refused row stays hash-only. **Attribution at the human send:** `session.send` accepts `source` only with the value `'voice'` and only from a human principal. It is an attribution hint and never a capability: any other value, or any other principal class, is `BAD_REQUEST{bad-params}`. The kernel hash-matches each content part, in order, against the attachment's pending turns. A turn is pending when it is forwardable, not yet forwarded, not intercepted, logged by the human's own `p:<ns>/voice` principal in the human's current attachment on that `hsid`, and received after the attachment started. If every part matches, `turn.sent` and `session.inject` carry `{source:'voice', voiceSessionId, voiceTurnSeqs:[…], voiceTurnSeq:<last>}` and the parts are joined with `' '`. If only some match (the human edited the composer), they carry `{source:'voice-edited', voiceSessionId, voiceTurnSeqs:[<matched>]}` and the parts are joined with `'\n'` as for any send. If nothing matches, there is no voice tag. Matched turns count as forwarded, so a later direct send of the same turn is `voice-stale-utterance`; if the human send fails, they are released. Attribution never refuses a human send. The 30 s freshness check and the latest-turn rule bind only the direct (voice principal) path; a pending dictation turn of the live attachment is kept past the pruning horizon, but never past the 256-turn cap. These payload fields are opaque to `event:v1`, so `clientApiId` and `ABI_ID` do not move. **Activity:** the client reports edges only, `request.voice.report{dictation:{activity:'speaking'|'transcribing'|'idle', holdToggles}}`: `speaking` from VAD start (or while push-to-talk is held), `transcribing` from the segment end, and `idle` after the append. The kernel holds the last report in memory, never as a log row, and serves it as `status.voice.dictation{activity, holdToggles, at}` (or `null` when there is none, or it is older than the 10-minute report TTL). `holdToggles` is a non-negative integer that never decreases on a voice connection (a new connection starts its own count); a bad shape is `BAD_REQUEST`. **Hold hotkey:** `gnomon-voice-io` reports `hold-toggle` (Carbon id 4, default `ctrl+alt+h`) on key down; the client increments `holdToggles` and reports it, and the GUI holds or releases its send countdown on each change.

**Stop speaking (T-2026-10-19-026).** The client reports its spoken-reply edges, `request.voice.report{playback:{playing}}`; the kernel holds the last one in memory (never a row) and serves `status.voice.playback{playing, at}` (`null` when none or past the report TTL). The human stops it three ways: the GUI's ⏹ "Stop speaking" beside the mic pill (shown only while the voice app is connected and `playback.playing`), Esc when no menu or dialog is open and focus is not in the composer, or the `gnomon-voice-io` hotkey `stop-speaking` (Carbon id 5, default `ctrl+alt+.`, acted on locally by the client). The GUI paths send `log.append request.voice.client{op:'stop-speaking'}`: human principals only (device or GUI credential), a narrowing with no card and no presence, and never a log row. It bumps `status.voice.stopSpeakingSeq` (a counter declared in `STATUS_NUMBERS`); the client re-reads status every 300 ms while it speaks and stops playback when that counter changes. The GUI re-reads status every 300 ms only while `playback.playing` (and for 4 s after a voice row, to see a reply start). A re-read, a scroll or the transcript's follow-end never stops speech, and barge-in needs speech at or above −42 dBFS. `clientApiId` and `ABI_ID` do not move. **TTS speed (bench, 2026-10-19, Apple M4, Kokoro-82M int8 en v0.19, warm, 3 sentences × 3 reps, `process.hrtime`, audio kept in memory):** real-time factor (synth time / audio time) at threads 2/4/6 was 0.71/0.69/0.68 at speed 1.0 and 0.77/0.67/0.81 at speed 1.1, about 2.5–3.1 s of synth per ~4 s sentence. Thread count barely matters past 2, and the run-to-run noise (±0.1) is larger than the differences. Kokoro on CPU runs at about 0.7× real time, so the reply pipeline must synthesize sentence N+1 while sentence N plays. `TTS_THREADS` stays 4 and `TTS_SPEED` stays 1.0, the voice's natural rate (1.1 saves no synth time per word).

**Table talk (TD-2026-10-20-294, T-2026-10-19-026).** Talking with a voice-attached session should feel like sitting at a table with someone who shows you things and talks you through them. The full protocol is `docs/guides/table-talk.md` (Paradigm protocol `P-gnomon-table-talk-voice-replies`). **Two channels:** the model's reply opens with a fenced `speak` block (1–3 spoken sentences, at most 300 characters, second person, pointing at the screen, ending with one clear question); code, paths, numbers and detail go below it as reference. Labelling has no fixed scheme: the agent picks letters, numbers, short names, positions or no label, whichever is clearest. **Projector (S1):** the kernel's speakable reply item is the FIRST `speak` block (markdown stripped, URLs and long digit or hex runs removed, capped at 300 characters on a sentence boundary; an unclosed fence runs to its first blank line) with `from:'block'`; without one it falls back to the `gnome:` line or the first paragraph with `from:'heuristic'`. The kernel's own words (the ask-waiting line, "Edited N files") carry `from:'kernel'`; a model ask-user question carries no `from`. Block text is only words: "approved" or "system:" inside it acts on nothing. **Per-turn guidance (S2):** while voice is attached to a session and its owner still owns it (`VoiceControl.guidanceFor(hsid)`), every `session.send` to that Claude session, typed or voice, writes the frozen guidance (`kernel/voice/table-talk.ts`, at most 160 words) as a SECOND text block of the user message, wrapped `<gnomon-voice>…</gnomon-voice>`. `contentHash` (on `session.inject` and `turn.sent`) stays the hash of the human's text alone, so replay binds as before; `turn.sent` adds an opaque `guidanceHash`. `gnomon trace` hashes the first text block of a transcript prompt as the content and allows at most one extra text block, only when that `turn.sent` carries `guidanceHash` and only with that hash; any other extra block is a trace mismatch (Aegis M2). Nothing is added when voice is not attached. The same spoken-text scrub (URLs, 12+ hex runs, 5+ digit runs) applies to heuristic and ask items (Aegis L2), and the voice client speaks a kernel-speakable row only when its principal is `p:<ns>/kernel` (Aegis L1). **GUI (S3):** a reply's `speak` block shows as a highlighted line at the top and is hidden from the body. The line's words come from the kernel's speakable row for that reply when there is one (`from:'block'`, tagged "said aloud"; `from:'heuristic'` shows no line); without one (voice not attached) the GUI's own parse is shown, tagged "spoken part" (Aegis L3). Lines that start with a bold label (any scheme) show as subtle chips. A replayed user message always keeps `content[0]`; a later text block is hidden only when its sha256 is the kernel guidance's or that turn's `guidanceHash`, so a human-typed `<gnomon-voice>` stays visible (Aegis M1). **System voice (S4):** `from:'kernel'` items are said by a different Kokoro speaker (`systemSidFor`, never the model's speaker) and the first sentence is preceded by a generated two-note earcon (no file). Model text (`from:'block'`, `'heuristic'`, or no `from`) never gets the system voice or the earcon. `from` and `guidanceHash` are opaque payload fields, so `clientApiId` and `ABI_ID` do not move.

**Honest scope.** Inside a live arm a decision rests on audio the voice child accepted, not on identity. Reversible cards are protected by the client-side guards and the kernel's echo cross-check, not by a secret: anyone or anything the microphone hears saying a phrase in a voice the recognizer accepts, outside our playback, can allow a tool call, answer a question or approve a plan, up to the arm's count, each held 2–10 s. Gate and judgment cards additionally need the handle and the challenge, which no session, mod, voice or browser-surface connection can read; whoever also sees the arming screen or hears its headphones can still pass them. Toggle 2 is a deliberate trade of presence per decision for presence per short, counted window. Detectable and attributable to "a speaker accepted inside arm `<armId>`", not prevented (§A.4).

**Falsifiers** (both OS unless stated): `G-VOX-08` toggle custody — `voice.approvals` in `.gnomon/project.yaml` (base revision or worktree) enables nothing and logs `config.ignored`; widening without root presence is impossible; `[gate]` without `reversible` and `[judgment]` without `gate` are refused; the toggle-2 and judgment cards and prompts carry the warning byte-for-byte; narrowing needs no presence and ends affected arms within 1 s · `G-VOX-09` self-echo rig, AEC on and off, speakers: an agent turn, a gnome line, a readback and a card body containing the approve phrase (exact, punctuation, casing and phonetic variants) plus every handle-list word yield zero decisions; TTS output never contains an unmasked phrase; an utterance overlapping playback + tail is refused `voice-echo`; a card body matching the grammar is flagged `voice-phrase` · `G-VOX-10` replay — a recording of an earlier valid utterance is refused `voice-handle`, `voice-stale-utterance` or `voice-echo`; another process rendering audio yields `voice-playback` · `G-VOX-11` arm lifecycle — an arm needs fresh presence on a wrapper or capture-excluding native connection (browser ⇒ `voice-surface`); a socket voice client cannot be armed (`voice-custody`); a second voice connection ends the arm and its intent is `voice-conn`; idle, hard cap, decision limit, screen lock or sleep, connection close, narrowing and config change each end the arm within 1 s; the prompt names repos, class, binary sha, both `connId`s and expiry · `G-VOX-12` secrets — no session, mod, voice or browser-surface connection can read a live handle or challenge through `log.read`, `ref.resolve`, `status` or a relay; the chain holds only hashes before use; a live handle in forwarded text rotates it · `G-VOX-13` targeting — `card two` resolves against the deck that was spoken, unchanged by a later card; a deck older than `deckMaxAgeMs` refuses; a label matching 0 or > 1 cards refuses; a free word always takes readback + `confirm`; n-best ambiguity refuses; with 5 pending gate cards an intent decides exactly the card whose handle was spoken at its current `contentHash`, and a changed card gets a new handle · `G-VOX-14` class gating — with toggle 1 only a gate card is refused `voice-class`; without the sub-opt-in a judgment card is refused `voice-class`; every kind in the never-list is refused `voice-hand-only` or `voice-ineligible` for any approving decision with every toggle on, while `deny`/`reject` on the same kinds under toggle 1 is accepted with no hold (D66), except on `incident` and `daemon-stop`, where it is refused `voice-hand-only` (TD-2026-10-19-677); a card in another repo is refused `voice-project` · `G-VOX-15` hold — undo, `Esc` or Cancel inside the hold leaves no `approval.decide`, the card `pending` and the tool not run; after the hold exactly one decision signed under `gnomon:approve-voice:v1`; a deny skips the hold · `G-VOX-16` gate arm — speakers instead of headphones ⇒ `voice-route`; one wrong challenge ends the gate arm; a 4th gate decision ⇒ `voice-rate`; expiry at the configured cap with no refresh; a config above 60 min is refused at sign time; a gate card without a logged readback or with `confirm` after the window is refused · `G-STR-08` strictness overlay — a trip during a live reversible or gate arm ends it within 1 s; after the level loosens the arm is still gone and nothing re-arms; with the repo at L1 a gate arm cannot open and a gate-class intent is refused `voice-strictness`; at L2 an approving intent is refused while a `deny` is accepted; an arm opened at L1 or above with `unlimited` configured takes the default count · `G-BRK-06` signature domains — a voice decision verifies only under `gnomon:approve-voice:v1`, a hand signature never verifies as voice nor the reverse, `gnomon log verify` classifies both, and `approval.suspect` fires on any voice decision missing its arm, enrolled speaker or (gate, judgment) readback · `T-VOX-02` decision-phrase false accept / false reject on the founder's rig over 8 h of ambient corpus (music, video, podcasts, a second speaker, the agent's own TTS) plus a scripted founder set, per mode (PTT / conversation), with method; toggle 2's switch stays disabled until it has been run once and reported — availability gated on measurement, not on an invented threshold.

---

## 15. Security model

### 15.1 Threats and what holds (Aegis stage-1: the OS user)

| # | Threat | Mitigation | Residual |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Forged env identity | key/device-stamped socket identity (I2) | Warpline unmarked CLI shells until P8-D16 |
| 2 | Session approves/resumes/resolves/installs for itself | `approve` HUMAN_ONLY; `decisionSig` with approver in preimage by a hardware approvals key with presence (fresh, or cached ≤ 15 min on the one `connId` that passed presence, for `tool.permission` allow/deny, `ask-user`, `plan`; `always` is fresh); `use-api-token`/`install` need the root key (presence); session sandbox denies `~/.gnomon/keys`, the endpoint port and other processes' info; `approval.suspect` flags no-TTY, session-ancestry, wrong-presence or wrong-connection decisions | a sandbox-escaped process holding a human-class connection's socket issuing a **cached-class** decision within that connection's unlock window — detectable by `approval.presence{cached}` + `connId` + `cli` ancestry, not prevented (D38, §A.1); with voice approvals on, an intent inside a live voice arm from anyone or anything the voice child accepts as the founder's enrolled voice — for gate and judgment cards only with the handle and challenge read off the arming connection (§14.7, §A.4) |
| 3 | Runaway fan-out / quota drain / founder lock-out | metering only (P8-D92): every permit attributed, per-message usage from stream events, per-user/repo/session/model/backend spend and subscription-window usage in `status`, informational `budget.ladder` rows, quota errors recorded, GUI | **visible, not prevented**: by founder ruling P8-D92 nothing caps, reserves, pauses or kills a runaway fan-out or a quota drain, so the founder can be locked out of the subscription window by their own armada (law 2: stated, not hidden); out-of-kernel `claude -p` (visible count) |
| 4 | Prompt injection (tool results, KNOT payloads `descriptors.ts:52-61`, notebooks, neurons, voice transcripts, mod output) | untrusted-content sentence; T3 as untrusted; `inject.receipt`; reviewer has no tools; voice text is data; `source: voice` tag | the reading model |
| 5 | Env/config bypass | no `*_DISABLE`; root-signed config (hardware presence); `GNOMON_HOME` a test-only home seam the daemon never sees (§2.4); project scope cannot set policy | an attacker with the presence prompt (i.e. the human) |
| 6 | API-credit billing by accident or by an agent | law 7 preflight on env + every settings layer + `mcp.json` env blocks + base URLs; mid-session layer writes stopped (`G-SUB-05`); per-use root-signed card; token injected into one process; indicator + events | a layer the scan does not know (new Claude Code setting) — the scan list is versioned per `claude` version in `G-SUB-01` |
| 7 | Socket/pipe hijack or relay | 0600 socket / helper-created pipe with owner-only DACL + `FIRST_PIPE_INSTANCE`, `serverSig` as the server anchor (the pipe-PID check is a diagnostic, S29-K6), 0700 home / owner-only ACL, mutual auth bound to endpoint path, single-use nonce | no peer credentials in Node on unix sockets |
| 8 | Supply chain | law 6; kernel zero runtime deps (`G-SEC-01`); pi-ai, React and Excalidraw pinned exact in userland packages only; the GUI bundle fetches nothing at runtime; signed helpers verified by checksum at start | — |
| 9 | Malicious or buggy mod | out of process under the default-deny `mod` profile, run from an immutable pinned snapshot, declared effects enforced at the boundary and by profile, closed kit + sanitizer, append-only card slots, no credential, caller-hsid proxying, host-stamped `relayedFor`, host-filled Tier 0 lines, `install` card + rollback to a previous snapshot | a mod's `tool.call` output is model-visible text (threat 4), logged by the bridge as `inject.receipt` |
| 10 | Log rewrite / db deletion | mandatory signatures, two anchors (file + keystore via helper) on the P8-D82 cadence, critical kinds anchored before their effect (an ordering guarantee, P8-D83), boot-classified truncation that is never accepted silently, presence-gated `daemon.log.gap`, durable `daemon.anchor.failed` markers, bounded lag on open with the P8-D85 cross-boot relax, missing-db rule (§3.2) | same-user rewrite of both anchors + re-sign with on-disk Ed25519 keys (root- and approval-signed rows excluded); a same-user unsandboxed process that stops the kernel and deletes up to the last 1023 non-critical rows on macOS, or any number on Windows or without the helper (§A.1(g)); until T-2026-10-17-009, no recovery from a truncation short of deleting the home (P8-D84, §A.6) |
| 11 | Project files set policy | base-revision read; gate-class on `.gnomon/**`, `.claude/**`, `CLAUDE*.md`, test/package/pipeline files, AND `.git/config`/`hooks`/`info`/`.gitattributes` via `gitHash` | a hook path the hardening flags do not neutralize (D12 class) |
| 12 | Oracle runs agent code on the key box | default-deny `gnomon-oracle` profile (copy of worktree, no network, no keystore/mach-lookup, escape tests) | sandbox-exec deprecated / Windows restricted-token fidelity (§A.2); sessions are sandboxed for key access and settings writes — the oracle sandbox protects oracle integrity, not keys |
| 13 | Shared `.git` write by a session (hooks, fsmonitor, filters, includes) | hardening env + flags on every kernel git call; `filter.*`/`merge.*.driver`/`diff.*.textconv`/`include*` refused; `git` sandbox class; `gitHash` gate-class | git features outside the list |
| 14 | Voice spoofing / audio exfiltration | voice as its own low-scope principal; speaker id is not authentication; enrolled voiceprints local and `secret`; unenrolled speech not forwarded by default; audio not kept; text-only to Claude; voice decides only through a broker-signed, presence-backed arm on the supervised child, per-project root-signed grants, phrase mask, half-duplex, echo cross-check, hold and undo, and for gate class the kernel's handle and challenge | microphone access is the OS's permission, not Gnomon's; a live relay or clone of the founder's voice plus sight of the arming screen inside an armed window (§14.7, §A.4) |
| 15 | Windows read-denial fidelity | `WRITE_RESTRICTED` restricts writes only; helper ACL/AppContainer work for read denial; `partial` reported honestly in `status` | on Windows key-read denial inside sandboxes is `partial` until proven (§A.3) |
| 16 | Browser-borne attack on the loopback endpoint (another tab, DNS rebinding, CSRF, token theft, markup or hidden Unicode in block strings, a **browser extension** with all-sites access) | `#web-endpoint`: `127.0.0.1` only, single-use launch token delivered privately and cleared from the fragment, GUI token in page memory only, no reconnect grace (Reconnect = presence), `serverSig` verified in the page, bundle hash pinned, HTTP/WebSocket parsing in a sandboxed transport child, origin lock (one origin), `Host` allowlist, no cookies + `Sec-Fetch-Site` required, no CORS header, strict CSP, auth deadline, per-connection rate limits, DOM-text rendering + Unicode rules (`G-WEB-01..08`, `G-GUI-07`, `G-GUI-11`); the wrapper is the default surface and the browser surface is badged (D56) | an **extension** with all-sites access reads page memory, sends `isTrusted` events and uses this connection's cached unlock (S25-L1 — why the wrapper is the daily surface and the user-gesture check is not a defence against it); a same-user **unsandboxed** process reading the launching CLI's memory within 60 s; a stolen GUI token inside that connection's cached-unlock window (§A.1) |
| 17 | A non-conformant community client misleads its user (draws a fake card, hides a real one) | kernel-key-signed cards and `actions[]`; presence prompt text composed from the kernel's card record (native or helper-window on Windows); `declared` conformance in `status.clients[]`, never `passed`; redacted class unless `log.read:sensitive` is granted (`G-CLI-02`, `G-CLI-03`) | cached-class decisions on a misleading client within its own connection's window (§A.1); the user's choice of client |
| 18 | A linked mod folder changes under the user (edit, `git pull`, dependency install) or a session edits it | immutable pinned snapshot per approval (nothing loads from the folder); folder watch + rehash; pause before the next load; re-approve card listing the changed files and any manifest widening with `package@version` from the lockfile; symlink/junction/hardlink/`.node` refused at copy; sessions denied writes to linked folders; dev mode only for tier `local`, capped at `read`, expiring, badged (`G-MOD-08`, `G-MOD-09`, `G-MOD-11`) | a change between two watch events on a filesystem without change notification affects only the **next** approval (the running snapshot is immutable; full rehash on every start and `inspect`) |
| 19 | A sandboxed session races the GUI attach (polls other processes for a launch URL, connects to the endpoint first, becomes the human principal) | token never on any argv (stdin to the wrapper, Apple Event on macOS, token-less URL + presence on Windows); `session` profile denies loopback connects to the endpoint port, process info on other processes (on macOS including `mach-lookup` of `com.apple.sysmond`, which `pgrep` uses — p10m) and unix-socket connects to the user's tmux/screen servers (`G-SBX-08`); a launch token reused inside its TTL revokes the session that used it and logs `socket.auth.theft` (after the TTL: `socket.auth.replay`, log only); sessions cannot read browser profiles (S29-K7); the attach and Reconnect prompts name `connId` and the open-session count (D55, `G-WEB-05`, `G-WEB-08`) | Windows `partial` where process-info reads cannot be denied (§A.3); an unsandboxed same-user process, which already owns the device key (§A.1); on macOS the stock launchers (`open`, `osascript`, `lsregister`) are denied exec (P8-D80), but an app started through LaunchServices by a launcher the session builds itself runs unsandboxed with full same-user rights; it still needs the single-use token or a Reconnect presence touch, and every approval still needs presence (accepted, P8-D79/D80, §A.2) |
| 20 | Spoken approval by someone or something other than the founder (TV, a call, a colleague, agent TTS, a replayed or cloned voice) while voice approvals are armed | toggles off by default, root-signed per project; arm = fresh presence, short, counted, dropped on screen lock; arming only from the wrapper or a capture-excluding native client; enrolled speaker; phrase mask, muted grammar during playback, half-duplex, echo cross-check, playback gate, interception, `voice-phrase` flag; hold + undo; handle + 2-word challenge + headphones for gate class; hand-only kinds; armed indicator + Disarm; `presence: voice-armed` + `approval.suspect` | a replayed or imitated founder voice saying a phrase outside our playback inside a live arm, deciding an eligible reversible card — or, for gate class, by someone who also reads the arming screen or hears its headphones; Windows capture exclusion and audio-render detection `partial` until the M4.1 probe proves them (§A.4) |
| 21 | Pairing MITM, phishing or impersonation (an intercepted invite, a relay in the middle, a root ground to the founder's `ns`, injected text that talks the human into pairing) | root-signed, human-only `gnomon link pair` with no staging form; single-use out-of-band invite with a 128-bit PSK; `XXpsk3`; a 6-word SAS compared on both screens; fresh root presence on both sides; peers compared by full `rootKeyId`, `ns` for display only; 30-day grants (`G-LNK-02`, `G-LNK-03`) | a human who confirms a mismatched SAS; a peer's self-asserted name (shown as unverified) |
| 22 | A paired peer (a rogue teammate, a compromised machine) injects instructions, floods cards or prompts, or tries to spend | no ABI for peers (no verb, relay, read or decision); pull-only co-work inbox; a brief waits for a fresh-presence accept; `peer-envelope:v1`, `session.inject{origin:'peer'}` or `inject.receipt`, sticky taint, the `peer` flag (voice-ineligible, no `always`, fresh presence for teammate-tainted `tool.permission`); per-peer limits on messages, briefs and peer-caused cards (`G-LNK-04..07`, `G-LNK-12`) | the reading model (threat 4); a human who accepts a malicious brief |
| 23 | Leak on egress (secrets, founder-private events, keys or credentials reaching a teammate or the relay) | one kernel egress filter over append-time labels (§9.1 defaults); `secret` never crosses; teammates get `team`/`org` only; fresh-presence `handoff`/`declassify` cards listing every item; secret scan; projection, never relabelling in place; the never-cross list; the link child holds no key (`G-LNK-08`, `G-LNK-11`) | best-effort secret scanning; a mislabelled item; whatever the recipient does with data already sent (revocation is not retroactive) |
| 24 | A malicious or compromised relay (tamper, replay, reorder, drop, metadata collection) | end-to-end Noise between kernels (pinned static keys, AEAD, counters, forward secrecy for live sessions); acks; consumed `briefId`s; the relay holds no plaintext and no key that opens content (`G-LNK-10`) | metadata — who talks to whom, when, how much; store-and-forward items lack forward secrecy against later theft of the receiver's link key; a relay that drops everything (availability only) |
| 25 | A stolen linked machine, or a teammate gone rogue | grants expire after 30 days and renew only with root presence; revocation from any human-class connection without presence, effective within 1 s (`G-LNK-09`); a thief needs root presence to pair anew and fresh presence to accept a brief; teammate links need `full` link-key custody | a thief who passes presence on the stolen machine (the login-password fallback, §A.5) acts within the grant's lifetime; everything already sent |
| 26 | An agent forces tightening to wear the founder down (deliberate reverts, contrived KNOTs, override bait) until approvals become rubber stamps | tightening removes autonomy, never adds cards per decision (B14, P8-D86); override-class signals at a capped weight; a tightened repo's cards batched; only kernel-observed signals count (B11) — §3.4 | a repo held at L1 or L2 by an agent costs throughput, not attention; `T-STR-01`'s share-of-time field falsifier (§20) watches for it |
| 27 | An agent games the level down (suppressing or disguising reverts, straddling a threshold, a sawtooth, respawning to shed history, a peer asserting "all clear") | reverts detected as content restoration whatever the commit says; non-fast-forward and deleted watched refs trip; decayed score keyed by node lineage; loosen window ≥ 3× tighten window and recall to the previous peak; positive evidence + dwell + fresh presence per step; absence and unknown data never loosen; peers move nothing (B11–B13) — §3.4 | git writes outside the kernel are visibility only (`status.unobservedRefWrites`), and they block loosening rather than counting |
| 28 | An agent games the review cap (declares rounds, re-ids or splits a node, respawns, asks for `reconsider` early to escape review) | rounds are kernel-observed reviewer results keyed by node lineage; the cap refuses the reviewer permit; the `reconsider` body is kernel-composed; no `reconsider` decision merges, approves or certifies; `extend` is fresh presence and capped at 2 — §7.3 | a reviewer that paraphrases a finding under another class (OPEN 21); a split across registrations more than 24 h apart |
| 29 | A card's agent-written test, risk or revert text misleads the founder ("tested", "low risk", a revert command that does harm) | `agentClaims` sanitized and fenced as `agent-claimed`; `verified{n, of}` only from kernel-signed `oracle.result` ids; kernel risk floor and `claim-mismatch`; `irreversible` ⇒ fresh presence, no voice; revert text never executed or offered as a control (B16) — §3.4 | the founder reading the claim instead of the kernel strip |
| 30 | A process of the OS user re-appends an old, validly root-signed grant-table line (an install row after an uninstall, an older pin after a re-pin) to revive a mod or a removed snapshot (M1.11 slice 3, Aegis C1) | the grant reader rejects any install or uninstall row whose createdAt is not later than the newest pin row already read for that mod (`replay`); the writer stamps every pin row createdAt = max(now, newest pin for that mod plus one millisecond) by the same rule, so a clock step back or two pins in one millisecond still apply, and it reads the table back and refuses unless the row is the effective pin (an uninstall card reports nothing unless it took effect) | **accepted until the grant table is hash-chained**: deleting a line (an uninstall or a later pin) still restores the earlier effective row; the table is append-only by convention, not by construction |
| 31 | Any process of the OS user pauses a mod (`gnomon mod pause` writes a marker in the home; pausing only narrows, so it is not a human-only verb) to hide a view or silence a tool (M1.11 slice 3, Aegis C2) | a pause is visible at once and persistently: the status `mods` block lists every marker (`why: user`) whether or not the mod ever ran and across restarts, and the Workshop shows `paused` from the `mod.paused` row; everything that relies on the paused mod fails closed (relays, commands, tools, admission, a plain start refuse `PAUSED` from the first call after the marker, before the child is reaped) | **accepted**: a denial of service by the OS user, never a widening; `gnomon mod start` (human-only) clears it |

### 15.2 Key custody (D38/D39)

Root and approvals keys in hardware (Secure Enclave / TPM) via `packages/gnomon-helper` (macOS: a Developer-ID-signed, entitled `.app`-bundled helper with an embedded provisioning profile, keys in its keychain access group — OPEN 1, S29-01; Windows: a signed Rust helper using the Passport KSP with `NCRYPT_USE_CONTEXT_PROPERTY`, hardware-backed keys only — P8-D71, S29-K4); the root key is used for registry/grant/config/install/api-token/bundle-pin writes only — rare, prompted per signature; the approvals key signs `approve` with the presence classes of §3.4, unlocked per human-class connection — the unlock (an evaluated LAContext / authenticated NCrypt handle) is state in the helper keyed by `connId` or `armId`, so a helper crash drops every unlock and arm (S29-K5) — and is never on disk. The macOS helper is an XPC service under launchd that answers only the Developer-ID-signed kernel executable (Node SEA) through a peer code-signing requirement and refuses to run sandboxed (P8-D68, S29-K1). The **kernel key** (Ed25519, §3.1) lives in the OS keystore item `gnomon-kernel-key` behind the helper (macOS: Keychain item in the helper's access group, released only to the signed kernel — `full` only under D68's design; Windows: Credential Manager, same-user readable — `partial`, §A.3), is loaded into kernel memory at start, signs everything the kernel asserts (`serverSig`, cards' `actions[]`, `oracle.result`, kernel events), and is rotated by `gnomon key rotate kernel` (root-signed, old key retired in the registry, old signatures stay verifiable). Device keys Ed25519 files 0600 (owner-only ACL on Windows), one per machine under one principal (Q1), used for client auth only; agent keys files; session/mod/voice keys ephemeral or scoped; sessions, oracles, mods and the transport children cannot read any key file or keystore item (sandbox profiles). The **link key** (X25519, §3.1, §3.9) has the kernel key's custody — keystore item `gnomon-link-key` behind the helper, released only to the signed kernel, `full` on macOS only under D68's design, `partial` on Windows — and is rotated by `gnomon key rotate link` without touching the kernel key; a teammate link requires `full`. Peer public material is pinned in `~/.gnomon/keys/peers.jsonl`. Keys never under a repo, never in git. No key that can approve is ever on disk. Revisit trigger per §A.1.

### 15.3 Socket auth, env, no bypasses

§4.9; §3.3 allowlist/scrub; `G-SEC-02` envKeys ⊆ allowlist ∪ additions; the daemon reads no `GNOMON_*` (the clean re-exec drops them and passes the home as `--home`, §2.4); the one `GNOMON_*` the CLI reads is `GNOMON_HOME`, a test-only seam that selects the home and never a policy value (S16-6, amended in v3.8).

### 15.4 Capabilities and grants

Capabilities: `log.append:<ns>.*` · `log.read` · `log.read:sensitive` (grantable to a community client's device key, §9.3) · `turn.text:attached-session` (a mod reads the assistant and user text parts of the sessions it is attached to — never `tool_input`, tool results or `session.inject` bytes; named on the install card; A24-T1) · `ref.resolve` · `resolver:<scheme>` · `permit.reserve:<kind>` · `oracle:<type>` · `band:read` · `hook:<event>` · `mod:<effect>` · `voice` · `knowledge:provide` (carries `read:[paths]`, the only way a mod profile gains a read path outside its own dir, listed on the card) · `knowledge:federate` (first-party only: `mod.command` through `kernel.call`, §8.10) · `voice.approve:<class>` (derived only from a `grant.voice-approvals` row, never from a manifest; §14.7) · `link:send` (first-party only — the cowork mod: the kernel carries its `modns.cowork.out` events over a link after the egress filter; never in a community manifest; §3.9). Grant rows (`~/.gnomon/grants.jsonl`, root-signed): `grant.capability{manifestHash | deviceId, capabilities[], read?: [paths]}`, `grant.bind{principal, manifestHash, kinds[]}`, `grant.trust{manifestHash, tier, maxKnowledgeTier?}`, `grant.install{manifestHash, packageHash, cardId, source: package | linked:<path>, dev?: {until}}`, `grant.device{deviceId}`, `grant.voice{speakerId}`, `grant.egress{manifestHash, hosts[]}` (Anthropic API hosts refused; never together with `knowledge:provide`), `grant.voice-approvals{repo, classes: [reversible] | [reversible, gate] | [reversible, gate, judgment]}` (widening only; narrowing is the kernel event `approval.voice.policy.narrowed`, so I5 holds), `grant.peer{peerRootKeyId, peerKernelKeyId, peerLinkKeyId, peerNs, relationship: self | teammate, scopes, maxAudience, relay, until}` (pairing and renewal only, on a `peer-pair` card with root presence; revocation and expiry are the kernel events `link.revoked` / `link.expired`; §3.9). Project manifests may request, never grant.

### 15.5 Sandbox profiles (D13, D25, S16-3/4, S22-C3/H1/H8, A23)

Eight named profiles per platform, versioned and hashed into the events they protect:

| Profile | Used for | macOS `sandbox-exec` (default-deny) | Windows (restricted token + ACL + job object; dsh `packages/sandbox/sandbox-windows-acl/src/index.ts:4,28`, `ffi.ts:253`, `runner.ts:4` @ `86a402200d`, design note `.agents/notes/implemented/feature/2026-08-08-windows-acl-restricted-token-sandbox.md`) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `gnomon-oracle` (Move 2) | `test`, `merge-ci` | deny-default file read/write, mach-lookup, process-exec, network; allow read: the worktree copy, toolchain dirs (node, git, `/usr`, `/System`, `/Library/Developer`), `/dev/null`; allow write: the copy dir + a private `TMPDIR`; allow exec: node, git, the project's test binaries by path; escape tests `G-SBX-04` (`osascript`), `G-SBX-05` (`launchctl`), `G-SBX-06` (`open`) | `WRITE_RESTRICTED` token (`CreateRestrictedToken` + `DISABLE_MAX_PRIVILEGE` + `LUA_TOKEN`) with the copy dir and `%TEMP%` granted write, `~/.gnomon` and the user profile's credential stores denied by ACE, job object with no network via WFP (helper; mechanism per the note below), escape tests `G-SBX-04w..06w` (PowerShell `Start-Process` elevation; `schtasks`; `explorer`/`rundll32`); reports `full | partial` honestly like dsh — `partial` where reads cannot be denied |
| `gnomon-reviewer` | the reviewer `claude -p` | as oracle but: real `HOME` with `~/.gnomon`, `~/.ssh`, `~/.aws`, `~/.npmrc`, `~/.config/gh` denied; mach-lookup to `securityd` allowed (Claude's OAuth item); network only to the kernel's loopback proxy carrying the Anthropic allowlist (P8-D17, S22-M4); **weaker than oracle, stated** | same denies by ACE; Credential Manager reachable; outbound only to the loopback proxy |
| `git` | every kernel git call | oracle profile + read of the repo; writes only the worktree target | same |
| `session` | `claude -p`, `gnomon-local`, the `gnomon-mods` bridge, their tool children | allow-most profile that denies: reads of `~/.gnomon/keys/**` (link key pins included), `~/.gnomon/warpline/**`, `~/.gnomon/link/**` (inbox, outbox, quarantine — S32 §6), `~/.gnomon/head`, the Warpline token files, every browser profile directory (Safari, Chrome, Edge, Firefox) and the wrapper's WebView data directory (S29-K7); `mach-lookup` of the helper's XPC service name (defence in depth — the helper's peer requirement is the boundary, D68; `securityd` stays reachable for Claude's OAuth item, since SBPL cannot filter per keychain item); **writes** under `~/.gnomon/**`, to every Claude settings layer (`~/.claude/settings*.json`, managed files, project `.claude/**`, `CLAUDE*.md`) and under every **linked mod folder** (list regenerated at each spawn); for a Claude Code session, **one write allow**: `(allow file-write* (subpath "<home>/.claude/projects"))` after the `~/.claude` deny (last match wins), so transcripts save and `--resume` works, with the `projects` dir itself (literal), every deny inside it and the steering regex re-rooted at it re-denied after it, and the rest of `~/.claude` and `~/.claude.json` still denied (TD-2026-10-19-814, probe p-resume darwin; Aegis M1/L3; §3.3, profile v6; the accepted residual reach is in §3.3); AppleEvents, `launchctl`, mic/camera (TCC); **`process-exec` of `/usr/bin/open`, `/usr/bin/osascript` and `lsregister` (in `LaunchServices.framework`'s `Support/` directory)** (P8-D80; realpath'd; verified by re-running p10m against the generated profile in M1.7) — LaunchServices itself has no working deny on macOS (p10m tried `launchservicesd` and `com.apple.lsd.*` mach-lookup denies), so a launcher the session builds itself still starts an unsandboxed app — accepted residual, P8-D79/D80, §A.2; **unix-socket connects** to the user's tmux (`/private/tmp/tmux-<uid>`) and screen socket directories (`network-outbound (remote unix-socket (subpath …))`, p10m); **outbound loopback to the web endpoint's port** (`network-outbound` remote-port deny, regenerated when the port changes — a running session keeps the port it was spawned under and the kernel never reuses a port while such a session lives); **process information on other processes** (`process-info*` denied except `self`, `sysctl` process lists denied, and `(deny mach-lookup (global-name "com.apple.sysmond"))`, without which `pgrep` reads every argv from root `sysmond` over XPC — p10m) — D55; every path in the profile realpath'd (p10m); `ps` cannot run under it (setuid); Claude Code 2.1.302 verified working under it (p10m-claude-compat); **requires macOS 15+ AND System Integrity Protection enabled** (P8 note, CI runs 39100281741 and 39100811385: below 15, and on GitHub's macos-15 runner where SIP is off, a relocated platform binary is not SIGKILLed — launch constraints are not enforced without SIP; the darwin backend refuses `sandbox-unavailable` with `{floor: 'macos', have, need}` below 15 and `{floor: 'sip', have: 'disabled'|'unknown'}` when `csrutil status` is not a plain "enabled", and `status` shows the session sandbox `unavailable`, reason `macos-floor` or `sip-disabled`); with the AppleEvent deny the profile also denies `(mach-lookup (global-name "com.apple.coreservices.appleevents"))` (appleeventsd, profile v2; a `com.apple.coreservices.*`/`com.apple.lsd.*` deny would take LaunchServices from Claude) — Claude Code verified working under it (p10m-claude-compat-generated, 2026-10-17); whether it stops a non-Apple sender is OPEN 5; does not confine tools, worktree or other network; `G-SBX-04..06` and `G-SBX-08` run against this profile | ACEs denying the same paths and writes (linked folders included); Credential Manager items for Gnomon ACL'd to the interactive token only; `schtasks`, WMI, `Start-Process -Verb RunAs` denied via job limits + helper; loopback to the endpoint port blocked by the WFP mechanism S29-08 selects (see the note below the table); browser-profile read denies `partial` like every Windows read deny; `tasklist`/WMI/`NtQuerySystemInformation` process listing denied through the restricted token where the OS allows — `partial` on read denial and on process-info denial, reported honestly |
| `mod` | every mod child | default-deny: reads of the SDK + its own pinned snapshot + `~/.gnomon/mods/<name>/state/` + the `read:[paths]` of its `knowledge:provide` grant (listed on the card); writes only `~/.gnomon/mods/<name>/state/` and `blobs/`; network only to the kernel egress proxy port (none for a knowledge provider); no exec; its own source folder and `.git/**` unreadable | same via ACL + job object; `partial` on read denial |
| `transport` | the `gnomon-web` child (HTTP + RFC 6455 parsing, static bundle) | default-deny with no `network-bind`, `network-inbound` or `network-outbound` rule: the kernel listens on `127.0.0.1` (IPv4 literal, OS-chosen ephemeral port), accepts each connection and hands the connected socket to the child over its IPC channel; the child opens, binds and listens on no socket (probe P14b-web, T-2026-10-18-020). Read the pinned GUI bundle directory and `/dev/null`; stdio and the IPC channel to the kernel; no other file, no keystore, no `mach-lookup`, no exec (`G-WEB-07`). (SBPL cannot name a loopback address: `localhost` in a `local` filter matches every configured interface, T-2026-10-18-017.) | the same hand-off: the kernel listens on `127.0.0.1` and passes each connected socket to the child over its IPC channel (libuv duplication); the child has no network at all (restricted token + job object, or an AppContainer with no capabilities); read the bundle directory only, no exec; pending a Windows parity-phase probe (the P14b-web twin of P14b-w); `partial` on read denial |
| `voice` | the supervised `gnomon-voice` child (approvals-capable under an arm, §14.7) | default-deny: mic (TCC), read its models and `~/.gnomon/voice/prints/`, write `~/.gnomon/voice/**`, Core Audio process-object queries, stdio to the kernel; no network, no exec, no keys, no keystore | restricted token + job object: WASAPI capture and audio-session enumeration, same paths by ACL, no outbound (WFP), no exec; `partial` on read denial |
| `link` | the `gnomon-link` child (transport to the relay over a kernel-dialed socket, §3.9; P8-D74, P8-D90) | default-deny with **no** `network-outbound` and no `network-bind`. The kernel dials the relay `ip:port` from root-signed config and hands the connected socket to the child over its IPC channel. The child opens no socket that reaches the network and redials only by asking the kernel. (SBPL cannot name a host: P14, only `*` or `localhost` compile.) Stdio and the IPC channel to the kernel; no listen, no file read beyond its own binary and `/dev/null`, no keystore, no `mach-lookup`, no home, no exec (`G-LNK-11`; probe P14b) | the kernel duplicates the socket to the child. Restricted token + job object, or an AppContainer, with no network capability; no listen, no exec, no file read beyond its binary. The S29-08 allow-list only if P14b-w fails; `partial` on read denial |

Windows network rules (S29-K3). WFP conditions match app id, user / security descriptor (`ALE_USER_ID`), AppContainer package SID, address and port — never a job object — and adding a filter needs BFE rights (Administrators or Network Configuration Operators). Every "WFP" cell above therefore means one of three mechanisms, chosen by probe S29-08 on the founder's Win11 PC from a standard-user account (P8-D69): (1) a per-session **restricting SID** in the session's token matched by an `ALE_USER_ID` filter, added by the helper; (2) the same filter added by an **elevated install-time service** (one UAC prompt at install, then non-elevated per spawn); (3) an **AppContainer** with `internetClient` only, which blocks loopback by default. If all three fail, Windows has no endpoint-port deny: the attach race rests on presence alone, `session.sandbox` reports `partial` for it, and §20 kill criterion (e) applies to the **Windows browser surface** (it already attaches by presence on every launch); the wrapper stays.

macOS profile generation (p10m, p10m-claude-compat; P8-D79, P8-D80):
- **Paths.** SBPL path filters match resolved paths, so the generator realpaths every directory before writing it (`/var/folders/…` → `/private/var/folders/…`, `/tmp` → `/private/tmp`). An unresolved path is a deny that silently does not apply.
- **Process table.** `process-info*` and `sysctl` rules hide the process table only from direct reads; the `sysmond` mach-lookup deny closes the XPC path that entitled tools use.
- **`ps`.** `sandbox-exec` will not exec setuid binaries such as `/bin/ps` under any profile, so `ps` failing is never evidence of a deny, and nothing Gnomon runs inside a session depends on it.
- **Sockets.** The tmux socket deny is proven. The screen deny is by analogy until M1.7 re-runs p10m against the generated profile. Other user sockets p10m reached (the ssh-agent socket, Chrome's `SingletonSocket`s) are not denied by an unproven rule; they sit in §A.2's residual class.
- **Launchers (P8-D80).** `process-exec` of `/usr/bin/open`, `/usr/bin/osascript` and `lsregister` is denied by realpath; M1.7's re-run of p10m asserts each exec fails and that `G-SBX-04` (`osascript`) and `G-SBX-06` (`open`) hold against the session profile.
- **Not covered:** a self-built launcher that calls LaunchServices directly (accepted, P8-D79/D80) and same-user signals, both stated in §A.2; TCC grants of the responsible process (Accessibility / Screen Recording / Input Monitoring), inherited by every session, and the shared pasteboard (`com.apple.pasteboard.1`), reachable — §A.2(v). Recommendation (OPEN 22): gnomond launched by launchd or the signed app, not from an interactive terminal, so the responsible process holds no TCC grants.

Rule: a profile that cannot be applied (API missing, platform `partial`) ⇒ the oracle refuses to run (`UNSUPPORTED`) and the session or mod refuses to spawn unless the human approves a `gate-class` card naming the degraded protection — never a silent downgrade (Odysseus containment posture, 01); `mod.sandbox{full|partial}` and `session.sandbox{full|partial}` are logged and shown in `status`.

### 15.6 Mod trust tiers

Grant-table labels; runtime enforcement = out-of-process host + declared effects + the default-deny `mod` profile (D33, S22-C3), a genuine first among the reference harnesses (21).

### 15.7 Project policy and gate-class (S-C4, S16-7)

`.gnomon/project.yaml` (`mode`, `ci`, `repoId`, `model` default) committed, read from the base revision. Gate-class = any change to `.gnomon/**`, `.claude/**`, `CLAUDE.md`, `CLAUDE.local.md`, test config, `package.json` scripts, lockfile scripts, pipeline files, `.git/config|hooks|info`, `.gitattributes`, user-scope `~/.claude/**` observed by `layersHash`/`gitHash` at any spawn/respawn or mid-session — detected by **hash comparison**, not diffs, so gitignored and user-scope layers count. `project.yaml` never carries voice-approval settings: such keys are ignored and logged `config.ignored` — voice approvals are a root-signed grant (§14.7).

## 16. Windows parity (`~windows-parity`, P8-R4)

| Piece | macOS / Linux | Windows | Tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| Kernel endpoint | unix socket 0600 (`$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` fallback) | named pipe `\\.\pipe\gnomon-<sha>` created by the signed helper with a DACL for the current user SID only + `FIRST_PIPE_INSTANCE` (S22-H7); the server is authenticated by `serverSig` only — the PID comparison is a helper/Python-client diagnostic (S29-K6) | `G-WIN-01` both transports pass the §4.9 handshake (a forged `serverSig` refused, a squatted first instance fails closed) and `G-SOCK-*` |
| Home dir + modes | `~/.gnomon` 0700, files 0600 | `%USERPROFILE%\.gnomon` with an owner-only DACL applied via the helper (`icacls` fallback), inheritance removed, verified by reading back the ACL | `G-WIN-02` ACL read-back = expected |
| Root + approvals keys | Secure Enclave P-256 with presence via the signed `.app`-bundled helper, in its keychain access group (S29-01); cached unlock = an evaluated LAContext in the helper | Windows Hello P-256 in the Passport KSP (`MS_NGC_KEY_STORAGE_PROVIDER`) with `NCRYPT_USE_CONTEXT_PROPERTY` for the kernel's text, refused unless `NCRYPT_IMPL_TYPE_PROPERTY` reports hardware (S29-K4); cached unlock = an authenticated NCrypt handle in the Rust helper (P8-D71); same-user processes can open NGC keys by name (S29-K2, §A.1); `CredRead`-style stores are not used for approval-capable keys | `G-WIN-03` root-signed write and fresh-class decision prompt; a decision without presence is refused; absent helper ⇒ refuse; the prompt shows the kernel's text natively or in the helper window (`status.presencePromptText`, S25-M1) |
| Kernel key custody | Keychain item in the helper's access group; the helper is an XPC/launchd service answering only the Developer-ID-signed kernel SEA (peer code-signing requirement) and refuses to run sandboxed — `full` only under this design (P8-D68, S29-K1) | Credential Manager generic credential, same-user readable (`partial` until the helper's protected store is proven) | `G-WIN-03` (custody row) and `status.kernelKeyCustody` |
| Session denies for the attach race (D55) | `sandbox-exec`: endpoint-port `network-outbound` deny, `process-info*` + `kern.proc` sysctl deny + `mach-lookup` deny of `com.apple.sysmond` (p10m), tmux/screen unix-socket deny, `process-exec` deny of `open`/`osascript`/`lsregister` (P8-D80), linked-folder write deny, browser-profile read deny, every path realpath'd; `ps` cannot run (setuid); a self-built LaunchServices launcher and same-user signals not covered (§A.2, P8-D79/D80); Claude Code verified working (p10m-claude-compat) | endpoint-port WFP filter keyed by the S29-08 mechanism (restricting SID + `ALE_USER_ID`, elevated install-time service, or AppContainer; §15.5 note — never "on the job object", S29-K3), restricted-token process-info denial where the OS allows, ACEs on linked folders and browser profiles; `partial` reported where a read cannot be denied; no mechanism ⇒ presence only and kill criterion (e) for the Windows browser surface | `G-SBX-08` both |
| Launch-token hand-off | wrapper: stdin; browser: helper → LaunchServices `GetURL` Apple Event, token from stdin; a consumed token replayed after its TTL only logs (S29-K7) | wrapper: stdin; browser: token-less URL via `ShellExecute` + Reconnect presence (no private URL channel to a browser) | `G-WEB-05` both (no argv ever carries the token) |
| Chain anchor | file (64 rows / 50 ms) + Keychain generic password via the helper (1024 rows / 1 s; critical kinds before their effect, an ordering guarantee, P8-D82/D83); each anchor records the boot session (`kern.bootsessionuuid`; Linux `boot_id`) — the keystore anchor resists a same-user writer | file + Credential Manager generic credential via the helper, same cadence — keystore `partial` (same-user writable), so truncation is detectable only against accidents (§A.1(g)); boot id from the helper, `unknown` until the parity phase; renames atomic but not durable at return (residuals below) | `G-LOG-06` both |
| Process isolation + kill | `detached`, own pgid, staged control message → SIGTERM → SIGKILL | job object `KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE` via the signed helper (`gnomon-helper.exe`); control message → stdin close → `TerminateJobObject`; fallback `taskkill /T /F` logged as `partial` | `G-RUN-03` both |
| Interrupt | stream-json `control_request{subtype:'interrupt'}` | same (never `GenerateConsoleCtrlEvent`) | covered by `G-RUN-03` both (the staged stop begins with the control message) |
| Sandbox | `sandbox-exec` profiles (deprecated but functional) | restricted token + ACL + job object backend (deepseek-harness design), `full|partial` reported; `partial` for read denial until the helper's AppContainer/LowBox or dedicated-SID path is proven | `G-SBX-*` both; `G-WIN-06` `status` shows `partial` on a stock Windows box and every oracle refuses to run |
| Daemon activation | client activation; launchd 8.1 | client activation; Task Scheduler / service 8.1 | `G-DMN-03` both |
| Claude Code preflight paths | `claude` binary by explicit path — root-signed config, else known locations (`~/.local/bin/claude`, p01), never `PATH`; `~/.claude`, `/Library/Application Support/ClaudeCode/managed-settings.json`, managed preferences | `%USERPROFILE%\.claude`, `C:\Program Files\ClaudeCode\managed-settings.json`, `managed-settings.d/`, HKLM/HKCU policy keys (`C:\ProgramData\ClaudeCode` retired at v2.1.75); Git for Windows required by Claude Code; `claude.exe` by explicit path under the same rule, known locations recorded by P01 in the Windows parity phase | `G-SUB-01` both |
| Local engines | llama.cpp (Metal), Ollama, LM Studio, vLLM, `mlx_lm.server` (Mac boost) | llama.cpp (CUDA/Vulkan), Ollama, LM Studio; no MLX | `G-LCL-01` both |
| Voice engines | sherpa-onnx (CPU/CoreML EP) + optional FluidAudio | sherpa-onnx (CPU/DirectML EP); WASAPI capture | `G-VOX-01` both |
| Voice approvals (D58) | supervised child under `sandbox-exec` `voice` with TCC mic attribution (M4.1 probe; else talk-only until the helper gains a peer-PID + code-signature op, no `armCustody: client` on macOS, TD-2026-10-19-194); handle region a wrapper-owned native panel with `NSWindow.sharingType = .none` (ScreenCaptureKit behaviour on macOS 15+ is probe S29-10, M1.0); other-audio check via Core Audio process objects (macOS 14.2+); arm drop on `CGSessionCopyCurrentDictionary` lock state and sleep | restricted-token `voice` profile; peer check `GetNamedPipeClientProcessId` in the client fallback; `SetWindowDisplayAffinity(WDA_EXCLUDEFROMCAPTURE)`; `IAudioSessionManager2` active sessions; CapabilityAccessManager mic-use check; arm drop on `WTS_SESSION_LOCK` and sleep; `partial` until the M4.1 probe proves capture exclusion and render detection | `G-VOX-11`, `G-VOX-12` both |
| GUI shell | Tauri wrapper (WKWebView) by default; browser via the helper's LaunchServices hand-off (Safari/Chrome/Firefox) with the extension-risk badge | Tauri wrapper (WebView2) by default; browser via `ShellExecute` through the signed helper (Edge/Chrome/Firefox) + Reconnect presence, with the badge | `G-GUI-03`, `G-GUI-12` both |
| Web endpoint | `gnomon-web` transport child (`node:http` on `127.0.0.1`, ephemeral port) under `sandbox-exec` `transport` | same code path under the restricted-token `transport` profile | `G-WEB-01..08` both |
| Linked mod snapshot + watch | copy to `pinned/<packageHash>/` refusing symlinks and hardlinks (`nlink > 1`); `fs.watch` (FSEvents), 500 ms debounce, rehash on start | copy refusing junctions/reparse points and hardlinks; `ReadDirectoryChangesW`, same debounce and rehash | `G-MOD-08`, `G-MOD-11` both |
| Paths + hashes | POSIX | long paths (`\\?\`), `core.longpaths=true`, case-insensitive FS handled in ref/alias tables; `layersHash`/`gitHash` normalize CRLF and path case | `G-WIN-04` |
| Env allowlist | `HOME USER …` | + `USERPROFILE SYSTEMROOT WINDIR SystemDrive ProgramFiles COMSPEC PATHEXT APPDATA LOCALAPPDATA TEMP CLAUDE_CODE_GIT_BASH_PATH` | `G-SEC-02` both |
| Link transport (P8-R9, §3.9, P8-D90) | `gnomon-link` under `sandbox-exec` `link`: no `network-outbound`, no `network-bind`, no listen; it uses only the connected socket the kernel dialed to a relay `ip:port` from root-signed config and handed over its IPC channel, and redials only by asking the kernel (probe P14b) | restricted-token or AppContainer `link` profile with no network capability; the kernel duplicates the connected socket to the child (probe P14b-w); the S29-08 allow-list only if P14b-w fails; `partial` reported where a read cannot be denied | `G-LNK-01`, `G-LNK-11` both |
| Link key custody | Keychain item in the helper's access group, released only to the signed kernel SEA — `full` under P8-D68 | Credential Manager generic credential, same-user readable — `partial`: `self` links only, teammate links refused, until the helper's protected store is proven | `G-LNK-02` both; `status.linkKeyCustody` |
| Memory budgets (P11, M1.2) | `phys_footprint` (`footprint -p` / `TASK_VM_INFO`), never RSS, which keeps freed-but-reusable pages on macOS (p11: RSS 248 MB vs. 39.8 MB footprint); Linux CI: `Pss` from `/proc/<pid>/smaps_rollup` | private bytes (`PROCESS_MEMORY_COUNTERS_EX.PrivateUsage`), measured in the Windows parity phase | P11 (probe) |
| Relay host | `gnomon-relay` (Node, zero deps) as a user process on the always-on machine, listening on a configured LAN address | same; its inbound firewall rule is a one-time admin step on the relay host only, never on a kernel host | `G-LNK-01`, `G-LNK-10` both |

Rule: CI runs the `G-` matrix on macOS and Windows runners, and platform-independent tests run on Windows CI from day one (A23-5); a test marked platform-specific must have a sibling or an explicit `n/a` row with a reason (`G-WIN-05` enforces). FluidAudio and MLX are the only Mac-only components and both are optional. Under P8-D78 every Windows cell above is built and proven in the **Windows parity phase**, after the macOS pass; until then Windows CI runs the platform-independent tests on every PR, and the macOS pass waits on no Windows cell.

**CI status (2026-10-17).** The first all-green run is `39073693925` on draft PR #49. It ran tests on macOS 14, Ubuntu and Windows (Node 24): contracts 125, and kernel 170 of 171 on Windows, where the marked POSIX files are excluded. It also ran the probes on macOS and Windows, the native builds and the Warpline guard. Exclusions are marker-enforced: `vitest.config.ts` excludes every test file whose `// @gnomon-platform:` marker omits the running platform. A registry test proves, per platform, that the excluded set equals the registry rows that omit that platform (`G-WIN-05`).

**Windows residuals of the kernel log (S34-27, S34-28, T-2026-10-17-013(c)).** These are stated, not yet closed:
- **Renames are not durable at return.** On win32 the head anchor's tmp + rename and a segment's create and seal renames are atomic but not durable when they return. Node's rename (`MoveFileEx`) does not pass `MOVEFILE_WRITE_THROUGH`, and a directory cannot be opened for a flush, so the directory fsync is skipped by design. A power cut can undo a rename that already returned.
- **`fsync` needs a writable handle** (`FlushFileBuffers`). A read-only handle fails `EPERM`, so files are opened `r+` for the flush on Windows.
- **The boot id is `unknown`** until the helper supplies one. With `unknown`, the P8-D85 cross-boot relax never applies and a truncation always classifies `tamper`. Every state that a rolled-back rename or a lost WAL tail can leave therefore fails closed (`anchor-lag`, `anchor-missing` or `truncated`); none opens silently. Recovery is the P8-D84 path.
- **The fix is helper work** in the Windows parity phase (P8-D78): the helper supplies a write-through rename and a real boot id.

On POSIX, `fsyncDir` is a no-op only for a directory fsync the filesystem does not support (`EINVAL`, `ENOTSUP`/`EOPNOTSUPP`, `EISDIR`, `EBADF`). Any other error, `EIO` above all, is rethrown (S34-28); previously every error was swallowed.

## 17. Migration from 7.x

1. Envelope + identity adapters (`paradigm.event` mirror; root + device keys; roster gkeys).
2. One runner, two backends: constellation and flow become socket clients; the GUI replaces the founder's day-to-day interactive Claude Code use for Gnomon-managed sessions; the Claude Code plugin keeps working for sessions opened by hand.
3. Approval broker: constellation cards, flow approvals/step-up, Symphony file-requests → `card:v1` renderers.
4. Manifest-generated plugin files (`plugin.json`, `.mcp.json`, `hooks.json`, `FAMILY_META`; `generate-hooks.mjs` subsumed).
5. Ref resolvers.
6. Learning: `inject.receipt` + `credit`; `appliedCount` frozen; teacher flywheel.
7. Contracts: `paradigm pipeline plan` compiles + `contract.register`; `paradigm_pipeline` gains `compile|claim|progress`.

**Never moves:** `.purpose`, `portal.yaml`, symbol index · neurons vault · Warpline fabric/objects/refs/keys/tokens · flow chain · constellation op log · `~/.claude/projects/**` · notebook YAML · lore/decision YAML. **7.x keeps working:** `G-MIG-01` (suite passes with `~/.gnomon` absent). **Conductor:** `ClaudeStreamSession.swift` (`:183 --dangerously-skip-permissions`) deleted in M3.6 with `GnomonClient.swift`; `G-MIG-02`. **flow/constellation:** `HeadlessRunner` (`headless.py:313,510`) retired after `G-RUN-04`; `createAgents` (`agents.ts:121`) keeps its API; `tokenFor` (`agents.ts:165`) stays for constellation's own MCP auth.

## 18. Package layout, naming, versioning

