packages/gnomon/             @a-company/gnomon 8.0.0             bin: gnomon, gnomond, gnomon-web, gnomon-link — ZERO runtime deps (G-SEC-01); gnomond also built as a Developer-ID-signed Node SEA (P8-D68)
 src/                                    (every path below sits under src/, as in the repo's other packages — Arky 29)
  kernel/{identity,log,runner,supervisor,broker,budget,scheduler,manifest,refs,socket,web,link,sandbox,modhost}/   node: built-ins only; import fence; web = the endpoint rules + the transport child's supervisor (never an HTTP parser in the kernel); link = pairing, Noise over node:crypto, the gnomon-link:v1 frames, egress filter, outbox/inbox
  transport/                             gnomon-web: loopback HTTP + RFC 6455 server framing over node:http, static bundle with bundleHash check; runs under the transport profile; forwards records over stdio
                                         gnomon-link: outbound TCP to the relay, length-prefixed ciphertext only, no keys; runs under the link profile
  adapters/{claude-code,local}/          claude -p stdio driver + fixtures; gnomon-local stdio driver (JSONL) + fixtures — no model loop here
  platform/{darwin,win32,linux}/         endpoint, acl, jobobject, sandbox backends, folder watch, snapshot copier; talks to gnomon-helper for keys, presence, pipe, anchors, kernel-key item, URL hand-off
  clients/{ts,python}/                   thin socket clients (constellation, flow); the conformance runner
  cli/ hooks/ sandbox/
 test/                                   incl. the import fence (G-KRN-02), the no-dependencies check (G-SEC-01), the platform registry (G-WIN-05)
 probes/<id>/probe.mjs, probes/run.mjs   M1.0 probes, zero deps, no build; results in probes/results/<id>.<darwin|win32>.json (probeResult:v1, §19.1)
packages/gnomon-helper/      @a-company/gnomon-helper 1.0.0      darwin: SwiftPM, Developer-ID-signed `.app`-bundled helper with an embedded provisioning profile, an XPC service under launchd answering only the signed kernel SEA, refusing to run sandboxed (Secure Enclave keys, presence, helper-held unlocks per connId/armId, Keychain anchors + kernel-key item, LaunchServices URL hand-off from stdin)
                                                                 win32: Rust (`windows` crate, toolchain pinned with the wrapper's, P8-D71), signed per P8-D72 (Passport KSP keys with NCRYPT_USE_CONTEXT_PROPERTY, hardware-backed only, Windows Hello presence + prompt-text window, helper-held unlocks, pipe DACL/first-instance, job objects, the S29-08 endpoint-port filter, ACLs, anchors, kernel-key item, ShellExecute of the token-less URL)
                                                                 prebuilt binaries with checksums in the release gate; verified at start (G-HLP-01)
packages/gnomon-local/       @a-company/gnomon-local 8.0.0       userland; zero runtime deps (re-derived pi rpc shape, ratified P8-D95); --mode rpc child; no cloud providers
packages/gnomon-gui/         @a-company/gnomon-gui 8.0.0         the localhost GUI page: React SPA, virtualized transcript, decision stack, sessions sidebar, Shuttle view, palette, themes, Reconnect, extension-risk badge, canvas surface (Excalidraw, assets self-hosted); served as static bytes from a root-signed, hash-pinned path; no runtime fetch; stores nothing but the kernel public key pin
packages/gnomon-wrapper/     @a-company/gnomon-wrapper 8.0.0     Tauri shell (Rust; WKWebView / WebView2): token on stdin, in-process navigation to 127.0.0.1, IPC disabled for page content, native serverSig check, dock/taskbar, notifications, presence Reconnect; signed with OPEN 1's identity
packages/gnomon-mod-sdk/     @a-company/gnomon-mod-sdk 1.0.0     mod-abi:v1 types, kit, test harness, example mod, link/dev tooling, the gnomon-mods MCP bridge (userland, per session, writes inject.receipt)
packages/gnomon-mods/        @a-company/gnomon-mods 8.0.0        first-party mods on mod-abi:v1: workshop/, gnome-persona/, doodle/ (incl. the doodle-shape rasterizer), knowledge/ (the federator + library providers: notebooks, neurons, lore, decisions, Steward memory), cowork/ (pull-only co-work inbox, brief drafts; §3.9) — each with fixtures and golden renders
packages/gnomon-relay/       @a-company/gnomon-relay 8.0.0       zero deps; the self-hosted blind relay (§3.9, P8-D74): Noise IK admission against member link keys, frame forwarding, size-capped store-and-forward of ciphertext; runs on the relay host only, never inside a kernel
packages/gnomon-voice/       @a-company/gnomon-voice 8.0.0       sherpa-onnx engines, VoiceEngine interface, FluidAudio optional backend (darwin); own principal
plugins/paradigm/mods/shuttle/   optional Claude Code Mod band (read-scoped)

Deliverable: release-gate — the package-level checks `G-CON-01`, `G-CON-02`, `G-SEC-01`, `G-SEC-02`, `G-MIG-01`, `G-LAW-06`, `G-WIN-05`, `G-CLI-02` bind here.

Scaffold (Arky 29, P8-D72): every Gnomon package is `"private": true` and outside the root `build`/`test` scripts until the 8.0 cut (own `build:gnomon`/`test:gnomon` scripts and `.github/workflows/gnomon.yml`), so 7.x releases cannot break (`G-MIG-01`); Gnomon packages declare `engines.node >=24` while the repo stays `>=20`; the kernel's `package.json` has no `dependencies` key — `gnomon-contracts` is a devDependency bundled by tsup.

Relations: `@a-company/paradigm` 8.0.0 depends on `@a-company/gnomon`, `@a-company/gnomon-gui`, `@a-company/gnomon-wrapper` (prebuilt, signed binaries with checksums, like the helper), `@a-company/gnomon-mods`, `@a-company/gnomon-local` and `@a-company/gnomon-relay` (only `@a-company/paradigm` publishes); the kernel depends on none of them and reads the GUI bundle only as a static directory named and hash-pinned in root-signed config; `@a-company/warpline` stays 0.x, untouched by Gnomon's build (P8-D16's `refs.watch` + keyed-only `resolve` are Warpline's own changes); plugin version bumps in lockstep; `abiId`, `modAbiId`, `clientApiId` and `kitId` are each frozen per release and each moves only with its own schema (`linkApiId` likewise, with `gnomon-link:v1`) — `gnomon-contracts` semver bumps when any of them moves (A24-2); pi-ai, React, Excalidraw and Tauri pinned exact with their MIT/Apache notices in `THIRD_PARTY.md`; a community GUI depends on `@a-company/gnomon-contracts` only. **Symbol ids below the spec's nodes (P8-D81).** The package-level components #gnomon-contracts, #gnomon-helper, #gnomon-wrapper and #gnomon-probes, and the contract sub-components of #gnomon-contracts — #contract-event-v1, #contract-ids, #contract-principal, #contract-ref-v1, #contract-refusal-v1, #contract-card-v1, #contract-gnomon-rpc-v1 and #contract-client-api-id — are `.purpose` symbols, not Appendix A nodes; they are written here without code spans so the compiler (§7.1) extracts none of them, and `packages/gnomon/test/symbol-allowlist.json` lists each with its reason for the symbol-drift check.

## 19. Delivery — four moves, ONE Move 1 (D36), proper-first

Each task: acceptance oracles (test ids; every one runs on macOS + Windows unless `n/a` with a reason) and a falsifier. Field metrics are `T-` telemetry (§20). Proper-first means the foundation lands before anything that decorates it: kernel, endpoints and their security model, the GUI shell and the mod host in Move 1; contracts and the armada in Move 2; the learning loop, knowledge search and the two expressive first-party mods in Move 3; voice in Move 4.

### 19.1 Move 1 — kernel, two stdio backends, web endpoint, GUI, mods, fuse, clients (`$move-1`)

Ordering (A23-5 inside one move): probes first; Windows CI on platform-independent tests from day one; each test lives with the task that can pass it; the `gnomon-oracle` profile moves to M2.3. There are no α/β/γ checkpoints inside Move 1 (P8-D57); the move is sized as it is and passes as a whole. P8-D78 sequences that whole pass by platform — macOS first, then a Windows parity pass when the founder is ready — and is not a checkpoint: neither pass is a reduced scope (see Pass below). Windows-only probes and work are marked *Windows parity phase*: the Rust helper, Passport KSP keys, the WFP/AppContainer mechanism, the named pipe, the WebView2 wrapper, and the Windows halves of the probes. Windows CI runs the platform-independent tests throughout.

**Entry preconditions (P8-D57).** Move 1 does not start until (1) the Atelier aesthetic session (`.atelier/sessions/2026-10-15-gnomon-aesthetic`) is **complete**: final papercraft and phosphor themes, colour and type boards decided, motion specs written, and the contrast fixtures of §11.3 committed (OPEN 9 closes on that day); (2) OPEN 1's signing identity exists, because M1.1's helpers and M1.13's wrapper are signed with it. **Scaffold (PR-0, Arky 29):** the package skeletons of §18 (private, outside the root build, Node ≥ 24 — P8-D72), `.github/workflows/gnomon.yml` (test matrix macOS/Windows/Linux on Node 24, `probes-ci`, unsigned `native` builds; signing secrets stay out of CI), the platform-derived rollup fix in `install-deps`, `probes/run.mjs` and the `probeResult:v1` schema. PR-1 runs the mac probes, PR-2 the CI probes; contracts (PR-3), the kernel log behind a `Signer` seam (PR-4) and the daemon-lifecycle core (PR-5) follow — none of them waits on a probe.

| Task | Scope | Acceptance oracles | Falsifier |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1.0 Probes | the merged probe set below (Arky 29 P00–P12 + Aegis 30 S29-01..10, deduplicated, + P13–P14 for linking); each probe is `packages/gnomon/probes/<id>/probe.mjs` (zero deps, no build) run by `node packages/gnomon/probes/run.mjs <id>`, writing `packages/gnomon/probes/results/<id>.<os>.json` as `probeResult:v1 {probe, os, osVersion, arch, toolVersions, verdict: pass\|fail\|partial, evidence, specRefs, at}`; results are committed from hardware runs only; the `gnomon-notary` notarytool keychain profile is set up here (P00, P8-D70); `p-log-fsync` follow-ups (S33-09, S33-10): arm G (forced `F_FULLFSYNC` at 1/64) and the human-cadence arms H1–H3 are **done** (P8-D83); what remains is the keystore-anchor arm (the helper round trip) once P04 lands, and the manual power-cut run at release candidate (§3.2); the founder creates a Developer ID provisioning profile for App ID `<TEAM>.gnomon.helper` before P04; the Mod-socket spike is deferred to M3.5 | `G-WIN-03` (probe form) | a probe shows a ruling cannot hold ⇒ a re-plan memo before any build that depends on it |

**M1.0 probe set (Arky 29 + Aegis 30, merged; P13–P14 added for linking from 31/32, P14b from 37; P8-D69/D70).** Ordered by how much of the spec a failure would break. Where a probe appears in both reviews it keeps both ids. **Runs on:** *mac* = the founder's Mac; *Windows PC* = the founder's Win11 x64 with TPM 2.0 + Hello, from a standard-user account (P8-D69); *CI* = `.github/workflows/gnomon.yml` on macos-14 / windows-latest — mechanics only: CI has no Hello or TPM and runs as admin, so its results are uploaded as artifacts and never committed. *Windows parity phase* (P8-D78) marks a run deferred to the Windows parity pass. **Status** is as of v3.8 (macOS 15.7.3, Node 26.0.0): `pass`, `partial`, `fail` or `pending`, per committed result file. The scaffold's own `p-harness` (runner round-trip) passed and is not a spec probe.

A–F: B p99 1.2–7.1 ms with 0.94 % over 5 ms; C p99.9 7.84 ms; D and E within raw's envelope; a p99.9 ≈ 7.8 ms stall floor appears in some runs for every arm, raw SQLite included (research 33). G: bulk p99 0.27 ms (0.089 % over 5 ms), critical p99 490 ms (p50 56 ms). H1 fails the 25 ms budget: critical p99 26.7 ms at 3×10⁵ rows, and 2.9 s and 6.8 s in two 10⁶-row runs. H2 is unreachable from Node: bundled SQLite and libuv have no barrier-only path, so H2b substitutes and matches H3. H3 passes: critical p99 0.19 ms at 3×10⁵ rows, and 10.2 ms over 94 criticals at 10⁶ rows. The 10⁶ runs were made under concurrent load (load average 12–15, data volume 95 % full); their bulk tails are load-inflated, and only the H1-vs-H3 contrast within one run is controlled. The keystore arm is pending P04 |
| P14 | link-outbound: the `link` profile allows TCP only to the relay `ip:port` pairs in root-signed config (connect elsewhere, listen, file read, exec all refused) — `sandbox-exec` on macOS; on Windows the S29-08 mechanisms as an allow-list, then an AppContainer with `privateNetworkClientServer` | §15.5 `link`, §16 link row | mac; Windows PC (Windows parity phase) | Windows fails ⇒ `link` reports `partial` and linking from that machine is `self`-only | **fail** (mac, as written: SBPL compiles only `*` or `localhost` as a host, so `*:P` admits any host and `localhost:P` admitted the en0 address; every other refusal held) → re-planned, research 37 (P8-D90, superseded by P14b); Windows half: Windows parity phase |
| P14b | link-fdpass: a kernel stand-in dials the relay `ip:port` and hands the connected socket to the child (`subprocess.send(msg, socket)`), which runs deny-default with no `network*` allows: (i) full-duplex read/write on the handed socket, 4 MiB frames both ways; (ii) the peer's close is seen; (iii) a second handoff after a kernel redial works; (iv) the child's own connect to the same `ip:port`, another port and another host, UDP, listen and unix-socket connect are all refused; (v) file read, home listing and exec are refused, as P14. Windows (P14b-w): the same via libuv duplication into a restricted-token child, then into an AppContainer with no capabilities | §3.9 Architecture, §15.5 `link`, §16 link row, `G-LNK-11` (P8-D90) | mac; Windows PC (Windows parity phase) | (i)–(iii) fail ⇒ fall back to port-only `*:P` and take the §A.5(h) reach residual to the founder; P14b-w fails ⇒ the S29-08 allow-list, else `link` reports `partial` and linking from that machine is `self`-only | **pass** (mac, `p14b-link-fdpass.darwin.json`: 4 MiB each way with matching sha256 in 7 ms; the peer's close seen; a second handoff after redial answered `pong`; the child's own connects to the same peer, another port and the en0 host, UDP, listen and unix-socket connect all EPERM; file read, home listing and exec EPERM; the unsandboxed control reached all nine); Windows half (P14b-w): Windows parity phase |
| P14b-web | web-handoff (T-2026-10-18-020): the kernel listens on `127.0.0.1` (`pauseOnConnect`) and hands each accepted socket to the transport child (`subprocess.send('conn', socket)`), which runs deny-default with no `network-*` rule: (i) full duplex on the handed socket, 4 MiB frames both ways; (ii) the client's close is seen; (iii) repeated handoffs work; (iv) inside the child, bind/listen on 0.0.0.0, ::, the en0 address, 127.0.0.1 and ::1, `listen()` with no address, UDP bind, outbound connect and unix-socket connect are all EPERM. Windows: the transport profile is the Windows parity phase | §11.2 rule 1, §15.5 `transport`, §A.7, `G-WEB-01`, `G-WEB-07` | mac; Windows PC (Windows parity phase) | (i)–(iii) fail ⇒ the LAN-listen residual of §A.7 goes to the founder | **pass** (mac, `p14b-web-handoff.darwin.json`: 4 MiB each way with matching sha256 in 8 ms; the client's close seen; 5 repeated handoffs answered; all 13 child-originated bind/listen/UDP/connect/unix attempts EPERM; the unsandboxed control reached all 13); Windows half: Windows parity phase |

Pass (Move 1), split by platform (P8-D78): the **macOS pass** is the bar below met on macOS; the **Windows parity pass** is the same bar met on Windows when the founder is ready. Each is the whole bar on its platform, not a reduced checkpoint, so this is platform sequencing, not the α/β/γ checkpoints P8-D57 declined. The bar: the founder drives a Claude session and a local session from the GUI **in the wrapper** and **in a badged browser** on the platform under test, in both themes, deciding cards from the stack; a page reload costs one touch and loses nothing; a `model: local` automation runs unattended on llama.cpp and an incapable one waits on a `fallback` card; a 5-session fan-out is stopped by the fuse with receipts; law 7 refuses an API-key environment and a loopback override; a page on another origin cannot reach a verb, a session cannot reach the endpoint port, and a replayed launch token revokes its user; flow and constellation run on the kernel path and the Python client passes conformance; one agent-written mod is staged by the agent, installed by the human through a card, runs default-deny from its snapshot, and renders in the GUI; a mod cloned from a repo is linked through a card, edited, pauses, and is re-approved from the list of changed files while the old snapshot keeps running until then; the Workshop shows the fleet with caps that move only while streaming; Windows shows `partial` honestly where it is (Windows parity pass).

**Release gate beyond the founder (P8-D84).** The macOS pass makes Gnomon the founder's daily driver; it does not make it shippable. Until M1.3, the keystore anchor is a stub, so a truncated log cannot be acknowledged and its only recovery is deleting the home (§3.2, §A.6). No build goes to any second user until task T-2026-10-17-009 lands. That task (Aegis S34-07, S34-08, S34-10) brings three things:
- (a) `gnomon log quarantine`: a human-run recovery that moves the whole home aside intact as evidence and starts a new log. The new log's genesis is a kernel-signed row naming the quarantined log id and head. It must exist before M1.3 makes a missing db refuse, and it is never an in-place downgrade. **Status: built** (T-2026-10-17-009(a), on the P8-D89 reservation): interactive-terminal gate until presence (M1.3); at M1.3 the keystore anchor item must move with the evidence.
- (b) Keystore anchor records authenticated by the helper with a key that user processes cannot reach, and verified on read. On macOS this is a data-protection keychain item in an entitlement-gated access group. On Windows it is the helper under a separate principal, or the classification is marked advisory; until then every Windows truncation is unrecoverable, because the boot is `unknown`.
- (c) Seam hardening: the test seams (`keystoreAnchor`, `bootSession`, `openForRecovery`/`acknowledgeGap`) move behind an internal test entry, and `presenceToken` becomes a branded type minted only by the presence module. A tripwire test proves the CLI and daemon cannot reach `acknowledgeGap` before presence lands.

**Before dogfood GA, and before Move 2 (TD-2026-10-19-908, TD-2026-10-19-648).** Two pieces of work sit between the macOS pass and the founder's everyday use. (1) Drain and resume across a kernel update (§2.4 rule 11, T-2026-10-19-014) is built before dogfood GA, Claude sessions first and `gnomon-local` second; until it lands, a kernel update ends every session. (2) `gnomon guide` (§5, T-2026-10-19-015) is built before Move 2 starts. The `log.read` cursor and the reservations of §5 ship in the batched T-2026-10-19-001 move, which is one `clientApiId` move.

## 20. Falsifiers, telemetry gates, kill criteria

Telemetry gates: `T-SHU-01` Mod holds a socket (optional) · `T-LOG-05` 30 days without an un-enveloped Gnomon-sealed strand · `T-FLD-01` ≥ 1 KNOT hydrated, zero skin-scoped W3 · `T-FLD-02` progress moves with no human edit · `T-VOX-01` voice-to-voice p95 < 800 ms local · `T-VOX-02` decision-phrase false accept / false reject, per mode, with method (gates toggle 2's availability) · `T-GUI-01` founder's daily driver is the GUI after two weeks (≥ 70 % of Gnomon-managed sessions opened from it) · `T-LCL-01` ≥ 1 real automation per day runs `model: local` without fallback · `T-GNM-01` choice-picked vs typed follow-ups, `raw` open rate and closing-prose delta, reported with method · `T-DDL-01` doodles opened per week, ops and exports per doodle · `T-LNK-01` briefs sent, accepted, rejected and expired per week, and co-work messages sent and read per week, each `{n, of}` — if accepted briefs stay near zero for a month after M2.8 passes, the handoff thesis is false · `T-REV-01` review convergence per `(repo, mode)`: rounds-to-pass `{p50, p95}`, cap hits, `extend` and `restart` counts, repeated and out-of-scope drops, and the claimant response mix, each `{n, of}`, reported beside §8.6's `laterBroke/passes` as recall · `T-STR-01` strictness cost (n:human-time-guardrail-metric): share of time each repo spends at L ≥ 1, human approvals per landed node `{p50, p95}`, time nodes wait on the founder, revert rate `{n, of}`, false trips (loosened within 7 days with no further signal), and run-mode changes by direction, each `{n, of}`.

Falsifiers: 1 daemon adoption (two weeks, > 50 % of real sessions outside the kernel) · 2 kernel creep (`G-KRN-02`) · 3 stream-json drift (> 1 breaking change/month) · 4 sixth ledger (`T-LOG-05`) · 5 empty denominator (`T-FLD-01` zero) · 6 learning (week-4 credited reuse < 25 %, 100 % certification survival, or both liveness gates at zero) · 7 shell (`T-GUI-01` fails: the founder keeps opening Claude Code interactively for Gnomon work) · 8 local lane (`T-LCL-01` fails for a month after the probe said capable) · 9 performance (idle memory > 150 MB by §16's per-OS metric, bulk append p99 > 5 ms or > 0.1 % over 5 ms at 10⁶ rows, GUI keystroke-to-paint > 50 ms at 200k events) · 10 accepted-risk tripwire, stated honestly: the detector catches `approval.decide` events whose `presence` class is wrong for their kind, whose `cli` ancestry contains a session, or `billing.api.active` without a root-signed card the founder signed, or an `approval.decide{presence:'voice-armed'}` without a preceding `approval.voice.arm` for the same pair, beyond its arm's class, after its hold was withdrawn, without an enrolled speaker, or (gate, judgment) without readback, handle and challenge — it cannot catch a decision made from a TTY'd process, from a stolen GUI token on its own connection, or from a browser extension inside that connection's cached unlock window (§A.1's accepted residual), nor a voice decision made by someone who reproduced the founder's voice (and, for gate class, read the handle and challenge) inside a live arm (§A.4); any detection ⇒ founder-only mode ends and §A revisit work is pulled forward · 11 Windows parity (`G-WIN-05` reports an `n/a` row without a reason, or a Move pass fails on Windows only) · 12 client-API churn (`clientApiId` changes more than once per release, a mod-ABI change moves `clientApiId`, or a community client breaks on a patch release) · 13 expressive layer over instrument (the Workshop or the gnome stage becomes what the founder watches instead of the transcript and stack: `raw` open rate falls while lines shown rise — both default to off-able and the measurement is `T-GNM-01`) · 14 voice approvals (any `approval.decide{presence:'voice-armed'}` traced to agent TTS, a card body, a playback overlap or a replayed recording ⇒ the kernel narrows every project's voice approvals to off until the founder re-grants, and §A.4's revisit work is pulled forward) · 15 kernel linking (any peer byte that reached a model without a `session.inject{origin:'peer'}` or `inject.receipt`, any `secret` item or never-cross item seen on the wire, any brief that spawned, injected or spent before a fresh-presence accept, or any kernel listening off loopback ⇒ the kernel revokes every link until the founder re-pairs, and §A.5's revisit work is pulled forward) · 16 review convergence (`T-REV-01`: the cap does not lower rounds-to-pass, or `laterBroke/passes` rises after M2.10 ships ⇒ the cap is hiding defects; raise `maxRounds` or rework diff-only review) · 17 strictness (`T-STR-01`: approvals per landed node fall while the revert rate rises ⇒ time was moved, not saved; or a repo sits at L ≥ 1 for more than half of a month after M3.4 ⇒ the signals or thresholds are noise and approval fatigue is the risk; either ⇒ re-derive the weights and thresholds before any second user).

Kill criteria: (a) falsifier 1 or 7 trips twice running; (b) falsifier 3 trips and plugin-side integration cannot enforce permits or law 7; (c) the founder's honest read after Move 2 is that Claude Code alone with native Task and worktrees did the work better; (d) no local model can complete a real automation after Move 1 on either machine (the owned-harness justification fails); (e) the localhost web model cannot pass `G-WEB-01..08` on both OS — then the GUI ships only inside the desktop wrapper and the browser path is removed, not weakened; on Windows this also applies to the browser surface alone if probe S29-08 finds no endpoint-port deny (§15.5); (f) the wrapper cannot be signed or cannot pass `G-GUI-12` on either OS — then the browser is the only surface and its badge becomes a blocking warning on first launch.

## 21. 8.1 for teams (from 18)

Team trust set (cross-signed roots; `signer` already on rows) · signed log-segment exchange between colleagues' kernels, merged by causal order (`logId` + `audience` already in the envelope) — 8.x linking (§3.9) carries briefs and co-work messages only, never log segments · shared Warpline fabric as the team's verifiable state of record (cross-kernel Warpline sync after T-2026-10-14-003, P8-D76) · internet reach for links through an overlay or the nevr.land relay (P8-D74) · approver roles + quorum (`approverPolicy` already on cards) · unattended grants (QM: never inherited; delegated messages are data) · credential broker + egress proxy · per-person/team cost rollups from metered permits (`org`/`team` already in scope) · read-only share snapshots with revocable links · voice transcripts shared text-only with consent · shared doodles as an `audience` projection with a CRDT layer (Automerge for its history fit) when the first colleague appears, and multi-human presence on the sheet · Gnomon as a QM decision provider and merge gate; neurons as a QM memory provider ("QM swarms admit they cannot merge") · launchd/Task Scheduler/service activation · containers on a separate host for oracles · Claude-Code-to-local-endpoint if Claude Code gains a first-party local mode (D37) · desktop wrapper distributed through the stores (notarized, signed; auto-update) · a community mod registry with the `signed:<gkey>` tier and a **verified** conformance badge for community GUIs (a nevr.land-run suite whose report the kernel can check — 8.0 shows only `declared`) · a private launch-token channel for Windows browsers if WebView2/Edge gain one · an envelope-contribution RPC for persona instructions if the skill route proves unreliable · Windows `full` sandbox via AppContainer/LowBox · a shorter or GUI-session-bound approvals unlock window if §A.1's trigger fires.

## A. Accepted risks (founder-only 8.0; each with a revisit trigger)

### A.1 Approvals and the cached unlock (P8-D38 supersedes the key-file part of P8-D12)

No approval-capable key is on disk: root and approvals keys are non-exportable hardware keys with presence (D39), which closes the C1/H1 escape class Aegis rated critical ("read `approve.key`"). The cached unlock is **bound to one human-class connection** (`connId`) and `always` is a fresh-presence decision (S25-H1, S25-C2), so the 8.0 rule is already the connection binding. Presence proves a touch, not what was approved, against a same-user process that can reach the key (S29-K2): such a process can show the kernel's exact wording and collect a real signature over its own content. macOS closes this with the helper's keychain access group (S29-01) plus D68's caller check — only the signed kernel reaches the helper; on Windows any same-user process can open an NGC key by name, so the phishing residual stands there (§A.3). What remains accepted: (a) the approvals key stays unlocked in the helper (an evaluated LAContext / authenticated NCrypt handle, S29-K5) for up to 15 idle minutes per attached connection (and never longer than 60 minutes or 64 signatures from the presence that opened it), so whoever **holds that connection's socket** during the window — the page's own JavaScript (a browser extension with all-sites access, S25-L1), a process that stole that connection's GUI token, or a same-user process that owns the device key and passed presence on its own connection — can obtain a **cached-class** decision (`tool.permission` allow/deny, `ask-user`, `plan`) on that connection — never a fresh-presence class (gate-class, install, `always`, KNOT resolve, verdict, cloud fallback, budget resume, api-token), and never from another connection; (b) the shell-ancestry check that flags such decisions is evaded by a process with its own TTY; (c) on Windows, read-denial inside sandboxes is `partial` until the helper's AppContainer path is proven, so a sandboxed session could read the device key (not an approval key) and reach the socket as the device — but it still cannot pass a presence prompt, so it gains no unlock, and it cannot reach the web endpoint's port or list other processes where the OS lets the profile deny it (D55); a sandboxed session holds no device key on macOS at all (S25 verdict); (d) the launch token lives for ≤ 60 s in the launching CLI's memory and on the private channel to the wrapper or browser, the GUI token in page memory for the connection's life, and the kernel public key as a pin in the page (public material) (§11.2); (e) on Windows the kernel key is in Credential Manager, same-user readable, so a same-user **unsandboxed** process could forge kernel-signed rows (`oracle.result`, `card.actions[]`, `serverSig`) until the helper's protected store is proven — `status.kernelKeyCustody: partial` says so (§A.3); on macOS custody is `full` only because the kernel is a Developer-ID-signed SEA and the XPC helper answers no other peer (P8-D68) — a kernel run any other way (from source, unsigned) reports `partial` and carries the Windows residual; root-signed and approval-signed rows are not forgeable without a touch on either OS, subject to the phishing residual above; (f) with voice approvals on (D58), a voice arm is a further, named exception to the per-connection binding — presence per short, counted window instead of per decision — whose residual is stated in §A.4; (g) (P8-D82, research 33) A same-user unsandboxed process can SIGSTOP the kernel and delete up to the last 1023 non-critical rows (≤ 1 s) on macOS without detection. On Windows (keystore `partial`), or with the helper absent, it can delete any number. Critical kinds are anchored before their effect, so they can be erased only before they take effect. Revisit: P04 cost data, or any second human. Mitigations: `presence` class, `connId` and `cli` provenance on every decision; `approval.suspect`; mandatory signatures and two anchors; kernel-owned namespaces; the wrapper as the default surface. Honest scope: detectable and attributable to "a holder of this connection during its unlock window", not prevented (`G-HON-06` enforces wording). Revisit trigger: before any second human, shared fabric, or 8.1 — and immediately on falsifier §20.10 — shorten or remove the cached window, or require presence per cached-class decision on browser-surface connections.

### A.2 Sandbox profiles, not containers (P8-D13; Windows per P8-R4)

Oracles and reviewer run under default-deny `sandbox-exec` profiles on macOS (deprecated by Apple, functional, escape-tested) and under a restricted-token + ACL + job-object backend on Windows modeled on deepseek-harness's rung (which itself reports `full|partial` and documents why AppContainer and mxc were rejected). Neither is a container; a profile that cannot be applied refuses rather than degrades. The reviewer profile is weaker than the oracle profile (Keychain/Credential Manager reachable for Claude's OAuth; Anthropic egress through the kernel's loopback proxy). Sessions are sandboxed for key access, settings writes and launcher escapes; mods run default-deny. p10m measured three macOS residuals of the `session` profile, all stated here:
- **(i) LaunchServices — ACCEPTED (P8-D79) and HARDENED (P8-D80).** The `session` profile denies `process-exec` of `/usr/bin/open`, `/usr/bin/osascript` and `lsregister` (by realpath; M1.7 verifies it by re-running p10m against the generated profile), so a session cannot use the stock launchers. LaunchServices itself has no working deny (no mach-lookup deny p10m tried stops it), so a launcher the session compiles itself and that calls LaunchServices directly still starts an app **outside** the sandbox; that self-compiled launcher remains the stated residual. An app escaped this way runs with **full same-user rights**, the reach of any unsandboxed same-user process (§A.1): it can read the device-key file and reach the socket as the device; it can write settings layers, which the layer watch turns into `gate-class` cards (§3.3); it can reach the endpoint port; and it can stop the kernel and delete the un-anchored log tail (§A.1(g)). Approvals still need presence: it cannot pass a presence prompt, sign with the hardware root or approvals keys, or reach the macOS kernel key past D68's code-signing check. The boundary is presence: Touch ID on Reconnect/attach and on every gate. The same class covers other launch paths p10m found reachable, namely Chrome's `SingletonSocket` (ask a running browser to open a URL) and the ssh-agent socket (use, not theft, of loaded ssh keys).
- **(ii) Same-user signals.** No SBPL rule covers signals. A session can signal any same-user process, the kernel and the transport child included, so it can kill or stop the daemon, but killing it gains no authority. The session's stdio closes, so its driver ends. A stopped kernel stalls the session once its stdout pipe fills. The XPC helper is launchd-managed, and its exit drops every unlock and arm (S29-K5). 8.0 has no external supervisor: the kernel comes back at the next client activation, and its orphan sweep logs `session.orphaned` (§2.4). This is an availability residual, and launchd activation (8.1) is the fix.
- **(iii) `ps` and unknown sockets.** `ps` cannot run in a session (setuid), so no claim rests on it. A user socket the generator does not know is reachable until a rule is proven for it; tmux is proven and screen is pending M1.7.
- **(iv) Copied launchers and persistence (M1.7, Aegis M1/M2).** A copy of `launchctl`, `osascript` or `open` does not bypass the path denies of (i) on macOS 15: launch constraints SIGKILL an Apple platform binary started from another path, sandboxed or not, and also after an ad-hoc re-sign (measured on darwin 24.6, exit 137, asserted by the session-sandbox test and the p10m-claude-compat-generated re-run). On macOS 14 (CI run 39100281741), and on macOS 15 with SIP disabled (GitHub's macos-15 runner, CI run 39100811385: the copied `launchctl` exited 1, not 137), a copy runs; the copied `osascript`'s check exited 0 from inside the profile (a copied `launchctl` could not submit a job: launchd refuses submit, bootstrap and list from any `sandbox-exec`'d caller). That exit 0 is not proof an AppleEvent was delivered — `tell application "System Events" to get name` is answered by AppleScript locally (measured 2026-10-17 under a deny-all `mach-lookup` profile: still "System Events", exit 0) — but it is not proof of a deny either. So the session profile **requires macOS 15+ AND SIP enabled** (P8 note): below either the darwin backend refuses to spawn a session at all (`sandbox-unavailable`, floor `macos` or `sip`; SIP read from `csrutil status` in platform code, cached); CI (SIP off) runs the floor refusal, and on macos-15 also RECORDS (never asserts) the copied-`osascript` exit under the generated profile regardless of SIP; no test claims to prove AppleEvent blocking — the only copied-launcher assertion is the 137 kill, on SIP-on macOS 15+. Defence in depth for senders the launch constraints do not kill: `(deny appleevent-send)` plus `(deny mach-lookup (global-name "com.apple.coreservices.appleevents"))` — its coverage of a non-Apple sender is unproven (OPEN 5). The self-built LaunchServices launcher of (i) remains the residual. **Copied-launcher protection therefore depends on three things, none of them ours: SIP enabled, macOS 15+, and Apple's per-binary launch-constraint coverage** (which platform binaries carry a constraint is Apple's choice per release; a binary without one runs from a copy even on a SIP-on macOS 15 host). The AppleEvents class reports `partial` in `status` while OPEN 5 is open. The session profile write-denies the claude binary (its realpath, its versions directory and every known-location link, Aegis H1), `~/Library/LaunchAgents`, the shell rc files (`.zshrc`, `.zprofile`, `.zshenv`, `.zlogin`, `.bashrc`, `.bash_profile`, `.profile`), `~/.gitconfig`, `~/.config/git` and `~/.ssh`; the kernel executes only a private copy of the pinned claude bytes under `<home>/bin` (the Gnomon home). Also write-denied: `.bash_login`, `.zlogout` and `~/.config/fish`. Accepted residuals, not write-denied: other repositories' `.git/hooks` and `.git/config`, `~/.local/bin` beyond the claude link, other tools' dotfiles and `~/.config/**` (editor, package-manager and language-toolchain configs), and login items registered through an app escaped per (i). `crontab` and `at` are setuid and cannot run under `sandbox-exec`. Revisit trigger: every macOS update re-runs the copied-launcher check (test/session-sandbox.test.ts and the p10m-claude-compat-generated probe), because the launch-constraint behaviour it relies on is Apple's, not ours; the probe report states that the old copied-`osascript` check ("reached System Events", CI runs 39100281741 and 39100811385) proved nothing — `get name` sends no AppleEvent.
- **(v) Inherited TCC grants and the pasteboard (Aegis, M1.7) — ACCEPTED RESIDUAL, OPEN 22.** TCC grants of the responsible process (Accessibility / Screen Recording / Input Monitoring) are inherited by sandboxed sessions: TCC attributes a request to the responsible process of the process tree (for a daemon started from Terminal or iTerm, that terminal app), so if gnomond is started from an interactive terminal holding those grants, a session can use them (synthesise input, capture the screen, read keystrokes). The shared pasteboard (`com.apple.pasteboard.1`) is reachable from the session profile (no deny; what a deny would cost Claude Code is unmeasured). No session probe measures either yet (founder task). Mitigation (recommendation, §2.4, §15.5): launch gnomond by launchd or the signed app, not from an interactive terminal, so the responsible process holds no TCC grants. Revisit trigger: the founder's decision on OPEN 22, and the 8.1 launchd/service activation.
- **(vi) Mod profile residuals (M1.11, Aegis C5) — ACCEPTED, stated.** File writes to a mod's `state/` and `blobs/` are granted for the whole run when ANY of its tools declares `write`. The RPC boundary scopes effects per call, but the profile cannot, so a read-only tool of a write-declaring mod can still write those two directories. The host never does file I/O inside them: its store is the sibling `state.db`. Before every start it refuses a `state/` or `blobs/` that is not a real directory at its expected path. A mod child also inherits the responsible process's TCC grants and the pasteboard, as in (v). Egress tunnels are closed when the call that opened them ends. A mod can also break its own `state/` or `blobs/` check, for example with a link or swap it plants inside them. The only effect is to block its own next start (`SANDBOX`, fail closed); it never widens the profile. A test shows the mod cannot rename `state/` itself, because the profile gives it no write on the parent.

Claude Code 2.1.302 works under the hardened profile (p10m-claude-compat). Aegis's dissent (jobs never on the key-holding box) stands recorded. Revisit trigger: as A.1, plus any public repo or untrusted contributor, plus any macOS release that changes `sandbox-exec` or LaunchServices behaviour (re-run p10m); target = rootless containers on a separate host (TD-735 §7).

### A.3 Windows fidelity

`WRITE_RESTRICTED` tokens restrict writes, not reads (S22-H8): until the signed helper proves an AppContainer/LowBox or dedicated-SID read-denial path and its tests are green on a Windows runner, Windows `session`/`mod`/`transport`/`oracle` profiles report `partial` in `status`, every oracle refuses to run (§15.5 rule), and the key split's "sessions cannot read keys" holds on Windows only for the hardware-backed root/approvals keys (unreadable by construction), not for the Ed25519 device/agent key files nor the kernel key's Credential Manager item (`status.kernelKeyCustody: partial`, §3.1). Process-information denial for the attach race (D55) is likewise `partial` on Windows where the OS offers no token-level denial; the endpoint-port filter (if probe S29-08 finds a mechanism, §15.5) and the presence prompt remain. Any same-user process can open a Windows Hello (NGC) key by name, so the presence-phishing residual of §A.1 has no Windows mitigation in 8.0 (S29-K2). Accepted for the founder's own Windows machine; 8.1 requires `full`.

### A.4 Voice approvals (P8-D58–D62)

Voice decisions are opt-in per project (root-signed toggles, all off by default) and need a presence-backed arm; inside a live arm the boundary is audio, not identity. With toggle 1 on and a reversible arm open, a person — or a recording, a call, a television — that the microphone hears saying a phrase in a voice the recognizer accepts as the founder's, outside our playback, can allow a tool call, answer an `ask-user` or approve a plan: up to 30 times in ≤ 60 min by default (≤ 240 min and up to unlimited decisions configurable, D64), each held 2–10 s behind an echo the founder may not be watching; reversible targeting rests on the client-side guards and the kernel's echo cross-check, not on a secret. With toggle 2 on and a gate arm open, the same person who can also read the arming screen or hear its headphones can pass the handle and the 2-word challenge and approve up to 3 gate-class decisions in 5 min by default (≤ 60 min and up to unlimited decisions configurable, D64), in push-to-talk or conversation mode (D65); with the judgment sub-opt-in, a KNOT resolution or a verdict — a content judgment the founder may not have read — rests on hearing alone, and the challenge proves who spoke, not that the diff was read. Speaker recognition is not authentication: a voice clone or a live relay passes it, and nothing in sherpa-onnx detects spoofing. The playback, TTS-state, route and speaker checks are claims of the voice client, not verified by the kernel; they stop accidents, not a compromised client. If the child is a socket client (`armCustody: client`, Windows only; macOS has no client custody, TD-2026-10-19-194), a replaced voice binary can be armed by an unwary touch where the peer check is unavailable. On Windows, capture exclusion and audio-render detection are `partial` until the M4.1 probe proves them, and mic denial for sessions is not enforced. What the kernel does verify: each arm began with a hardware presence prompt naming the pair; only the paired voice connection used it; gate and judgment decisions echoed single-use secrets no session, mod, voice or browser connection could read; nothing hand-only was decided; every voice decision is `presence: voice-armed` and signed in its own domain. Detectable and attributable to "a speaker accepted inside arm `<armId>`", not prevented. Toggle 2 is a deliberate trade of the fresh-presence guarantee for speed, chosen by the founder under a warning, and is stated that way rather than called presence. Revisit trigger: any falsifier-14 detection; a second human in the room or on the machine; before any second human principal or 8.1 — then voice gate arms are removed or replaced by companion presence per decision.

### A.5 Kernel linking (P8-R9, P8-D73–D76)

A paired peer is a trust relationship, not a sandbox (§3.9). What the kernel verifies: the peer holds the link key pinned at a pairing that both humans confirmed with presence and a matching SAS; every frame is authenticated end to end, in order and within the grant's scopes; nothing a peer sends reaches a verb, a decision, a permit or a presence prompt; every peer byte a model reads is logged before the read, tainted and flagged; nothing labelled `secret`, nothing on the never-cross list and — to a teammate — nothing above `team`/`normal` leaves without a fresh-presence card. What is accepted: (a) **injection across kernels** — brief instructions, co-work text and attachments are untrusted content that may carry what a peer session was itself injected with; envelopes, receipts, taint and the `peer` flag make it visible and keep it away from voice, `always` and cached teammate approvals, and the residual is the reading model (threat 4); a human who accepts a malicious brief starts a session on it. (b) **A teammate can lie**: the name shown is self-asserted (marked unverified), and data sent is the recipient's — revocation is not retroactive, and the recipient kernel's redaction is that kernel's honesty, not ours. (c) **Labels and the secret scan**: egress is only as good as the append-time labels (§9.1 defaults) and a best-effort pattern scan; a mislabelled or undetected secret in an item the human allowed crosses. (d) **The SAS** rests on the human comparing six words on two screens. (e) **The relay** sees link-key hashes, who talks to whom, when and how much, and can drop traffic; store-and-forward items are sealed with Noise `X`, so a later theft of the receiver's link key opens queued items still held (≤ 7 days). (f) **A stolen machine**: link verbs rest on root presence, which on macOS falls back to the login password unless `biometryCurrentSet` is required (OPEN 18); within a grant's lifetime (≤ 30 days) a thief who passes presence can accept briefs and send under the founder's grants until a revocation from another machine lands. (g) **Windows custody**: the link key on Windows is `partial` like the kernel key (§A.3), so a same-user unsandboxed process could read it and impersonate that kernel to its peers; Windows kernels are limited to `self` links until the helper's protected store is proven. Detectable and attributable to "peer `<rootKeyId8>` over link `<linkKeyId8>`", not prevented. Revisit trigger: the first teammate pairing; any falsifier-15 detection; before internet reach (an overlay or nevr.land) — then re-review pairing, the relay and the egress filter against an untrusted network. §A.1's trigger "a second human" means a second **local** principal; link peers are covered here.

### A.6 Kernel-log recovery window (P8-D84)

Accepted dev-only. Until M1.3, the keystore anchor is a stub that reports `unavailable`, so no authoritative boot session exists. Every truncation therefore classifies as `tamper` and cannot be acknowledged (§3.2): a log that loses its tail, whether to a power cut or to an attacker, refuses to open. The escape is `gnomon log quarantine` (§3.2): the home moves aside intact as evidence and a new log starts with a signed `daemon.log.quarantined` genesis row; until M1.3 its human gate is an interactive terminal confirmation only, recorded as `authorizedBy: 'interactive-confirm'`, which is no proof of presence: any same-user process that owns a terminal can pass it. The founder ruled out any in-place downgrade, because a downgrade path is exactly what an attacker would use. Windows keeps the window after M1.3 until its helper supplies a boot id and an authenticated anchor (§16). Revisit trigger: T-2026-10-17-009 lands (quarantine, authenticated keystore anchors, seam hardening). It is the release gate for any second user (§19.1).

### A.7 Transport-child compromise (M1.4, Aegis)

Accepted. The web endpoint's transport child relays every GUI frame, so code execution inside it is a compromise of the GUI sessions it carries: it can read and forge their frames and act as the human principal with the GUI token those sessions hold. It cannot reach a key, the kernel log or the home, cannot mint a launch token, and cannot answer presence (the `transport` profile, §15.5). On macOS it opens no network socket of its own (it receives connected sockets from the kernel, §15.5): every bind, listen and outbound connect it attempts is EPERM (P14b-web, T-2026-10-18-020), so a compromised child cannot be reached from the LAN. The Windows `transport` profile is the Windows parity phase (P8-D78). Two kernel-side residuals of the hand-off, accepted: (1) Node's IPC reader parses the child's fd-3 bytes as newline-delimited JSON with no length bound before the kernel's guards run (any message kills the child), so a compromised child can make the daemon buffer and parse an arbitrarily long line — a denial-of-service surface only, it steers no kernel logic; (2) after `stop()` or an endpoint that went down (restart cap, failed respawn) the port is released, so another local process can bind it before the next `gnomon open`; a page opened later goes to the port in its new launch URL, and an old tab's reload reaches whatever squats the old port (no token, no kernel key, §11.2 rules 2–6 still apply to the real endpoint). The exposure is bounded by that profile, the 12 h session cap and `gnomon open --revoke-all`; the kernel kills the child on the first record out of shape. Revisit when approvals from the GUI land (M1.6): a presence-class decision must not be satisfiable by a forged frame alone. On a Homebrew Node build the profile also grants read of Homebrew's `Cellar/` and `opt/` (Node links about twenty dependency kegs) and its `etc/openssl@3`, so the child can learn which Homebrew formulae are installed; it reads no user file there.


### A.8 Live relay of hello+auth (P8-D93, Aegis)

Accepted residual. clientNonce closes replay of a recorded hello. It does not bind the transport. A same-uid or root process that can rename the kernel's unix socket and listen at its path can relay a live hello+auth and hold a connection authenticated as the victim (frames carry no MAC). The web port is narrower: the relay must present the identical host:port. Tracked: T-2026-10-18-024. Revisit trigger: T-2026-10-18-024 (transport binding), and before M1.3 Reconnect ships on a non-loopback path.

### A.9 Audited repo hooks pin the command, not its scripts (P8-D94, Aegis)

Accepted residual. An approval pins a repo hook's command string (its id hashes the source layer, event, matcher, command and timeout). It does not pin the files that command runs. A hook such as `bash scripts/check.sh` stays approved when `scripts/check.sh` changes, whether by a repo change, a pull, or an edit made from inside a session, because the worktree is writable. Every repo-hook card states this in a kernel-written line. Tracked: T-2026-10-18-027 (hash the repo-relative files a command references into the hook id). Revisit trigger: T-2026-10-18-027, and before an untrusted contributor's repo is opened under `audited`.

§14.6's list binds `G-VOX-08..16` to `#gnomon-voice` by the ancestor rule; §16's voice-approvals row adds `G-VOX-11` and `G-VOX-12` to `~windows-parity`; `G-GUI-13` reaches `#gui` from §11.7. §2.1's table is the first appearance of most symbols — including `#web-endpoint`, `#workshop-mod`, `#gnome-persona-mod`, `#doodle-mod` and `#knowledge-query` — and `G-KRN-01`, `G-KRN-02` and `G-SEC-01` sit in §2.1 prose, so they bind to every node mentioned there (section-level rule); §2.1's prose deliberately names no other id, so the transport child's isolation test is referenced there by rule number, not by id. `#gui` first appears in §1 (non-goals); `#backend-adapter` in §2.2, whose bullets also name `#gui`, `#gnomon-voice`, `#claude-adapter` and `#local-runner`, so `G-ADP-01`, `G-ADP-02` and `G-GUI-05` land on all five; `~windows-parity` in the preamble's platform rule (which is why it carries `G-HON-01`); `$voice-turn` in the §14.2 heading, whose ids (`G-VOX-02`, `G-HON-07`) also land on `#local-runner`, named in that section; `$doodle-session` in the §12.7 heading beside `#doodle-mod`, so both carry `G-DDL-01..05` (§12.7 names the CSP fixture by rule number, not by id, so `G-WEB-06` stays off the Doodle nodes). §3.4's heading names `#approval-broker` and `#budget-fuse`, so its prose `G-BRK-05` binds to both. §7.3's certification paragraph is prose, so its ids bind to every node mentioned in §7.3 — `^certified` and `#oracle-sandbox` (named in the `test` row). §8.10's heading names `#knowledge-query`, so `G-KNW-01..04` bind there alone; §9.1's `inject` row names the learning loop in plain words on purpose, so §9.1's prose `G-HON-03` does not reach `#learning-loop`. Inside §11, only §11.2 (`#web-endpoint`) and §11.6 (`#workshop-mod`) name a node; every other §11 subsection takes the ancestor path to §11's heading node `#gui`, which is why `#gui` carries `G-WEB-01..08`, `G-GUI-11`, `G-GUI-12` and `G-HON-07` (from §11.1 and the §11.7 falsifier list) and `#web-endpoint` carries `G-GUI-07` and `G-GUI-11` (rule 6 and §15.1 row 16), `G-SBX-08` (rule 2b), `G-WEB-07` (rule 1b, also §4.9's WebSocket paragraph, which names `#web-endpoint`) and `G-WEB-08` (rules 1a and 2a). §12.5's prose names `#mod-host` (`G-MOD-08`, `G-MOD-09`, `G-MOD-11`); §12.6 names `#gnome-persona-mod` (`G-GNM-01..03`); §12.2, §12.3, §12.4 and §12.8 name no node and have no noded ancestor, so their ids are orphans. §19.x tables bind row-by-row (Scope spans) plus the `$move-N` heading node, which is why `#socket` and `#web-endpoint` share M1.4's ids (incl. `G-WEB-01..08`), `#mod-host` and `#workshop-mod` share M1.11's (`G-MOD-01..11`, `G-WSP-01`), `#manifest-loader` carries M1.5's `G-WIN-04`, `#session-runner`, `#supervisor` and `#claude-adapter` share M1.7's (now with `G-SBX-08` and `G-WIN-06`), `#scheduler` and `$daemon-lifecycle` share M1.9's (with `G-WIN-02`), `#oracle-sandbox` carries M2.3's (with `G-WIN-06`), `#learning-loop` M3.1's, `#knowledge-query`, `#gnome-persona-mod` and `#doodle-mod` M3.7–M3.9's; M1.13's row names one node span, `#wrapper-channel` (`gnomon-wrapper` itself is a package, not a symbol), so its ids reach `$move-1` and that node; §19.1's entry-preconditions paragraph names the contrast fixtures by section, not by id, so nothing from it binds to every row's nodes. §13.2's `G-SUB-01` and §9.7's `G-HON-*` reach `#local-runner` and `~ample-internal-logs` through their parent headings (ancestor rule). §10's law table has no heading node, so rows without a node span (laws 1–6) take the ancestor path to `orphanTests`, while the law-7 row binds its `G-SUB-*` ids to `^subscription-only`; §16's table binds through its `~windows-parity` heading (`G-GUI-03`, `G-GUI-12`, `G-WEB-01..08`, `G-MOD-08`, `G-MOD-11`, `G-SBX-08` among them); §15.1's row 16 names `#web-endpoint` and binds `G-WEB-01..08` + `G-GUI-07` + `G-GUI-11` to it, while its other rows (incl. rows 18 and 19) are orphans. §5, §12.2–12.4, §12.8, §15.1 (rows without a span), §15.5, §17, §18's `Deliverable:` line, §20, §22 and §A.1 mention ids in sections with no node and no noded ancestor, so those ids are orphans. The suffixed range `G-SBX-04w..06w` appears only in §15.5 (and in §0, which is skipped) and expands with its suffix; §7.1 rule 4 names the ordinals rather than an id on purpose, so the rule's example does not bind to `#contracts-compiler`. `T-` ids are telemetry, never oracles. §0 (with §0.1–§0.8 inside it) and this appendix are skipped; the `Deliverable:` line is in §18.
